- **2026-09-29 · The dev seed gives `admin@smog.test` a dev-only password (`smog-dev-admin`).** `scripts/seed.ts` hashes it with Better Auth's scrypt format (`<hex salt>:<hex key>`, N=16384, r=16, p=1, 64-byte key, NFKC) and a fixed salt, so `seed/dev.sql` stays deterministic. It replaces any other credential of that user. A `@smog/auth` test applies the real seed and signs in with it. Supersedes the "admin has no password" part of the seed entry above. Alternative: OTP/magic link only (slower manual testing and e2e).
- **2026-09-29 · CSRF defence in depth on `/api/rpc` and `/api/openapi`: an origin check before anything else.** SameSite=Lax on the session cookie stops cross-site POSTs, but not a same-site, cross-origin page (another Worker on the same `workers.dev` subdomain, a sibling subdomain, another `localhost` port), and oRPC accepts CORS-simple bodies (`multipart/form-data` with a `data` field, urlencoded, `text/plain`), so a plain HTML form was a valid rpc call. `checkOrigin` (`@smog/rpc`, first in `rpcHandlerOptions`, so both transports get it before `RL_API` and the session read) answers a non-GET/HEAD request with a defined `FORBIDDEN` (403) when `Sec-Fetch-Site` is present and is neither `same-origin` nor `none`, or when `Origin` is present and is not `new URL(SITE_URL).origin` (in `dev`, any `http://localhost:<port>` is also accepted, for the Expo web and tooling ports). A request with neither header (the native app, curl, the SSR server client) passes: it carries no ambient cookie. The predicate is exported as `isForeignRequest(request, env)` for cookie-authenticated endpoints outside oRPC: the `/api/analytics` relay (spec §12, phase 4) must apply it too. Consequence: each environment's `SITE_URL` must be the origin browsers use (as for Better Auth's `trustedOrigins`). Alternatives: oRPC's `SimpleCsrfProtectionHandlerPlugin` + link plugin (a custom header forcing a preflight; needs a Scalar exemption and client wiring in both apps), rejecting non-JSON bodies (blocks the phase 7 logo upload). Why: Fetch Metadata plus Origin covers every current browser, needs no client change and keeps the native app working.

- **2026-09-30 · Staging Turnstile test-key hostname.** Cloudflare's always-pass test secret returned `hostname: example.com` on the deployed staging password-reset flow. Better Auth now allows that synthetic hostname only when `ENVIRONMENT=staging` and the secret exactly equals Cloudflare's always-pass test secret. The site hostname remains allowed; production and real-key staging retain the site-only allowlist, and unsuccessful Siteverify responses are still rejected. Handler regression tests cover those boundaries. Alternative: require a real widget on staging (incompatible with the existing always-pass staging setup), or disable hostname validation (unnecessarily broad).

## App shells (phase 2 task 9)

- **2026-09-29 · One auth flow for both apps: `useAuthFlow` + `createFlowActions` in `@smog/auth/react`.** The flow is a reducer (`authFlowReducer`: email → method → password / code / inbox → done) plus commands (`authFlowCommands`: validate, `started`, run the action, dispatch the outcome), tested with plain calls in `src/use-auth-flow.test.ts` (`bun test src`). `createFlowActions(client, options)` binds the calls to either Better Auth client through a structural `FlowAuthClient` type (`guard/client-types.ts` checks both clients satisfy it), sends the Turnstile token as `x-captcha-response` on every captcha-guarded call and asks for a fresh token after each (`onCaptchaUsed`), and maps Better Auth error codes to `auth.errors.*` keys (`authErrorKey`). Each app keeps only its UI (kit components) and its platform bits: passkeys on web, the native Apple sheet on iOS, how a social sign-in ends (`socialFlow: redirect | session`). Alternatives: a component per app with its own flow logic (written twice), or a shared headless component (kits differ too much between web and native). The password bounds (8/128) and the code length (6) are now constants in `fields.ts`, set explicitly on the server and checked by the forms first.
- **2026-09-29 · Web theme and language are cookies read on the server.** `theme` (`system|light|dark`) and `locale`, one year, `Path=/`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` outside dev, written by the `setTheme` / `setLocale` server functions. The root route's loader (`getShell`: locale, theme, session user, public auth config, dev-tools flag; `staleTime: Infinity`, re-run by `router.invalidate()` after a change) gives `<html>` its `lang` and the `dark` class in the server HTML. `system` adds a one-line inline script in `<head>` that sets `dark` from `prefers-color-scheme` before the first paint; `<html>` carries `suppressHydrationWarning` for that one attribute, and `useSystemDark` follows later changes. `color-scheme` comes from `@smog/styles/theme.css` (`:root` light, `.dark` dark). Changing the language also saves it on the signed-in user (`updateUser({ locale })`) for emails. [Phase 4 fix wave A] Now through `account.updateProfile({ locale })`; `/update-user` is not routed. The web theme is not mirrored into local-store: the server needs it on the first request, and a cookie is the only thing it sees. Alternatives: localStorage + a blocking script for every theme (flashes when the script is late, and SSR cannot know the theme), or a `?lang` prefix (spec §9: no locale prefix).
- **2026-09-29 · The shell seeds the auth state from the server session.** `AuthStateProvider` on the site gets a session hook that returns the loader's user while Better Auth's client is still pending, so a signed-in user never sees "Sign in" flash in the header; after that the client's `useSession` is the source (sign-in and sign-out update it at once). `/account` checks the session in `beforeLoad` (`getSessionUser`) and redirects to `/sign-in?redirect=/account`. `?redirect=` only accepts same-site paths (`safeRedirect`, tested against `//host`, `/\host` and full URLs).
- **2026-09-29 · `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` is a public var; `publicAuthConfig` says which methods to show.** `workerVarsSchema` gains an optional `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` (empty means unset). `publicAuthConfig(env)` in `@smog/config/env/worker` returns `{ google, apple, turnstileSiteKey }` (a provider shows only when its client id and secret are both set); the site reads it in the root loader and the app through a new public procedure, `system.authConfig`. Staging sets Cloudflare's always-pass test site key, which pairs with the test secret staging may use; production gets the real key before launch (without it the web shows no widget and the captcha plugin rejects the guarded calls, so a missing key fails visibly, never open).
- **2026-09-29 · Mobile auth gaps, confirmed.** (1) Turnstile: with `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` set, Better Auth's captcha plugin refuses the Expo app's email calls (sign-up, password sign-in, codes, resets) because it sends no token; `packages/auth/test/captcha.test.ts` now proves it. There is no safe exemption: the Expo client identifies itself only with `expo-origin` / `origin` headers, which any script can send, so exempting them would switch the captcha off for everyone. The app therefore shows no widget, and in staging/production its email methods fail with "security check failed" until the phase 4 WebView widget (a site page with the widget, whose token the app passes on) lands; Apple (native idToken) and Google (browser session) are not captcha-guarded and work. (2) Magic links are hidden in the app, and the verification and reset links point at the site: those links open in the browser, which gets the session, and the app cannot adopt a browser session yet (Better Auth's Expo plugin appends `?cookie=` to deep-link redirects, but only its OAuth session reads it). After verifying, the user signs in in the app with the password or a code. (3) Passkeys stay web only (earlier entry).
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage` 2.2.0 (catalog), not 3.x.** The local-store's native adapter and the app use Expo SDK 57's pinned version: expo-doctor's SDK check fails on 3.1.1, and a JS package newer than the native module it talks to is a runtime risk. `@smog/local-store` moves its peer and dev dependency to the catalog entry. `expo-apple-authentication` ~57.0.2 (the SDK 57 version) joins the app for Sign in with Apple on iOS (`usesAppleSignIn`, config plugin).
- **2026-09-29 · A user without a name is shown by their email.** Code and magic-link sign-ups create users with an empty name; `toAuthState` falls back to the email, so the avatar and menus always have a name (axe caught an unnamed avatar).
- **2026-09-29 · Header links to pages that do not exist yet are plain links.** Gestures, Favorites, Lists and Sponsor (and the footer's Privacy and Terms) arrive in phases 3–5; typed router links can only point at routes that exist, so these are `<a href>` for now and 404 until then. Search is an icon button to `/gestures`.
- **2026-09-29 · `/dev/ui` uses the root providers.** The gallery no longer creates its own i18n, toast and tooltip providers (two toast regions would share one name); the page language is the shell's, and `?lang=` still overrides it for the gallery subtree. The e2e browser runs with `locale: "nl-BE"`, so Accept-Language keeps the pages Dutch as before.
- **2026-09-29 · Mobile app shell.** `AppProviders` creates one Expo auth client, one oRPC client (with its cookie), one QueryClient and the native local store; theme (`setColorScheme`) and language (`setupNative({ preference })`) follow the stored preferences. Tabs are Expo Router's native tabs (SF Symbols on iOS, Material symbols on Android); the auth screens are a modal stack; Settings has language, theme, the account entry and the version row that unlocks the developer tools after 5 taps (dev and staging builds; it stays unlocked until the app restarts). Local runs need `apps/mobile/.env` (from `.env.example`), since the clients are now created at start.
- **2026-09-29 · Shell keys added (task 9).** `nav.gestures`, `nav.legal` (the footer nav's name), `a11y.userMenu`, `home.tagline`, `footer.madeBy` (the credit line, a whole sentence per locale), `auth.noAccount` / `auth.haveAccount` / `auth.signUpLink`, `account.*` (placeholder page), `settings.*` (section titles) and `devTools.unlockHint` (plural) / `devTools.unlocked`.
- **2026-09-29 · Site Vitest: a warm-up hook instead of a long test timeout** (revised in the Task 9 review). The first request per test file transforms the whole server entry (the app shell and the kit included): ~25 s alone, over 60 s under `turbo run test`. `test/warm-up.ts` requests `/` in a `beforeAll` under `hookTimeout: 180_000`, so `testTimeout` stays 60 s and a hung test still fails fast.
- **2026-09-29 · `safeRedirect` checks its output, not only its input** (Task 9 review C1). Dot segments collapse `/.//evil.com`, `/a/..//evil.com` or `/%2e//evil.com` into `//evil.com` once `URL` normalises them. The helper rejects backslashes, control characters and, in the path, encoded `/`, `\`, dot segments and control characters (`%2f`, `%5c`, `%2e%2e`, `%09` …) before parsing; parses against a probe origin and requires the same origin; and re-checks the normalised `pathname + search + hash` (no `//`, `/\`). Encoded slashes stay allowed in the query (`?q=a%2Fb`). `/verify-email`'s Continue navigates with the router (`navigate({ href })`), a second same-site layer. Every attack input is in `test/redirect.test.ts`.
- **2026-09-29 · `TextLink` in both kits** (Task 9 review I1). `@smog/ui-web` `TextLink` (`tone` primary/default/muted, `asChild` via Radix Slot to style a TanStack `Link`, the spec focus ring, hover underline, the 44 px `hitArea`), and `@smog/ui-native` `TextLink` (a pressable `Text`, `accessibilityRole="link"`, 44 pt `hitSlop`). Every text link in both apps uses it (auth switch, forgot password, footer credit, the header logo). Added in new files; only the kits' `index.ts` and `API.md` gained a line. Alternative: a `link` variant of `Button` (a button box does not sit inside a sentence).
- **2026-09-29 · The guest import sheet is mounted in both app roots.** `GuestImportSheet` (site `components/`, mobile `src/`) renders `useGuestImport()` from `@smog/account/client`: the prompt with the counts ("3 favorieten en 1 lijst importeren?"), Import / Not now (`dismiss`), the error line, then the result (imported favorites and lists, `skipped` unknown gestures, `overLimit` with `LISTS_MAX` / `LIST_ITEMS_MAX`). Closing the sheet hides it for this page or app run only; "Not now" and "Done" dismiss it for this account on this device. The two files share their shape but not code: they are thin kit renderings of the one hook (the hook, counting and import logic live in `@smog/account`).
- **2026-09-29 · Auth errors are wired to their input.** `authErrorField(error)` (`@smog/auth/react`) names the input an `auth.errors.*` key is about (email, name, password, repeat, code); the forms pass it to that `Field` (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`, `role=alert`) and show only step-level errors (rate limit, captcha, generic) as a separate alert. The code's description is the code field's hint. Mobile "resent" notices are polite live regions.
- **2026-09-29 · Recorded for later tasks** (Task 9 review). CSP: the constant `SYSTEM_THEME_SCRIPT` needs a build-time `sha256-…` in `script-src`, TanStack Start's inline scripts need the router's `ssr.nonce`, and Turnstile needs `challenges.cloudflare.com` in `script-src` and `frame-src`; the security-headers task must add them or the no-flash theme silently breaks. QueryClient: it is created per document in the root component today (no cross-request leak); phase 3's SSR prefetching moves it into `getRouter()` (router context) with `@tanstack/react-router-ssr-query`. `/account` reads the session twice on SSR (`getShell` and its `beforeLoad`); the guard can reuse the root loader's user once router context carries it. Passkey conditional UI (`autoFill`) is not wired, so the email input no longer claims `webauthn` autocomplete.

## Email

- **2026-09-29 · React Email 6 (`react-email` 6.11.0) as one package.** `@react-email/components` is deprecated; React Email 6 exports the components and `render` from `react-email`. Templates are JSX, so interpolated user data is escaped although i18next runs with `escapeValue: false` (tested with a `<script>` name); links must be http(s). `renderEmail(template, props, locale)` returns `{ subject, html, text }` (plain text via `render(…, { plainText: true })`).
- **2026-09-29 · Email senders.** `createEmailSender({ environment, binding, kv })`: `dev` → `DevEmailSender` (logs `[email] …` and keeps the latest 50 messages in the KV key `dev:mail`, newest first); `staging`/`production` → `CloudflareEmailSender` on the `send_email` binding `EMAIL` (in `env.staging`/`env.production`, with `allowed_sender_addresses: ["noreply@smog.vlaanderen"]` as a second safeguard; the sender parses `Name <address>` into the binding's `{ name, email }`). `MemoryEmailSender` is for tests. Every send goes through `sendEmail()`, the one seam that moves to the email queue in phase 6. `/dev/mail` (HTML list; bodies in sandboxed `srcdoc` iframes) and `/dev/mail.json` (`{ messages: StoredEmail[] }`, newest first, `sentAt` in ms; the e2e reads it) exist **only when `ENVIRONMENT` is `dev`** and are 404 otherwise. That fails closed: the mailbox holds sign-in links and codes, and staging sends real email, so its mailbox would be empty anyway. **Deviation from spec §9** ("/dev/mail (dev + staging only)"). `release-config-check` also requires every wrangler env's `vars.ENVIRONMENT` to equal its name.
- **2026-09-29 · The email theme is resolved from `@smog/styles` tokens.** `packages/email/src/theme.ts` reads the light colour roles, spacing, radius and type scale from `@smog/styles/tokens` and turns them into inline `px`/hex values (email clients have no CSS variables and no reliable dark mode). The one non-token value is the 560 px email width.

## Analytics

- **2026-09-29 · [rule change: less data] Signed-in users are identified by user id only**; email and name are no longer sent to OpenPanel.

## i18n

- **2026-09-29 · Unified language fallback `nl`** on both apps (the old web used nl, native used en). The detected device or browser language is used when it is en/fr/nl.
- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/i18n` shape: one `translation` namespace typed from `nl.json`, three entry points.** `.` (catalogues, `resources`, `TranslationKey`, `resolveLocale`, `createI18n`, `formatDate`, `formatList`; safe in the Worker), `./native` (`setupNative`, the only file that imports `expo-localization`, an optional peer) and `./react` (re-exports `I18nextProvider`, `Trans` and `useTranslation` from `react-i18next`, and loads the `CustomTypeOptions` augmentation). Kits and apps import the hooks from `@smog/i18n/react`, so there is one `react-i18next` version and the typed keys always apply. `createI18n` returns a new synchronously initialised instance (`initAsync: false`, inline resources, no detector, `useSuspense: false`); both apps pass it to `I18nextProvider` instead of the global `initReactI18next` plugin, which would share one instance across SSR requests. `Locale`/`LOCALES`/`DEFAULT_LOCALE` stay in `@smog/config/constants` and are re-exported. Alternatives: namespaces per feature (later phases can add them through `ResourceNamespaceMap`), or kits depending on `react-i18next` directly (the augmentation could be missing from their program).
- **2026-09-29 · Locale resolution order: device preference, cookie, `Accept-Language` (by q-value), device locales, `nl`.** `resolveLocale` takes an extra `preference` source (the locale stored in local-store preferences on mobile) ahead of the brief's `cookie`/`acceptLanguage`/`device`. Matching is on the primary subtag (`fr-BE` → `fr`, `EN_us` → `en`); `q=0` entries are ignored.
- **2026-09-29 · Catalogue parity is checked on base keys; plurals follow CLDR per locale.** i18next resolves plurals through `Intl.PluralRules`, and French has a `many` category (for example 1 000 000), so fr plural keys carry `_one`/`_many`/`_other` while nl/en carry `_one`/`_other`. `catalog.test.ts` compares key sets after stripping plural suffixes, requires exactly the locale's CLDR categories on each plural key, and compares `{{placeholders}}` per base key (`count` excluded). A missing key in en/fr is also a type error (`resources` `satisfies Record<Locale, { translation: typeof nl }>`).
- **2026-09-29 · The guest import prompt is composed, not one two-count sentence.** `auth.import.prompt` = "{{items}} importeren?", with `auth.import.favorites` / `auth.import.lists` as plural pieces joined by `formatList` (`Intl.ListFormat`, falling back to the catalogue's `common.and` where Hermes lacks it). This skips zero counts ("12 favorieten importeren?" instead of "12 favorieten en 0 lijsten"). Alternative: one sentence with i18next nesting (`$t(…, {"count": …})`), which cannot drop a zero part.
- **2026-09-29 · Dates are formatted in `Europe/Brussels` with `nl-BE`/`en-BE`/`fr-BE`** (the same Intl locales as `formatMoney`), so the Worker (UTC) and the device render the same day.
- **2026-09-29 · Kit keys added in phase 2 (task 7).** `a11y.notification` (Radix's per-toast label; `a11y.notifications` names the region), `a11y.morePages` (pagination ellipsis), `a11y.steps` and `a11y.stepCompleted` (Stepper), and `devTools.gallery.*` (sample copy for `/dev/ui`), in nl/en/fr. The kit's other strings already existed (`kit.*`, `a11y.*`, `states.*`).

## Guests

- **2026-09-29 · Guest → account import lands with the phase 3 features.** `account.importGuestData` and the `importGuestData()` client helper need the favorites and lists services, so phase 2 only ships `@smog/local-store` (the data it will import and clear).
- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/local-store` shape.** Mutators are pure `(data) => data` functions passed to `store.update` (`store.update(toggleFavorite(id))`), so they are testable without a store and `update` serialises them through a promise queue. `preferences.locale` defaults to `null` (follow the device) rather than `nl`, so a guest who never chose a language still gets device detection. The native adapter loads `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage` (an optional peer) lazily, so importing it never fails on web or in tests; `createAsyncStorageAdapter(fake)` is the testable core. The React hook returns the defaults on the server and during hydration, so SSR markup always matches; the store then re-renders with the hydrated data.
- **2026-09-29 · `@smog/local-store`: a failed persist is not retried.** When the adapter write fails, `store.update` logs `[localStore] Failed to persist data` and rejects (the caller shows or logs it). The change stays in memory only until the next `update` (or a storage event) runs `sync()`, which reloads what storage holds and so drops it; memory and disk do not drift apart for long. By design (phase 2 task 4). Alternative: keep the change and retry the write later (a guest would keep seeing data that is lost on the next launch). Why: storage is the source of truth for guest data; a visible failure beats a silent loss.

## Gestures and search (`@smog/gestures`)

- **2026-09-29 · One definition of a `gesture_fts` row, in `@smog/db` (`rebuildGestureFtsSql`).** The row is rebuilt from the tables (`DELETE` + `INSERT … SELECT` name, keywords, **published** category names, description), so statements placed after the writes of a D1 batch index what that batch wrote. `@smog/gestures/server` `reindexGesture` / `reindexGestureStatements` run it; the dev seed renders the same statements into `seed/dev.sql` (`SQLiteSyncDialect`), so the seed stays in `packages/db` and imports nothing from a feature. The gesture id is inlined as a quoted literal (quote doubling), because drizzle-orm 0.45's D1 `batch()` cannot bind the parameters of a raw `db.run(sql)` statement (it reads `stmt.bind` on an object without `stmt`). Unpublished category names are not indexed, so publishing, unpublishing or renaming a category must reindex its gestures (admin, phase 5). Alternatives: the builder in `@smog/gestures/server` with the seed moved to a root `scripts/seed-dev.ts` (root scripts type-check with Bun types only, and importing `./server` pulls rpc/auth/Workers types into them); a TS row builder from values (cannot see the other writes of the same batch).
- **2026-09-29 · Ranking details beyond spec §7.1.** Queries and field values are compared as their letter/digit tokens joined by one space (`normalizeQuery`: `normalizeText`, then split like FTS5 `unicode61`), so a word boundary is "start or after a space" and `"hond!"` equals `hond`. A query with no tokens (only spaces or punctuation, e.g. `"*-(`) is an empty query: the browse list. A multi-word query that matches no single value as a whole (words in different fields or out of order, which FTS5's `AND` finds) still matches at the word-boundary tier, weighted by the field of its weakest word; without it, such FTS hits would be dropped. The typo tier compares name, keywords and categories only (the spec's projection; descriptions would add noise), with the optimal-string-alignment Damerau–Levenshtein distance. Ties after the name use the id, so the order is total. `search` keeps the best 200 FTS candidates by `bm25` (column weights 10/5/3/1, like the field weights); `total` counts direct matches (≤ 200) plus typo matches; the browse list has `score` 0 and null `matchedField`/`matchType`. Typo matches on the page are hydrated with one `byIds`-style query, so the sponsored-video override is always current.
- **2026-09-29 · `catalog:version`: a random id per bump, a fixed sentinel when missing, never written by reads.** `bumpCatalogVersion(kv)` writes `newId()` and is the only KV write; KV has no atomic increment, so two concurrent counter bumps could both write n + 1 and leave an isolate on a projection it built between them. A missing key (a new namespace) reads as `INITIAL_CATALOG_VERSION` (`"initial"`) with no write, so the first searches after a deploy never hit KV's one-write-per-second-per-key limit. The typo tier is best effort: if KV or the projection load fails, `searchGestures` logs `[gestures] Failed to load the typo tier, …` and answers with the direct matches. Only the resolved projection (plain data) is cached per isolate; no in-flight promise is shared, because in workerd awaiting a promise whose I/O belongs to another request can hang or throw once that request ends. Concurrent cold misses therefore each load the catalogue, which is cheap at its size. Other isolates see a bump within KV's propagation delay (up to a minute); only typo matches for the change are delayed, because the direct tier reads D1. Writes outside the services must bump too: `seed:dev` puts a new version in the local KV, and the Convex data migration (phase 8) must bump or its warm isolates keep the old projection. Alternatives: an incrementing counter (as first sketched); initialising the key on the first read (a write on the read path).
- **2026-09-29 · Catalogue order is `gesture.sort_name, id` with a seekable keyset.** `sort_name` is `gestureSortName(name)` (`normalizeText`: lowercase, no accents), set by every writer (the gestures/admin services, the seed, the data migration, the `makeGesture` factory); the column has no SQL default, so a Drizzle insert without it does not type-check. SQLite `NOCASE` folds only A–Z (`Één` sorted after `Zus`), SQL cannot strip accents, and D1 cannot register a collation. Index `gesture_published_sort_name_idx (sort_name, id) WHERE published_at IS NOT NULL` (migration `0002_gesture_catalog_order`, which replaces the `(published_at, name)` index; it was amended in place because no remote D1 had applied 0002; it adds the column with a `''` default only for the `ALTER`, and backfills `lower(name)`, which a seed or data-migration run replaces with the exact value). list, the empty-query browse list, the sitemap, the projection and related-gesture ties sort by it; search ties still use `Intl.Collator("nl")` on display names (they agree on accents; `sort_name` has no tertiary level). The cursor predicate is `sort_name >= ? AND (sort_name > ? OR id > ?)` because SQLite seeks the index on that form and scans it for the row-value form `(sort_name, id) > (?, ?)`; a query-plan test asserts the seek. Cursors are an opaque base64url JSON of `[sort_name, id]` (`encodeCursor`/`decodeCursor` in `@smog/utils`, reusable by favorites and lists), capped at 1024 characters in the contract; one that does not decode is `VALIDATION` (`fieldErrors.cursor`). Categories (tens of rows) still sort by `sort_order, name COLLATE NOCASE`.
- **2026-09-29 · `RpcContext.kv` (the `KV` binding).** The catalog version key needs KV in the procedures; the spec's context list (§7) had no KV. `makeRpcContext` stubs it (tests pass `env.KV`).
- **2026-09-29 · Correlated subqueries name both sides.** Drizzle renders columns unqualified in single-table selects, so `${gesture.id}` inside a subquery resolved to the inner table (wrong counts, missing sponsored video). The gestures queries alias inner tables and qualify the outer `gesture` row explicitly (`ref(alias, column)`, names still taken from the schema).
- **2026-09-29 · Small public-read choices.** `bySlug` prefers a slug over another gesture's legacy id, and credits a sponsor only when the running sponsorship has `ends_at`; `related` returns `[]` for an unknown slug (no extra lookup); `categories` is capped at 100 and `sitemap` at 50 000 rows (the sitemap protocol limit); `byIds` dedupes and keeps the order asked. List filters and `byIds` bind one JSON parameter (`json_each`), so D1's 100-parameter limit never applies.
- **2026-09-29 · Known tokeniser and ordering gaps.** `normalizeQuery` strips every combining mark (`normalizeText`), while FTS5 `unicode61 remove_diacritics 2` keeps marks in non-Latin scripts and treats emoji as token characters. The two sides can therefore disagree for Devanagari or emoji queries; this does not matter for the nl/en/fr catalogue. The spec's "or by the first category when asked" browse order (§7.1 step 1) is not offered yet, because no screen needs it; add an `order` input when one does. A typo match from a stale projection that has been unpublished since is dropped at hydration, and `total` subtracts it.
- **2026-09-29 · Hook tests run on Bun with happy-dom.** `bun test --preload ./src/testing/happy-dom.ts src` (`@happy-dom/global-registrator` 20.14.5, `@testing-library/react` 16.3.3 + `@testing-library/dom` 10.4.2); the fake provider is a real oRPC client (`createRouterClient`) over an in-memory implementation of the contract, so inputs and outputs are validated as in production. `@tanstack/react-query` 5.104.0 joins the catalog; features take it as a peer (the apps own the `QueryClientProvider`). Stale times: catalogue, detail and related 5 min, categories 1 h, search 1 min. `useGestureSearch` debounces only the text (250 ms); category changes apply at once; filters are sent sorted so equal filters share a cache entry. The client entries and the bun tests type-check without Workers types (`tsconfig.client.json`).

## Favorites (`@smog/favorites`)

- **2026-09-29 · Favorite summaries come from `@smog/gestures/server`, injected by `@smog/api`.** `favorites.list` returns `GestureSummary[]`, whose SQL (sponsored video, published categories) lives in `findGesturesByIds`. A feature may not import another feature's `./server` (spec §4.1), so the router is a factory: `createFavoritesRouter({ findSummaries })`, and `appRouter` passes `findGesturesByIds`. A page is two D1 queries: the favorite ids (keyset), then their summaries. Lists can reuse the pattern. Alternatives: copying the summary columns into favorites (written twice, and the sponsored-video rule could drift); moving them into `@smog/db` (touches gestures and db while lists is built in parallel).
- **2026-09-29 · Features may import another feature's `./contract`.** Both `./schema` and `./contract` are client-safe (spec §4.1, amended), so `@smog/config/boundaries` allows `@smog/feature:*/contract` for features (never `./server` or `./client`). The favorites hooks type `gestures.byIds` with `GesturesContract` from `@smog/gestures/contract`. Alternative (the first version): a mirror of `byIds` in the favorites contract with a drift test in `@smog/api`, which wrote the procedure twice.
- **2026-09-29 · Adding needs a published gesture; removing always succeeds (controller ruling).** `add` and `toggle` (when it adds) answer `NOT_FOUND` for an unknown or unpublished gesture and write nothing (the check and the guarded `INSERT … SELECT` run in one D1 batch). `remove` is an unconditional, idempotent `DELETE` of `(user_id, gesture_id)` and always returns `{ favorite: false }`, so a stale heart (a gesture unpublished while its page is open) can always be cleared; this deviates from the task brief, which had `NOT_FOUND` on `remove` too. `toggle` deletes first (`DELETE … RETURNING`) and adds only when nothing was deleted, so it removes a hidden favorite as well; it is not atomic (two concurrent toggles can both add), which is why the hooks send `add`/`remove`. `ids` and `list` skip favorites of unpublished gestures but keep the rows, so republishing brings them back. Deleting a gesture cascades.
- **2026-09-29 · Favorite order is `created_at DESC, gesture_id DESC` on `favorite_user_created_idx`.** Migration `0003_favorite_user_created_idx` adds the index `(user_id, created_at, gesture_id)` (drizzle-kit generate); it covers the ids and the order, so `ids` and a `list` page seek it without a sort (a query-plan test asserts the covering-index search and no temp B-tree). The cursor is `encodeCursor([createdAtMs, gestureId])` with the range form `created_at <= ? AND (created_at < ? OR gesture_id < ?)`. `ids` is capped at `FAVORITE_IDS_MAX` (5000, far above the catalogue), so the whole heart state loads in one call. If lists also adds a 0003, the controller renumbers at merge.
- **2026-09-29 · `useFavorites` details.** While auth is `loading` the hook is `loading` and queries neither source (no guest flash). Signed in, a flip sends `add` or `remove` for the state the user saw (idempotent, so a second tab cannot invert it). The optimistic state is the pending flip mutations (`useMutationState`, in call order) laid over the cached server ids: `Mutation.state.variables` is set synchronously by `mutate`, so a second tap in the same tick reads the first and the last tap wins; a failed flip rolls back by leaving the pending set (and `toggle` rejects); an ids refetch in flight cannot overwrite a newer tap; `onSuccess` writes the confirmed flip into the cache so nothing flickers before the refetch. Every flip shares one mutation `scope` (`favorites`), so the server applies them in tap order (a slow `add` cannot land after a later `remove`); per-gesture scopes are not possible with one `useMutation` (its scope is fixed). `favorites.ids` and `favorites.list` are invalidated once the last flip settles (`isMutating === 1`). Query keys carry the user id, and once auth is known the hook removes every favorites query keyed by another user id, so a sign-out or an account switch drops the old account's cache. `items` filters by the current ids, so a removal hides its card at once. Guests' items are `gestures.byIds` pages of 100, newest first, keyed by the whole id order (`keepPreviousData` avoids a flash). `useFavorites({ items: false })` skips the list for a heart button. Alternatives: `setQueryData` in `onMutate` with `onError` rollback (the first version: two taps in one tick both read the old cache, and `cancelQueries` reverts an in-flight fetch over the optimistic write); the `toggle` procedure from the client (a stale client flips the wrong way).

## Lists (`@smog/lists`)

- **2026-09-29 · List items get their gesture summaries from `@smog/gestures` by injection.** `@smog/lists/server` exports `createListsRouter({ findSummaries })` (the option name favorites uses), and `@smog/api` passes `findGesturesByIds` from `@smog/gestures/server`; a feature never imports another feature's server, and the summary SQL (sponsored-video override, published categories) stays written once. The guest hooks call `gestures.byIds` through the app client, typed with `Pick<GesturesContract, "byIds">` from `@smog/gestures/contract` (features may import each other's `./contract`; the earlier shape copy is gone). Guest detail calls it in chunks of 100 (the `byIds` limit), keyed by the sorted id set, so a local reorder or removal does not refetch. Alternatives: copy the summary columns into lists (written twice); move them into `@smog/db` (a shared-package change while favorites was built in parallel; still possible later); hydrate accounts' items on the client (one more round trip, and the contract returns summaries).
- **2026-09-29 · [rule change] At most 100 lists per account and 500 gestures per list.** `create` and `addItem` answer `INVALID_STATE` at `LISTS_MAX` / `LIST_ITEMS_MAX` (in `@smog/lists/schema`). The limit is part of the write, not a pre-check: `create` is `INSERT … SELECT … WHERE (SELECT count(*) FROM list WHERE owner_id = ?) < 100 RETURNING`, and the add's `INSERT … SELECT … HAVING count(*) < 500`; no row back means full (or, for an add, that a concurrent add of the same gesture won, which a follow-up read tells apart and answers `{ added: false }`). Concurrent creates or adds at the edge therefore make exactly one more (tested). Guests get the same limits on the device (`INVALID_STATE`-shaped `ORPCError`), so an import never meets a guest list the account could not hold; the guest import (task 4) should cap lists at `LISTS_MAX` rather than 50 and keep a same-name merge within `LIST_ITEMS_MAX`. Migrated data (phase 8): the Convex migration must report any account over 100 lists or list over 500 items before import; an over-long list is split into consecutive lists (`Name (2)`, …) of at most 500, so no gesture is dropped and every list stays reorderable. `mine` returns every list in one bounded read, and `get`, `shared.get` and `reorder` stay bounded (phase-3 constraint: every list query is bounded). The old app had no limits; the guest import (≤ 50 lists of ≤ 500 ids) fits. Alternative: paginate `mine` (the contract has no cursor, and no screen pages lists).
- **2026-09-29 · Positions, unpublished gestures and concurrency.** An add is one `INSERT … SELECT coalesce(max(position) + 1, 0) … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (plus the `updated_at` bump) in one batch, so concurrent adds get distinct dense positions and a repeated add is a no-op (`{ added: false }`). A remove shifts the rows after it down and deletes the row in the same batch (`{ removed }`). The owner's add reads ownership, publication and presence in one query, so it is one read plus one batch. A reorder must be exactly the list's **published** gestures (what the owner sees; missing, extra or duplicate ids → `INVALID_STATE`); unpublished ones keep their relative order after them, and only the payload's and the hidden rows are rewritten, so an add landing between the read and the write keeps its `max + 1` place. Unpublished gestures are hidden from `get`, `shared.get` and `itemCount` but stay in the table (they reappear when republished); adding an unknown or unpublished gesture is `NOT_FOUND`. A deleted gesture's `ON DELETE CASCADE` leaves a gap in the positions until the next reorder (the order stays correct). A full re-densify with `UPDATE … SET position = (correlated count over list_item)` was rejected: SQLite gives no snapshot guarantee for a subquery over the table being updated.
- **2026-09-29 · Share links and the shared view.** `share.create` inserts with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (the partial unique index `list_share_active_role_uq`) and reads the active link in the same batch, so concurrent creates agree on one token; `share.revoke` sets `revoked_at` (a no-op without an active link); regenerating is `useShareLinks().regenerate(role)` = revoke + create. `shared.get` is public and batches the head (list, role, owner name) with the items resolved from the token, so an unknown or revoked token (or a list id tried as a token) is `NOT_FOUND`. `ownerName` is `user.name` trimmed, else `lists.ownerFallback` in the request locale; the email and ids are never returned. `shared.addItem/removeItem` check the session first (`UNAUTHORIZED`), then the token (`NOT_FOUND`), then the role (`FORBIDDEN` for a view link); an editor's add records `added_by` = the editor. `url` is `SITE_URL` (trailing slashes dropped) + `/lists/` + token. D1 batch rows are keyed by column name, so the joined `list.name` / `user.name` are aliased (`list_name`, `owner_name`).
- **2026-09-29 · Lists hooks.** `useLists()` (`{ lists, status, error, create }`), `useList(id)` (`{ list, status, notFound, error, update, remove, addItem, removeItem, reorder }`), `useShareLinks(id)` (`{ requiresAccount: true }` for guests and `loc_` lists, else `{ links, status, create, revoke, regenerate }`) and `useSharedList(token)` (`{ data, status, notFound, canEdit, requiresSignIn, addItem, removeItem }`). A `loc_` id always reads the device; a server id for a guest is `notFound` without a call. Guests' `create` / `update` apply the contract's Zod rules (trim, 1..80, ≤ 280, empty description → none). A guest `reorder` follows the server rule: the payload must be exactly the shown ids (those with a published summary), else an `INVALID_STATE` `ORPCError`; ids without a summary (unpublished, deleted, unknown: a device list has no cascade) keep their order after them. Accounts: `removeItem` and `reorder` are optimistic on the cached detail and roll back on any error (then refetch), but only while the cache still holds their own edit, so a later overlapping edit is not replaced by an older snapshot; `addItem` refetches (there is no summary to insert). Mutations invalidate the whole `lists` key (the detail, the `mine` counts and the share flags change together). Stale times: lists and details 1 min, a shared list 30 s. Typed errors (`NOT_FOUND`, …) are not retried. A local list's `itemCount` counts the ids on the device (unpublished ones included; the detail shows only published ones).

## Account (`@smog/account`)

- **2026-09-29 · The guest import composes the favorites and lists statement builders, injected by `@smog/api` (review fix I1).** `@smog/lists/server` exports `insertListsStmt` (`INSERT … SELECT … ORDER BY key LIMIT max(0, LISTS_MAX - count)`), `appendItemsStmt` (appended at `max(position) + row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY list)`, insertable pairs only, while `size + row_number() <= LIST_ITEMS_MAX`, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), `touchListsWithNewItemsStmt` and `unplacedItemsStmt`; `@smog/favorites/server` exports `insertFavoritesStmt` (the published guard, `created_at` one millisecond apart ending at `at`). These return statements that have not run yet. `createList`, `addItemToList` and `addFavorite` are now their single-row case, so each rule (cap inside the write, dense positions, when `updated_at` moves) is written once. The lists and favorites tests are unchanged. `createAccountRouter(deps)` takes them as `ImportDeps`, typed structurally as functions returning a `RunnableQuery` (no import of another feature's server), and `appRouter` wires them the way it passes `findSummaries`. The account owns only the read batch, the name matching and planning, and the consent statement. The import is still one read batch plus one atomic write batch (at most six statements), each statement binding one JSON array. The account's D1 tests run the real builders: `test/deps.ts` imports them by relative path, because a feature may not depend on another's `./server` and `@smog/api` has no D1 in its tests. It is the only such import, and test-only. Alternatives: the import's own copy of the SQL (the first version; the lists rules were written twice); fake builders in the tests (they would not test the composed statements); a boundaries exception for feature tests (a checker change for one file).
- **2026-09-29 · Import semantics and result.**
  - Favorites are deduped and inserted oldest first, so the device order survives as the newest-first order.
  - Lists match on `name.normalize("NFC").trim().toLocaleLowerCase("und")` (JS, since SQLite `lower()` folds A–Z only). With two same-name account lists, the most recently changed one wins. Two same-name guest lists merge into one. A merge appends the missing items in order and ignores the guest description. A merged list's `updated_at` moves only when it received items.
  - `skippedUnknownGestures` counts distinct unknown or unpublished ids; the client adds the malformed ids it never sent.
  - [deviation, review fix I2] The result has `lists`, one outcome per guest list in the order sent: `{ status: "created" | "merged" | "notCreated", unplaced: gestureId[] }`. `notCreated` means the account is at `LISTS_MAX`, and a guest list that folds into such a list is `notCreated` too. `unplaced` are published items that did not fit under `LIST_ITEMS_MAX`. The counts `listsOverLimit` (the `notCreated` ones) and `itemsOverLimit` stay.
  - `listsMerged` counts `merged` outcomes, so a second, identical import answers `listsMerged > 0` with nothing added.
  - The input takes up to `LISTS_MAX` (100) lists rather than the brief's 50, per the lists decision above.
- **2026-09-29 · Concurrent duplicate imports (review M1): guarded per tab, accepted across tabs.** Two imports of the same device data running at once each plan a new list with its own id, so they could create two same-name lists. `useGuestImport` keeps one import in flight per user (a second `accept` gets the same promise, and another user gets `null`). Two tabs that share localStorage and both accept within the same request window can still duplicate a list. Nothing is lost when that happens (duplicate names are allowed), and the next import merges into the newest. A server-side exact-name guard in `insertListsStmt` would change `create`, which allows duplicate names on purpose.
- **2026-09-29 · Imported consent is dated when the guest decided.** The `consent_event` row (purpose `analytics`, source `import`, `policy_version` = `@smog/config/constants` `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` `"2026-09-29"`) gets `created_at = min(decidedAt, now)`, so the current state (the newest row) stays a newer decision the account already made. It is skipped when the account has an analytics row at least that recent, or when its newest row is already this import (same value and policy), so a retry never appends it twice, even with a device clock ahead. Alternative: `created_at = now` (an old device choice would override a newer account choice).
- **2026-09-29 · Client import details.**
  - What `importGuestData({ store, client, untitledListName? })` sends:
    - unique, well-formed favorites (the oldest 1000; the rest stays for the next import);
    - the first `LISTS_MAX` lists, with names and descriptions trimmed and cut to the list limits by code point (never splitting an emoji, measured in UTF-16 units like Zod);
    - for each list, its unique well-formed ids (at most 500);
    - the consent choice once the guest decided.
  - [review fix I3] Bad local data never fails the call. Ids that fail `gestureIdSchema` are dropped from the payload and counted into `skippedUnknownGestures`. An empty name becomes `untitledListName` (the hook passes `auth.import.untitledList`; the default is `—`).
  - On success it clears what the server stored:
    - favorites, by id, together with any malformed ids;
    - lists that were `created` or `merged`; a list with `unplaced` items stays with only those items, and a `notCreated` list stays whole;
    - the consent choice, if `decidedAt` still matches.

    Anything added during the call stays, as do recent searches and preferences. On failure it logs and rethrows, and the device is untouched.
  - `useGuestImport()` returns `{ pending, status, result, accept, dismiss }`:
    - `pending` is set only when the auth state is `signedIn` (never while `loading`), the store has loaded, the device has well-formed favorites or lists, and the user id is not in `preferences.importDismissedFor`.
    - `accept` never rejects (it resolves `null` and sets `status: "error"`). It keeps one import in flight per user. On success it invalidates the `favorites` and `lists` queries.
    - `dismiss` never rejects (it logs).
    - A rejected `store.ready` is logged.
    - The status resets when the user changes.
  - `@smog/local-store` data is now version 2: `preferences.importDismissedFor` (`dismissImportFor(userId)` keeps the newest 20), added by migration `MIGRATIONS[1]`. The storage key stays `smog:guest:v1`, so no data moves.
  - i18n adds `auth.import.skipped` (plural), `auth.import.overLimit` and `auth.import.untitledList`.
- **2026-09-29 · Account deletion is Better Auth `deleteUser`: the password, or a fresh session without one (phase 4).**
  - `createAuth` enables `user.deleteUser` without `sendDeleteAccountVerification`. Typing `DELETE` is the confirmation.
  - [review fix I1] The HTTP routes are off: `disabledPaths: ["/delete-user", "/delete-user/callback"]` makes them 404 (tested), and `auth.api` calls are not affected. `account.delete` is therefore the only way in, so the typed `DELETE` and the rate limits cannot be skipped.
  - [review fix, minor] An account with a password (a `credential` account with a hash) must send it, even on a fresh session (`PASSWORD_REQUIRED`, 400). A stolen session alone never deletes it. An account without one (passkeys, Google, Apple) needs a session younger than `freshAge`, which Better Auth checks (`SESSION_NOT_FRESH`, 403: sign in again).
  - `session.freshAge` is pinned at one day. That is Better Auth's default, and it is global: it also gates listing sessions, unlinking a provider and adding a passkey, so a shorter window would force a re-sign-in on those too.
  - `account.delete({ confirm: "DELETE", password? })` calls `auth.api.deleteUser` with the request headers, so Better Auth reads the session again. The procedure adds three errors to the shared map: `PASSWORD_REQUIRED`, `SESSION_NOT_FRESH` and `INVALID_PASSWORD` (400, a wrong password).
  - [review fix I2] The procedure is rate-limited twice through `RL_AUTH` (5/60 s in staging and production): once per IP (`<ip>:account.delete`) and once per user (`user:<id>:account.delete`). Better Auth's own limiter does not see `auth.api` calls. With the per-user key, a stolen session used from many IPs still gets at most 5 password guesses a minute. A dedicated binding with a lower limit was the alternative. It was not taken because the binding only offers 10 s or 60 s periods, so 3/60 s would barely differ from 5/60 s, while it would need a new binding in every wrangler env plus `RATE_LIMIT_BINDINGS`.
  - Nothing runs before or after the delete. Better Auth deletes the sessions, the accounts and the user, and the foreign keys cascade the rest. Tests cover passkeys, favorites, lists, items, shares and consents; `audit_log.actor_id` and `list_item.added_by` become NULL. Sponsor rows have no user reference, so they stay.
  - The client (`useDeleteAccount({ signOut })`) then signs out (a failure is only logged), resets the whole local store, clears the query cache and refetches the session.
  - Alternatives: an email confirmation link (Better Auth's `sendDeleteAccountVerification`), or a shorter `freshAge`.
- **2026-09-29 · Consent state and the hook.**
  - `account.consent.get` returns the newest analytics row. Rows in the same millisecond are ordered by rowid. `policyVersion` is the version that row refers to (`null` while undecided).
  - [review fix, minor] `needsDecision` (in `consent.get`, `consent.set` and `useConsent`) is true while undecided, or when the newest decision is a yes given under another `policyVersion` than `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` (`needsConsentDecision` in `./schema`). A no under an older policy stays a no, because nothing is sent either way.
    - Bumping `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` therefore asks every signed-in user who said yes again. Bump it only when what analytics processes changes.
    - While a yes is outdated, `useConsent` returns `analytics: null` (nothing is sent until the user says yes again) and does not mirror it to the device.
    - The local store keeps no policy version, so a guest is asked only while undecided.
  - `consent.set` takes `source: web|mobile` (default `web`). The hook sends `mobile` when `navigator.product === "ReactNative"`.
  - `useConsent()` returns `{ status, analytics, needsDecision, set }`. For a guest it uses the local store. Signed in, the server log is the source and a decided server state is mirrored to the local store. While the account has no decision, the device's choice is left alone (the guest import carries it) and `analytics` is `null`.
  - `analytics` is `null` while `loading` or on `error`, so a gate built on it never sends too early.
  - A failed `set` rejects and changes nothing on the device.
- **2026-09-29 · The export format (version 2).**
  - Dates are ISO 8601 strings, because the file is for people. The API elsewhere uses epoch milliseconds.
  - Sign-in methods are Better Auth provider ids with their link dates, plus passkey names and dates. The export never holds a token, a password hash, a public key or a credential id.
  - Share links are the active ones only, with their URLs. The URLs come from `@smog/lists/server` `shareUrl`, which `@smog/api` injects like the import's builders.
  - [review fix, minor] Share links are bearer links: anyone with one opens the list. The export is therefore sensitive, and the UI must say so next to the download (i18n `account.export.sensitive`).
  - Sponsorships are grouped per checkout (sponsor contact, invoice request, and each sponsorship's gesture, display name, `hasLogo`, status and dates). There are no payment ids, amounts or logo keys. They are matched by `lower(sponsor.email) = lower(user.email)` only when the email is verified. The comparison is case-insensitive because sponsors type their address, so `sponsor_email_idx` goes unused (a small table).
  - The invoice rows come from their own query: D1 batch results are keyed by column name, so a join with the sponsor's `name` and `email` would mix them up.
- **2026-09-29 · `updateProfile` writes `user.name` and `user.locale` with Drizzle.** It does not go through `auth.api.updateUser`, because Better Auth keeps no session cache here, so the next session read sees the change, and the Zod contract already validates both fields (name trimmed 1..80, locale `nl|en|fr|null`). The hook refetches the session after a save.
  - [review fix, minor] This depends on "no cookie cache" (`@smog/auth` `createAuth`, and Auth above). If `session.cookieCache` is ever enabled, a name changed here stays stale in the session for up to its `maxAge`. At that point, either route this write through `auth.api.updateUser` (which refreshes the cached session) or clear the cache cookie after the write.

## Consent UI and account screens (phase 4 task 3)

- **2026-09-30 · `ConsentBanner` is one kit component on both platforms.** The spec's inventory (§16) names a `ConsentBanner`, so both kits export it with the same choices (`onAllow`, `onDecline`, `busy`). On the web it is a non-modal labelled region fixed to the bottom (`inline` for the gallery), with no close button: nothing is sent while undecided, so the page stays usable. On native it is a bottom sheet (`open`, `onDismiss`, `onOpenPrivacy`); dismissing it leaves the decision open and hides it until the next launch. The two choices are the same size (Allow is filled, Only necessary outlined), as in the old app. Both apps show it while `useConsent().needsDecision`, which also covers a signed-in yes under an older `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION`. The save step (`useConsentChoice`: busy, a boolean result, never rejects) is in `@smog/account/client`, so the apps add only the toast. The mobile harness now defaults to a decided device, so the other screen tests never see the sheet. Alternatives: a web-only name (`ConsentSheet` on native), or a modal banner (it would block the site for a choice that has a safe default).
- **2026-09-30 · A device's consent choice reaches the account at sign-in, and the prompt waits for the import sheet (review I1).**
  - When the account never decided (`consent.get` has no `decidedAt`) and the device has a choice, `useConsent` sends it by itself (`importGuestConsent`, the import procedure with only `consent`, source `import`). Before, only the guest-import sheet carried it, and that sheet shows only with favorites or lists, so the user was asked again.
  - While that is on its way, `needsDecision` is `false` and `analytics` stays `null` (nothing is sent). After the server answers, `consent.get` is read again (`fetchQuery`) before the device copy is cleared: the server may have kept a newer decision instead (another device), and the device value is never written into the cache. One carry per user and store runs at a time (all `useConsent` instances share it). A failed carry is logged and retried on the next consent read (focus, reconnect) in the same session [re-review M-a, M-b].
  - [re-review N1] Only a guest's own choice is ever carried or imported. The device consent has an optional `mirroredFrom` (the user id) when it is a copy of an account's decision (the mirror and a signed-in `set` write it; a guest's `setConsent` has none). On a shared device, user A's mirrored choice therefore never reaches user B's log: B, whose account never decided, is asked. The field is additive and optional, so stored v2 data still parses. The consequence, accepted: a guest's newer "no" on this device after the account's older "yes" is not sent; the account's decision wins. [Phase 4 fix wave B, I7] A guest no longer reads the copy as consent: it counts as undecided, so the guest is asked (see that section).
  - A yes under an older policy is never carried: the account asks again, as before.
  - `useGuestImport().accept` also refetches `account.consent` (the sheet's import may have carried it).
  - `useConsentPrompt()` (`@smog/account/client`) is the prompt's state for both apps: open while `needsDecision` and no guest import is offered, so the import sheet and the consent prompt are shown one after the other, never stacked. `useConsentChoice({ onSaveFailed })` is the switches' save step.
- **2026-09-30 · The web banner reserves its height (WCAG 2.4.11, review I4).** While it shows, an in-flow spacer of its measured height (`ResizeObserver`) follows the page, so the footer can be scrolled into view, and `html` gets `scroll-padding-bottom` of the same height, so a focused control is never scrolled under the banner. The e2e checks with `elementFromPoint` that the footer links at 390 px are uncovered, scrolled to the bottom and when focused.
- **2026-09-30 · The mobile export file does not stay on the device (review I3, re-review M-d).** iOS: `shareJsonFile` deletes the cache file in a `finally`, because `shareAsync` resolves once the sheet closes. Android: `shareAsync` can resolve while the receiving app still reads the content URI (for example a background upload to Drive), so deleting it there could break the share. The file stays until the next `sweepExportFiles()`: at launch, before the next export is written, and when the account is deleted (in a `finally`, even when the sign-out throws). At most one export is ever on the device. A timed delete was the alternative, but no delay is safe for a slow receiver.
- **2026-09-30 · Account form logic is headless in `@smog/account/client` (review I6).** `useChangePasswordForm(actions)` (fields, the order of the checks, which field an error belongs to), `useProfileForm(account)`, `useDeleteAccountForm`, `useActionFeedback` (an action's toast, including "sign in again") and `useActionErrorMessage` hold the rules once. The apps keep the markup, their kit's toast and their router. The section owns one `useSignInMethods`, so one method change runs at a time (review M6). On mobile, "linked" is said only once the profile read after the browser flow shows the link, because a closed browser also resolves without an error (review M10).
- **2026-09-30 · `E2E_PORT` moves the site e2e off 5173.** Several checkouts on one machine each start a dev server, and `reuseExistingServer` would run the tests against another checkout's server. With `E2E_PORT` the config starts `vite dev --port <port> --strictPort` and the helpers use that origin. `.dev.vars` then sets the matching `SITE_URL` (it overrides the `wrangler.jsonc` var). CI keeps 5173.
- **2026-09-30 · Sign-in method logic is shared in `@smog/account/client`.** `useSignInMethods({ client })` (change password with `revokeOtherSessions: true`, link, unlink) and `usePasskeys({ client })` (list, add, remove) take structural Better Auth client types, as `@smog/auth`'s flow actions do. The web and Expo clients both fit them, so the package imports neither. They never reject: a result carries an `AccountActionError`, and `accountActionMessage` / `deleteFailureMessage` give both apps the same i18n keys. Better Auth 1.7 unlinks by account row id (`unlinkAccount({ accountId })`), so `unlink(provider)` reads `listAccounts` first. `SESSION_NOT_FRESH` becomes "sign in again": signing out and opening sign-in, then coming back to the account.
- **2026-09-30 · Unlinking follows Better Auth's rule: the last account stays.** Better Auth refuses to remove the user's last `account` row (`FAILED_TO_UNLINK_LAST_ACCOUNT`), and passkeys are no account. `canUnlink(methods, provider)` mirrors that (a password, Google or Apple must remain), and the screens disable Unlink with "this is your only sign-in method" instead of letting it fail. A user whose only account is Google but who has a passkey therefore cannot unlink Google. Email codes and links always work for a verified address, so nobody is ever locked out. Alternative: `allowUnlinkingAll` (Better Auth would then allow an account with no row at all).
- **2026-09-30 · "Set a password" is the reset flow.** An account without a password (migrated, code, link, Google or Apple) opens `/forgot-password` (mobile: the forgot-password screen). That page already has the Turnstile widget the reset request needs, and the reset creates the credential account (spec §6). Changing an existing password uses `changePassword` in a dialog (web) or an inline form (mobile).
- **2026-09-30 · One language control: the display language is the user's language (review I2, ruled).** The brief listed a language under Profile and under Preferences. Two controls fought: the site's display switch overwrote `user.locale` behind the Profile select, and mobile never wrote it. Now there is one control, under Preferences. For a signed-in user it also writes `user.locale`, which is the language of our emails: on the site through the root's `setLocale` (`updateUser`; [Phase 4 fix wave A] now `account.updateProfile`, like mobile), on mobile through `useAccount().updateProfile({ locale })` (the device default, "Device language", writes `null`). The Profile section has only the name and the read-only email. A hint under the language says so when signed in (`account.preferences.languageHint`).
- **2026-09-30 · Guests get `/account` too.** The route no longer redirects guests to sign-in. It shows a sign-in prompt, the device preferences and the analytics switch (guest data never leaves the device, so there is no export or deletion). On mobile, settings keeps the switch and the privacy link, a signed-in user opens `settings/account` from the settings row (sign-out moved there), and a guest's row goes straight to sign-in.
- **2026-09-30 · Deletion: sign out, read the session again, then clear everything (review M1).** `useDeleteAccount` resets the local store, awaits `useAuthState().refresh()` (new, an awaitable `refetch`), then calls `queryClient.clear()` (queries and mutations; the mobile persisted cache follows it). Clearing while the session still read signed in made the mounted `account.me` and `consent.get` refetch for the deleted user (401s in the e2e). The apps leave the screen only after the deletion resolves (web `/`, mobile back, or home when there is no back stack), so the next page's user queries are already off. The dialog's state (`typed`, `password`, `ready`, staying open while the call runs, the refusal's words) is `useDeleteAccountForm` in `@smog/account/client` (review I6), and the apps render it with their kit. A refusal keeps the dialog open with the reason, and `SESSION_NOT_FRESH` offers "Sign in again".
- **2026-09-30 · [dep] `expo-file-system` ~57.0.7 and `expo-sharing` ~57.0.22 for the mobile export.** These are the SDK 57 pins from `bundledNativeModules.json`, and app-only, so they are not in the catalog. The export is written to the cache directory as `smog-export-YYYY-MM-DD.json` (the new `File` / `Paths` API) and opened in the share sheet (`application/json`, UTI `public.json`). The old app shared the JSON as text (`Share.share`).
- **2026-09-30 · Kit fix: `SegmentedControl` segments grow from their content (`flex-auto`).** From `sm` up the control is `w-auto`, and the segments' `flex-1` (basis 0) squeezed them, so "System", "Dark" and "Nederlands" truncated (on the account page and in the header's sheet). With `flex-auto` a full-width control still fills its row, and an auto-width one fits its labels.
- **2026-09-30 · Jest in `apps/mobile` and `@smog/ui-native`: `testTimeout` 10 s and `maxWorkers: "50%"`.** On a 4-core machine, turbo runs both jest packages (3 workers each by default) and the Workers vitest pools at once. The first render of a suite (expo-router, NativeWind, transforms) then passed jest's 5 s default, so `release:check` failed on existing suites (`app-shell`, `screens`, the `chip` `beforeAll`) that pass alone. Uncontended, every test is under 1 s, so 10 s still surfaces a real slowdown early (review M3).
- **2026-09-30 · Passkeys stay web-only (D-PASSKEY).** The mobile account screen does not mention them. The site lists them with their name (or "Passkey") and date, and adds and removes them. Better Auth requires a fresh session to add one.

## Legal pages, app links and legacy redirects (phase 4 task 4)

- **2026-09-30 · Legal texts are one TypeScript module per language in `@smog/i18n/legal`.** `src/legal/{nl,en,fr}.ts` each hold both documents (`privacy`, `terms`) as sections with stable ids (the same anchors in every language) and blocks: a paragraph, a list, an address (`lines`) or the place of the analytics switch (`consentControl`). Inline text uses two marks only, `**bold**` and `[text](href)`, parsed by `parseLegalInline` (hrefs must be a site path, `https://` or `mailto:`, else the text stays plain). The site renders them with kit typography (`LegalPage`: numbered sections, a contents list, the switch in the cookies section). Alternatives: Markdown files (a parser dependency for two pages) or i18n JSON keys (hundreds of keys, and the catalogue test would not keep lists aligned). A test keeps the three languages aligned (same section ids, same block shapes and list lengths, the same links except the per-language Belgian DPA site). Dutch prevails: en and fr show `legal.translationNotice` with a Dutch-labelled "Lees de Nederlandse versie" link (`lang="nl"`) to `?lang=nl`, which shows the Dutch text in place (the article gets `lang="nl"`, and a note links back to the translation). [Review fix I2] It never calls `setLocale` or `updateUser`: reading the binding text must not change the site language or the account's email language. Internal links in the texts (`/account`, `/privacy`) are router links (review M14).
- **2026-09-30 · The legal texts have their own effective date; `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` was not bumped.** [Review fix M2: superseded the first version, which showed `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` as the date.] `LEGAL_EFFECTIVE_DATE` (`@smog/i18n/legal`, now 2026-09-30) is the "last updated" date of both pages; the owner sets it at cutover (docs/LEGAL-SIGNOFF.md). A test keeps `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION <= LEGAL_EFFECTIVE_DATE`, so correcting the date never asks anyone for consent again. The rewrite changes processors (Cloudflare, Better Auth, Turnstile; no WorkOS/Convex) and sends less to analytics (user id only, no name or email), so no stored yes is asked again. The text changes from the old page: new processor list, guests on the device only ("never leaves the device"), no guest retention line, identification by user id only, account deletion as implemented (cascade, sessions revoked, audit logs unlinked, sponsor data kept), consent history, Turnstile; the terms add sign-in methods, device-only guest data, the 7-day re-edit link and the renewal (D-RENEW, never automatic). The en/fr DPA links go to the authority's home page in that language (the Dutch deep link is the old one).
- **2026-09-30 · The privacy text says what the code does (review I3, I4).** Analytics: the web relay (`@smog/analytics/server`) forwards the event with the visitor's IP address (`x-client-ip`) and user agent to the self-hosted OpenPanel, which derives a pseudonymous device profile and an approximate location; the relay keeps neither. The app sends to the same OpenPanel with its SDK's device id. The website stores no analytics identifier on the device; only the consented `sessionStorage` sign-in mark (`smog:analytics:sign-in`). Retention promises: account data, lists and consent history while the account exists; admin logs at most 3 years; sessions expire 7 days after last use (Better Auth's default `expiresIn`, refreshed daily) or at sign-out; codes and magic links are single-use and valid 5 minutes, verification and reset links 1 hour (`packages/auth/src/server.ts`); expired sessions, codes and links are erased within 30 days. The admin-log and expired-row purges do not exist yet: a phase 6 carry in PROGRESS makes them required before cutover, because the text promises them. Turnstile is described for every captcha-guarded form, Cloudflare includes the sponsor-video render (Workflows, Containers), and device-only guest data names the optional analytics as its one exception.
- **2026-09-30 · Legacy redirects run first in the Worker's `fetch`.** `apps/site/src/lib/legacy-redirects.ts` holds the table; `legacyRedirect(request)` answers GET/HEAD with a 301 whose `Location` is a same-site path (relative, so never another host), else Start handles the request. Rules: `/sponsors/re-edit` → `/sponsor/edit`; `/sponsors/success` and `/success` → `/sponsor/success` with `paymentId` renamed to `payment` (inventory R-13); `/sponsors/<rest>` → `/sponsor/<rest>` only for plain segments (`[\w.~-]`), so encoded slashes or backslashes are not redirected; the table matches case-insensitively, as the old router did (`/Login`, review M5); `/login` → `/sign-in`; `/callback` → `/` **without** its query (it only ever held a spent WorkOS code; every other rule keeps the query); a trailing slash is ignored; `/gestures?category=<Name>[,…]` → the slugs when any value is not already a slug, other params kept. [Review fix M3] A name resolves to the published category whose name slugifies the same (the catalog snapshot, else D1, loaded only for such a URL), so a slug the migration de-duplicated (`gevoelens-2`) is found; else `slugify(name)`. The category table keeps no old names, so a category renamed after the old link was made cannot be found: that link then filters by a slug that matches nothing (documented, accepted). The 301s leave the Worker before Start, so they carry none of the phase 4 task 6 response headers unless that middleware wraps the Worker `fetch` (a PROGRESS carry, review M4). `/gestures/<convexId>` stays in the gesture loader (it needs D1) and is tested through the Worker. Alternative: Start global request middleware (the Worker entry is the spec's place for the middleware chain and needs no router work).
- **2026-09-30 · App-link files are static assets typed by `public/_headers`.** Workers static assets are served before the Worker runs, so the (phase 4 task 6) headers middleware cannot type them; `_headers` sets `Content-Type: application/json` for both files (checked with `vite preview`; Vite's dev server serves `public/` itself and leaves the extensionless AASA untyped, which the e2e notes). The AASA uses the current `appIDs` + `components` format with `/gestures/*` and `/lists/*`; assetlinks keeps the old Play signing fingerprint. `release-config-check` evaluates `apps/mobile/app.config.ts` and fails when the AASA app id (team + bundle), the paths (the verified intent filters' `pathPrefix` + `*`, and their exact `path`s such as `/magic-link/app`, the phase 4 task 5 hand-off), the assetlinks package, a fingerprint's format or the `_headers` types disagree. The fingerprint is only format-checked, and `associatedDomains` is not tied to the deployed host. iOS passkeys would need `webcredentials` in the AASA and in `associatedDomains`; `checkAasa` ignores unknown keys, so adding it will not break the check (review M10).
- **2026-09-30 · [deviation] Open in app goes through the app, not the bare universal link.** The button's `href` is the page's universal link (`${SITE_URL}/gestures/<slug>`), but iOS and Android keep a same-site tap in the browser, so on a click: Android uses an `intent://gestures/<slug>#Intent;scheme=smog;package=be.zias.smog;S.browser_fallback_url=<Play>;end` link (Chrome opens Play when the app is missing), iOS sets `smog://gestures/<slug>` only. [Review fix I1] There is no timed App Store fallback on iOS: Safari's "Open in the app?" sheet keeps the page visible, so a timer would send people who have the app to the store. The banner shows a separate "Get the app" App Store link on iOS instead (`OpenInAppBanner storeUrl`). Check the scheme hand-off on a real iPhone before launch. The banner sits under the gesture's actions (a `GestureDetailView` `appLink` slot), and the home banner under the hero, so nothing above them moves when they mount after hydration (review M6). The inventory's fallback (the same https page) would only reload it. It shows on iOS and Android browsers only (the old UA test, or touch below 1024 px; iPadOS counts as iOS), after hydration.
- **2026-09-30 · The app banner.** `AppBanner` (kit) sits at the top of the home page, on mobile browsers only, once `useConsent()` is ready with no decision pending (never stacked with the consent banner), until dismissed. The dismissal is `preferences.appBannerDismissedAt` in the local store, optional and additive (stored v2 data parses; no version bump, like `consent.mirroredFrom`). Account deletion resets the whole store, so the banner comes back afterwards; accepted (review M12). The gate is the pure `shouldShowAppBanner` (tested). It renders on `/` only, so it never shows on the app hand-off pages (`/magic-link/app`, `/turnstile-bridge`, phase 4 task 5), which the redirect table also leaves alone (tested). No confetti (motion for its own sake). The store links use lucide icons and text, not the official badges; the badges are an owner choice (docs/LEGAL-SIGNOFF.md). Store URLs, the scheme and the Android package are in `@smog/config/constants`; `app.config.ts` keeps its literals and the release check guards them.

## Learning pages (phase 3 task 6)

- **2026-09-29 · SSR data: an isomorphic oRPC client in the router context, dehydrated with `@tanstack/react-router-ssr-query` 1.167.3 [dep].** `getRouter()` creates one QueryClient, one oRPC client and its query utils per request (server) or document (browser), in the router context. `createAppApiClient` (`src/lib/api.ts`, `createIsomorphicFn`) is `createRouterClient(appRouter, { context })` on the server (`src/server/in-process-api.ts`: the context of `/api/rpc` built from the request, with the session the shell already read) and the `/api/rpc` client in the browser; Start's compiler drops the server branch from the client build (checked: no drizzle or router code in `dist/client`). Loaders prefetch with the query-option factories the feature hooks call themselves (`gestureSearchOptions`, `gesturesBrowseOptions`, `gesturesPageOptions`, `categoriesOptions`, `gestureOptions`, `relatedOptions` from `@smog/gestures/client`, `sharedListOptions` from `@smog/lists/client`; review fix I2, tested in the features' `hooks.test.tsx`), and the integration dehydrates successful queries only (errors are retried by the client), so the hooks read the SSR data without a second fetch (`e2e/search.spec.ts` asserts no `/api/rpc/gestures/*` call after load). The root document keeps mounting `QueryClientProvider` itself (`wrapQueryClient: false`). [review fix I1] A render keeps `RL_API`: `limitRender` asks it once per `Request` (memoised, key `api:<ip>` like `/api/rpc`); over the limit the page answers 429 and every in-process call is `RATE_LIMITED`, so no search runs. Only the origin check is skipped, because loaders only read. Alternatives: HTTP from the loader to `SITE_URL` (review M5: a round trip through the edge and a second rate-limit hit), server functions per query (a second transport with its own keys).
- **2026-09-29 · One session read per request.** `requestSession(request)` (`src/server/session.ts`) memoises Better Auth's `getSession` per `Request` (WeakMap); the shell loader and the in-process client share it, so gesture detail SSR is the session + `bySlug` + `related` (3 D1 reads) and search SSR the session + `search` + `categories`.
  - [fix wave A5] That was 3 procedures, not 3 D1 reads: a cold search could read D1 three times on its own (candidates, projection, typo hydration), so the worst case was 5. It is now at most 3; see "Search SSR in 3 D1 reads" under the phase 3 fix wave.
- **2026-09-29 · Desktop master–detail on `/gestures` is a masked route.** From `lg` the results are dense rows; a row links to `/gestures?…&selected=<slug>` masked as `/gestures/<slug>` (TanStack Router route masking), so the URL reads the gesture page, a reload or a new tab opens the full SSR page, and the detail loads beside the rows (`useGesture`). Below `lg` cards link to the page itself. Both variants render and CSS shows one, so SSR and hydration agree without a media query. `selected` is not a loader dependency. Alternatives: a layout route rendering the list around `$slug` (the full page would SSR the search too, past the 3-query budget), `history.pushState` by hand.
- **2026-09-29 · Browse without a query pages the catalogue; a query shows the ranked top 48.** An empty `q` uses `useGestures` (keyset pages of 50, "Meer laden"); a query uses `useGestureSearch({ limit: 48 })` and says "Verfijn je zoekopdracht" when `total` is larger (search has no offset). The URL `q` follows the field after the same 250 ms debounce, with `replace`.
- **2026-09-29 · [superseded 2026-09-30 by "`lists.containing` and one `useListMembership`" below] "Save to list" reuses `useList` per list.** The ListPicker needs each list's membership; while it is open, one `ListMembership` per list runs `useList(id)` (device lists locally, account lists through `lists.get`, the same cache as the list page) and the toggle calls that hook's `addItem`/`removeItem`, so limits, optimism and invalidation are not written again. The cost is one `lists.get` per list while the picker is open (at most `LISTS_MAX`). A `lists.containing(gestureId)` procedure would make it one read; recorded for a later task.
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `@dnd-kit/core` 6.3.1, `@dnd-kit/sortable` 10.0.0, `@dnd-kit/utilities` 3.2.2 for list reorder.** Pointer and keyboard sensors (space, arrows, space; Escape cancels) with Dutch/English/French announcements and instructions (`lists.reorder.*`); the row menu has move up/down as well. The order shows at once and rolls back with a toast if the hook rejects it. `@dnd-kit/react` (0.5) is the rewrite but not stable yet.
- **2026-09-29 · [dep] `qrcode.react` 4.2.0 for the gesture QR code** (the old web app's library): an SVG, black on white in both themes so it scans, in a Dialog from the detail's overflow menu with the URL and copy. The PNG download of the old dialog is not ported yet. [fix wave A3] It is now: see "QR PNG download" under the phase 3 fix wave.
- **2026-09-29 · `robots.txt` is a route, not `public/robots.txt`.** The `Sitemap:` line must be absolute and `SITE_URL` differs per environment. [review fix I4] Production keeps the old bot rules (AhrefsBot, SemrushBot, MJ12bot and DotBot get `Disallow: /`; everyone else is allowed except `/admin`, `/api/`, `/dev/`, `/account`); staging and dev answer `User-agent: *` / `Disallow: /` so a duplicate site is never indexed (`src/lib/robots.ts`). `sitemap.xml` lists `/`, `/gestures` and every published gesture (`gestures.sitemap`, with `lastmod`); sponsor and legal pages join when they exist.
- **2026-09-29 · SEO head.** `seoHead` (`src/lib/head.ts`) sets title, description, canonical, Open Graph (`og:image` is the Mux thumbnail at 1200 px on a gesture, `og:locale` `nl_BE`/`en_BE`/`fr_BE`) and JSON-LD, escaped for `<script>`. Gestures get a `VideoObject` (name, description, thumbnail, HLS `contentUrl`, url); [review fix I3] `gestures.bySlug` now returns `publishedAt` and `updatedAt` (epoch ms, no extra query), which become `uploadDate` and `dateModified`. A legacy id's 301 keeps the query string and runs before `related` is read. Home gets `WebSite` + `SearchAction` (inventory P-13). Favorites, lists and shared lists are `noindex`.
