The i18n blocks `sponsor.preview` (task 8) and `admin.sponsorships.retryRender` (task 7) exist empty in nl/en/fr; `admin.sponsorships.renderJobs` already existed.

## The render gate, the worker classes, provisioning checks and the CI lane (phase 7 task 2)

- **2026-10-04 · The render gate (ruling 2).** `wrangler.jsonc` declares no `workflows`, `containers`, Durable Object binding or DO migration, at the top or in an env (`release-config-check` refuses each). `apps/site/render-config.ts` adds them at build time from the Cloudflare Vite plugin's `config` hook (`vite.config.ts`): `renderBindings(env)` is the `RENDER_WORKFLOW` Workflow (`smog-<env>-render`, class `RenderSponsorshipVideo`) and the container block (`smog-<env>-renderer`, class `SmogRenderer`, `standard-2`, at most 1/2/4 instances in dev/staging/production, the `RENDERER` binding and the `renderer-v1` `new_sqlite_classes` migration); `applyRenderGate({ env, renderMode, flag, devMode })` adds nothing for `fake`, the Workflow for `local` (dev only, otherwise an error), and the Workflow plus the container block for `container` only with `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1`; `container` without it fails the build with `[render] RENDER_MODE=container needs SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1 (…)`. A flag value other than `1`, `0` or unset is refused as a typo, never read as off. Because production's `RENDER_MODE` is already `container`, a production build fails until production gets the flag (phase 8 carry); staging stays `fake`. The image and build context paths are absolute (from `import.meta.url`): the plugin applies the hook's result after resolving the file's own container paths (`customizeWorkerConfig` → `defu`, which concatenates arrays), so a relative path would land verbatim in `dist/server/wrangler.json` and resolve against `dist/server/`. Turning the flag off after a deploy with it leaves the container application idle; the class is not deleted (no migration is sent and it stays exported; deleting it needs a `deleted_classes` migration). (Amended by the phase 7 fix wave (pipeline and infra): the off switch is two steps, the env's `RENDER_MODE` back to `fake` first, then the flag removed; the flag alone, with `container` still committed, fails every deploy.)
- **2026-10-04 · The legacy `containers[].image` form with `migrations`.** The newer `images` / `scheduling_policy` form needs the declarative `exports` map, which cannot be combined with `migrations` (ruling 2). Checked: a scratch copy with staging at `RENDER_MODE=container` and `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1` builds, passes the deploy guard, and `wrangler deploy --env staging --dry-run --containers-rollout=none` lists `RENDERER (SmogRenderer)`, `RENDER_WORKFLOW (RenderSponsorshipVideo)` and the container `smog-staging-renderer`; a dry run without `--containers-rollout=none` needs Docker. The same copy without the flag fails the build with the gate's message.
- **2026-10-04 · Staging's deployed config is unchanged.** `CLOUDFLARE_ENV=staging bun -F @smog/site deploy:dry` before and after this task, in the same checkout, builds a byte-identical `dist/server/wrangler.json` (across checkouts it is identical except `configPath` and `userConfigPath`, which hold the absolute checkout path; `workflows: []`, `durable_objects.bindings: []`, `migrations: []`, no `containers`) and the same binding list. The only difference is the Worker bundle: the two exported classes and `@cloudflare/containers` (upload 7046.88 → 7094.26 KiB, gzip 1679.39 → 1691.54 KiB). With the gate off, the hook returns nothing for staging and production, so the plugin's config is the file's.
- **2026-10-04 · `SMOG_DEV_RENDER_MODE` (dev's local mode).** `.dev.vars` is read at runtime, too late to add a binding, so `SMOG_DEV_RENDER_MODE=local bun dev` chooses local mode when Vite starts: the hook sets `vars.RENDER_MODE` and adds `RENDER_WORKFLOW` (checked: Miniflare's local explorer lists `smog-dev-render` bound as `RENDER_WORKFLOW`). It accepts `local` or `fake`, only with `CLOUDFLARE_ENV=dev`; `apps/site/turbo.json` passes it to `dev` and adds `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE` to the `build` task's `env` (turbo's strict env mode would drop it, and it changes the output).
- **2026-10-04 · The worker classes.** `src/worker.ts` re-exports `RenderSponsorshipVideo` (`src/worker/render-workflow.ts`, a `WorkflowEntrypoint<Env, { renderJobId }>` whose `run` logs `[render] RenderSponsorshipVideo is wired in task 6 (job …)` and returns `{ outcome: "noop" }`; nothing creates an instance yet) and `SmogRenderer` (`src/worker/renderer.ts`, `Container` with `rendererOptions(env)`: port 8080, `sleepAfter` 10m, `enableInternet`, `envVars` `RENDER_ENVIRONMENT` and `REMOTION_LICENSE_KEY` only when set; passed as constructor options so they are testable without a container runtime). They are exported always: a class deployed once must stay exported (`release-config-check` checks it). The bindings are typed by hand in `src/render-env.d.ts` (task 1 Minor 3), optional on both the global `Env` and `Cloudflare.Env`, because `wrangler types` never sees them. The `@cloudflare/containers` knip ignore is gone.
- **2026-10-04 · Deploy guard additions.** On the built `wrangler.json`: `RENDER_MODE=fake` builds no Workflow, container, `RENDERER` binding or migration; `container` builds the `RENDER_WORKFLOW` Workflow, the `SmogRenderer` container and the `RENDERER` binding; `local` is refused; every `containers[].image` and `image_build_context` is absolute and exists. On every build (`--bundle` too): `dist/server` carries none of `remotion` (`remotion_delayRenderHandles`), `@remotion/renderer` (`chrome-headless-shell`, `@remotion/compositor-linux-x64-gnu`), `@remotion/bundler` (`remotion-webpack-bundle-`) or `mediabunny` (`Mediabunny was loaded twice`); a test reads each marker from the installed package. `REMOTION_LICENSE_KEY` joins the client secret markers. The client entry-chunk check of ruling 1 is task 8's.
- **2026-10-04 · Provisioning probes.** `ensure-cloudflare-resources.ts` runs `wrangler workflows list`, `docker info` and `wrangler containers list`, read only and before anything else, only when the env's `RENDER_MODE` is `container` and `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1` (`renderPipelineEnabled`); a refusal stops the run with a `[provision] Workflows: …` or `[provision] Containers: …` line naming the missing permission or the missing Docker daemon. It creates neither: the deploy registers the Workflow and the container application. Registry push is proven only by the first real deploy. With the gate off it runs nothing new (test). `deploy.yml` passes `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE: ${{ vars.SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE }}` to the resources step and the deploy step; as the job has an `environment`, a GitHub environment variable of that name wins, so staging and production each get their own.
- **2026-10-04 · Review fixes (task 2).**
  - The deploy job stops at its first Cloudflare step when the gate cannot hold: `ensure-cloudflare-resources.ts` refuses `RENDER_MODE=container` without `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1` (`renderGateRefusal`) before any wrangler call. So a wrong-order flip, or production today, no longer applies the D1 migrations before the build fails.
  - In `container` mode the deploy guard also requires the migration that creates `SmogRenderer` (`new_sqlite_classes`).
  - `release:check:core` now runs `bun -F @smog/site deploy:dry` after `build`, so CI builds the staging config, runs the gate's `config` hook and the deploy guard, and runs `wrangler deploy --dry-run` on every push. It needs no credentials; it passed with every proxy pointed at a closed port. `release-config-check` requires the step. (Amended by the phase 7 fix wave (pipeline and infra): as written it passed no flag, so the owner's flip of staging to `container` would have turned every CI run red; `deploy:dry` now passes `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1` and `--containers-rollout=none`, and the core lane also runs `deploy:dry:render`.)
  - `checkRenderClassExports` reads the module's real exports with `Bun.Transpiler#scan`, so an export written in a comment or a string does not count.
  - The ensure dry run prints Docker as `(cd apps/site && docker info)`, the same format as the wrangler lines.
- **2026-10-04 · `release-config-check` additions.** The render keys absent from `wrangler.jsonc`; `RENDER_MODE` one of the three modes and `local` only in dev; the flag passed to both deploy steps from `vars`; the two class exports; the four lanes in `ci.yml` and a `release:check:<lane>` script per lane that `release:check` runs. Task 1 Minor 5: `checkRequiredConfig` now defaults to `requiredWorkerConfig(env, RENDER_MODE)` at each env's mode in `wrangler.jsonc` instead of the static `REQUIRED_WORKER_CONFIG`, and any list it is given must cover what each env's mode needs, so flipping staging to `container` requires `MUX_TOKEN_ID`, `MUX_TOKEN_SECRET` and `MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET`.
- **2026-10-04 · The fourth lane, `render`, as a placeholder.** `ci.yml`'s matrix is `[core, tests, mobile, render]` (still `fail-fast: false`, deploy still needs the whole `release-check` job). `release:check:render` (`scripts/release-check-render.ts`) prints that the image arrives in task 4 and exits 0 only while `packages/render/container/Dockerfile` does not exist; once it does, the placeholder fails (in CI, or locally with a Docker daemon), so task 4 cannot land without the lane. Without a daemon locally it prints `[render] Docker daemon not available: the render lane runs in CI only (not equivalent)`. `release:check` runs the four lanes in order.

## The composition, the metadata reader and the font (phase 7 task 3)

- **2026-10-04 · [parity] The overlay layout is the one production rendered: the old `SPONSOR_OVERLAY_CONFIG` preset (task 3 review, I-3).** (**Superseded by the task 9 parity walk:** the preset below only rendered sponsorships without a preview; every sponsored video of the current old flow is the wizard preview, rendered with the API router's 22 / 76 / 3.8 / 87. `RENDER_OVERLAY_LAYOUT` holds those again.) The old API sent `overlayConfig: getSponsorOverlayConfig()` with every render (`apps/server/src/services/sponsorship.ts`, then `compose.ts` → `inputProps.overlayConfig`). Since commit 42dd4f4 (2026-03-01), that preset (`packages/types/src/presets.ts`) has been:
  - the logo box at 15 % × 15 %, centred on (50 %, 78 %);
  - the text at 4 % of the height, with line 1's top at 85 % and x at 50 %, in `#00805f`;
  - a 1 s fade over the last 5 s.

  `RENDER_OVERLAY_LAYOUT` now holds exactly those values: `logo: { centerX: 0.5, centerY: 0.78, size: 0.15 }` and `text: { fontSize: 0.04, y: 0.85 }`. At 1080 × 1920 the logo box is 162 × 288, centred on (540, 1497.6); line 1's top is at 1632, and the base font size is 76.8 px.

  The spec's 22 % / 76 % / 3.8 % / 87 % were `DEFAULT_OVERLAY_CONFIG`, the composition's fallback for when no config was passed. The old server never left it out after March 2026. The same numbers appear in the 2026-09-29 "Overlay layout stays fixed" entry, the phase 6 CSS preview and ruling 7.7.

  The owner can override this with a one-line change to the constants, plus the figures in the geometry, fit and DOM tests.
- **2026-10-04 · `SponsoredVideo` is the old composition, ported (ruling 5).** `SponsoredVideo({ background, logoUrl, displayName, durationInFrames, width, height })` is an `AbsoluteFill` on black, with the background `cover`-fitted and `SponsorOverlay` on top.
  - **Background.** While rendering (`useRemotionEnvironment().isRendering`) the video is an `OffthreadVideo`: it is frame accurate and fetched server side, so Mux's CORS headers do not matter. In the Player it is an `Html5Video`. `{ kind: "image" }`, the Player's fallback, is an `Img`.
  - **Why not `@remotion/media`.** The old code used its `Video`. It works, but it adds a WebCodecs path in both places for no gain here. It is not claimed to fail.
  - **The overlay's math** is pure and tested against the old formulas:
    - `overlayStartFrame` is `durationInFrames − 5 × fps`. It is negative for clips under 5 s, so the overlay is there from frame 0.
    - `overlayTiming` uses `spring({ damping: 200, durationInFrames: 1 s })` for the opacity and a 30 → 0 px `translateY` on the whole overlay. It returns `null` before the start.
    - `overlayGeometry`:
      - the logo box is `size × width` by `size × height`, centred on `(centerX × width, centerY × height)`, with `objectFit: contain`;
      - line 1's **top** is at `y × height`;
      - line 2's top is line 1's `+ 1.2 × fontSize + 0.3 × fontSize`.
  - **Dropped:** the old outer flex column with `paddingBottom`. It positioned nothing, because every child is absolute with its own `top` and `left`.
- **2026-10-04 · [parity change] The line height is pinned to 1.2.** The old divs had `line-height: normal`, which is the font's own metric: about 1.16 for DejaVu, which the old image picked for `system-ui`. Pinning it keeps the glyph tops stable across fonts, and the same in the render and the Player. It does not reproduce the old offset exactly: the difference is a few px at 1920. Both lines are `nowrap`, weight 600, in `text.color`, and horizontally centred with `left: 50%` + `translateX(-50%)`, as before.
- **2026-10-04 · [improvement] Long names fit (`overlayFontSize`).** The size is `min(text.fontSize × height, the size at which the wider line fills 90 % of the width)`, one size for both lines.
  - Text width scales with the size, so each line is measured once at the base size, through a `measure(text, fontSize, line)` port.
  - A short name keeps the old size exactly.
  - Line 2's offset uses the fitted size, so the two lines stay together when they shrink.
  - The review still of a 35-character "W…" name at 1080 × 1920 shows both lines inside the frame. The old overlay clipped such a name off both edges.
- **2026-10-04 · Names in any script render without crashing (task 3 review, I-1).**
  - **The problem.** `displayNameSchema` allows any script. Measuring a name that is drawn in a fallback font with `validateFontIsLoaded: true` throws "font is not loaded". That failed the render and blanked the Player.
  - **Measuring.** The composition measures with `@remotion/layout-utils` `measureText`, in the overlay stack, only after the font has loaded. Only the fixed Latin intro is measured with `validateFontIsLoaded: true`: it is Latin with more than 4 distinct characters, so it proves the load. The name is measured without validation, as it is drawn.
  - **The font stack** is `"SMOG Overlay", "Noto Sans", sans-serif`:
    - Inter 600 in the latin, latin-ext, cyrillic, cyrillic-ext, greek, greek-ext and vietnamese subsets. Each is scoped by `unicode-range`, so only the files a text uses are fetched.
    - then Noto Sans;
    - then the generic family.
  - **Ruling on other scripts.** Task 4's image installs Debian's `fonts-noto-core` (Arabic, Hebrew, Devanagari, Thai and more), so those scripts draw in Noto in the render. CJK (`fonts-noto-cjk`, about 100 MB) is not installed. It draws in whatever fallback exists, possibly as tofu. In the Player, the viewer's own fonts apply.
  - **The family name** is "SMOG Overlay", not "Inter", so the overlay's `FontFace`s never mix with the site's UI Inter.
  - **The test.** A DOM test renders a Cyrillic name and an Arabic name. Its fake `measureText` throws on a validated measure of text the font does not cover.
- **2026-10-04 · The overlay font loads once, under `delayRender` (ruling 6).**
  - **The files.** `OVERLAY_FONT_FILES` are the seven Inter 600 `.woff2` files, each with `@fontsource/inter`'s own `unicodeRange`. The ranges overlap on a few combining marks, which both files draw. They are loaded with `@remotion/fonts` `loadFont({ family: "SMOG Overlay", weight: "600", format: "woff2", unicodeRange, url })`.
  - **The loader.** `createOverlayFontLoader(loadFont)` loads once per document and forgets a failure, so the next call retries.
    - `loadOverlayFont()` is exported for the wizard, which calls it before mounting the Player.
    - `isOverlayFontLoaded()` lets an overlay mounted afterwards skip the wait.
  - **In the composition.** `SponsorOverlay` holds the frame with `useDelayRender().delayRender` until the font has loaded. It continues the handle in the effect cleanup that follows the commit showing the text, or on unmount. Until the font has loaded, the overlay renders nothing.
- **2026-10-04 · A failure cancels a render and shows the Player's fallback (task 3 review, M-2 and M-7).** `useFailure(message)` gives a stable handler.
  - While rendering (`useRemotionEnvironment().isRendering`), it calls `cancelRender(new Error(message, { cause }))`.
  - In the Player, it stores the error and throws it during the next render, so the Player's `errorFallback` shows it. Before, the result was a blank overlay and an unhandled rejection.
  - It covers the font load, the logo `Img`, the background image `Img` and the Player's `Html5Video`.
  - The messages are fixed, because Remotion's own media errors name their `src`: "the overlay font could not be loaded", "the logo could not be loaded", "the background image could not be loaded" and "the background video could not be played".
- **2026-10-04 · `sponsoredVideoPropsSchema` (Zod 4).**
  - `background` is `{ kind: "video" | "image", src: non-empty }`.
  - `logoUrl` is non-empty or `null`. It is the server's `http://127.0.0.1` URL or the wizard's object URL, so it is not `z.url()`.
  - `displayName` is 1..`DISPLAY_NAME_MAX`.
  - `durationInFrames` is an integer ≥ 1.
  - `width` and `height` are even integers ≥ 2.

  `<Composition>` has no `schema` prop. The Root's exported `calculateMetadata` only hands back the props' size and duration.
- **2026-10-04 · `readSourceMetadata(url, { openInput? })` (`./metadata`, `src/metadata/index.ts`).**
  - **How it reads.** A `mediabunny` `Input` over `UrlSource`, with no retries of its own (the Workflow step and the Player's fallback decide). It calls `computeDuration()` and the primary video track's `getDisplayWidth/Height()` (after rotation and aspect ratio), and disposes the input in `finally`.
  - **What it answers.** `{ durationInFrames: max(1, ceil(duration × 30 − 1e-6)), durationInSeconds, width, height }`.
    - Both sizes are **rounded down to even**: H.264 needs even sizes, and the old code did not round.
    - The epsilon stops a frame-exact duration from counting one frame too many through float error: 62 / 30 × 30 = 62.00000000000001 (review M-1).
  - **`SourceUnreadableError`** is final (the server's `sourceUnreadable`). It covers a source with no video track, an `UnsupportedInputFormatError`, a non-finite duration and a size under 2 px.
  - **`SourceFetchError`** covers everything else: mediabunny's `Error fetching <url>: <status> <text>` for any non-2xx answer (such as an expired signed master URL's 403), or a network fault.
    - It has `retryable: true`, and `status` is parsed from mediabunny's message or is `null`.
    - Its message is fixed: "the source could not be read (HTTP <status>)".
  - **No URLs in messages.** Both error messages are URL-free, and the tests check this with mediabunny's real message shape and a `?token=` URL. The `cause` is the original error, which does contain the URL, so the server must never log or answer `cause` unscrubbed (a task 4 carry; review I-2).
  - **Deviations from the brief:**
    - the injection point is `openInput`, a factory for the `Input`-shaped `MetadataInput`, rather than a `source`, because the error paths need a fake input, not fake bytes;
    - the file is `src/metadata/index.ts`, where task 1's `./metadata` export already pointed.
- **2026-10-04 · The composition's DOM tests run in `@remotion/player`'s `Thumbnail` under happy-dom.**
  - **Separate pass.** `*.dom.test.tsx` files run in a second `bun test` with `--preload @smog/config/testing/happy-dom`, so the bundling smoke and the pure tests keep a clean global scope.
  - **Mocked modules.** happy-dom loads no font and lays nothing out, so `@remotion/fonts` and `@remotion/layout-utils` are module mocks. The fake `measureText` throws when it is called before the fake load has resolved, or on a validated measure of text outside the font's subsets. `remotion`'s `useDelayRender` and `useRemotionEnvironment` (and `OffthreadVideo`/`Html5Video`) are module mocks too, for the composition's own calls only.
  - **What the tests assert:**
    - the positions: line 1 at 1632 px, line 2 at line 1 + 1.5 × fontSize, and the logo box;
    - every `delayRender` handle is continued once;
    - a font failure cancels a render and shows the Player's fallback;
    - a failed background image shows the fallback;
    - both background branches.
  - **Dependencies.** `@smog/render` gains the devDependencies `@remotion/player` (catalog), `@testing-library/react` 16.3.3 and `@testing-library/dom` 10.4.2, the versions the other packages use. knip's `ignoreDependencies` keeps only `@remotion/renderer` (task 4).
  - **No browser.** The real render with Chrome is task 4's `test:render`. No test in `bun run test` starts a browser.

## The render server and the container image (phase 7 task 4)

- **2026-10-04 · The render server, as built (ruling 7).** `createRenderServer({ env, health, log, metadata, renderer, uploader })` (`packages/render/src/server/server.ts`) is pure. `main.ts` wires the real ports: `readSourceMetadata`, `createRemotionRenderer` (`render.ts`) and `createFetchUploader` (`upload.ts`). The bun tests use fakes (36 tests, no Chrome).
  - **Refusals** are all `422 invalidInput`, with the contract's codes only. A body over 4 MiB is read through `@smog/utils` `readCappedBody` (a new dependency, which the boundaries already allowed) and answers 422 rather than 413, so every answer is a `RenderResult`. A logo whose magic bytes are not the type it claims is `logoUnreadable`.
  - **The slot** (amended by the task 4 review, minors 1, 2 and 11). The body, the URLs and the logo's magic bytes are all checked before the slot is claimed, so a same-job request with a bad logo never cancels a good attempt. A request for the job already running takes the slot at once (so another job is `busy` meanwhile, with no gap to race into), aborts the running attempt's `AbortController` with `RenderAbortError("superseded")` and waits for its `finally`: its temp directory `<RENDER_TMP_DIR>/<jobId>-<n>` is deleted and its logo unregistered. Three overlapping requests for one job end with the latest one rendering. The abort is bridged to `makeCancelSignal()` in the Remotion port and passed to the upload's `fetch`. The superseded request answers `500 renderFailed` "superseded by a newer request for the same job" (nobody reads it) and is logged at `info` as `render superseded`, not as a failure. Another job is `503 busy`, logged as a `warn`.
  - **Bounded attempts (review I-3).** An attempt is aborted when its caller disconnects (`request.signal`; `RenderAbortError("disconnected")`) and after `RENDER_ATTEMPT_TIMEOUT_MS`, 19 minutes, just under the `render` step's 20-minute timeout (`"deadline"`). Both answer `renderFailed` (retryable) and free the slot. Every stage (the metadata read, the render, the upload) races the attempt's signal, so a port that ignores it cannot hold the slot. `drain(timeoutMs)` refuses new renders and waits for the running one; `main.ts` passes 14 minutes on `SIGTERM` (the platform's grace period is 15), after which the attempt is aborted (`"shutdown"`) and awaited.
  - **Remotion's own log lines are scrubbed too (review I-1).** Remotion's `Log` writes with `console.*` directly, naming the signed source URL ("Downloading <url> failed (will retry)", a failed resource's proxy URL with `src=<url>`, compositor errors). `main.ts` calls `scrubConsole()` first: every string argument goes through `scrubUrls`, every `Error` through `describeError` (name and message, never the cause) and any other object through its inspected, scrubbed form. `scrubUrls` matches `http(s):`, `ws(s):`, `blob:`, `file://` and `data:<type>/`, so prose such as "No such file:" keeps its words.
  - **One upload retry (review minor 6).** A network fault or a 5xx on the `PUT` is retried once after 2 s (a Mux direct-upload URL takes a fresh `PUT`); a 4xx is final. A transient fault no longer costs a whole re-render through the Workflow's step retry.
  - **The logo route** serves only the current job's file, and only to a loopback peer (`Bun.Server.requestIP`). The server binds `127.0.0.1` when `RENDER_ENVIRONMENT=dev` (`serve`, and the render lane's container on `--network host`) and `0.0.0.0` otherwise: the Container is reached through its Durable Object, from outside the container's loopback.
  - **`idleTimeout: 0`.** `Bun.serve`'s default idle timeout (10 s) would cut a `POST /render` that answers after minutes.
  - **Errors** (task 3 review I-2 and M-6):
    - `SourceUnreadableError` maps to `sourceUnreadable` (422, final).
    - `SourceFetchError` maps to `renderFailed` (500, retryable), with its fixed message.
    - The uploader maps an HTTP refusal or a network fault to `uploadFailed` (502).
    - Anything else (Remotion's `OffthreadVideo` errors, which name their `src`) is `renderFailed` with `Name: message`.
    - Every message and log value goes through `scrubUrls`, which replaces any `http(s):`, `ws(s):`, `data:`, `blob:` or `file:` URL with `<url>`. An error's `cause` is never logged or answered.
  - **Logs** are `[render] <event> {json}` lines with ids, sizes and times, never a URL: `source read`, `source downloaded {bytes, ms}` (`renderMedia`'s `onDownload`, for M-6), `render progress` every 25 %, `rendered {frames, ms}`, and `uploaded {bytes, ms, uploadMs}`.
  - **`serve`** sets `RENDER_BUNDLE_DIR=$PWD/.render-bundle` (gitignored in `packages/render/.gitignore`). `main.ts` builds the bundle there when it has no `index.html`, importing `./bundle` only then, because the runtime image has no `@remotion/bundler`. `GET /health` answers `{ ok, version: "remotion 4.0.532", browser }`, where `browser` is how `ensureBrowser` found Chrome.
- **2026-10-04 · The image (ruling 17), as built: `packages/render/container/Dockerfile`.**
  - **The bases.** `oven/bun:1.3.11` and `oven/bun:1.3.11-slim`, pinned by their multi-arch index digests (`sha256:0733e503…` and `sha256:478281fd…`). Their amd64 config history reads `debian.sh … 'trixie'`, checked against Docker Hub and the registry on 2026-10-04. Bun 1.3.11 is the repository's `packageManager`.
  - **The stages:**
    1. `prune` runs `bunx turbo@2.11.5 prune @smog/render --docker --production`.
    2. `build` installs the frozen pruned lockfile, then runs `bun src/server/bundle.ts` → `/app/bundle`.
    3. `deps` runs `bun install --frozen-lockfile --production --omit=peer --filter @smog/render`, then `bun src/server/ensure-browser.ts` with only `packages/config/src` and that file copied first, so a source change keeps the Chrome download layer (review minor 4).
    4. `sources` copies the `config`, `styles`, `utils` and `render` sources and deletes every `*.test.ts(x)`.
    5. `runtime` copies, largest and least changing first, `node_modules`, `packages/render/node_modules` (Chrome), the sources and `/app/bundle`, each with `--chown=1001:1001`. It runs as the `renderer` user (uid/gid 1001) from `WORKDIR /app/packages/render`, with `ENTRYPOINT ["/usr/bin/tini", "--"]` and `CMD ["bun", "src/server/main.ts"]`.
  - **`--omit=peer` (review I-2).** `@smog/styles` declares a `nativewind` peer, and Bun installs peers, and devDependencies of workspace dependencies, even with `--production --filter`: the install brought React Native, Hermes, Reanimated and Metro, 326 MB of `node_modules`. With `--omit=peer` it is 38 packages and 94 MB; `react` and `react-dom` are direct dependencies of `@smog/render`, so Remotion's peers stay satisfied, and `test:render` passed against a server running from exactly that tree. Marking the peer optional in `@smog/styles` (`peerDependenciesMeta`) was tried first and changes nothing here: Bun still installs `nativewind` as a devDependency of the workspace. The lane logs the image's `node_modules` size.
  - **`tini` (review minor 5)** is the entrypoint, so Chrome's orphaned helpers are reaped (Containers cannot pass `--init`) and `SIGTERM` is forwarded to Bun. It is one apt package, in the library layer's `apt-cache policy` list.
  - **Deviation from the ruling's copy list ("`/app/bundle`, the deps `node_modules` and `src/server`").** The server imports `../contract`, `../compositions/props` and `../metadata`, plus `@smog/config`, `@smog/styles` and `@smog/utils`, so it needs more than `src/server`. The image takes the whole `packages/render/src` minus its tests, plus those three packages' sources.
  - **The working directory is `packages/render` in `deps` and `runtime`.** Remotion resolves its browser download directory from the nearest `package.json` above the cwd, so `ensureBrowser()` puts Chrome Headless Shell in `packages/render/node_modules/.remotion`, and the server must look there.
  - **Checking the layout here.** There is no Docker daemon here, so the image cannot be built. Instead, the exact layout was rebuilt by hand from a `.dockerignore`-shaped copy of the repository: the prune, the frozen and production installs, the bundle, and the sources minus tests. `main.ts` served from it, and `test:render` passed against it.
  - **The library names (trixie).** packages.debian.org and the Debian mirrors are blocked from this sandbox. Every library name in the list is the one Playwright's own `debian13-x64` Chromium dependency list uses (`playwright-core`, installed here): `libasound2t64`, `libatk-bridge2.0-0t64`, `libatk1.0-0t64`, `libcairo2`, `libcups2t64`, `libdbus-1-3`, `libgbm1`, `libnss3`, `libpango-1.0-0`, `libxcomposite1`, `libxdamage1`, `libxfixes3`, `libxkbcommon0` and `libxrandr2`. The list also has `ca-certificates`. Both apt layers run `apt-cache policy` for their packages, so the CI build log proves every name. `apt-get install` fails the build on a wrong one.
  - **No `HEALTHCHECK`.** The slim image has no curl, and the Container is probed through its Durable Object.
- **2026-10-04 · [amends ruling 17] Noto fonts in the image (task 3 review I-1).** The runtime stage installs Debian's `fonts-noto-core` (`--no-install-recommends`) in its own layer, so Arabic, Hebrew, Devanagari, Thai and the other scripts it covers draw in Noto Sans after the bundled Inter. This replaces ruling 17's "No system fonts". `fonts-noto-cjk` is not installed (about 100 MB): CJK draws in whatever fallback exists.
  - **The size.** The render lane logs each apt layer's size from `docker history`: "layer fonts-noto-core: …" and "layer Chrome libraries: …". Here it cannot be measured; read it in the first CI run.
  - **`test:render`'s second case** renders a short Arabic name without a logo, and it must succeed (it also carries the full-size ΔE gate). Locally it drew in the machine's own fallback (still in `sdd/phase-7/task-4-out`).
- **2026-10-04 · The `turbo prune` extra workspaces are trimmed with `--production` (ruling 17; corrected by the task 4 review, minor 3).** A plain `turbo prune @smog/render` also adds `@smog/jobs`, `@smog/email` and `@smog/i18n`: the root package's devDependency `@smog/jobs` (which `scripts/cron.ts` and `scripts/release-config-check.ts` import) counts as a dependency of every workspace. `turbo prune @smog/render --docker --production` (2.11.5) leaves the root's devDependencies out and outputs only `config`, `render`, `styles` and `utils`. Its `out/json` still installs with `--frozen-lockfile` (601 packages, `@remotion/bundler` included, since an external devDependency of `@smog/render` stays), so the image uses it and no longer deletes pruned manifests. `@smog/utils` is a real dependency (`readCappedBody`).
- **2026-10-04 · The render lane (ruling 16), as built: `scripts/release-check-render.ts`.**
  - **CI.** CI builds the image in the `render` matrix leg with `docker/setup-buildx-action@v4` and `docker/build-push-action@v7` (`context: .`, `platforms: linux/amd64`, `load: true`, `tags: smog-renderer:ci`, `cache-from`/`cache-to: type=gha,scope=smog-renderer`). The shared `bun run release:check:${{ matrix.check }}` step passes `SMOG_RENDER_NO_BUILD=1` for that leg; `release-config-check` requires that step unchanged, so the env var stands in for `--no-build`, which the script also accepts. `actions/upload-artifact@v7` uploads `packages/render/.render-out/` (`render-lane-output`) even when the lane fails.
  - **The script:**
    1. logs the image size, the two apt layers and the runtime `node_modules` size (`du` in a throwaway container);
    2. starts the container with `--network host`, `RENDER_ENVIRONMENT=dev` and `RENDER_ALLOW_HTTP=1` (dev only, so the loopback http source and sink are allowed);
    3. waits up to 2 min for `GET /health`, each poll bounded to 5 s;
    4. runs `test:render` on the host with `RENDER_SERVER_URL`;
    5. prints the container's log (the render times), then removes the container.
  - **Locally** it builds the image itself (`smog-renderer:local`) when `docker info` succeeds. Otherwise it prints the "not equivalent" line and passes. In CI a missing daemon or a missing Dockerfile fails.
  - **`test:render`** (`test/render/render.e2e.test.ts`) goes through `test-deadline.ts --minutes 20`, with `bun test --timeout=900000`. Without `RENDER_SERVER_URL` it starts `src/server/main.ts` itself on port 3092 (`RENDER_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE` is passed through).
  - **What `test:render` checks.** The source is the fixture, served with byte ranges by a host server, and the upload goes to a host sink (`test/support/servers.ts`). The logo is an uncompressed PNG of about 2 MiB (1.98 MiB, just under `RENDER_LOGO_MAX_BYTES`). The name is the 35-character "Bakkerij Van den Broeck & Zonen bv.". It checks:
    - the `PUT`'s method, `Content-Type` and `Content-Length`;
    - with `mediabunny`: codec `avc`, 360 × 640, and 60 ± 1 packets;
    - the decoded frame count;
    - the ΔE probe below.
- **2026-10-04 · [deviation from ruling 16] The ΔE probe reads the uploaded MP4's last frame, not a host `renderStill`.**
  - **How.** The frames are decoded with Remotion's own ffmpeg, the `@remotion/compositor-*` package that `@remotion/renderer` installs. Its build has no `select` filter and no `rawvideo` muxer, so all frames go through `image2pipe` with the `rawvideo` codec.
  - **The rule** (made robust by the task 4 review, minor 7). It counts pixels within CIE76 ΔE < 10 of `#00805F` in line 1's rows, `floor(0.85 × h)` to `ceil((0.85 + 1.2 × 0.04) × h)`, which is rows 544–575 at 640. The full-size gate runs on a short name, where line 1 keeps its base size: at least 300 px (759 measured). The 35-character render, where both lines shrink to about 15 px, only checks that the line is there: at least 100 px (307 measured). A missing or wrong-colour line scores about 0 against the magenta fixture. Both renders log the counts at ΔE < 6, 8, 10, 12 and 15.
  - **Why.** It checks what the image itself produced: its Chrome, its fonts and its encoder. The host needs no browser and no second bundle.
  - **The artifact.** The same frame is saved as `render-last-frame.png` for the parity review, with the MP4s.
  - **Why the change.** With the long name the count falls steeply with the cut-off (ΔE < 6: 144, < 8: 220, < 10: 307, < 12: 387), so a 2–3 ΔE shift in the image's rasterisation would have failed the old 200 bar. The short name's line 1 has 431 px even at ΔE < 6.
- **2026-10-04 · The source fixture `packages/render/test/fixtures/source-2s.mp4`.** It is 21,900 bytes: H.264, 360 × 640, 30 fps, 60 frames, no audio.
  - **How it is made.** `bun -F @smog/render fixture` renders it with `crf: 30` (for size) from `src/server/fixture-card.tsx`: a hue-shifting background and the frame number. That file is a Remotion entry of its own, bundled only by `fixture.ts`; `fixture.ts` never imports it, so `registerRoot` never runs in Bun.
  - **Regenerating.** Only to change the fixture, with a browser (`RENDER_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE`), then commit it.
  - **The test.** `test/fixture-metadata.test.ts` (in `bun run test`) reads it over HTTP with `readSourceMetadata` (60 frames, 360 × 640) and pins its size under 100 KB.
- **2026-10-04 · Render time and the instance type (ruling 3): `standard-2` stays.**
  - **The fixture.** The 2 s 360 × 640 fixture with the 2 MiB logo renders and uploads in about 6.1–6.9 s here (4 cores, `RENDER_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE` = Playwright's `chromium_headless_shell-1194`; Remotion's own download was not needed).
  - **A larger clip.** For a worst case, a 30 s 1080 × 1920 source (900 frames) was rendered with the whole server pinned to one CPU (`taskset -c 0`, so Chrome and the compositor shared it). It took 302 s, about 0.34 s per frame.
  - **The threshold.** At that rate a 1080 × 1920 clip reaches 10 minutes (half the `render` step's 20-minute timeout) at about 55 s. Gesture clips are a few seconds to tens of seconds, so `standard-2` (1 vCPU) stays.
  - **Caveats.** The Containers vCPU is not this machine's core, and the CI lane logs its own times. Phase 9's latency check on staging (the p95 per clip length) is the real measure; switch to `standard-3` if it shows renders near 10 minutes.
- **2026-10-04 · The first CI run of the render lane (run 37182551197, commit `a7652cf`, before the review fixes): green.**
  - **The image** was 969 MiB. The `fonts-noto-core` layer is 45.5 MB, the Chrome library layer 255 MB. Every apt name installed. `/health` reported `remotion-headless-shell` and Remotion 4.0.532 (Remotion's own download, in the image).
  - **Render times on the runner:** 60 frames in 5.6 s, and the Arabic render in 5.5 s, both `200`.
  - **The green probe found 225 px in CI** against 307 locally: the image's Chrome and fontconfig rasterise the shrunk line differently. That confirmed the review's margin concern; the short-name gate (at least 300 px, 759 locally) replaces the 200 px bar.
  - **The artifact upload found nothing.** `packages/render/.render-out` is a dot directory, and `actions/upload-artifact` skips hidden files unless `include-hidden-files: true`, which the step now sets; `checkRenderLaneBuild` requires it.
  - **Noise.** The lane's pre-clean `docker rm --force` now drops its stderr: "No such container" is the normal case.
  - `--omit=peer` should shrink the image by about 230 MB; the next run's `[render] node_modules` and image size lines will show it.

## `@smog/video` for renders (phase 7 task 5)

- **2026-10-04 · The Mux shapes, checked against `@mux/mux-node` 15.5.0.** mux.com is not reachable from the sandbox, so the endpoints and fields were read from the official SDK's generated types (the OpenAPI spec): `GET /video/v1/playback-ids/{id}` → `{ id, policy, object: { id, type: "asset" | "live_stream" } }`; `PUT /video/v1/assets/{id}/master-access` `{ master_access: "temporary" | "none" }` → the asset; the asset's `master_access` and `master?: { status?: "ready" | "preparing" | "errored", url? }` (absent while access is off and once the 24 h URL expired); `PUT /video/v1/uploads/{id}/cancel` (no body) → the upload, and it "only succeeds if the upload is still in the `waiting` state". The refusal's status code is not documented, so `cancelUpload` does not depend on it (next entry).
- **2026-10-04 · `cancelUpload` → `{ state: "cancelled" | "already-final", assetId }` (review I-3, M-1).** A 2xx is `cancelled`. A 404 is `already-final`. Any other 4xx except 401/403/429 re-reads the upload: it is `already-final` when the upload is unknown or past `waiting`, and the error is rethrown when it is still `waiting`. Outages and 401/403/429 throw without a re-read. `assetId` is the asset the upload already has, read from the cancel answer or the re-read. **Nobody else deletes that asset, so the caller must** (Task 6, in `render` for the previous upload and in `fail`). That is the leak the review found: an `asset.ready` that was forwarded while still current, and only became superseded afterwards.
- **2026-10-04 · `enableMasterAccess` reads before it writes, and does not trust a stale flag (review I-2).** It `GET`s the asset first:
  - `already-on` with no `PUT` only when `master_access` is `temporary` **and** `master` is present with a status other than `errored`;
  - an unknown asset → `missing`;
  - otherwise one `PUT` → `enabled`.

  The SDK says `master` disappears when the 24 h URL expires, but it does not say that the flag reverts. A flag still set without a master, or an errored master, is therefore asked for again. A replayed `source-lookup` still sends no second `PUT`, because the first one makes `master` exist (tests: enable twice → one `PUT`; a stale flag → one `PUT`).
- **2026-10-04 · `masterState` never fails on a new Mux state.** `master.status` and `master_access` are read as strings in `muxAssetDataSchema`, not enums, so an unknown value cannot make `getAsset` (the gesture upload flow) refuse the whole asset. `ready` needs a URL; anything not `ready`/`errored` is `preparing` (the polls continue and end in the rendition fallback); no `master` or an unknown asset is `none`. `MasterState` is a union: only `ready` carries `url`, which is signed and never logged by this package.
- **2026-10-04 · `assetIdForPlayback` is `null` for a live stream's playback id**, as for a 404: only an asset can be a render source.
- **2026-10-04 · The render upload sends `cors_origin` (amends ruling 4.6.3, review I-1).** `createRenderUpload(mux, { corsOrigin, renderJobId, test })` sends:
  - `cors_origin` (the `SITE_URL` origin);
  - `new_asset_settings: { passthrough: "render-job:<id>", playback_policies: ["public"] }`;
  - `timeout: 3600`;
  - `test: true` only when asked.

  It sends no `master_access` (parity note) and no `static_renditions` (the player streams HLS). Ruling 4.6.3 said "no `cors_origin`". However, `UploadCreateParams.cors_origin` is **required** in `@mux/mux-node` 15.5.0, which is generated from Mux's OpenAPI spec, and the old `uploadToMux` sent it in production. It is harmless for the renderer's server-side PUT, which sends no `Origin`. The fake now refuses a create-upload body without `cors_origin` (400), so a regression fails the tests. The returned `url` is signed: never logged, never step output. `RENDER_JOB_PREFIX`, `renderJobPassthrough` and `renderJobIdOf` live in `uploads.ts`. `renderJobIdOf` is `null` for an empty id or any other prefix; the old `render:` prefix is not one.
- **2026-10-04 · Static renditions as the fallback source.** `renditionUrls(playbackId)` is `https://stream.mux.com/{id}/highest.mp4`, then `/high.mp4` (the id URI-encoded). `firstReachable(urls, fetch, { timeoutMs = 10 000 })` `HEAD`s each URL with `AbortSignal.timeout`, in order, and returns the first that answers exactly `200`. A network error or timeout counts as unreachable, and it returns `null` when none answers. Its warning names only the file (`highest.mp4`) and the error's name, never the URL or the error message, so a signed URL passed to it cannot leak (review M-2).
- **2026-10-04 · Render webhook events (ruling 9).**
  - **Mapping.** `toRenderMuxEvent` maps the four routed types for a `render-job:` passthrough. An asset event needs an `upload_id`. `asset.ready` carries the first public playback id; the errored ones carry Mux's message (asset `errors.messages` joined, upload `error.message`, else the error type).
  - **Order.** `handleMuxWebhook` checks for a render event first, so a render passthrough never reaches the gesture records.
  - **Without `onRenderEvent`**, the event is logged and answered `200 IGNORED` with no KV write.
  - **With it:**
    1. The dedupe marker is read first (`DUPLICATE`).
    2. For `asset.ready` **and `asset.errored`** (review M-9), `isCurrentUpload(renderJobId, uploadId, assetId)` is asked. `false` deletes the asset and answers `SUPERSEDED`, with nothing forwarded.
    3. Otherwise `onRenderEvent` answers `sent` (`OK`) or `gone` (`GONE`).
    4. The marker is written only after that.
  - **Failures.** Any throw is `503 UNAVAILABLE` with no marker, so Mux's retry runs everything again. That covers KV, either hook, and the delete (next entry). The response codes are in `docs/API.md`.
- **2026-10-04 · `isCurrentUpload` counts a `succeeded` job with this upload as current, and gets the asset id (amends ruling 9's B-3 wording; review M-6).**
  - **Why `succeeded` counts.** Under ruling 9 as written ("`queued`/`running` and `mux_upload_id` equals `uploadId`"), two cases find the job `succeeded`, count it as not current, and **delete the committed video**: a late `asset.ready` (Mux delivers late, or the Workflow committed through its `ready-poll` steps first), and a redelivery after the first marker write failed.
  - **The rule.** The hook is `true` when:
    - `mux_upload_id = uploadId` and the job is `queued`, `running` or `succeeded`; or
    - the event's `assetId` is the job's `mux_asset_id` or the sponsorship's `video_asset_id` (defence in depth: a committed asset is never deleted, whatever the upload ids say).
  - **What is still deleted.** The asset of a `failed` job, and of an upload superseded by a later attempt.
  - **What Task 6 must keep.** The rule holds only if Task 6 **never clears or overwrites `render_job.mux_upload_id` on commit or fail**. Task 6's one-row D1 read implements it, and the option's JSDoc states it.
- **2026-10-04 · A failed delete of a superseded asset is a 503 (amends ruling 9's "logged and swallowed", review M-8).** `deleteAsset` treats 404 as done, so a retry is idempotent. Answering 503 without the marker gets up to 24 h of Mux retries for free, where swallowing the error leaked the asset for good. Without a Mux client there is nothing to retry with, so the asset is logged, kept and marked.
- **2026-10-04 · The Mux fake for renders.**
  - **New endpoints in `createFakeMux`:**
    - `GET /video/v1/playback-ids/:id`;
    - `PUT /video/v1/assets/:id/master-access`: `temporary` sets `master: { status: "preparing" }` when the master is missing or errored, `none` drops it, anything else is a 400;
    - `PUT /video/v1/uploads/:id/cancel`: an upload that is not `waiting` is refused with 400.
  - **Create upload** refuses a body without `cors_origin` (400), as Mux's schema requires.
  - **Masters.** `readyMaster(assetId)` / `errorMaster(assetId)` set the master. The ready URL is `<masterOrigin>/master/<assetId>/master.mp4?signature=fake-…`, signed-looking on purpose so the Workflow's "no signed URL in step state" test has something to catch.
  - **Uploads.** Every PUT keeps the bytes on the asset (`file`). A render's asset gets its own random playback id, while gesture and picker assets share the fixed e2e sample id (review M-4). The lookup returns the first match, which the JSDoc states.
  - **The fake server** serves masters on its own origin, with `GET`/`HEAD` and one byte range: `a-b`, `a-` and `-n` get 206, unsatisfiable gets 416, and an invalid range (`bytes=-`, a reversed range) is ignored and gets 200 (RFC 9110, review M-5).
    - It serves the asset's uploaded file, else `masterFile`. That is `FAKE_MUX_MASTER_FILE`, by default `packages/render/test/fixtures/source-2s.mp4` once Task 4 commits it; without a file the answer is 404.
    - Turning master access on readies the master after `readyAfterMs`.
    - `emitWebhook(event)` and `POST /__fake/webhook` `{ type, data }` sign and POST any event to the webhook URL, for local runs.
  - **Unchanged:** the gesture paths and their tests.

## The Workflow (phase 7 task 6)

- **2026-10-04 · `runRenderJob` is the Workflow; the class is wiring.** `@smog/sponsorships/server` `runRenderJob(step, deps, { renderJobId })` runs ruling 4's steps over a `RenderStep` port (`do(name, config, fn)`, `sleep(name, ms)`, `waitForEvent(name, { type, timeoutMs }) → payload | null`) and the ports `{ db, mux, readLogo, renderer, queues, siteUrl, environment, clock }`. `apps/site/src/worker/render-workflow.ts` `RenderSponsorshipVideo.run` adapts `WorkflowStep` and builds the ports from `siteEnv()` (`renderDeps()`, a method so the introspection test can swap the Mux client and the renderer). The adapter maps a non-retryable `RenderJobFailure` to `cloudflare:workflows` `NonRetryableError` with the same message, and a `waitForEvent` timeout (`WorkflowTimeoutError`) to `null`.
- **2026-10-04 · A failure's code lives in its message.** `RenderJobFailure(code, detail, { retryable })` has the message `[render:<code>] <detail>`; `toRenderJobFailure` recognises the instance or that message (a `NonRetryableError`, or a failed step's error read back from engine storage on a replay, which is a structured clone, not the class). Codes: the renderer's six plus `jobNotRunning`, `logoMissing`, `muxAssetErrored`, `muxAssetTimeout`, `muxUnavailable`, `rendererUnavailable`, `sourceUnavailable`, `workflowUnavailable`; anything else is `unexpected`. `summariseRenderError` (`code: detail`, else `name: message`, every `http(s)://` and `data:` URL replaced by `[url]`, whitespace collapsed, ≤ 300) is what `failRender` stores and the log prints; the watchdog's `instanceErrorSummary` now uses it (one URL stripper; task 7 carry).
- **2026-10-04 · Step configs are numbers of milliseconds, all explicit (`RENDER_STEP_CONFIG`).** `start` 3 × 5 s constant, `source-lookup` 3 × 10 s exponential, `source-poll-<n>` 2 × 5 s, `source-resolve` 3 × 10 s exponential, `logo` 3 × 5 s, `render` 2 × 1 min exponential with a 20 min timeout, `ready-poll-<n>` 2 × 10 s, `commit` and `fail` 3 × 5 s exponential; every other timeout 1 min. The waits are `RENDER_WAITS` (30 polls 10 s apart, a 1 h event wait, 15 polls 2 min apart).
- **2026-10-04 · `RENDER_WATCHDOG_CEILING` is a real upper bound, not the plan's 2 h 40.** `stepWorstCaseMs` counts every attempt timing out plus every retry delay (by backoff); `RENDER_WORKFLOW_MAX_MS` sums the whole straight path at its worst (30 source polls, the hour's wait and 15 polls, `commit` and `fail` included): **19,860 s = 5 h 31 min**, and the ceiling adds 30 min: **21,660 s = 6 h 01 min** (task 6 review I-2 corrected an earlier "about 4 h 40 min"). A shorter figure could terminate a slow but healthy run. The cost: a hung job reaches the admin's `render_failed` email after about 6–7 h (the ceiling plus the hourly sweep). `runStaleSweep` passes it; task 7's temporary `RENDER_WATCHDOG_CEILING_MS` is gone. A test sums every step's worst case (each poll config × 30 and × 15) plus the waits, checks it equals `RENDER_WORKFLOW_MAX_MS`, and pins both values, so a config change shows in review. (Superseded by the phase 7 fix wave (pipeline and infra): the render slot waits add 12 × (5 min + 63 min); the ceiling is 19 h 37 min.)
- **2026-10-04 · The ceiling clock is not reset by a render attempt (task 7 carry; supersedes the clock note in the task 7 entry).** `setRenderUpload` stores `mux_upload_id` only while the job is `running` and writes `updated_at = updated_at` (the column's `$onUpdate` would otherwise move it to now). The clock therefore runs from `queued → running` (`markRenderRunning`), which is what the ceiling was derived from; no `started_at` column or migration is needed. `markRenderRunning` answers `started | already-running | final | missing`, and `already-running` does not touch the row either.
- **2026-10-04 · `mux_upload_id` is never cleared.** Only `setRenderUpload` writes it; commit and fail leave it, so it stays the committed (or last) upload, as task 5's `isCurrentUpload` rule needs.
- **2026-10-04 · The source steps keep no signed URL.** `source-lookup` returns `{ assetId, master: status }` (it reads the master's status once after turning access on, so a master that is already ready needs no poll); the polls return only the status; `source-resolve` returns `{ kind: "master", assetId }` or the public rendition URL. The signed master URL is read again inside `render`; if the master stopped being ready in between, `render` falls back to the renditions, else `sourceUnavailable`. `firstReachable` uses the Mux client's `fetch` (the global one in the Worker). The test scans every step output of a full run for a `mux.com` URL with a signature/token parameter and for size < 16 KiB.
- **2026-10-04 · The logo step re-reads in `render`.** `logo` checks `MEDIA` (non-empty, ≤ 2 MiB, PNG/JPEG/WebP by its bytes with the checkout's `sniffLogoType`) and returns `{ bytes, sha256 }`; `render` reads it again, checks the same way and that the hash matches (`logoMissing` otherwise), and sends `data:<sniffed type>;base64,…` (chunked base64, a 2 MiB logo is too long for one `String.fromCharCode`).
- **2026-10-04 · `render`: cancel, create, store, render.** It refuses without a renderer (`rendererUnavailable`, before any upload) and when the job is not `running` (`jobNotRunning`: the watchdog failed it). The previous upload is cancelled; an `already-final` one with an asset has that asset deleted unless it is the gesture's own or the sponsorship's video (task 5 I-3). Cancels and deletes in `render`, `commit` and `fail` are best effort (404 done, failures logged and swallowed): an asset made later from an uncancelled upload is not current, and the webhook deletes it. (That covers only an asset whose `asset.ready` comes after the failure; the phase 7 fix wave (pipeline and infra) also releases the upload on the watchdog's failures.) The upload is `createRenderUpload` with the `SITE_URL` origin as `cors_origin` and `test: true` in dev only.
- **2026-10-04 · The commit decides by the sponsorship's `video_asset_id`.** After `completeRender`, the job counts as committed when the sponsorship holds the new asset: then the previous sponsored asset read in `start` is deleted unless it is the new one or the gesture's own. Otherwise the new asset is deleted. Ruling 4.8's test was "the job's `mux_asset_id` is not the new asset", but `completeRender` stores the asset on the job even when the sponsorship has left `rendering` (it only skips the transition), so that test would keep an asset nobody shows; the sponsorship's column is the one that says whether the video is used. A replayed commit finds itself committed, deletes nothing new (the previous asset's delete is then a 404) and returns `completed`.
- **2026-10-04 · An engine abort is not a failure (task 6 review I-1).** The engine throws `Error("Aborting engine: …")` into the Workflow's code to pause, terminate or restart it (from `step.do` after a stored result, `step.sleep` and `step.waitForEvent`; `ABORT_REASONS` in Miniflare's `workflows-shared`). `run`'s catch-all used to treat it as a job failure, so pausing an instance (most likely during the hour's wait) ran `fail`: `render_failed`, the admin emails, and a `failed` instance on resume. `runRenderJob` now rethrows any error whose message starts with `ENGINE_ABORT_PREFIX` before `failJob`; the job stays `running`, and the watchdog leaves a `paused` instance alone until the ceiling (ruling 12). Tests: a pause during the ready wait and at a sleep is rethrown, with no `fail` step and no email.
- **2026-10-04 · The non-retryable message is summarised (task 6 review M-2).** The adapter throws `NonRetryableError(nonRetryableMessage(failure))`: `[render:<code>] <detail>` with the detail's URLs replaced by `[url]` and at most 300 characters, so nothing signed reaches the instance's stored error. `toRenderJobFailure` also drops the closing quote of a replayed `Step threw a NonRetryableError with message "…"`.
- **2026-10-04 · The failure path.** `run` catches what escapes a step after its retries (or a failure outside a step: a Mux error event, 15 polls without a ready asset) and runs `fail`: `failRender`; for a job that is (now) `failed`, its keyed `admin_render_failed` emails with `onFailure: "throw"` (the step retries a queue outage; the email consumer's `email:sent:<key>` marker makes a replay send nothing new); and the job's upload cancelled. A job that already `succeeded` is a no-op and nothing is cancelled. The instance completes with `{ outcome: "failed", code }`.
- **2026-10-04 · The event type (ruling 9, I-4), checked.** Cloudflare's Workers API docs give the `waitForEvent`/`sendEvent` type as at most 100 characters matching `^[a-zA-Z0-9_][a-zA-Z0-9-_]*$` (a `.` is `workflow.invalid_event_type`). The installed Miniflare (`workflows-shared`) does not validate it at all, so the tests cannot catch a bad one. `renderEventType(uploadId)` is `mux-asset-<uploadId>` for 1–80 letters and digits (Mux upload ids), else `mux-asset-<first 32 hex of SHA-256>`; both sides call it.
- **2026-10-04 · The starter (`worker/render.ts`).** `fake` → `fakeRenderStarter` (unchanged); `container`/`local` → `workflowRenderStarter(RENDER_WORKFLOW, …)`: `create({ id: renderJobId, params: { renderJobId } })`. An error whose message holds `instance.already_exists` (Miniflare: `(instance.already_exists) Workflow instance with id "…" already exists`, pinned by a test against the real binding) counts as started; any other is thrown so the message retries. No binding → `failRender("workflowUnavailable: …")` and its emails. `renderStarterFor` (sponsorships) and `pendingRenderStarter` (jobs) are removed; the site tests that kept a job `queued` with them use a no-op test starter.
- **2026-10-04 · The webhook's render hooks (`renderWebhookHooks`).** With `RENDER_WORKFLOW`: `onRenderEvent` gets the instance (id = job id; `instance.not_found` → `gone`), answers `gone` for `complete`/`errored`/`terminated`, else `sendEvent({ type: renderEventType(uploadId), payload })`; any other failure throws (503, Mux retries). `isCurrentUpload` is `isCurrentRenderUpload`, one row (job + sponsorship) with task 5's rule. **An unknown job counts as current**, so its asset is never deleted: until phase 8 separates the Mux environments, staging and production receive each other's `render-job:` events, and a job this env does not know is another env's; forwarding it finds no instance (`GONE`). (Superseded by phase 8 task 4: the passthrough carries the env, another env's event is `IGNORED` before any hook, and an unknown job of this env is not current, so its asset is deleted.) Without the binding there are no hooks: render events are logged and answered `IGNORED`. The route also passes the env's Mux client, for the superseded-asset delete.
- **2026-10-04 · The spec's separate `upload` step is folded into `render` (amends spec §8.2.3, ruling 4.6).** The renderer PUTs to the upload URL itself, and a retried render must never PUT to a URL an earlier attempt may already have filled, so each `render` attempt creates its own upload and cancels the previous one; there is nothing left for an `upload` step to do.
- **2026-10-04 · The local loop by hand is not run in task 6.** (Done in task 9: `bun -F @smog/site render:local-loop`, see there.) It needs task 4's render server (`bun -F @smog/render serve`), which runs in parallel and is not on `develop` yet (`packages/render/src/server` does not exist at 8fb1083). The loop (`SMOG_DEV_RENDER_MODE=local`, the server with Chrome from `/opt/pw-browsers`, the dev server with the Mux fake server and its `emitWebhook`, a seeded paid sponsorship reaching `in_review` with a real overlay video) moves to the merge of tasks 4 and 6 (task 9's hardening), and its timing is recorded there.
- **2026-10-04 · The test-only `RENDER_WORKFLOW` binding.** `apps/site/vitest.config.ts` adds `miniflare.workflows.RENDER_WORKFLOW` (`smog-dev-render`, `RenderSponsorshipVideo`); `RENDER_MODE` stays `fake`, so only the render tests create instances, and the watchdog's behaviour in fake mode is the same (an unknown instance is re-enqueued, as with no binding). The introspection tests mock `source-lookup`, `source-resolve` and `render` (`mockStepResult`/`mockStepError`): the happy path ends with `mockEvent`; a 422 is a `mockStepError(NonRetryableError, 1)` followed by a successful mock that a retry would have reached, so a `failed` outcome proves no retry. The third test (an event sent before the wait) runs the real `render` step against the Mux fake with a renderer held by a KV flag: a promise from the test's own request context cannot be used, because workerd cancels the instance's context as hung when it waits on another context's promise.

## Render job control (phase 7 task 7)

- **2026-10-04 · `retryRender` (A-27) is the job builder with a `retried` option.** `createRenderJobStatements(db, { retried: { actorId } })` reads the sponsorship expecting `render_failed` (and no active job), and its batch starts with `transitionStatements(render_failed → rendering, render_retried)`, then the existing `rendering` guard, the active-job guard, the new job (`attempt` = the highest + 1, a new id that is its Workflow instance id) and `render_started`. `retryRenderStatements` wraps it and refuses with `SponsorshipActionError` (`notFound`, or `stale` for any other status); two concurrent retries both build a plan, and the second batch fails its transition guard (`stale`). The admin handler (`AdminSponsorshipServices.retryRender`, injected by `@smog/api`) appends `sponsorship.retry_render { renderJobId, attempt }` to the same batch and enqueues `render.requested` afterwards with the default (log on failure): the watchdog re-sends a lost one, so a queue outage does not fail an action that committed. The input is `{ id }` like every other `admin.sponsorships` action (the brief wrote `{ sponsorshipId }`; the slice's convention wins).
- **2026-10-04 · The render watchdog (ruling 12) is `reconcileRenderJobs`, in the hourly stale sweep after the payment reconciliation.** It reads two seeks on `render_job_status_updated_idx` (migration 0011; query-plan test): `queued` with `updated_at` older than 10 minutes, and, only with a Workflow binding, every `running` job (the phase 7 fix wave (pipeline and infra): without one, the `running` jobs past the ceiling); each oldest first with its own budget of 25 (`RENDER_WATCHDOG_BUDGET`), so old queued jobs cannot starve the running ones or the reverse (review M-7). The table of ruling 12 is implemented exactly, plus one row; `rollingBack` (a status the installed runtime types list and the ruling does not) is treated as active. Details:
  - A re-enqueue first claims the job (`updated_at = now` where it is still `queued` with the `updated_at` read), so a second run within 10 minutes sends nothing (run twice, one effect) and two overlapping runs send once.
  - (Superseded by task 6: `setRenderUpload` keeps `updated_at`, so the clock runs from `queued → running` without resets, and the ceiling is `RENDER_WATCHDOG_CEILING`, 6 h 01 min.) The running ceiling is measured from the job's `updated_at`. Task 6's `render` step writes `mux_upload_id`, which bumps `updated_at` (`$onUpdate`), so each render attempt restarts the clock: the effective bound is the ceiling plus the time before the last render attempt, still finite. `RENDER_WATCHDOG_CEILING_MS` here is the plan's figure (2 h 40 min + 30 min); Task 6 derives `RENDER_WATCHDOG_CEILING` from `RENDER_STEP_CONFIG` and should pass it (the function takes `ceilingMs`).
  - The ceiling row calls `terminate()` before `failRender("timed out")` (B-3), so a late commit cannot land after the failure. A `terminate` that throws (the instance ended in between) leaves the job for the next run, which then sees the ended instance and fails it through its own row (tested). When `terminate` succeeds and `failRender` then throws, the next run likewise sees `terminated` and fails the job as "workflow terminated", counted under `renderFailed` instead of `renderTimedOut`: the outcome is right, only the reason and the counter differ (review M-4).
  - **Addition to ruling 12 (review M-5): a `queued` job whose instance stays active (`queued`, `paused`, …) gets the same ceiling, measured from the job's `updated_at`,** then `terminate()` and `failRender("timed out")`. The ruling's table left such a job untouched for ever (an instance stuck in the engine's own queue, or paused by hand).
  - The types follow the runtime's names (review M-1): `WorkflowInstanceStatus` is the string union (`InstanceStatus["status"]`), `WorkflowInstanceState` the `{ status, error }` that `instance.status()` answers, and `WorkflowStatusPort.status(id)` answers `WorkflowInstanceState | "not-found"`.
  - An instance's error is summarised by `instanceErrorSummary` (`workflow <status>: <name>: <message>`, every URL replaced by `[url]`), then capped at 300 by `failRender`.
  - A job whose port call fails is logged and skipped; the others continue. Counted in `[cron] stale {…}` as `renderRequeued`, `renderFailed`, `renderTimedOut` (prefixed, because the stale result already has a payment `failed`).
  - The site adapts `RENDER_WORKFLOW` in `worker/scheduled.ts` (`workflowStatusPort`): `get(id)` throwing an error whose message holds the code `instance.not_found` (Miniflare's `get` throws `new Error("instance.not_found")`, its engine `(instance.not_found) Instance does not exist`) is `"not-found"`. **Only that code** (review I-1): any other error, "Workflow not found" or "script not found" during a deploy included, is thrown, and the watchdog leaves the job for the next run instead of failing a healthy render. The site's test pool has no Workflow binding before Task 6, so the code is pinned by the adapter's tests and Miniflare's source; Task 6, which adds the test `RENDER_WORKFLOW` binding, adds the test of `get()` for an unknown id against it. No binding is `null`.
- **2026-10-04 · Force expire deletes through `@smog/video` `deleteAsset`.** The admin's hand-written `DELETE` is gone: the same function as the expiry sweep (404 is done, other statuses throw and are logged by both `[video]` and `[admin]`, and the expiry stands). Closes the phase 6 cleanup carry (ruling 13).
- **2026-10-04 · The retry button sits in the status card and in the render jobs section.** Both are the same `RetryRender` component: a confirm dialog naming the sponsorship and gesture, then a toast and a polite live-region announcement (`admin.sponsorships.retryRender.*`, nl/en/fr). Focus returns to the button when the dialog closes; once the refetched detail has left `render_failed` and the button is gone, focus moves to the announcement instead of the page body. While a retry runs, both buttons wait (`useRetryRenderPending`, `useIsMutating` on the procedure's mutation key; review M-2), so a second confirm cannot follow the first. The trail's `render_started` line now names its attempt (`Poging 2`), so a retry reads as such. The `renderJobs.retryLater` copy is removed and `actions.notes.renderFailed` points at the render jobs section.
- **2026-10-04 · The e2e seed's `sponsorship` op seeds a failed first render (Minor 4).** For `status: "render_failed"` it also inserts a `failed` `render_job` (attempt 1, id `<id>-job-1`, error `renderer answered 500`) and its `render_started`/`render_failed` trail, inserted, never updated. The admin spec retries it on a gesture of its own (an unpublished `Zzrender <word>` gesture and category made by the test through the admin API, removed in `afterAll`; review M-6), so no shared seed gesture changes state under another spec, and sees `in_review` with attempt 2 in fake mode.

## The wizard preview (phase 7 task 8)

- **2026-10-04 · `SponsorPreview` (S-10, ruling 8).** The review step and the re-edit page mount the real `SponsoredVideo` in `@remotion/player` over `https://stream.mux.com/{id}/highest.mp4`, then `/high.mp4` (`use-source-metadata.ts`: `readSourceMetadata` in the browser, cached per playback id for the page's life, failures too; an unmount or a new id disposes the `mediabunny` input). One Player at a time; the review step's gestures are a toolbar of poster toggles (`aria-pressed`, one tab stop, arrows/Home/End move the focus, Enter/Space chooses, the focus stays). It loads paused on the last frame, muted, with the kit's Play/Pause and "Show the ending" (seek to `overlayStartFrame`, clamped at 0, and play); Play from the last frame replays from 0. `loadOverlayFont()` resolves before the Player mounts; props pass `sponsoredVideoPropsSchema`, and an invalid name (empty, too long, while typing) keeps the last valid one, so the Player never remounts for a name or logo. Neither MP4, or a playback error (`errorFallback`): the same composition over the Mux thumbnail (720 × 960, 6 s) with a note; if that image fails too, the poster and the note.
- **2026-10-04 · Client only, lazy (I-6).** `preview-slot.tsx`: `import.meta.env.SSR ? null : lazy(() => import("./sponsor-preview"))`; the server and the first client render show the poster in the same fixed 3:4 box with the controls disabled, so nothing shifts. Only `sponsor-preview.tsx` imports `@remotion/player`, `@smog/render/composition` and `use-source-metadata` (`mediabunny`), and a unit test scans the folder for it. The frame is `role="img"` named "Preview for {name}"; the composition inside is `aria-hidden` (its `Img`s have no `alt`).
- **2026-10-04 · Review fixes (task 8 review M-1 to M-5, M-7).** A font that fails to load mounts no Player: the poster shows with its own note (`sponsor.preview.unavailable`), never the "cannot be played" note. The font state starts from `isOverlayFontLoaded()`, so a second preview (a picker switch) announces no loading state. The controls stay enabled from the first frame through a fallback remount (no-ops without a Player handle), and when every fallback fails while a control has the focus, the focus moves to the note (`tabIndex=-1`). Dutch: "kan hier niet worden afgespeeld". The re-edit page cannot show a kept logo (the stored logo is admin-only, `/api/logos/$key`; a token-guarded read would be a new authenticated path, not this task's), so under the preview it says the current logo stays and is not shown (`sponsor.preview.currentLogo`) until a new file is chosen; carried to task 9. (Superseded by task 9: a token-guarded read, `POST /api/sponsor/reedit-logo`, shows the kept logo; the note and its key are gone.) A unit test bundles `preview-slot.tsx` with `Bun.build` and `import.meta.env.SSR` defined both ways: the server bundle has `LazySponsorPreview = null` and no path to the Player module.
- **2026-10-04 · `numberOfSharedAudioTags={0}`.** The Player's shared `<audio>` tags load a silent `data:audio/mp3`, which the CSP's `media-src` refuses (the CSP e2e caught it). The composition has no sound, so the Player gets none; the CSP is unchanged.
- **2026-10-04 · The deploy guard's entry-chunk check.** The client build writes its Vite manifest (`environments.client.build.manifest`), kept out of the deployed assets by `public/.assetsignore` (`.vite`, merged into the plugin's own). `checkClientRenderIsLazy` allows the markers only in the `sponsor-preview.tsx` dynamic entry and the chunks it imports statically that no other root (an entry, a lazy route) imports (review M-6): not in an entry chunk, not in a route chunk, not in a chunk shared with one. None of those may contain `remotion_delayRenderHandles` (remotion) or `Input has been disposed` (mediabunny's `Input`; its load-time "loaded twice" check is tree-shaken from the browser build). `--dist <dir>` lets a fixture test run the whole `--bundle` guard on a built tree (a `dist/server` chunk with Remotion, an entry chunk with Remotion). The Player chunk is about 650 kB (186 kB gzip), loaded on the review step only.
- **2026-10-04 · The e2e source is VP9, not H.264.** Playwright's Chromium (`/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194`, and the CI download) is an open-source build without H.264: an H.264 MP4 reads (mediabunny) but the `<video>` errors, so the Player falls back to the image. `apps/site/e2e/fixtures/preview-2s.mp4` is a 2 s 360 × 640 VP9-in-MP4 clip (3.9 kB, made with Remotion's bundled ffmpeg from generated PNG frames): a flat slate background with the frame number in large seven-segment digits and a small square crossing the top, so Play visibly advances, and nothing in the lower half where the overlay goes (review I-1: the first clip had a dark band exactly behind the overlay, which made the screenshots look like a contrast defect). The sponsor e2e uploads an opaque 160 × 160 logo (`e2e/fixtures/logo.png`, a green ring on white) instead of the old 1 × 1 50 %-alpha pixel. The clip is answered for `stream.mux.com/*/{highest,high}.mp4` with byte ranges and CORS (`stubMuxRenditions`). Task 4's H.264 `source-2s.mp4` stays the render's fixture; it cannot feed the e2e Player. Real Mux renditions are H.264, which every shipping browser plays.
- **2026-10-04 · Site dependency: `mediabunny`** (catalog) for the browser `UrlSource` input; `remotion` is not a direct site dependency (nothing in the site imports it; knip). The knip ignore for `@remotion/player` is removed. `sponsor.review.previewLabel` moves to `sponsor.preview.label`.

## Hardening, parity, the staging switch and docs (phase 7 task 9)

- **2026-10-04 · [parity] The overlay layout is the old router's 22 / 76 / 3.8 / 87 (supersedes the task 3 review's I-3).** The parity walk traced which config made the old system's sponsored videos (`origin/master`):

  `RENDER_OVERLAY_LAYOUT` is `logo: { centerX: 0.5, centerY: 0.76, size: 0.22 }`, `text: { fontSize: 0.038, y: 0.87 }` again. At 1080 × 1920: the logo box 237.6 × 422.4 centred on (540, 1459.2), line 1's top at 1670.4, the base size 72.96 px, line 2 at line 1 + 1.5 × the size. The contract, geometry, fit and DOM tests pin these; spec §8.2.1 and the 2026-09-29 layout entry are back to them.
- **2026-10-04 · [parity] The walk, beside the old composition.** Checked on the local `test:render` stills and the local loop's MP4 (`render-last-frame.png`, `render-arabic-last-frame.png`, `local-loop.mp4`; the CI lane's artifact cannot be downloaded from this sandbox, whose `gh` reaches api.github.com only, and the lane runs the same test in the image):
  - **Timing:** nothing before `durationInFrames − 5 × 30`; a `spring({ damping: 200 })` over 30 frames for the opacity; `translateY` 30 → 0 px. A clip under 5 s has the overlay from frame 0, as the old negative start frame did.
