  - **Text:** line 1's top at `0.87 × height` (556.8 px at 640), line 2 at `+ 1.2 × size + 0.3 × size`, both centred, `nowrap`, weight 600, `#00805F` (`tokens.color.brand.green`, the old `#00805f`). The ΔE probe finds 550 brand-green px in line 1's rows for a short name and 193 for the 35-character name (gates 300 and 100).
  - **Size and frame rate:** from the source (the fixture's 360 × 640 at 30 fps, 60 frames in, 60 frames out, H.264).
  - **Documented differences (each with its own entry):** the bundled Inter 600 instead of `system-ui` (DejaVu in the old image); a long name shrinks to fit 90 % of the width; sizes rounded down to even; a pinned line height of 1.2; `OffthreadVideo` instead of `@remotion/media`'s `Video`; the bundle built with the image instead of per request; the source found by `GET /playback-ids/{id}` instead of paging the asset list; the master polled every 10 s (30 times) instead of every 2 s; the rendered asset's upload sends `cors_origin` = the `SITE_URL` origin instead of `"*"` and no `master_access`.
  - No stored old sponsored video was given by the owner; comparing one frame of it stays open (PROGRESS).
- **2026-10-04 · The local render loop runs (`bun -F @smog/site render:local-loop`).** Opt-in, outside `bun run test` and `release:check` (it needs Chrome; `RENDER_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE` or Remotion's download). `apps/site/scripts/render-local-loop.ts` starts the Mux fake in its own process (so it can read the uploaded file back), the render server on 3002 (the dev `RENDER_LOCAL_URL` default) and `vite dev` with `SMOG_DEV_RENDER_MODE=local` and the fake's URL, token and webhook secret. As the seeded admin it makes an unpublished gesture of its own whose playback id one ready fake asset carries, uploads a logo, seeds an `open` checkout with that logo through `/dev/e2e-seed` and calls `admin.sponsorships.markPaid`. That is the real path: `payment.settled` → the job → `render.requested` → `workflowRenderStarter` → the Workflow (`start`, `source-lookup` with master access, one 10 s poll, `source-resolve` on the master, `logo` from `MEDIA`, `render` against the real server, the fake's `video.asset.ready` webhook → `sendEvent` → `ready-<uploadId>`, `commit`). It waits for `in_review`, checks the job is `succeeded`, the sponsorship's video is the render's playback id (not the fake render), the source's master was readied, and the MP4 is H.264 at 360 × 640 with 60 frames, saves it in `packages/render/.render-out/` and removes what it made. The local dev mailbox (KV `dev:mail`, shared with the e2e) is saved before and put back after: the first full e2e after a loop run failed the CSP spec's `/dev/mail` test on the loop's emails, whose images pointed at the loop's port. Measured here: `in_review` 20.6 s after mark paid (about 10 s of it the first master poll, 6 s the render), 97.8 kB, the brand-green overlay and the uploaded logo in the last frame.
- **2026-10-04 · The render server rebuilds its bundle on each dev start.** The loop's first run rendered the old layout: `serve` (and a local `test:render`) built `.render-bundle/` once and kept it, so a changed composition rendered stale, silently. `bundleAction({ canBundle, environment, hasBundle })` now builds when the bundle is missing, and in `RENDER_ENVIRONMENT=dev` on every start where `@remotion/bundler` resolves (`bundlerInstalled()`), about 15 s. The image has no bundler, so it keeps using its own bundle, also in the CI lane, which runs it with `RENDER_ENVIRONMENT=dev` for `RENDER_ALLOW_HTTP`.
- **2026-10-04 · The re-edit preview shows the kept logo (task 8 review M-5).** `POST /api/sponsor/reedit-logo` with `{ token }` in a JSON body (never the URL, which Workers Logs record) answers the stored logo of the open re-edit link's own sponsorship (`readReeditLogo` in `@smog/sponsorships/server`). It reads the link the way `reedit.get` does (the SHA-256 of the token, the `reedit` purpose, unused and unexpired), then requires `changes_requested`, a paid logo and the object, and sniffs the type from the bytes. The token is the only input, so no other sponsorship's logo is reachable. A foreign origin is `403`, `RL_API` per IP applies, and every refusal (no, malformed, wrong, used or expired token, another status, no logo, a missing or non-image object) is the same `404`. The answer is `private, no-store` with `nosniff`, `same-origin` CORP and the sandboxing CSP, so nothing is cached across tokens. `useKeptLogo` reads it into a `Blob` for the preview until a new file replaces it; a refused read shows no logo. `sponsor.preview.currentLogo` is removed. The admin-only `/api/logos/$key` is unchanged. A route, not an rpc procedure, because the answer is bytes. Alternative: a data URL from `reedit.get` (2.7 MB of JSON on every read of the link).
- **2026-10-04 · The review screenshots take Goedemorgen.** In a full `sponsor.spec.ts` run with `SPONSOR_SHOTS_DIR` set, the paid-flow test had already taken Broer, so the screenshot test could not select it. It now selects Goedemorgen, which no test sponsors (the R-11 test only preselects it).
- **2026-10-04 · The staging switch is prepared, not flipped.** `release-config-check` passes on the committed files, staging's built config is unchanged (`deploy:dry` against `aa77d2c`), and the scratch-copy gate build of task 2 was re-run on the final tree (container without the flag fails, with it the build, the guard and `wrangler deploy --dry-run --containers-rollout=none` pass). The owner's flip and the staging smoke are in PROGRESS. (The phase 7 review found the gap this missed: that gate build ran by hand, with the flag and `--containers-rollout=none`, while CI's core lane ran `deploy:dry` with neither, so the flip itself would have failed CI. Fixed in the phase 7 fix wave (pipeline and infra).)

## CI test hang: streamed output, bounded runs (2026-10-04)

- **2026-10-04 · What hung.** Deploy runs 37161447962 (6e9fbe2) and 37170326892 (41507ef) were cancelled at the 45-minute job timeout in the tests lane. In both, every turbo task printed its group except `@smog/site:test` (a log fetched with a line limit loses its head, which makes `@smog/config`, `@smog/utils`, `@smog/video`, `@smog/styles` and `//:test:scripts` look missing too; the full log has them). The orphans at cancel were the same both times: `bun` → `bash` → `node` (the site's `vitest run`), one `esbuild` service and one `workerd` whose pid follows Vitest's own, and no `bun test` process. A live local reproduction (another worktree's release check, 2026-10-04) showed the mechanism: all 28 site test files reported done (`✓ test/… (n tests)`), but no summary for 49 minutes; Vitest's main process and the **first** pool worker's `workerd` were both idle in `epoll`, with the WebSocket between them still open. That worker never sent `testfileFinished`, so Vitest's `pool.run` for its file never resolved. Vitest has no timeout for that wait (`teardownTimeout` only bounds the close after the summary). It happens inside `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` 1.3.6 (the latest; there is no newer release to take) and was not narrowed further. Reproduced here once in 5 full `CI=true bun -F @smog/site test` runs (2 on 4 CPUs green; 3 under `taskset -c 0,1` with the results cache removed, so one worker runs the files in CI's order: two green, one stuck after its 22nd file, `test/admin-gate.test.ts`, until the wall-clock limit fired after 25 minutes), on a machine with a load average of 20–30. With one worker the files after the stuck one never start. The dump matched the CI orphans and the live hang: Vitest idle, one `workerd` idle (9.8 CPU seconds), the loopback server and the socket to that `workerd` open.
- **2026-10-04 · `StallReporter` ends a run whose pool stopped after a file, and the wrapper retries it once.** `@smog/config/testing/vitest` counts the run's files. When a file has ended and for 3 minutes no file is open, no next file has started and the run has not ended (Vitest bounds a worker start at 90 s), it prints `[vitest-stall] n of N test files finished (p passed, f failed), m never ran …` and exits 0 when every file ran and passed (the parallel case: only a teardown error the stuck worker might have sent is lost), 1 when a file failed, and 75 (EX_TEMPFAIL) when files never ran and none failed. `scripts/test-deadline.ts` runs a command that exits 75 once more, under the same overall limit, with a `::warning` in GitHub Actions. A retry is honest here: the stuck worker is the pool's bug, not a test's, and a second stuck run fails the lane.
- **2026-10-04 · turbo streams the test output.** The root `test` script runs `turbo run test test:scripts --log-order=stream`: in CI turbo defaults to `grouped`, which holds a task's output until it ends, so a hung task showed nothing. Lines keep the `<package>:test:` prefix.
- **2026-10-04 · Shared Workers-pool settings.** Every `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin` config spreads `WORKERS_POOL_TEST_OPTIONS`: `testTimeout` 30 s, `hookTimeout` 60 s, `teardownTimeout` 30 s (the site overrides 60 s and 180 s), and the reporters: `verbose` in CI (every test as it ends) or `default`, plus `StallReporter`. It prints `[vitest] start <file>` for each file in CI and, when no test has finished for 2.5 minutes (above the site's warm-up), `[vitest-stall]` with every open file, whether it is loading/in hooks or running, its running tests and for how long. Its types are structural, so `@smog/config` does not depend on Vitest.
- **2026-10-04 · D1 migrations run in a `beforeAll`.** `@smog/db/testing/apply-migrations` was a top-level `await` in a setup file, which nothing bounds; it is now a `beforeAll`, so a stuck D1 call fails the file after `hookTimeout` and names the hook. A setup file's hooks run before the test file's, and no test file touches D1 at import time.
- **2026-10-04 · A wall-clock limit on every test command (`scripts/test-deadline.ts`).** Each command of every package's `test` script (Vitest, `bun test`, Jest) and the root `test:scripts` runs as `bun <root>/scripts/test-deadline.ts -- <command>`. The command gets its own process group. After 25 minutes (`--minutes`, or `SMOG_TEST_DEADLINE_MINUTES`) the wrapper prints `Test command hung` with the command and directory (an `::error` annotation in GitHub Actions), the processes left with their state and CPU seconds (a busy loop is `R` with growing CPU, a deadlock `S`), and a Node diagnostic report for every Node process in the group (`--report-on-signal` via `NODE_OPTIONS`, SIGUSR2: the JS stack and the active handles); then it kills the group (SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 10 s) and exits 124. It forwards SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP to the group, and on a normal exit kills anything the command left behind. The site's Vitest took up to 20 minutes here on 2 loaded CPUs (about 5 in CI), so 25 avoids false alarms and still fails well inside the job's 45. knip's Bun plugin only finds test files through a script that starts with `bun test`, so the workspaces that relied on it list their test files as entries in `knip.json`.
- **2026-10-04 · Bun stays on 1.3.11.** 1.4.2 is out, but nothing points at `bun test`: no `bun test` process was left in either hang, and every `bun test` is bounded by the wrapper anyway.
- **2026-10-04 · Reading the next hang.** A `[vitest-stall] n of N test files finished` line is this pool bug, handled (and retried once when files never ran). Otherwise find the `[test-deadline]` block: the command names the package and runner; for a Workers-pool Vitest the last `[vitest-stall]` names the file and whether it was loading (no test started: a module fetch or a wedged `workerd`) or running a test; the process list says whether `workerd` burns CPU; the Node report shows what Vitest's main process waits on.

## Phase 7 fix wave (pipeline and infra)

From the whole-phase reviews of the render pipeline (C-1, I-1, I-2, M-1 to M-5) and of the render server, image, CI and deploy (C-1, M-1 to M-5).

- **2026-10-04 · No capacity is a wait, not a failure (pipeline C-1).** One container per job with `max_instances` 2 (staging) and 4 (production) and `sleepAfter` 10 min failed every job past the 2nd or 4th of a checkout: three `render` attempts in about 3 minutes all met the containers library's capacity refusal. Two changes:
  - **A container is stopped as soon as its render answers.** `httpRenderer` takes a `release(renderJobId)`; `rendererFor` passes `getContainer(env.RENDERER, id).stop()` (SIGTERM over RPC; the server has no render running then and exits at once), run after the answer is read, whatever it was, and also after a network fault; a failing stop is logged and swallowed. `sleepAfter` 10 min stays as a backstop. A retry of the same job starts the container again. If an attempt that went on past its step timeout answers late, its stop reaches a container running the next attempt: the server's SIGTERM drain lets that render finish (≤ 14 min) and refuses new requests, which the Workflow then sees as `busy` or a retryable failure.
  - **`busy` is a wait.** The containers library's own `503` that is not a `RenderResult` ("There is no Container instance available", `container.js` `containerFetch`) is read as `{ ok: false, code: "busy" }`, the same as the server's own `503 busy`. `renderStep` returns `{ busy: true }` for it (so it uses none of the step's retries; the attempt's upload is cancelled by the next attempt), and `runRenderJob` loops `render`, then `render-slot-wait-<n>` (`step.sleep`, 5 min) and `render-<n + 1>`, at most `RENDER_WAITS.renderSlotWaits` = 12 times (an hour of waiting), then fails with `rendererBusy` (final). Step names stay deterministic: the first attempt is still `render`, so the introspection tests' mocks are unchanged. No Workflows concurrency setting is used (none limits instances of a Workflow by a resource).
  - **The ceiling counts the waits at their worst.** A `busy` answer may come after failed attempts of the same `render-<n>` step, so each slot is counted as its sleep plus `stepWorstCaseMs(render)` (63 min): `RENDER_WORKFLOW_MAX_MS` grows by 12 × 68 min to **68,820 s = 19 h 07 min**, and `RENDER_WATCHDOG_CEILING` is **19 h 37 min** (test pinned). The cost is that an instance stuck in an *active* state is terminated later; an instance that errors, ends or disappears is still failed by the next hourly run whatever its age.
  - Tests: a renderer busy 3 times then free (one attempt per step, a cancelled upload per busy attempt); busy for ever (`rendererBusy`, the emails, every upload cancelled); five jobs over a renderer with 2 slots run concurrently, with real waits, all commit and at most 2 render at once; the site's `httpRenderer` maps the library's 503 to `busy`, keeps the server's own, and stops the container after a result, a network fault, and when the stop itself fails.
- **2026-10-04 · A failure releases the job's upload wherever it happens (pipeline I-1).** `dropUpload`'s use on a failed job is `releaseRenderUpload(mux, job)` (cancel; an `already-final` upload's asset deleted unless it is the gesture's own or the sponsorship's video), run by the Workflow's `fail` and now by every watchdog failure. `reconcileRenderJobs` and `runStaleSweep` take `mux: Mux | null`; the stale cron passes `createMux(siteEnv().worker)`. So an asset whose `asset.ready` was handled before the instance errored (a D1 incident in `commit` and `fail`, or an infrastructure fault) is deleted when the watchdog fails the job.
- **2026-10-04 · A Workflow that never starts fails its job (pipeline I-2).** A `queued` job whose instance is not found was re-sent every hour for ever (`requeue` moves `updated_at`), and an `unknown` or unreadable instance was skipped for ever. Now:
  - `watchdogQuery` also selects `created_at`, which nothing moves (the seek is unchanged; its query-plan test passes).
  - A `queued` job created more than `RENDER_NEVER_STARTED_MS` (6 h) ago whose instance is not found, `unknown`, whose `status()` keeps throwing, or for which there is no binding at all, fails with `workflowNeverStarted: the Workflow did not start within 6 hours (<why>)`: `render_failed`, the admin emails, the retry button. A best-effort `terminate()` comes first when an instance may exist (a terminate error is logged, the failure goes ahead).
  - A `running` job created longer ago than the ceiling whose instance is `unknown` or unreadable fails the same way, as `timed out (status unknown)` / `timed out (status unreadable)`.
  - Within those bounds nothing changes: re-sent, or logged and left for the next run.
  - `workflowNeverStarted` joins `RENDER_JOB_FAILURE_CODES` (with `rendererBusy`), so the stored error reads `code: detail` like the Workflow's own.
- **2026-10-04 · `setRenderUpload` is a compare-and-set; Mux calls time out (pipeline M-1).** It writes only `WHERE status = 'running' AND mux_upload_id IS <the id the attempt read>` and answers `set`, `not-running` or `superseded`. On `superseded` (an earlier attempt that went on past its timeout stored its upload first), `render` cancels its own new upload and throws a retryable error, so the step's next attempt starts from the stored upload; the webhook never sees a current upload swapped under the instance's wait. `muxRequest` passes `AbortSignal.timeout(30 s)` (`MUX_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, `Mux.timeoutMs` for tests), so a hung Mux call ends well inside its step.
- **2026-10-04 · `fail` finishes a commit that committed (pipeline M-2, M-5).** When `fail`'s `failRender` answers `noop` because the job already `succeeded` (`completeRender` committed, then `commit` was cut short), it runs the commit's cleanup: the previous sponsored asset read in `start` is deleted unless it is the video now or the gesture's own. The instance then completes as `{ outcome: "noop" }`, not `failed`, so it never contradicts D1. Test: every commit attempt commits and loses its result while Mux refuses the previous asset's delete; `fail` deletes it, no email is sent.
- **2026-10-04 · `POST /api/sponsor/reedit-logo` reads at most 1 KiB (pipeline M-3).** `readCappedBody(request, 1024)` then `JSON.parse`; a longer body is the same `404 NOT_FOUND` as any other refusal, before any D1 read (a token is 43 characters). Test: a valid token padded past 1 KiB is refused, padded under it is served.
- **2026-10-04 · Switching back to `fake` with jobs in flight (pipeline M-4, infra M-4).** Without a binding the watchdog now also reads the `running` jobs past the ceiling (the same seek with `updated_at < now − ceiling`) and fails each with `workflowUnavailable: the render pipeline was turned off while this job ran`, releasing its upload. `queued` jobs are re-sent (the fake starter finishes them) and fail past the never-started bound. The off switch is documented as two steps in PROGRESS, `render-config.ts` and AGENTS.md: the env's `RENDER_MODE` back to `fake` first, then the flag removed (the flag alone, with `container` committed, fails every deploy on purpose); in-flight jobs should drain first, because with `fake` the Mux webhook answers render events `IGNORED`.
- **2026-10-04 · CI's staging dry runs survive the flip (infra C-1).** The core lane's `deploy:dry` passed no flag, so the owner's step 3 (staging `RENDER_MODE=container`) would have turned every CI run red and staging could never deploy again; with only the flag, wrangler's dry run needs Docker and builds the whole image.
  - `deploy:dry` is `CLOUDFLARE_ENV=staging SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1 bun run deploy --dry-run --containers-rollout=none`. With `fake` the flag adds nothing: staging's built `wrangler.json` with and without it is identical (checked on this tree). With `container`, `--containers-rollout=none` and `--dry-run` keep wrangler offline (`getContainerMetadataForRolloutSkip` returns early) and Docker-free. The wrong order still fails where it should: the deploy job's resources step refuses before anything runs (`renderGateRefusal`), and a build without the flag fails.
  - **The gate-on path runs in CI before the flip.** `deploy:dry:render` builds staging as `container` without editing `wrangler.jsonc`: `SMOG_DRY_RENDER_MODE=container` (read by the Vite hook, `applyRenderGate({ dryMode })`: `container` only, staging or production only, and still needing the flag; it sets the var) with `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1`, then `deploy-guard.ts --dry-run`, then `wrangler deploy --env staging --dry-run --containers-rollout=none`. The deploy guard refuses a build made with `SMOG_DRY_RENDER_MODE` unless it runs with `--dry-run` (`checkDryRenderOverride`), and the `deploy` script never passes it, so the override cannot reach a real deploy. The core lane runs it before `deploy:dry`, so the `dist/` it leaves is the real staging build.
  - `release-config-check` requires both scripts in `release:check:core` and every part of each (`checkSiteDryRuns`: the staging env, the flag, `--dry-run`, `--containers-rollout=none`, and for the gate-on run the override and the guard's `--dry-run`), with tests.
  - **Proven on a scratch copy** with `env.staging.vars.RENDER_MODE = "container"`, `SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE` unset in the environment (as in CI) and `docker` replaced by a stub that exits 127: `release-config-check` ok; `deploy:dry` exit 0 (`deploy-guard: ok (staging)`, the bindings `RENDERER (SmogRenderer)`, `RENDER_WORKFLOW (RenderSponsorshipVideo)`, `RENDER_MODE ("container")`, the container `smog-staging-renderer` with the absolute Dockerfile path, `--dry-run: exiting now.`); `deploy:dry:render` exit 0 with the same; the resources step without the flag exits 1 with `[provision] env.staging has RENDER_MODE=container, which needs SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1 … Nothing was run.`; `vite build` without the flag exits 1 with the gate's message; the guard with `SMOG_DRY_RENDER_MODE` and no `--dry-run` exits 1.
- **2026-10-04 · The deploy job caches the render image (infra M-1).** After the flip every develop push rebuilt the ~1 GB image uncached and pushed most of it. Chosen: keep wrangler's Dockerfile build (the render gate, the guard's absolute-path checks and the container block stay as they are) and give it a warm cache. With the gate on (the resources step writes `render_pipeline=true` to `GITHUB_OUTPUT`, `renderPipelineOutput`), the job sets up a buildx builder and builds the same Dockerfile and context with `docker/build-push-action` (`load: true`, `provenance: false`, `linux/amd64`, `cache-from` the job's own GHA scope `smog-renderer-deploy` and then the render lane's `smog-renderer`, `cache-to` its own scope with `mode=max`, a scope of its own so the two writers never overwrite each other's index). The Deploy step then runs with `BUILDX_BUILDER` set to that builder, so wrangler's `docker build --load …` finds every layer in its cache, and with `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=0` on both builds, so unchanged render inputs give the same image, the same digest, and wrangler's container step reports no change instead of rolling the container. Rejected: pushing to the Cloudflare registry ourselves and pointing `containers[].image` at a reference, skipping the build when a hash of the render inputs is unchanged. It needs registry credentials in the job, a second source of truth for "unchanged" (a missed input would deploy a stale renderer) and a different container block than the one the guard checks. Not verifiable here (no Docker daemon, no credentials): the first flips should show `no changes smog-<env>-renderer` on a docs-only push; if the digest still moves, every deploy rolls the container, which PROGRESS owner action 6 now says.
- **2026-10-04 · The render lane starts the image as the Container does (infra M-2).** After `test:render`, `release-check-render.ts` starts the image again with `RENDER_ENVIRONMENT=staging` and no `RENDER_ALLOW_HTTP` (so the strict env schema, the `0.0.0.0` bind and the https-only rules apply), on port 8081 (`stagingRunArgs`), waits for `GET /health`, and posts a request that is valid but for its `http:` source (`httpSourceProbe`), which must answer `422 invalidInput` without touching the network. It runs whatever the render's result, and the lane fails if either fails.
- **2026-10-04 · A source over 120 s fails at once (infra M-3).** `RENDER_SOURCE_MAX_SECONDS = 120` (`@smog/render/contract`): about 110 s of 1080 × 1920 fits one 19-minute attempt on one vCPU, so a longer source would burn three attempts and then fail. After `readSourceMetadata` the server answers `422 sourceTooLong` ("the source is N s long; at most 120 s can be rendered"), a new final code in `RENDER_ERROR_CODES`, so the Workflow fails the job after one attempt. **[parity change]** The old renderer had no cap. The admin's render jobs list shows a hint under the error for `sourceTooLong`, `workflowNeverStarted` and `rendererBusy` (`admin.sponsorships.renderJobs.hint.*`, nl/en/fr): shorten or replace the gesture's video, check the deploy and the Workflows dashboard, or retry when fewer videos are being made. The wizard does not refuse long gestures (the preview is the UI fix wave's); phase 9's p95 check may move the cap.
- **2026-10-04 · The local render loop cleans up on Ctrl+C, and never deletes a mailbox it could not read (infra M-5).** Cleanups are registered as they are set up (the dev mailbox, the Mux fake, the children, the fixture) and run once, last first, a failing one logged, at the end of `main` or on `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` (then exit 130/143). The render server and `vite dev` start in their own process groups (`detached`), so a terminal Ctrl+C reaches only the loop, and the site is still up to remove the fixture. `devMailOf` answers `mailbox` (a JSON list), `absent` (wrangler's `Value not found`, on stdout or stderr) or `unreadable` (anything else, a warning before the value included); the restore puts back a mailbox, deletes only for `absent`, and leaves an unreadable one untouched with a warning.

## Phase 7 fix wave (UI and tests)

- **2026-10-04 · The preview cannot take the payment step down (whole-phase review I-1).** `PreviewSlot` wraps the lazy Player in an error boundary (`PreviewBoundary`, a class: React has no hook for it), one per gesture. A lazy chunk that does not load (a deploy replaced its hash, a dropped connection) or an error the Player throws outside the composition (the composition's own go to its `errorFallback`) shows the poster in the same frame, with `sponsor.preview.unavailable` and no controls, and logs `[sponsorPreview] Failed to load the preview:`. The review step, Turnstile and Pay stay; the wizard state in memory is kept. The lazy component is made by `lazyPreview(load)`, so the DOM tests give it a rejecting import and a throwing module (a process-wide `mock.module` of `./sponsor-preview` would break every other test). The sponsor e2e refuses the module (`src/components/sponsor/sponsor-preview.tsx` in dev, `assets/sponsor-preview-*.js` built) and still reaches the checkout. `/sponsor` and `/sponsor/edit` also get `errorComponent: RouteError`, the site's localized error state, instead of TanStack's English default.
- **2026-10-04 · The kept logo has browser evidence (I-2).** The screenshot matrix answers `POST /api/sponsor/reedit-logo` with the e2e logo for `07-edit` (the shot showed "keep your current logo" next to a preview without one) and waits for the `blob:` logo in the frame. A real-flow test in `csp.spec.ts` stores `e2e/fixtures/logo.png` in R2 through the public upload's signed fallback, seeds a paid `changes_requested` sponsorship with that `logoKey`, a paid logo and a re-edit token, opens `/sponsor/edit?token=…` under the enforced CSP and checks the read (200, `image/png`, `private, no-store`, `same-origin` CORP, no token in the URL), the composition's `blob:` image at its natural 160 px, and no violation. The e2e seed's `sponsorshipCheckout` takes an optional `token` for that (one gesture only; the token row is `<id>-0`'s): `reedit.get`'s `hasLogo` needs the paid `payment_item`, which the plain `sponsorship` op does not write.
- **2026-10-04 · A logo that does not decode is dropped in the Player, and still fails the render (M-1).** In the Player (`!isRendering`) the overlay's logo `onError` warns `[sponsorOverlay] The logo could not be loaded; the preview shows none:` and stops drawing that URL; the name and the video stay, and another logo (a new object URL) shows again. Before, it failed the composition, so the preview fell to the image and then the bare poster with the note that the *video* cannot play, and stayed there after a valid logo. While rendering it still cancels the render (`logoUnreadable`). The wizard gives no separate note: the checkout's own byte check refuses a file that is not PNG, JPEG or WebP.
- **2026-10-04 · The Player loads only the font subsets the name needs (M-2).** `@remotion/fonts` `loadFont` fetches every file it registers, so the Player fetched all seven Inter subsets (about 98 kB) first, and one failed subset (the unused greek-ext) made the preview "unavailable"; `font.ts` claimed the browser only fetches a subset a text uses, which holds for CSS `@font-face`, not for `FontFace.load()`. `overlayFontSubsets(text)` answers latin and latin-ext always (the fixed intro is Latin, and `measureText` validates the font on it) plus each subset whose unicode range holds a character of the name; `loadOverlayFont(text)` / `isOverlayFontLoaded(text)` load and check those, each file once, and a failed subset is forgotten and retried alone while the others stay loaded. The Player's overlay and the wizard pass the display name; a name typed later that needs another subset loads it while the Player stays. The render passes no text and loads all seven (local files), so a mistake in the range table could only show in the Player.
- **2026-10-04 · The browser bundles one demuxer (M-3).** `@smog/render/metadata` (the render server) keeps every format; the new `@smog/render/metadata/mp4` exports `readMp4Metadata`, the same reader (`metadata/read.ts`) with `[MP4]` only, which the site uses. A `formats` option on the server entry would not have helped: a default of `ALL_FORMATS` is a static reference, and the bundler keeps every demuxer it names. Mux's static renditions are MP4. The lazy `sponsor-preview-*.js` went from 646,246 B (185,172 B gzip; Ogg, FLAC, WAVE, Matroska, MPEG-TS, HLS and MP3 inside) to 478,994 B (143,641 B gzip; the ISOBMFF demuxer only), with the poster-size reader and the boundary added. `test/bundle.test.ts` builds the entry with Vite and asserts that `isobmff` is the only `mediabunny` demuxer directory in it.
- **2026-10-04 · One rendition list, one reader, one MP4 check (M-4).**
  - `renditionUrls` lives in `@smog/video/renditions` (`src/renditions.ts`, no Mux credentials: client safe, unlike the `.` entry). The render's `firstReachable` fallback and the wizard's `useSourceMetadata` both import it; the site's copy is gone.
  - `readSourceMetadata` (and `readMp4Metadata`) take an optional `signal`: an aborted signal opens nothing, an abort disposes the input (its fetches stop) and the read rejects with the signal's reason. The site's own `mediabunny` `Input` is gone, and so is its direct `mediabunny` dependency.
  - The rendered-MP4 check (`checkRenderedVideo`, `readRenderedVideo`: H.264, the source's even size, frames ± 1) is `@smog/render/testing/video` (`src/testing/rendered-video.ts`, its own entry so `./testing`, which the features' Workers tests import, carries no `mediabunny`). `test:render` and the site's local render loop both use it; its unit tests moved with it.
- **2026-10-04 · 3:4 fixtures, and the image fallback takes the poster's shape (M-5).**
  - The gesture library is 810 × 1080 (the old wizard showed its previews in `aspectRatio: "810/1080"`). The e2e Player clip `apps/site/e2e/fixtures/preview-2s.mp4` is now 360 × 480 (VP9 in MP4, 3.9 kB): the old 9:16 clip's top 480 rows, which hold the frame counter and the moving square, so the overlay's area stays empty. `packages/render/test/fixtures/source-3x4-2s.mp4` (H.264, 19.5 kB, 60 frames) is `source-2s.mp4` cropped to 360 × 480 from row 80, so the counter stays above the overlay. Both were made with Remotion's ffmpeg (`@remotion/compositor-linux-x64-gnu`): `ffmpeg -i preview-2s.mp4 -vf crop=360:480:0:0 -c:v libvpx-vp9 -b:v 0 -crf 40 -an -movflags +faststart` and `ffmpeg -i source-2s.mp4 -vf crop=360:480:0:80 -c:v libx264 -crf 30 -pix_fmt yuv420p -an -movflags +faststart`. `test:render` renders it once more (60 frames at 360 × 480, line 1 green ≥ 100 px at ΔE < 10; about 150 measured, against about 550 at 9:16: 18 px text with thinner strokes), and the metadata tests read it with both readers.
  - **The composition sizes 3:4 correctly; no change.** Everything is relative to the composition's own width and height, which come from the source: the logo box `0.22 × w` by `0.22 × h` with `contain` (a square logo draws 79 × 79 at both 360 × 480 and 360 × 640), the text `0.038 × h` (18.2 px at 480, so 0.0507 of the width instead of 0.0676 at 9:16) with line 1's top at `0.87 × h`. That is the old composition's behaviour too (it rendered at the source size), and fit-to-width simply starts at longer names on 3:4. The Player's frame is `aspect-3/4`, so a library gesture fills it with no letterbox bars.
  - The image fallback no longer assumes 3:4. It fetches the poster at width 720 as before, reads its natural size first (`poster-size.ts`, an `Image()`; `img-src` already allows `image.mux.com`, no CORS needed), rounds it down to even and makes that the composition's size, so a non-3:4 source keeps the render's relative positions instead of a cover-cropped 720 × 960 frame. A poster that does not load is the poster stage with the fallback note. While the size is read after a video failure the controls stay enabled, so a focused button keeps the focus. This supersedes the "720 × 960" in the task 8 `SponsorPreview` entry.
- **2026-10-04 · The picker shows whole names (M-6).** A toggle's name wraps onto two lines (`line-clamp-2`, `wrap-anywhere`) instead of `truncate`, so "Goedemorgen" is read whole, and the toggle's `title` holds the full name past the clamp.
- **2026-10-04 · Programmatic focus is visible (M-7, WCAG 2.4.7).** The preview's note drops `outline-none` for a `focus:` outline ring. The admin's retry announcement is rendered only on `render_failed` (visually hidden, so it exists before it speaks) and after a retry; once the refetched detail has moved on, it shows in the button's place with the focus ring, and that is where the focus goes. Detail pages without a failed render have no empty live region.
- **2026-10-04 · Tests that wait for what they assert (M-8).** The refused kept-logo test waits until the refusal was handled, not only sent: the stub's 404 body resolves `discarded` when it is cancelled unread and `consumed` when it is read, and the test requires `discarded`. The Workflow test's poll for the stored upload has a 20 s deadline and fails with `[test] the render step stored no upload`. The render server's drain test races the drain against a queued timer instead of sleeping 5 ms.
- **2026-10-04 · Stale comments (M-9).** `contract.ts` no longer mentions the deleted static preview: the wizard's steps read its constants and its preview mounts the composition. `font.ts` describes what `loadFont` really fetches (M-2).

## Foundations: dependencies, migration 0012 and the welcome claim, the `@smog/migrate-convex` skeleton (phase 8 task 1)

- **2026-10-04 · [dep] Biome stays on 2.5.14 (deviation from 2.5.15).** 2.5.15's `suspicious/noUnnecessaryConditions` reports 24 errors on unchanged code. All 24 were checked, and every one is a type-inference false positive:
  - TanStack Query's `isPlaceholderData` (the audit, gesture-list, sponsorship and user tables, `use-gesture-search.ts`) and `isPending` (the mobile home tab, email preview, maintenance card, Mux picker, video field) are reported "always falsy";
  - optional or infinite query `data` makes `?.`/`??` "unnecessary" (the wizard, the gestures route, three in `hooks.test.tsx`, the render-workflow test);
  - Expo Router's `useLocalSearchParams<{ q?: string }>` fields are treated as required (the search screen), and a ref's `.current` as never null;
  - `?.`/`??` after `JSON.parse(…) as PersistedClient` (`query-persist.ts`);
  - the request state `(await emailFailure.get()).failed`, a `boolean` the send path sets (`auth/src/server.ts`).

  2.5.14 reports none on the same tree. Passing would take wrong edits or 24 suppressions. **The bump waits for a Biome release on which `biome lint --only=suspicious/noUnnecessaryConditions .` is clean on these 24 sites**; the next dependency pass checks it.

The welcome hooks' pinned tests (`packages/auth/test/welcome.test.ts`, every path that verifies an existing account) pass unchanged on 1.7.7, so 1.7.6 is not kept (open risk 11 closed). Bun stays on 1.3.11.

The whole gate passes on them. `bun install` kept the hoisted `@tanstack/query-core` at 5.104.0 (its only hoisted dependant, `@orpc/tanstack-query`, takes it as a `>=5.80.2` peer) and nested 5.104.1 under `@tanstack/react-query`, which gave the oRPC query options a second `QueryFunction` type and failed `@smog/gestures`' types. So `@tanstack/query-core` is in the catalog at 5.104.1, next to `@tanstack/react-query`, and `@smog/rpc` (which owns the oRPC TanStack Query utils) depends on it as `catalog:`, satisfying that peer. `bun install` then resolves one copy. **The next react-query bump moves both catalog lines together.** Alternative: a root `overrides` entry (rejected: it is not next to react-query and is easy to forget). A stale `node_modules/.cache/tsc` build info hides or repeats such errors locally: delete it after a dependency change. Still behind on purpose, each with its earlier entry: React 19.2.3 and the Expo SDK 57 pins (React Native 0.86.3, Reanimated, Gesture Handler, Screens, Safe Area, SVG, WebView, Worklets, Async Storage, Jest 29).
- **2026-10-04 · [dep] `fflate` 0.8.3 in the catalog**, the importer's ZIP reader (Bun has no ZIP API). `@smog/migrate-convex` declares it now; task 5 imports it.
- **2026-10-04 · Migration 0012 `user_welcomed_at` (ruling 16, carry 6).** `ALTER TABLE user ADD welcomed_at integer` (generated) and, by hand below it, `UPDATE user SET welcomed_at = updated_at WHERE email_verified = 1`. No table rebuild, so 0007's `user_keep_one_admin` survives; the backfill writes only `welcomed_at`, so the `BEFORE UPDATE OF role` trigger never fires and it runs on a database with one admin. `test/migration-0012.test.ts` applies 0000–0011 and the dev seed, adds a verified and an unverified member, applies 0012, and checks: verified rows get their `updated_at`, unverified ones stay NULL, no `role` or `updated_at` changed, the trigger is still there and still aborts the last admin's demotion. Rehearsed on the local dev D1 the same way (0011, the seed, two members, then `migrate:dev`). The seed stays as it is (it runs at every migration level, including before 0012), so a fresh dev D1's admin has `welcomed_at` NULL; a verified account is never welcomed again either way, since only a verification or a verified sign-up sends it.
- **2026-10-04 · The welcome claim (ruling 16).** `welcome()` first runs `UPDATE user SET welcomed_at = now WHERE id = ? AND welcomed_at IS NULL RETURNING id` through Drizzle and enqueues only when a row comes back, so two concurrent verifications send one welcome (D1 runs one write at a time) and an account marked by the backfill or the Convex import is never welcomed. The claim sets `updated_at` to itself, so Drizzle's `$onUpdate` does not bump it: it is not a profile change (tested on a verified social sign-up, where the claim is the only update). A test also checks that neither the sign-in answer nor `get-session` carries the column. A claim that throws logs `[auth] Failed to claim the welcome email:` and skips the enqueue (a missed welcome is harmless; a duplicate is what the claim prevents). A claimed welcome whose enqueue then fails is logged as before and not retried. The outbox key `welcome:<userId>` stays as the consumer's second filter. The sign-in-only server neither claims nor sends. The column is server-only: not in `USER_ADDITIONAL_FIELDS`, and a test checks the sign-in answer's user has no `welcomedAt`. The phase 2 "CLI parity" was a one-time comparison of the hand-written tables with Better Auth's CLI output (phase 2 task 2), not a standing check, so it needs nothing for a column Better Auth does not know.

  - `test/core-rules.test.ts` scans every non-test file of `src/core`, comments stripped by a string-aware pass (a `/*` inside a string such as a glob opens no comment): no `Bun.*` and no Node global (`process.`, `Buffer`, `__dirname`, `__filename`) outside string literals; no `import(`/`require(` of a template literal (unverifiable); no `node:*`, `bun:*`, `bun` or `cloudflare:*` module; imports only `@smog/config`, `@smog/utils`, `@smog/db`, a feature's `./schema`, `zod`, `drizzle-orm` (each with subpaths) and relative paths that stay in `src/core`. Type-only imports count. The rule itself is tested on a source that breaks each part (`@smog/auth`, `@smog/rpc`, `@smog/video`, `@smog/jobs`, `@smog/email`, a feature's `./server`, `./client` and `./contract`, `fflate`, a dynamic import, a path into `src/cli`).

## The deploy config check and production's origin (phase 8 task 2)

- **2026-10-04 · The deploy config check (ruling 1; carries 3, 8, 19, 20).** `bun scripts/check-deploy-config.ts --env <staging|production> [--offline] [--warn-only]` holds `apps/site/wrangler.jsonc` `env.<env>` and the env's `wrangler secret list` to what a deploy needs.
  - **The file:** the render mode (unset is `fake`; `local` or an unknown mode is refused outside dev) gives `requiredWorkerConfig(env, mode)`, so staging on `container` needs the Mux trio. Every required var must be non-blank. `SITE_URL` must equal its own `https:` origin (no path, query or trailing slash), must not be a workers.dev host with other than four labels (`isPlaceholderOrigin`, shared with `ensure-cloudflare-resources.ts`), and must not be a reserved host (`localhost`, `.test`, `.invalid`, `.example`, `example.com/net/org`). The KV `id` of 32 zeros and the D1 `database_id` `00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000000` are refused **exactly**; any other id passes.
  - **The secrets (online):** every required secret, with its `wrangler secret put … --env <env>` command. `PROVIDER_SECRET_GROUPS` (`@smog/config/env/worker`): Google is `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` + `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`, Apple is `APPLE_CLIENT_ID` + `APPLE_CLIENT_SECRET` + `APPLE_APP_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER`; part of a group is an error, none of it is a line in the recommended list. `RECOMMENDED_WORKER_CONFIG` is the two providers and the OpenPanel pair; half the OpenPanel pair is a warning, not an error (the relay only drops events). The R2 tokens come together and need the `R2_ACCOUNT_ID` and `MEDIA_BUCKET` vars (`workerEnvSchema`'s rule, which otherwise fails the Worker on its first request). Production's required list is unchanged. Value rules (a `live_` Mollie key) stay in `workerEnvSchema`: `secret list` gives names only.
  - **Wrangler 4.147.0's output, pinned:** `secret list` defaults to `--format json` and prints `JSON.stringify(secrets, null, "  ")`, an array of `{ name, type: "secret_text" }`; `--format pretty` prints the banner and `Secret Name: …` lines. The script passes `--format json` explicitly. `parseSecretList` parses from the first line that starts with `[` to the last line that ends with `]`, so a banner, an update or proxy notice before it and a log notice after it are skipped; anything else (no array, an entry without a string `name`) throws. A Worker that does not exist answers `Worker "<name>" (env: <env>) not found.` (codes 10007 / legacy env), which `explainSecretListFailure` turns into "set its secrets first with `wrangler secret put <NAME> --env <env>`, which creates the Worker". `Authentication error [code: 10000]`, a 403 or "permission" names Account › Workers Scripts: Read (Edit includes it); the non-interactive no-token message names `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`; anything else keeps the exit code and wrangler's last line.
  - **Warn-only:** `--warn-only` is refused with `--env production` (exit 1, before anything runs). Otherwise every problem, and every failure on the way (a runner that throws, a non-zero exit or a signal, output that is not the array, an unreadable or unparsable `wrangler.jsonc`, a bad argument, a logger that throws), prints as `::warning::[deploy-config] …` and exits 0. A failed `secret list` still lets the file checks run, so one run reports everything.
  - **`deploy.yml`:** the `Check deploy config` step comes right after `bun install`, before `Ensure Cloudflare resources`, the migrations and the build, so with `SMOG_PROVISION_PRODUCTION=1` nothing is created before a missing secret is found. Staging runs `--env staging --warn-only || echo "::warning::…"` unless the staging environment variable `SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS` is `1`; the `||` keeps staging green even if the script itself crashes (a syntax error, a missing module). Production and an opted-in staging run `--env "$CLOUDFLARE_ENV"` and fail the deploy. `check-deploy-config.test.ts` runs that exact step from `deploy.yml` with `bash -eo pipefail` and a fake `bunx` first on `PATH` (an auth failure, a missing Worker, output that is not JSON, a wrangler killed by a signal): staging stays green with a warning, opted-in staging and production fail, and production is never refused for `--warn-only` (so it never got it).
  - **`release-config-check`:** `checkDeployConfigStep` asserts the step exists, runs before the resources step and the migrations, is skipped with the others when the Cloudflare secrets are absent, and passes `SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS: ${{ vars.SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS }}`; every `--warn-only` line carries a literal `--env staging`, sits under an `if` that tests staging and `"$SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS" != "1"`, and ends with `||`; and an enforcing `--env "$CLOUDFLARE_ENV"` line exists. `release:check:core` runs `bun scripts/check-deploy-config.ts --env staging --offline` after `deploy:dry`. Production is not checked offline in CI: its empty `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` / `R2_ACCOUNT_ID` and its KV/D1 placeholders are expected until launch.
  - Rationale: one script owns the before-deploy facts the Worker cannot check itself, at the earliest point. Alternatives: the deploy guard (it runs after the build, has no token and comes after the migrations); a Worker self-check (too late).
- **2026-10-04 · The deploy config check, review fixes.**
  - **A hang is bounded (I-1).** `spawnWithDeadline` runs `bunx wrangler secret list` and kills it after `SECRET_LIST_TIMEOUT_MS` (120 s). It answers exit 124 at once, without waiting for pipes a leftover child may hold. `listSecrets` also races any runner against the deadline (plus 1 s), so even an in-process runner that never settles becomes a problem, and under `--warn-only` a warning. The step has `timeout-minutes: 5` and `continue-on-error: ${{ env.CLOUDFLARE_ENV == 'staging' && vars.SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS != '1' }}`. `release-config-check` holds the bound (at most 5) and that exact expression. Tests: a runner that never settles (warn-only exits 0, enforcing exits 1), and `spawnWithDeadline` killing `sleep 30` after 200 ms.
  - **The env must match (m-3).** The script refuses, warn-only or not, when `CLOUDFLARE_ENV` is set and differs from `--env`, so a production job can never run the staging warn-only check. `release-config-check` requires `[ "$CLOUDFLARE_ENV" = "staging" ] && [ "$SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS" != "1" ]` with `&&` (a `||` is refused).
  - **The missing account id (m-1)** gets its own explanation naming `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, separate from the missing token.
  - **The `||` fallback (m-4)** is tested with a fake `bun` that crashes or is killed: staging stays green with `::warning::[deploy-config] the check itself failed`, and production fails.
  - **`SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS` scope.** `vars` resolves the environment variable first, then the repository variable. A repository-level `SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS=1` would therefore make staging enforce as well, so it belongs on the `staging` environment. Production ignores it.
- **2026-10-04 · Production's origin is `https://smog-site-production.zias.workers.dev` (ruling 2; carry 26, production half).** The old placeholder `smog-site-production.workers.dev` could never be a workers.dev host (`<script>.<subdomain>.workers.dev`). The new value is the account subdomain staging's host shows, and it is the default launch origin; moving to `app.smog.vlaanderen` is the owner's decision (the cutover runbook, task 11). `ensure-cloudflare-resources.ts`'s placeholder rule is now the four-label rule (`isPlaceholderOrigin`) for every env on `--create`, not a comparison with one production string, so neither domain path is assumed; a test keeps staging's host passing `--create`. `PRODUCTION_SITE_URL_PLACEHOLDER` is gone. The auth tests use the `zias` hosts. Staging's built `wrangler.json` (`deploy:dry`) is byte-identical to develop's.
  - **Owner action (review m-2), until task 11 moves it to PROGRESS:** confirm that production deploys to the **same Cloudflare account** as staging. `zias` is that account's workers.dev subdomain, so another account gives another host, and then `SITE_URL` changes. Before launch, set the production Turnstile widget's hostnames to include `smog-site-production.zias.workers.dev`, and the domain too on path (D).

## Site: the CSP report endpoint, the first production admin, and the analytics body reader (phase 8 task 3)

- **2026-10-04 · `POST /api/csp-report` (ruling 11, carry 17).** `routes/api/csp-report.ts` wires `server/csp-report.ts` (`handleCspReport`, `parseCspReports`, `redactReportUrl`; pure, `bun test`ed, plus a Workers-pool test through the Worker). A public endpoint, so it is built to give nothing away and keep nothing it does not need:
  - **Order:** the content type first (only `application/csp-report` and `application/reports+json`, parameters ignored; anything else an empty 415, before the rate limit is spent), then `RL_ANALYTICS` on `csp:<ip>` (no new binding), then the body through `readCappedBody` at 16 KiB (an empty 413; a broken body or `content-length` an empty 400). Every other answer is an empty `204` with `cache-control: no-store`: unparsable JSON and bodies without CSP entries included, so a caller learns nothing from the answer and nothing is echoed. Over the limit it answers 204 without reading the body or logging; a limiter that throws logs `[csp] Failed to check the rate limit:` and answers 204.
  - **The log:** one `console.warn("[csp] violation", { directive, blocked, document, source, line, disposition, count })` per **body**: its first violation and how many it held (review M-2: at 5 lines per body, a per-IP limit of 120/min was 600 lines/min, and Workers Logs bills per line). So the log volume is at most `RL_ANALYTICS` per IP. Only `csp-violation` entries of a Reporting API body are read. `directive` is `effective-directive` (or the first word of CSP 2's `violated-directive`), kept only when it looks like a directive name; `disposition` only `enforce`/`report`; `line` only a non-negative integer. The sample, the policy, the referrer, the status code and the user agent are never read into the line.
  - **The redaction (`redactReportUrl`):** origin plus path, never userinfo, query or fragment (`/sponsor/edit?token=abc#x` → `/sponsor/edit`), capped at 256 characters. Paths carry tokens too, so the segment after `/lists/` (share tokens, including migrated ones of any shape), `/api/auth/reset-password/` and `/api/logos/upload/` becomes `:token`. Repeated slashes are collapsed and these prefixes compared case-insensitively first, since the router serves `/Lists/<token>` and `//lists/<token>` too (review M-1). Any other segment of 32+ hex characters (a migrated share token is 32 lower-case hex, which looks like a slug) or of 20+ base64url characters that is not slug-shaped (a minted `newToken`) is masked anywhere. A CSP keyword (`inline`, `eval` …) is kept as is; another scheme keeps only the scheme (`data:`, `chrome-extension:`), `blob:` its origin. Anything else is `null`.
  - **No Origin check, no CORS, no OpenPanel relay**, as the ruling says. The path is an exact entry of the maintenance gate's `EXEMPT_PATHS` (the 503 page and `/sign-in` carry the CSP, so their reports must land; `/api/csp-report-x` is still gated, tested).
  - **The headers (`worker/headers.ts`):** `buildCsp` ends with `report-uri /api/csp-report; report-to csp` in every env (enforced in dev and production, `Report-Only` in staging, unchanged). Documents (HTML and redirects) get `Reporting-Endpoints: csp="<origin>/api/csp-report"` with **the request's own origin** (`respondSecurely`'s `requestUrl`, `request.url`), never `SITE_URL` (review I-1): a placeholder `SITE_URL` would have sent browsers' unredacted reports, query tokens included, to a host that is not ours, and any host mismatch (a custom domain, a preview URL) would make the endpoint cross-origin, where the route grants no CORS. An absolute URL rather than a relative one, so no browser's handling of a relative endpoint needs checking. A route's own `Reporting-Endpoints` wins, like every other header here. JSON answers get neither.
  - **The e2e** (`e2e/csp.spec.ts`) checks the directives and the header on `/`, and a real violation (an `img-src`-blocked image) raising `securitypolicyviolation` with `disposition: enforce`. Delivery is not intercepted: Chromium sends reports from its network service, batched.
- **2026-10-04 · `admin:grant --create` (ruling 18).** `bun run admin:grant --env <env> --create [--dry-run] <email>` runs two statements in one `wrangler d1 execute --command` (wrangler runs them in order, checked on the local D1): `INSERT INTO user (…) VALUES (<new UUID>, '', <email>, 1, …, 'admin', 0, NULL, now) ON CONFLICT (email) DO NOTHING RETURNING id AS created_id`, then the existing grant (`UPDATE … SET role = 'admin'`, unban). So a missing account is created verified, admin, with no credential, name `""` and `welcomed_at` set; an existing one gets exactly the old grant (its verification, name and `welcomed_at` untouched). Neither statement writes any role but `admin`, so nothing is demoted and 0007's trigger never aborts it; a re-run inserts nothing. `--dry-run` prints the command and the notes. Every argument is single-quoted for a POSIX shell (`'` as `'\''`), so pasting it expands no `$(…)`, backtick or `$var` from the email (review M-3; tested by running the printed line through `sh`). An unknown `--option` is now an error rather than read as an email.
  - **`--create` on staging or production needs `--yes`** (review I-2), as `bun run maintenance` does for production: a typo would make a verified admin of an address someone else may control, who could then sign in by code, even during maintenance. The refusal and the dry-run notes print the normalized address. `--dry-run` needs no `--yes`; dev needs none either.
  - **The SQL lives in `@smog/config/admin-grant` (deviation from the brief's file list).** The site's Workers-pool proof must run the script's own statements, and the site's composite `tsconfig` cannot include a file from the root `scripts/` (TS6059/TS6307). `@smog/config` is the one package both the root scripts and the site may import (as `@smog/config/maintenance` already is for `bun run maintenance`). It is pure strings: `buildGrantStatements(email, { create, id })` and `buildGrantSql` (them joined).
  - **Proof.** `scripts/admin-grant.test.ts` runs the statements on an in-memory SQLite with every migration (the insert, the existing-account fallback, the never-demotes case with another admin, the re-run, the dry run through the real script with no `PATH`, so it cannot spawn wrangler). `apps/site/test/maintenance-admin-signin.test.ts` (Workers pool) creates the admin with those statements, turns maintenance on, and drives the whole path: `send-verification-otp` (`sign-in`) through the sign-in-only server, the code from the dev mailbox, `sign-in/email-otp`, `/admin` still 503, `POST /api/maintenance/bypass`, then `/account` and `/admin` 200; no welcome email. `e2e/admin-settings.spec.ts` does the same against the dev server with the real script and wrangler on the local D1. **`AUTH_SIGN_IN_ROUTES` needed no change**: `email-otp/send-verification-otp` and `sign-in/email-otp` were enough for a credential-less admin.
- **2026-10-04 · The analytics relay reads its body with `readCappedBody` (carry 25, analytics part).** Its own reader is gone; the 4 KiB cap, the 413 before any read for a declared length, the counted and cancelled stream and the 400 for a client that goes away are `@smog/utils`' now. `@smog/analytics` declares `@smog/utils` (already allowed by the boundaries), which adds one line to `bun.lock`'s workspace entry for the package, a deviation from "only task 1 changes `bun.lock`" with no new package. One edge differs: a `content-length` of 16 digits or more was a 413 and is now a 400 (`readCappedBody` refuses it as malformed; a relay test pins it); no client sends one.

## Mux per env, static renditions, and the rejected video's retention (phase 8 task 4)

- **2026-10-04 · Render passthroughs carry the env (ruling 4, carries 2 and 22).** `renderJobPassthrough(env, jobId)` writes `render-job:<env>:<jobId>`; `renderJobIdOf(passthrough, env)` returns the id only for that env, and `null` for another env's and for the untagged phase 7 `render-job:<id>`. The Workflow's `render` step passes `RenderJobDeps.environment` (already there for `test` uploads) to `createRenderUpload`, and the Mux route passes `vars.ENVIRONMENT` to `handleMuxWebhook` (`environment`, required). `toRenderMuxEvent(event, env)` reads only this env's; an event that is still a render job's (`isRenderJobEvent`: any `render-job:` passthrough on the asset or the upload's settings) is answered `200 IGNORED` and logged `a render job of another env, ignored`: no hook, no `isCurrentUpload`, no delete, no KV marker. The format changes freely now because no real render upload exists yet (staging is `fake`, production never deployed). `render:local-loop` finds its asset with `renderJobIdOf(…, "dev")`.
- **2026-10-04 · An unknown job of this env is not current (ruling 12, review M-3).** `isCurrentRenderUpload` answers `false` when no `render_job` row matches, so its `asset.ready`/`asset.errored` deletes the asset (`SUPERSEDED`). This supersedes "an unknown job counts as current" in the phase 7 entry "The webhook's render hooks": that rule protected another env's assets while staging and production shared Mux events, and the env tag now keeps those out before the question is asked. Production keeps the existing (old) Mux environment; staging gets a new one; dev keeps the fake (owner actions, Task 11's runbook).
- **2026-10-04 · The Mux webhook reads its body with `readCappedBody` (carry 25).** The handler's own copy is gone; `@smog/video` now depends on `@smog/utils` (already allowed by `BOUNDARIES`). Same 1 MiB cap and statuses, with `readCappedBody`'s stricter `content-length` (at most 15 digits) and a cancel that cannot throw. This adds one workspace line to `bun.lock` (`@smog/video`'s dependencies), the only lockfile change, and no package version.
- **2026-10-04 · Static renditions (ruling 5).** `enableStaticRendition(mux, assetId, "highest")` is `POST /video/v1/assets/{id}/static-renditions` with `{ resolution: "highest" }` and returns Mux's 201 file (`highest.mp4`, `preparing`), or `null` for a 404. `getAsset` now carries `mp4Support` and `staticRenditions` (`muxAssetDataSchema` reads `mp4_support` and `static_renditions.{status, files[]}` as strings, so a new value never makes an asset unreadable). `staticRenditionState(asset)`:
  - the `highest` file (by `resolution`, or the name `highest.mp4`): `ready`; `errored` for a per-file `errored` **or `skipped`**; `preparing` for `preparing` and for any status Mux may add later, so nothing is requested, and billed, twice;
  - else a `high.mp4` (only the deprecated `mp4_support` makes that name): `legacy-mp4` when it is ready (its own status, else the object's, else ready: the legacy API listed only finished files), `preparing` while the object is preparing, `absent` when it errored;
  - else `absent` (no renditions, other resolutions only, `capped-1080p.mp4`, or `mp4_support` with no `high.mp4`: open risk 6, such an asset gets a new rendition).
  `MuxApiError` gains `retryAfterSeconds` (Mux's `Retry-After`, delta-seconds or an IMF date, else `null`) for the throttled `mux renditions` (task 9). The fake implements the POST (a second rendition of one resolution is a 400, as Mux refuses it), keeps `mp4Support` / `staticRenditions` on its assets, `readyStaticRendition(id)`, and `failNext(status, headers)`; the fake server readies a requested rendition after `readyAfterMs`. No real Mux call is made anywhere.
- **2026-10-04 · The rejected video's retention (ruling 13, carry 4).** `REJECTED_VIDEO_RETENTION_DAYS = 30` and `REJECTED_VIDEO_PURGE_PER_RUN = 20` (`@smog/sponsorships/schema`); the 30 days are open decision 5 in `docs/LEGAL-SIGNOFF.md`.
  - **When it was rejected** (`server/rejected-video.ts`): the latest `rejected` event's `created_at`; else, for a migrated row, the latest `legacy` event's `data.legacy.reviewedAt`, read as epoch ms (Convex's number, or a string of digits, which SQLite would otherwise read as a Julian day) or an ISO 8601 string (`unixepoch(…, 'subsec')`), whichever the importer writes; else unknown, and **no bound applies** (a bare migrated row can always be sent back for changes).
  - **`requestChanges` from `rejected`** is refused with `INVALID_STATE rejectedTooLongAgo` once the rejection is 30 days old or more (at exactly 30 days too, so the request and the purge share one cut-off). The read refuses with `SponsorshipActionError`, and the batch starts with `rejectedBoundGuard`, a `failWhen` on the same SQL, which `@smog/api`'s `sponsorshipRefusal` maps through `isRejectedTooLongAgo`. A request from `in_review` is never bounded, whatever older rejection it has. The reason's copy is `sponsorship.errors.rejectedTooLongAgo` in nl/en/fr (it names the 30 days; a unit test keeps the copy and the constant together), shown by the request-changes dialog's error toast like every other reason.
  - **The purge** is a fourth part of `runRetentionPurge`, which now takes `mux` (`createMux(worker)` in the cron; `null` without credentials, and then nothing is deleted and a warning is logged). It selects `rejected` sponsorships with a `video_asset_id` whose **`rejected` event** is at or before the cut-off, **oldest rejection first**, at most 20 deleted per run: **a migrated row with only a `legacy` event is never purged** (controller ruling; the first night after the import deletes nothing, and the old assets are an owner decision in the runbook). Per row: `deleteAsset` (404 is done), unless a gesture or a running sponsorship still uses the asset, then one guarded batch (`failWhen` the row is no longer `rejected` with that asset) clears `video_asset_id` and `video_playback_id`, keeping `updated_at` (the logo release counts its 30 days from it, and the sponsorship did not change). A Mux or batch failure leaves the row for the next night, and the run **looks past it** (review M1): a failed row is skipped for the rest of the run and the next page is read, up to 20 deleted and 40 rows tried, so up to 20 rows that fail every night cannot stall the rest. A Mux delete that succeeded before the clear failed is finished by the next run (404, then the clear; tested). It logs `[retention] rejected videos {"deleted":n,"failed":n,"remaining":n}` every run (`remaining` is a count of what is still past the bound after the run), and the cron result carries `rejectedVideosDeleted`, `rejectedVideosFailed` and `rejectedVideosRemaining`. A dry run only counts. The seek is `sponsorship_status_ends_at_idx` (`status = 'rejected'`) with the event read per row on `sponsorship_event_sponsorship_created_idx`, no table scan (the only scan is the bound skip list's `json_each`); the one temporary B-tree sorts the eligible rows for the oldest-first order, which stay few (query-plan test); no migration is needed. The purge's clear is the second writer of sponsorship columns outside `transition.ts` in the `no-direct-status` allow-list (`sweeps.ts`, no status).
  - `bun run retention --dry-run` (`scripts/retention.ts`) does not count rejected videos yet: the file is outside this task's assignment; Task 11 may add the count (its SQL is `rejectedVideoQuery`'s condition).
- **2026-10-04 · `render:local-loop` was not re-run in task 4.** It needs Chrome for the render server; the change it would exercise (the `render-job:dev:` tag through the Workflow's real `render` step and the webhook route into the waiting instance) is covered by the Workers-pool tests: `render-workflow.test.ts` (sponsorships, the upload's passthrough per env), `apps/site/test/render-workflow.test.ts` (the real `render` step against the Mux fake) and `apps/site/test/mux-webhook-render.test.ts` (a `render-job:dev:` event through the real route reaching the instance, which commits).

## Importer foundations: reader, export schemas, ids, pseudonymiser, emitter, report, wrangler runner (phase 8 task 5)

- **2026-10-04 · The export schemas (`src/core/export-schema.ts`).** One Zod object per table of the old `packages/convex/convex/schema.ts`, field by field, each with `_id` and `_creationTime`. `sponsorships.status` is a plain string in the old schema; here it is the enum of the eight values the old mutations write (`pending` … `cancelled`), so an unknown status is a malformed row rather than a silent miss in task 8's status table. `adminLogs.metadata` is `unknown`. `validateExport(files)` takes each table's `documents.jsonl` text:
  - a line that is not JSON, a row that fails its schema, or a repeated `_id` is a **blocker** naming the table and the `_id` (or the line when there is no `_id`), with Zod's path and reason and never a value;
  - an unknown field is a **warning** per table and field (its row count and the first 50 ids), and the field is dropped;
  - an unknown table is listed (`report.export.unknownTables`) and ignored, with one warning;
  - an absent table reads as empty with a warning, and an export with **none** of the nine is a blocker (a ZIP wrapped in an extra folder would otherwise read as nine empty tables);
  - rows are sorted by `_creationTime`, then `_id`, so the export's line order never changes a plan.

  Each table's issues go to its domain's section (`TABLE_DOMAINS`: `users`; `catalog` for categories and gestures; `learning` for favorites, lists and items; `account` for consents and admin logs; `sponsorships`). The reader's own findings go to an `export` section, the report's first, beside ruling 6's six domains (a small addition: they belong to no domain).
- **2026-10-04 · `legacyUuid(table, key)` (ruling 7).** The first 16 bytes of the Web Crypto SHA-256 of `smog-convex:<table>:<key>`, with the version nibble set to 8 and the variant bits to `10` (RFC 9562 §5.8). `table` is the D1 table the row goes to. A test checks it against `node:crypto` and that `z.uuid()` accepts it. The bits are set with arithmetic (`% 0x10 + 0x80`), as Biome refuses bitwise operators.
- **2026-10-04 · The pseudonymiser (B2).** `pseudonymiser(target)` is the one place a transform asks what a personal or secret field becomes: `email(id, real)` (`<legacyUuid>@staging.invalid`), `name(kind, n, real)` ("Gebruiker <n>" / "Sponsor <n>"), `vat` (`""`), `assetId` (NULL), `tokens(rows)` (none) and also `company` (NULL on staging: a company name can be a person's). Production returns every value as given. A staging plan of the fixture is checked to hold no fixture address, name, VAT number, token, IP address, user agent or WorkOS id (`FIXTURE_SECRETS`); tasks 7 and 8 extend that list with their fixture rows.
- **2026-10-04 · B1's rule, made precise (`resolveGesturePlayback`).** Over the gesture's own sponsorship rows, newest first (`_creationTime`, `_id`):
  1. an `active` row (it maps to `live` or `expiring`) whose `sponsoredVideoPlaybackId` is the gesture's `playbackId` → its `originalVideoPlaybackId`, no warning;
  2. else any row whose `sponsoredVideoPlaybackId` is the gesture's `playbackId` (a missed restore) → its `originalVideoPlaybackId`, warning `missedRestore`;
  3. else an `active` row whose `originalVideoPlaybackId` differs from the gesture's `playbackId` (changed during the sponsorship) → kept, warning `changedDuringSponsorship`;
  4. else kept, no warning.

  Case 3 looks at `active` rows only: after an expiry the old code restored the original, so a different video on an expired row's gesture is a later admin change, not something to warn about. The result names the sponsorships it restored from (`restoredFrom`; chains: see the review fixes below). `resolveGesturePlaybacks` resolves a whole export; task 7's catalogue and task 9's `mux renditions` call these, and nothing else decides it.
- **2026-10-04 · The input files (`src/core/inputs.ts`).** The WorkOS export is JSON (an array, or a list response `{ "data": [...] }`) or CSV with `id,email,first_name,last_name` in any order (other columns ignored; RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, a BOM), told apart by its first character; empty strings become NULL and a repeated id is refused. Email normalisation (trim, lower case) is task 7's. `mux-map.json` is `{ playbackId: { assetId, renditions? } }`, `renditions` one of ruling 5's states; extra fields are kept for task 9. `overlay-overrides.json` values go through the wizard's own `displayNameSchema` (trimmed, 1..35, one line). A bad file throws `InputError` naming the file and the key or row, never the value, and `plan` exits 2.
- **2026-10-04 · The SQL emitter (`src/core/emit.ts`, ruling 7).**
  - Every statement is one line with its values inlined, ending in `;`. Strings are quoted by doubling `'`; a line break inside a value becomes `'…' || char(10) || '…'` (`char(13, 10)` for CRLF), so a gesture description with paragraphs stays on one line; a NUL byte, NaN and Infinity are refused.
  - `insertRow(table, row, conflictTargets)` is the typed insert: keys are the Drizzle table's TS names (an unknown key throws), values go through each column's own `mapToDriverValue` (a `Date` for `timestamp_ms`, booleans as 0/1), columns come in table order, and it is rendered by `SQLiteSyncDialect` with the parameters inlined (a `?` inside quotes is left alone). At least one conflict target is required and each must be the table's own column; several give several `ON CONFLICT (…) DO NOTHING` clauses (`user`: `legacy_id` and `email`), which D1 accepts (the Workers-pool test). Drizzle's `$defaultFn`s do not run, so a transform that leaves out `created_at` fails loudly at apply rather than getting the plan time. `legacyIdRef(table, legacyId)` is the `(SELECT "id" FROM … WHERE "legacy_id" = …)` subquery children use for a parent whose id they cannot know (a claimed user).
  - Files are `<group>-<nnn>.sql` per ruling 14's groups (`10-users` … `50-sponsorships`, `90-fts`), at most 500 statements and 512 KiB each; an empty group still gets one empty file, so every manifest lists all six groups. `90-fts` is `rebuildGesturesFtsSql` for the transforms' `ftsGestureIds`, 200 per statement pair, rendered as the dev seed does.
- **2026-10-04 · `reset-imported` is generated at plan time, from the transforms' keys (ruling 14; deviation in the file name).** Each transform returns `ResetKeys`: row keys per table (a string, or a tuple in `RESET_KEY_COLUMNS` order for `payment_item`, `favorite`, `list_item`, `gesture_category` and `gesture_keyword`) and the migrated users as `{ id, legacyId }`. The statements delete by `json_each` lists (250 keys each) in RESTRICT order: `payment_item`; `sponsorship_token`, `sponsorship_event`, `payment`, `sponsorship`; `invoice_request`, `sponsor`; `list_share`, `list_item`, `list`, `favorite`, `consent_event`, `audit_log`; `gesture_keyword`, `gesture_category`, `gesture` (with the deleted gestures' `gesture_fts` rows), `category`; then users. A user row goes only when its `(id, legacy_id)` pair matches, so a claimed account (its own id) is never deleted, and an admin row only while another admin with no ban in force remains outside the statement's set (a `DELETE` does not fire 0007's trigger); then every migrated `legacy_id` is set to NULL, which frees the claimed accounts. The keys can exceed one file (a large import's favorites alone), so the reset is chunked like the other files, as `reset-imported-<nnn>.sql`, listed in the manifest's `reset` with their hashes; ruling 14's "`reset-imported.sql`" is these files. Tested as text and, on a D1 with every migration, for the guard and the claimed account.
- **2026-10-04 · The manifest and the export hash.** `manifest.json` holds `version`, the target, `--now`, the input hashes (`export`, `workosUsers`, `muxMap`, `overrides`; NULL when not given), the report's blocker and warning counts, and each file's group, statement count, bytes and SHA-256, in apply order, then `reset`. The export hash is a **content hash**: the SHA-256 of `<table>\t<sha256 of its documents.jsonl>` lines, sorted, so a ZIP and the same export unpacked plan to the same bytes, and nothing skipped (`_storage/`) counts. The manifest holds no path, so the same inputs and `--now` give byte-identical plans in any folder (tested: two directory runs and a ZIP run).
- **2026-10-04 · `plan` (`src/core/plan.ts`, `src/cli/main.ts`).** The core takes the files' text and returns every output file; the CLI only reads and writes. `TRANSFORMS` is empty until task 10 lists tasks 7 and 8's transforms; each is `(context) → { group, statements, resetKeys, sections, ftsGestureIds? }`, run concurrently (they are pure) and emitted in list order. `--report-only` writes `report.json` and `report.md` alone. The CLI first removes the plan files a previous run left in `--out` (only names it writes itself), so a smaller plan leaves no stale chunk. A plan with blockers is still written, and `plan` exits 1 with a line saying `apply` refuses it. It prints counts and the folder, never a value.
- **2026-10-04 · Task 1 review M6: every path option must be absolute.** `bun run migrate:convex` runs in `packages/migrate-convex`, so a relative path would silently mean the package's folder. Of the two fixes offered, the refusal is taken: `plan` refuses a relative `--export`, `--out`, `--workos-users`, `--mux-map` or `--overrides` with a message suggesting `"$PWD/<path>"` (exit 2), and the usage says so. The root script is unchanged (no `MIGRATE_CONVEX_CWD`), so the root manifest stays task 1's. `main.ts`'s `absolutePath` helper is there for the options of tasks 9 and 10. `out/` stays gitignored in the package for anyone who points `--out` there.
- **2026-10-04 · The shared wrangler runner (`src/cli/wrangler.ts`).** `createWrangler(env, run)` runs `wrangler` from `apps/site` with `--env <env>` and `--local` for `dev`, `--remote` otherwise: `d1 execute DB --json --command|--file`, `kv key get <key> --binding KV --text` and `kv key put`. A missing key is NULL both ways (local wrangler prints "Value not found" and exits 0; remote fails with a 404). `parseWranglerJson` takes the whole stdout if it parses, otherwise the longest run of lines from a line starting with `[` or `{` that parses, so a banner, a proxy line or an update box around the JSON is skipped. Errors name the command, the env, the exit code and wrangler's reason (its JSON `error.text` or its last line), never the SQL. `createFakeWrangler({ d1, kv, banner, trailer })` answers the same arguments from memory with wrangler's output shapes; tasks 9 and 10 use it, and no test runs the real wrangler.
- **2026-10-04 · The core's type check moves to the Workers base (deviation from task 1's layout).** Task 1 checked `src/core` on the `library` base with no types; once the core imports `@smog/db` (`D1Database` in its client) and `@smog/utils` (Web Crypto, `TextEncoder`), that cannot compile. `tsconfig.json` now extends `worker.json` with the feature packages' `types`, which still has no Bun or Node types (a probe file using `process`, `Buffer` and `Bun` fails). The Bun programme (`tsconfig.bun.json`) reaches the same `@smog/db` client through the core; `test/d1-ambient.d.ts` names `D1Database` there as Drizzle's `AnyD1Database`, since the Workers runtime types clash with Bun's. `biome.json` gives `packages/migrate-convex/src/cli/**` the `Bun` global, as `scripts/**` has.
- **2026-10-04 · The export reader (`src/cli/read-export.ts`).** A directory or a ZIP (by `stat`). Only `<table>/documents.jsonl` at the top level is read; any top-level name starting with `_` (`_storage/`, `_tables/`, …), `generated_schema.jsonl` and `README.md` are skipped. The ZIP is read whole and `unzipSync`'s `filter` skips the rest before inflating it (tested with a `_storage/` blob that is not UTF-8: a fatal decoder would throw on it). The text must be UTF-8. `test/fixtures/export/` has a real export's layout (`_storage/` with a blob, `_tables/`, a `generated_schema.jsonl`, a README) and one row per table; the ZIP is built in the test. knip's `ignoreDependencies` lines for `@smog/sponsorships`, `@smog/utils`, `drizzle-orm`, `fflate` and `zod` are gone.
- **2026-10-04 · Task 5 review fixes: the pseudonymiser covers every free-text and secret field (I1).** These amend the pseudonymiser entry above. On staging:
  - **List share tokens are replaced, not dropped.** `shareToken(key, real)` (`pseudoShareToken`) is the unpadded base64url SHA-256 of `smog-convex:staging-share:<key>`, `newToken()`'s 43-character shape, deterministic per share key. So every share row survives, the counts stay identical, and the share links can be rehearsed. `sponsorship_token` rows are still dropped (`tokens`), as B2 says.
  - **List names and descriptions.** List names become "Lijst <n>" (`listName`) and descriptions NULL (`listDescription`). They are free text a user wrote ("Lijst voor Jan").
  - **Free text in legacy data** becomes `[staging]` (`freeText`): the `legacy` sponsorship event's `sponsorName`, original `overlayText` and `rejectionReason`.
  - **An admin log's `metadata`** goes through `legacyMetadata`, which replaces the values of `STAGING_TEXT_KEYS` at any depth. That covers `reject_sponsorship`'s `reason` and any contact field; a gesture's or category's own input is public catalogue text and is kept.
  - **`invoice_request.name` and the sponsorship's `display_name`** go through `name("sponsor", n)`, as `sponsor.name` does.
  - **Kept on purpose, all documented in `target.ts`:** ids, dates, amounts, statuses, Mollie payment ids (a test-mode key cannot fetch them) and playback ids (the public video).

  The staging no-personal-data test now runs `plan` with `--workos-users`, `--mux-map` and `--overrides` fixtures (`test/fixtures/{workos-users.csv,mux-map.json,overlay-overrides.json}`). `FIXTURE_SECRETS` lists every address, name, company, overlay, override, list name and description, VAT number, token, asset id, IP address, user agent and WorkOS id among them. Tasks 7 and 8 must route those fields through these helpers and add their rows' values to the list.
- **2026-10-04 · B1 follows chains (I2).** A sponsorship copies the gesture's video when it is created, and the old `reject` (which has no status guard) never restored the gesture. So "A active, A rejected, B created and approved" leaves B's original pointing at A's sponsored video. After cases 1–4, while the result is another sponsorship's `sponsoredVideoPlaybackId` or `previewVideoPlaybackId`, the rule follows that row's `originalVideoPlaybackId`, warning `chainedRestore` each time.
