A row is followed once, so a cycle stops the walk; a result that is still a sponsor's video then warns `restoreEndsInSponsorVideo`, for the owner to set that gesture's video by hand. The result is now `{ playbackId, restoredFrom: string[], warnings: Warning[] }`: it holds every warning, not one.
- **2026-10-04 · `insertRow` refuses at plan time what D1 would refuse at apply time (I3, M4).** A NOT NULL column with no SQL default that is left out or set to NULL, and a string D1 cannot store as it is (a NUL byte, or a lone surrogate, which would be written as U+FFFD), throw a `PlanBlocker` naming the table and the column, with the row's legacy id and id. Drizzle's `$defaultFn`s are **never** run: `created_at`/`updated_at` default to `new Date()`, and a plan must not depend on the clock. Transforms set them, usually from the Convex row's dates. `plan` turns a `PlanBlocker` into a blocker of the table's domain and leaves that transform's statements out. Any other error still fails the plan. `validateExport` also reports such strings as a row blocker (`unwritableString`, with the field's path).
- **2026-10-04 · Smaller review fixes.**
  - **M1, negative numbers.** They are rendered in parentheses (`(-5)`), so `3 -${-5}` cannot become the comment `3 --5`.
  - **M2, missing KV keys.** `kvGet` reads only the KV API's key-not-found answer (`[code: 10009]` or `404: Not Found`) as a missing key. A missing binding or namespace, or "command not found", throws.
  - **M3, truncated exports.** The reader keeps `_tables/documents.jsonl`, the deployment's table list: rows with a `name` (or `tableName`); rows whose `state` is set and not `active` are skipped, and so are system tables. A listed table whose `documents.jsonl` is missing is a `missingTable` blocker. Without a readable list, an absent table stays a warning, plus a `noTableList` warning. The table list counts in the export's content hash.
  - **M5, composite keys.** `legacyKey(...parts)` is `JSON.stringify(parts)`, so a `:` in a slug or keyword cannot merge two keys. A row with one Convex `_id` uses the bare `_id`.
  - **M8, `--now`.** The usage says it defaults to the current time (recorded in the manifest) and that byte-identical plans need it.
  - **M6 and M7** go to task 11's runbook (its brief has the notes):
    - the reset fails loudly on rows made after an import (RESTRICT), so it runs right after a rehearsal;
    - the rehearsal starts with `plan --report-only` on a real dashboard export.

## Mobile environments: EAS profiles, origins and the store answers (phase 8 task 6)

- **2026-10-04 · The EAS profiles build for the site's origins (ruling 3; carry 26, mobile half; P-35).** `apps/mobile/eas.json` has three profiles, each with only the three public origin keys in `env` (reviewed in git):
  - `development`: dev client, internal, channel `development`, EAS environment `development`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` `http://localhost:5173` (dev's `SITE_URL`), host `localhost`;
  - `staging`: internal, channel `staging`, EAS environment `preview`, `https://smog-site-staging.zias.workers.dev`. The old `smog-site-staging.workers.dev` (also `app.config.ts`'s fallback) was not staging's host: a workers.dev host is `<script>.<subdomain>.workers.dev`;
  - `production`: store, `autoIncrement`, channel `production`, EAS environment `production`, `PRODUCTION_LAUNCH_ORIGIN`.
  - **`PRODUCTION_LAUNCH_ORIGIN`** (`@smog/config/constants`) is `https://smog-site-production.zias.workers.dev`, production's `SITE_URL` (path W). On path (D) the domain commit changes it with `SITE_URL`, or the owner moves it earlier for an app review on the domain (the runbook, task 11). Nothing else assumes either path.
  - **`app.config.ts`'s fallback host** (a build without `EXPO_PUBLIC_SITE_HOST`) is staging's host.
  - **`release-config-check` gains `checkEasProfiles`:** each profile must exist; `EXPO_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT` must be its env (`dev`, `staging`, `production`); development and staging must equal their env's `SITE_URL` and its host, production must equal `PRODUCTION_LAUNCH_ORIGIN` and its host; `env` may hold no other key (the OpenPanel pair belongs in EAS, so a secret in `eas.json` fails); `appVersionSource` must be `remote` while any profile has `autoIncrement`; the evaluated `app.config.ts` (with `EXPO_PUBLIC_SITE_HOST` unset) must fall back to staging's host. `PRODUCTION_LAUNCH_ORIGIN` differing from production's `SITE_URL` is a **warning** (`::warning::[releaseConfig] …`), not an error, since path (D) allows it for a while. `checkReleaseConfig(root, warnings?)` collects the warnings; the script prints them and still exits 0.
  - A profile with `extends` is not resolved: the check expects every profile to carry its own `env`, so an `extends` without it fails loudly rather than passing unchecked.
  - Alternative: derive the profile URLs from `wrangler.jsonc` in a dynamic config (rejected: `eas.json` is static JSON; a check keeps the reviewed values honest instead).
- **2026-10-04 · Remote app versions (ruling 3).** `cli.appVersionSource: "remote"`: `app.config.ts` is dynamic, so EAS cannot write a local `autoIncrement` back into it. `ios.buildNumber` and `android.versionCode` left `app.config.ts`. **Owner action (task 11 moves it to PROGRESS):** seed the remote counters once, before the first build, with `eas build:version:set`: iOS **51** and Android **80**, the last values in the stores (the old `app.json`), as Expo's docs ask; the first `autoIncrement` build is then 52 / 81. **Skipping the seed is not safe:** with no local value to start from, the remote counter starts at 1, and Play refuses a `versionCode` below 80. `version` stays `3.0.0` in `app.config.ts`. The `eas-cli` floor is `>= 24.10.0` (the CLI is the owner's, not a dependency).
- **2026-10-04 · OTA updates go through `scripts/eas-update.ts` (review I1).** `eas update` does not read a build profile's `env` from `eas.json` (Expo's docs), and on SDK 55+ it needs `--environment` and takes only that EAS environment's variables. The origin keys live only in `eas.json` (ruling 3), so a bare `eas update` would bundle JS without them (`parseMobileEnv` throws, or dev tools turn on with `EXPO_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT` unset), or with a local `.env`'s `localhost` and `dev`.
  - **The wrapper:** `bun -F @smog/mobile update -- --profile <development|staging|production> [eas update args]`. It loads `build.<profile>.env` into the process env (Expo's CLI keeps a set variable over `.env`), and runs `eas update --channel <profile channel> --environment <profile EAS environment> …` from `apps/mobile`, so the OpenPanel pair still comes from the EAS environment. It refuses to run without a known profile, refuses `--channel`, `--branch` and `--environment` (the profile decides them), and refuses a profile without its channel, environment or any of the three keys. Tested with a fake runner (`scripts/eas-update.test.ts`); it never calls `eas` in tests.
  - **`release-config-check` (`checkEasUpdateScripts`)** refuses a root or `apps/mobile` script that runs `eas update` itself, and requires the mobile `update` script to be the wrapper.
  - Alternative: also set the three keys as EAS environment variables (rejected: then they live in two places, and `checkEasProfiles` cannot see the EAS side). The runbook (task 11) uses the wrapper for every OTA update.
- **2026-10-04 · The OpenPanel pair lives in EAS environment variables.** One `EXPO_PUBLIC_OPENPANEL_CLIENT_ID` / `_SECRET` pair per EAS environment (`development`, `preview`, `production`), visibility "Plain text": a write-only client that ships in the binary anyway (spec §12), so "Sensitive" or "Secret" would protect nothing and hide it from the build logs only. It never enters git; `apps/mobile/.env.example` gives the `eas env:create` command. Without the pair the build has analytics off, which is safe.
- **2026-10-04 · `submit.production`.** iOS: `ascAppId` `6758547774` (the App Store id in `APP_STORE_URL`) and `appleTeamId` `96XKP6MU2A`. Android: `track: "internal"`, `releaseStatus: "draft"`, so a submission never reaches users without a promotion in Play Console. The Play service-account key is an EAS-hosted credential (uploaded by the owner with `eas credentials`), so no key path is in git. App Store Connect authentication is the owner's ASC API key, also EAS-hosted. No `eas` command needing a login was run.
- **2026-10-04 · The store privacy answers (carry 23; X-16; from the privacy text and LEGAL-SIGNOFF P2–P17, P29–P32).** Prepared here; the owner submits them (task 11 copies them into `docs/deployment.md`). Both forms answer for the **app** (the website is outside them).
  - **App Store privacy label ("App Privacy").**
    - *Data used to track you:* **none**. No data is linked with third-party data for advertising, and none goes to a data broker. No IDFA, no ATT prompt.
    - *Contact Info → Email Address, Name:* collected, **linked to the user**, purpose **App Functionality** (the account; sign-in codes and links, the sponsor emails come from the website). Optional: only with an account.
    - *Identifiers → User ID:* collected, linked, **App Functionality**; also **Analytics** (OpenPanel is linked to the user id only after sign-in, P15, and only with consent).
    - *Identifiers → Device ID:* collected, **not linked** before sign-in and linked after it, **Analytics** only, and only with consent (OpenPanel's pseudonymous device id). Answer "linked" to stay on the safe side.
    - *Identifiers → Device ID, again for App Functionality (review M2):* `expo-updates` sends a per-install `EAS-Client-ID` (`expo-eas-client`) to Expo's update server, which uses it only to serve and roll out updates. Expo processes it for us, but Apple counts data a third-party SDK collects, so declare it: **App Functionality**, not linked, not tracking.
    - *Location → Coarse Location (review I2):* collected, **Analytics** only, and only with consent; linked after sign-in, like the device id. OpenPanel derives an approximate location (country and region) from the IP address of each event (privacy text, section 6; P15).
    - *Usage Data → Product Interaction:* collected, **Analytics**, with consent only (which gesture was viewed or saved, screens; no search terms, names or emails, P8). OpenPanel is self-hosted (`analytics.zias.be`), so it is not a third party for the label, and there is no tracking.
    - *User Content → Other User Content:* favorites and lists (names, descriptions, items, share links), linked, **App Functionality**, only with an account (a guest's stay on the device, P3).
    - *Diagnostics:* **not collected** (no crash reporter; the developer logs stay on the device). The privacy text's "error information" under technical data is the server's own logs of requests, which are not data the app collects for diagnostics.
    - *Device signals by a third party (P32):* Cloudflare Turnstile runs in the challenge web view for the same forms as the website. Declare it as *Other Data Types → Other Data* ("device signals for fraud prevention"), collected by a third party (Cloudflare), purpose **App Functionality** (Apple counts fraud prevention and security there), not linked to the user, not used for tracking.
    - *Precise Location, Contacts, Health, Financial Info, Purchases, Browsing / Search History, Sensitive Info, Photos or Videos, Audio:* **not collected** by the app. Sponsorship payments happen on the website (Mollie), not in the app. A search term is sent to the API only to answer that search and is not stored; the recent searches stay on the device.
    - *The Google photo (P29):* the app shows initials only and never fetches it. The server keeps the URL Google sends with a Google sign-in (`user.image`) as part of the account; it is not the user's photo library, so *Photos or Videos* stays unchecked. If App Review asks, it is account data from the sign-in provider.
  - **Google Play data safety form.**
    - *Data shared with third parties:* **none** in Play's sense (service providers that process for us, Cloudflare, Mux, Expo, our self-hosted OpenPanel, are not "sharing"). *Sold:* none.
    - *Encrypted in transit:* yes (https only). *Deletion:* users can delete their account in the app (account screen) and on the website; a deletion URL is the website's account page.
    - *Personal info → Name, Email address, User IDs:* collected, **optional** (only with an account), purpose **Account management, App functionality**.
    - *App activity → App interactions:* collected, **optional** (consent), purpose **Analytics**. *Other user-generated content:* favorites and lists, optional, **App functionality**.
    - *Device or other IDs:* collected, optional, **Analytics** (the OpenPanel device id, with consent) and **Fraud prevention, security and compliance** (Turnstile's device signals, P32). Expo's update client id goes to a service provider, which Play does not count as sharing; it is collected for **App functionality**.
    - *Location → Approximate location (review I2):* collected, **optional** (consent), purpose **Analytics** (OpenPanel's country and region from the IP).
    - *Precise location, Financial info, Photos and videos, Messages, Contacts, Calendar, Files and docs, Health, Web browsing, App info and performance:* not collected.
    - *Not tracking, not advertising.* The app shows no ads.
  - Rationale: the answers follow what the code sends, not the old app's forms (WorkOS and Convex are gone, guests keep their data on the device). Alternative: declare OpenPanel as a third party (rejected: it is our own installation; the privacy text names it as a processor we host).
- **2026-10-04 · `SPONSOR_LINK_IN_APP` stays `true` for the first iOS review (carry 13).** The gesture screen's "Sponsor now" opens the site's wizard in the system browser: sponsoring a gesture is a service bought by a business for the public (the gesture shows the sponsor's name to every user), not digital content unlocked in the app, and the app sells nothing.
  - **The 3.1.1 fallback, written down:** if App Review rejects it under 3.1.1 (or 3.1.3), set `SPONSOR_LINK_IN_APP = false` in `apps/mobile/src/config.ts` and resubmit. iOS then drops the `available` card (the only one with a link) and the "available again from {date}" line; who sponsors a gesture stays shown, and Android keeps the link. The change is one constant, covered by `sponsor-cta.test.tsx`, so the resubmitted build needs no other work. A US-storefront external-link entitlement is not requested.

## Importer transforms: users, catalogue, learning, consents, audit (phase 8 task 7)

- **2026-10-04 · The transforms' shape.** `src/core/transform/{users,catalog,learning,account}.ts` each export one `Transform` of `plan.ts` (`(context) → result`; the brief's `(export, inputs, target, now)` is that `context`), for the groups `10-users`, `20-catalog`, `30-learning` and `40-account`. Each result is a `TransformResult` plus `rows` (the typed values given to `insertRow`, for the tests); the users result also has `claims` (below). Each transform recomputes what it needs from the export: `resolveUsers` (users.ts) maps every Convex user to its migrated account, a guest or "no email", and `catalogIds` (catalog.ts) gives every gesture's and category's `legacyUuid`. Both are pure, so the four transforms run concurrently and agree. Users are referenced through `legacyIdRef("user", …)` (a claimed account keeps its own id); gestures, categories and lists by their `legacyUuid`, which is always their id. Task 10 lists them in `TRANSFORMS`; until then `test/transform-plan.test.ts` passes them to `plan` itself.
- **2026-10-04 · Users (rulings 7 and 8).**
  - A guest is a row with no `workosId`; an upgraded guest (both ids) is a real user. Guests' favorites, lists, list items and consents are counted in the `users` section and never emitted.
  - The address is the WorkOS export's when it has one, else Convex's, trimmed and lower-cased. Counted: WorkOS differs from Convex, WorkOS fills a missing one, a user missing from the WorkOS export (`workosUserMissing`, a warning: its Convex email is used). The name is the WorkOS `first last`, cut to 80 UTF-16 units without splitting a surrogate pair, else `""`.
  - **Rows sharing a `workosId` are one user (review M-3).** The old `migrateGuestToUser` could give a guest row a WorkOS id another row already had. Real users are grouped by `workosId` first, oldest first; the group's address is the WorkOS one, else the first Convex address in age order, so a row without one is not dropped beside its twin. The `duplicateWorkosId` warning lists each group; the email merge below then works on these groups.
  - A real user with no address is dropped with a `noEmail` warning that counts what is lost: favorites (its default list's items included, since they are favorites now), its other lists with their items, and its consents. Its admin log entries and the list items it added to other lists keep a NULL actor, counted in the account and learning sections (review M-4).
  - **Duplicates** merge into the oldest account (`createdAt`, then `_creationTime`, then `_id`): its `_id` is the `legacy_id`, its `createdAt` the `created_at`; `updated_at` is the newest `lastActiveAt` of the group; the name is the first non-empty WorkOS name in age order; admin if any was. The `duplicateEmail` warning lists each group's kept and merged ids.
  - `privateRelay` is an info issue with the ids. `noAdmin` is a blocker when no migrated user is an admin.
  - **Statements, per user:** `UPDATE "user" SET "legacy_id" = ? WHERE "email" = ? AND "legacy_id" IS NULL`, then `INSERT … ON CONFLICT ("legacy_id") DO NOTHING ON CONFLICT ("email") DO NOTHING`. So an account made by `admin:grant --create` before the import (task 3's M-4) is claimed: one row, still an admin, its own id, the legacy id set, and the user's favorites and lists attached through the subquery (the Workers-pool test). No statement names `role` except the insert's column list. On staging both statements carry the pseudonymised address, which matches nothing.
  - **A differing role is the preflight's to report.** A plan cannot see D1, so the users result returns `claims` (each address as emitted, the legacy id and the role the import would insert); task 10's preflight reads the accounts with those addresses and reports the ones whose role differs.
- **2026-10-04 · The catalogue (ruling 9; carry 16 (d)).** Names are trimmed. Slugs are `slugify(name)`, `category` / `gesture` when that is empty (the admin service's fallback), then `-2`, `-3` … in `_creationTime, _id` order, so a newer export never moves an older slug (tested: a later "Mama" gets `mama-3` and a later "Mama 2" `mama-2-2`). `sort_order` is the 0-based rank by `normalizeText(name)`, ties in export order. A category has only `_creationTime`, so it is its `created_at`, `updated_at` and (when active) `published_at`. `playback_id` is `resolveGesturePlayback` over the export's sponsorships, and each of its warnings becomes a catalogue warning with the gesture and sponsorship ids. `mux_asset_id` is the Mux map's entry for the **resolved** id: without `--mux-map` one `noMuxMap` warning; with it, `muxAssetMissing` lists the gestures it does not cover. Keywords keep their first spelling of an exact duplicate (the primary key is `(gesture_id, keyword)`). A repeated category id links once; an unknown one is dropped and counted (`unknownCategory`). Every migrated gesture goes to `90-fts`.
- **2026-10-04 · Favorites and lists (ruling 9; carry 24).**
  - Favorites are `user_favorites` plus the items of each `isDefaultFavorites` list, keyed by the merged account and the gesture, at the earliest `created_at`. Default lists never become lists. **A shared default list's links stop working (review I-1):** the old app let the default "Favorieten" list be shared, but favorites are not a shareable list in the new model, so its view and edit tokens are counted (`defaultListShareTokensDropped`) and warned about with the list ids (`defaultListSharesDropped`), and those links answer not found. Over `FAVORITE_IDS_MAX` is a warning: all rows migrate, and `ids` shows the newest 5000.
  - Lists keep their `createdAt` / `updatedAt`. Names are trimmed and cut to 80, descriptions to 280; an empty name becomes `Lijst` (warning `emptyListName`; data, not UI copy), an empty description NULL. Items are ordered by the old `position, createdAt, _id`, a repeated gesture keeps its first item (the primary key is `(list_id, gesture_id)`), and positions are renumbered `0..n-1` per list part; positions are never used as an index. `added_by` is the mapped account or NULL (a guest's item on a real user's shared list stays).
  - **Long lists** split into parts of 500: part 1 keeps the list's `legacyUuid` and name; part `k` is `legacyUuid("list", legacyKey(listId, "k"))`, named `Name (n)` with the base cut so the whole stays within 80, and `n` the next suffix the owner has not taken (any of the owner's list names, including other parts). Every part keeps the list's dates and description. An account with more than `LISTS_MAX` lists after splitting is a `tooManyLists` blocker listing the account and its count.
  - **Shares:** only `visibility = "shared"` lists; `view` from `viewShareToken`, `edit` from `editShareToken` only with `allowSharedEditing`; ids `legacyUuid("list_share", legacyKey(listId, role))`, `created_at` the list's `createdAt`, `created_by` the owner, on part 1. Tokens left on a private list are counted and dropped. **An edit token on a list with shared editing off is dropped (review M-1):** the old app opened such a link read-only, but `list_share_active_role_uq` allows one active view share, so that link now answers not found; the `editTokenDropped` warning lists the lists, whose view links keep working. The `listBookmarks` info issue says that old `/lists/<convex list id>` links to an owner's own list answer not found, and which share links keep working: view links of shared lists and edit links of lists with shared editing on.
- **2026-10-04 · Consents and admin logs (ruling 9; carry 16 (c)).** Consent event ids are `legacyUuid("consent_event", legacyKey(_id, purpose))`; rows of guests, dropped or unknown users are counted. Admin logs older than three **calendar** years before `--now` are dropped (an info issue). The old app wrote `targetType` `category`, `gesture` and `sponsorship` (and the export may hold `user`); those keep their type and anything else becomes `system` with `target_id` NULL. `target_id` is the gesture's or category's `legacyUuid` when the export has it, the sponsorship's `legacyUuid` when the export has it (task 8's id; its file runs after `40-account`, and `target_id` has no foreign key), a user through `legacyIdRef`, else NULL; `bulk_update_gestures` joined several ids with commas, so its target is NULL. `actor_id` is `legacyIdRef` of the merged account, or NULL. `data.legacy` keeps the old `action`, `targetType`, `targetId` and `metadata` (scrubbed on staging by `legacyMetadata`).
- **2026-10-04 · The reset and claimed accounts.** A favorite's reset key is `(legacyUuid("user", legacyId), gesture id)`, which a claimed account's rows do not have. They still go on reset, with their imported gesture (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), and the Workers-pool test resets to nothing but the pre-existing admin, then re-applies to the same counts.
- **2026-10-04 · Fixtures and shared test files.** The fixture tables of task 7 (`users`, `categories`, `gestures`, `user_favorites`, `gesture_lists`, `gesture_list_items`, `user_consents`, `adminLogs`) keep task 5's first row and add every case of the brief; the WorkOS CSV gains two rows. The very large cases (a 501-item list, 100 and 101 lists, 5001 favorites) and B1's three sponsorship cases are built in the tests. Task 5's tests that assumed one row per table now count the fixture's rows (`export-schema.test.ts`, `plan.test.ts`). The Workers-pool smoke (`test/integration/transforms.test.ts`) reads the fixture through Vite's `?raw` imports (`test/integration/raw-imports.d.ts`), since workerd has no file system.

## Importer transform: sponsorships (phase 8 task 8)

- **2026-10-04 · The sponsorship transform (`src/core/transform/sponsorships.ts`, ruling 10).** `transformSponsorships(context)` is a pure transform in the `50-sponsorships` group; task 10 lists it in `TRANSFORMS`. It finds gestures and reviewers only through `legacyIdRef` (`legacy_id` subqueries), so it does not depend on task 7's transforms. It returns its rows as well as its statements, so tests read the values rather than parse SQL.
  - **Checkouts.** Rows are grouped by the trimmed `molliePaymentId`; a row without one is its own checkout. The checkout key is `legacyKey("mollie", id)` (or the row's `_id`), and `sponsor.id` / `payment.id` are `legacyUuid("sponsor" | "payment", key)`. The sponsor is the first row's contact (`contactFullName`, falling back to `sponsorName` with a warning, a blocker when both are empty; the email trimmed; the company trimmed, empty → NULL; over 120 characters cut with a warning), `locale = "nl"`, `created_at` the checkout's first `createdAt`. Rows of one checkout with different contacts warn `checkoutContactDiffers`.
  - **B3, the invoice request.** One per checkout whose rows asked for an invoice: `name = invoiceName ?? contactCompany ?? contactFullName` (each trimmed, empty counts as missing; over 160 cut with a warning), `email = invoiceEmail ?? sponsorEmail`, `vat_number = invoiceVatNumber` trimmed. A missing VAT is `""` with `invoiceVatMissing`; one that fails `normalizeBelgianVat` is kept as typed with `invoiceVatInvalid`. The service's mod-97 rule is never applied to imported rows. Every NOT NULL column is always set (the Workers-pool smoke applies them).
  - **The payment.** Only with a Mollie id: `kind = "initial"`, the Mollie id, `EUR`, the sum of the rows that have a payment part, `created_at` the first `createdAt`, `updated_at` the last `updatedAt`. Each row's part comes from the status table (`pending` has none). The checkout's status is `paid` if any part is, else `open` if any is, else `canceled`; mixed parts warn `checkoutMixedPayment`. `paid_at` is the earliest paid row's `createdAt` (risk 12). One `payment_item` per row with a part: `amount_cents = paymentAmount` (a non-integer or negative amount is a blocker), `includes_logo = hasLogo ?? paymentAmount === 6000`; an amount other than 5000 or 6000 warns `unexpectedAmount`. A paid row without a Mollie id has no payment and warns `paidWithoutMollieId`.
  - **The status table** is ruling 10's, from the old `sponsorships.ts` mutations. The 24-hour rule is strict: a `pending_payment` row is cancelled (its payment `canceled`) only when `updatedAt < now - 24 h`, the old `getStalePendingPayments` cut-off. `pending_approval` takes `sponsoredVideoPlaybackId ?? previewVideoPlaybackId`, and with neither it becomes `render_failed` (info `reviewWithoutVideo`). Other statuses take `sponsoredVideoPlaybackId`. `starts_at` / `ends_at` are kept only for `live`, `expiring` and `expired` (the old `startDate` is 0 until payment). `reminder_sent_at = renewalReminderSentAt` on every row.
  - **`display_name`.** The override when `overlay-overrides.json` names the row (counted `overlayOverrides`), otherwise the trimmed `overlayText` if `displayNameSchema` accepts it. Otherwise it is an `overlayOffender` blocker with one detail row per offender: `legacyId`, the old `status`, the `length` in characters, the `reason` (`tooLong`, `empty` or `controlCharacters`), the text and the `sponsorName`. The issue is marked `personal` on production. On staging the text column holds the pseudonymised "Sponsor <n>" and the sponsor name `[staging]`, so the report quotes nothing. The blocker is raised on **both** targets, so a staging rehearsal shows the offenders the production plan will have. An override for a sponsorship that is not in the export warns `unknownOverride`; an override over 35 characters is refused when the file is parsed (`InputError`, task 5). `display_name` is the overlay text, the text in the video. The old public credit used `sponsorName`, which survives only in the legacy event.
  - **The legacy event (M4).** One `legacy` `sponsorship_event` per row: id `legacyUuid("sponsorship_event", legacyKey(_id, "legacy"))`, `created_at = updatedAt`, `actor_id` the reviewer's `legacyIdRef` when `reviewedBy` is a user of the export (else NULL), and `data.legacy` = `{ status, reviewedAt, rejectionReason, previewVideoPlaybackId, originalVideoPlaybackId, sponsorName, overlayText (untruncated), hasLogo, durationYears, overlayImageStorageId }`, with absent fields as `null`. **`reviewedAt` is epoch ms**, as Convex stored it: task 4's `rejectedTooLongAgo` bound reads ms or ISO. The free text goes through `freeText`.
  - **Tokens (R-15).** A `reEditToken` that expires after `--now` becomes a `reedit` `sponsorship_token`: `token_hash = hashSponsorshipToken(raw)`, the same expiry, `created_at = updatedAt`, id `legacyUuid("sponsorship_token", legacyKey(_id, "reedit"))`. Expired tokens are counted (`reeditTokensExpired`). A token with no expiry is not migrated (`reeditTokenWithoutExpiry`), since the column is NOT NULL and the old code treated it as never expiring. A token that is neither a UUID nor a new token warns `reeditTokenShape`, and two rows with one token are a `duplicateToken` blocker. `reeditTokens` counts the export's eligible tokens on both targets; staging writes none.
  - **Blockers.** A row whose gesture is not in the export is `missingGesture` and is left out of the SQL. Two rows on one gesture whose **mapped** statuses are both in `BLOCKING_SPONSORSHIP_STATUSES` are `blockingConflict`, listing every row involved. These include `changes_requested`, which the old `BLOCKING_STATUSES` omitted. No `render_job` rows are made.
  - **Logos and assets.** `logo_key` is NULL. `overlayImageStorageId` rows are counted (`overlayImagesDropped`) and kept in the legacy data. A row in `in_review`, `render_failed` or `changes_requested` that paid for a logo is listed as info `logoNotStored` (I12: a new render has none). `video_asset_id` comes from the Mux map for the row's video; a video with no map entry warns `missingMuxAsset`. Staging writes NULL (`assetId`).
  - **Order and conflict targets.** `sponsor (id)`, `invoice_request (sponsor_id)`, `payment (id)`, `sponsorship (legacy_id)`, `payment_item (payment_id, sponsorship_id)`, `sponsorship_event (id)`, `sponsorship_token (id)`, in that foreign-key order. A Mollie id or token hash already on a foreign row is not a named target, so it fails loudly (ruling 7). The reset keys cover every table written.
- **2026-10-04 · The sponsorship fixtures (deviation in layout).** `test/fixtures/export/sponsorships/documents.jsonl` now holds fifteen rows (see the review fixes below) covering every old status and the edge cases of the brief, and `overlay-overrides.json` covers the 36-character overlay. Two of the brief's cases would block every plan of the fixture, so they live in `test/fixtures/sponsorships-blockers.jsonl` and the tests append them: the second blocking sponsorship on a gesture and the missing gesture. The "without an override" case is the fixture planned without `--overrides`. Six of the rows need gestures besides `Mama`. The `gestures` table is task 7's fixture, so those six rows are in `test/fixtures/sponsorship-gestures.jsonl`, and **task 10 must merge them into `gestures/documents.jsonl`** before the fixture plan has no `missingGesture` blocker. The rows' personal values are in `SPONSORSHIP_SECRETS` (`test/sponsorship-fixtures.ts`), spread into `FIXTURE_SECRETS`. Task 5's two tests that assumed one row per table now accept more.
- **2026-10-04 · The sponsorship smoke on D1 (`test/integration/sponsorships.test.ts`).** It seeds the export's users and gestures by legacy id, then applies the transform's statements for the fixture with its overrides and Mux map. Every CHECK, NOT NULL and foreign key holds, as does the one-blocking index. A second apply changes no count, and the reset deletes every row. The staging plan applies with the same counts and no token. The fixtures reach workerd as Vite `?raw` imports (`test/integration/raw-imports.d.ts`), since workerd has no file system.
- **2026-10-04 · Task 8 review fixes: ruling 10 amended (controller ruling I4, I1–I3, M1, M3, M5).** These supersede the entries above where they differ.
  - **I4, no local cancel of a Mollie payment.** As in phase 6 (D-STALE), a payment that has a Mollie id is never cancelled without asking Mollie. An old `pending_payment` row **with** a Mollie id is imported as `awaiting_payment` with an `open` payment carrying that `mollie_id`, whatever its age. The first hourly stale sweep then fetches it at Mollie and settles, revives or cancels it, so a payment that was paid before cutover but whose old webhook failed for good is not lost. Ruling 10's 24-hour rule now applies only to rows **without** a Mollie id.
  - **I1, a `pending_payment` without a Mollie id.** `createBulkSimplified` inserted rows before the Mollie payment existed. Such a row whose `updatedAt` is more than 24 hours before `--now` becomes `cancelled`, with no payment (no money is possible; counted `stalePendingPayments`). A newer one becomes `awaiting_payment` with an `open` `initial` payment and `mollie_id` NULL, plus its item. Otherwise nothing in the new system could reach it (the sweep and the admin's cancel and mark-paid all go through a payment), and it would block its gesture for good. Option (b) of the review was taken: the sweep's `cancelLocally` frees it after 24 h, and the admin keeps "mark paid".
  - **M1, a paid row without a Mollie id** (the old admin's mark-paid on a row that never reached Mollie) gets a `paid` payment with `mollie_id` NULL and its `payment_item`. That keeps the amount and `includes_logo`, which `readTokenLink` reads for the re-edit page. Both cases are info `paymentWithoutMollieId`. A row with no payment part (`pending`, a stale row without an id, an old `cancelled` row without an id) still gets no payment.
  - **M3, `marked_paid_manually`.** The old `admin.sponsorships.markPaidManually` logged `adminLogs { action: "mark_paid_manually", targetType: "sponsorship", targetId }`. For each sponsorship with such a log (the first one) whose payment maps to `paid`, the transform writes a `marked_paid_manually` event: `data = { paymentId }`, `actor_id` the logging admin, `created_at` the log's, id `legacyUuid("sponsorship_event", legacyKey(_id, "marked_paid_manually"))`. Counted `markedPaidManually`. A later Mollie `paid` for that payment is then flagged as a double payment by `settlePaid`, as for a native row.
  - **I3, mixed checkouts.** Rows of one Mollie payment whose payment parts mix `open` with `paid` or `canceled` are a **blocker** (`checkoutMixedPayment`), with one detail per row: the legacy id, the Mollie id (not personal), the row's payment part and its new status. A `paid` payment holding an `awaiting_payment` item would never be settled, and an `open` one holding a `cancelled` item would take that row's share silently when paid. The owner settles those rows in the old admin before the export. `paid` beside `canceled` only warns (`checkoutPartlyCancelled`): a cancelled row blocks nothing.
  - **I2, the old logo is never copied.** In the old legacy flow `overlayImageStorageId` held the logo itself as a base64 data URL. It would put a sponsor's logo on staging, and a statement over D1's 100 KB limit. `data.legacy.overlayImageStorageId` is replaced by `overlayImage`: `{ kind: "dataUrl", bytes, sha256 }` for a data URL (its decoded size and the SHA-256 of the URL), `{ kind: "storageId", sha256 }` otherwise, or `null`. The fixture holds a real 1x1 PNG data URL. A test checks that neither target's SQL contains it and that a 400 KB logo leaves every statement under 4 KB.
  - **M5, one overlay rule.** The offender's `reason` comes from `displayNameSchema`'s own first issue (`too_small` → `empty`, `too_big` → `tooLong`, else `controlCharacters`), and `length` is the length that schema measures.
  - **M6, recorded.** The old `reject` mutation had no status guard; only the admin UI limited it to `pending_approval`. A row rejected through the API from an unpaid status would import with a `paid` payment. That is unlikely, and it is accepted.
  - **Fixtures.** sp03 (an old `pending_payment` with a Mollie id) moves to its own gesture, since it is now blocking. New rows: sp13 (a recent `pending_payment` without an id), sp14 (an old one without an id) and sp15 (`pending_approval` without an id, marked paid by hand, with a logo). sp15's admin log is in `test/fixtures/sponsorship-admin-logs.jsonl`, because `adminLogs` is task 7's fixture; **task 10 merges it into `adminLogs/documents.jsonl`**, with the gestures file, which now has 11 rows. The blocker file gains a mixed checkout (bk03 paid, bk04 open, one Mollie id).

## The Mux commands and the `we_moved` email (phase 8 task 9)

- **2026-10-04 · `mux scan` writes the roles, and `mux renditions` reads them (carry from task 5).** `mux scan --export … --out …` looks up every playback id of the export (two GETs each: the playback-id lookup, then the asset) and writes `mux-map.json` as `{ "<playbackId>": { assetId, renditions, roles } }` plus `mux-scan.json` (the counts and the unknown ids). The roles are `gesture` (the video the migrated gesture gets: B1's `resolveGesturePlayback`, the one copy of the rule), `current` (the export's `gestures.playbackId`), `original`, `sponsored` and `preview`. An id this Mux environment does not know is null in the scan, a warning on stderr, and left out of the map, so `plan` stores NULL and warns. So `mux renditions --map` needs no `--export`: it targets the entries whose roles hold `gesture`, deduplicated by asset id, and refuses a map without `roles` ("write it with `mux scan`"). Its schema extends `muxMapEntrySchema` in `src/cli/mux.ts`; the plan's own schema is unchanged (a loose object, so `roles` passes through).
- **2026-10-04 · The map's `renditions` field stays optional.** It is the scan's snapshot, for the report and the owner. `mux renditions` never trusts it: it reads each asset's state live (`getAsset` → `staticRenditionState`) right before deciding, since a rendition may have been requested or finished since the scan. So the plan does not need it either.
- **2026-10-04 · `mp4_support` beside static renditions: recorded as `legacyMp4Conflict`, never changed (carry from the task 4 review).** Mux does not allow the deprecated `mp4_support` and the static renditions API on one asset. An `absent` asset that still has `mp4_support` (other than `none`; for example `capped-1080p`, whose file `renditionUrls` does not try) is still posted with `--apply`; when Mux refuses it (400, 409 or 422), the outcome is `legacyMp4Conflict` in the ledger and the summary, with the asset's `mp4_support`, and **nothing is changed on the asset**. The dry run already marks those ids `(mp4_support …)`. Alternative rejected: first setting `mp4_support` to `none` (a real, second change per asset that drops existing MP4 files, which a running app may still link). Whether to do that is the owner's decision per asset (runbook, task 11); a re-run after it requests the rendition. The Mux fake does not model the refusal, so the test puts a refusing `fetch` in front of it.
- **2026-10-04 · An `errored` rendition is posted again; a refusal is `failed`.** Ruling 5 posts for `errored`. The fake (like Mux, probably) refuses a second `highest` while the errored file exists; the outcome is then `failed` with the status, the command exits 1, and the owner deletes the errored rendition in the dashboard before a re-run. `@smog/video` has no rendition DELETE, and task 9 does not add one (its files are task 4's).
- **2026-10-04 · Throttles per bucket, 429 and ledgers (`src/cli/remote.ts`; task 9 review finding 1).** Mux limits POSTs and other requests in **separate buckets** (high-priority token: POST bucket 20, refilled 1/s; others 100, refilled 5/s; low-priority token: POST 4 at 1/s; others 20 at 1/s), so `mux scan` and `mux renditions` keep two throttles: POSTs at most **1 per second**, GETs at `--get-rate` per second (default **2**, at most 4, ruling 5's cap). **The owner runs both with a low-priority Mux token** of the production environment (created for the migration and revoked after it), so they never drain the buckets the live site's direct uploads use; with that token `--get-rate 1` avoids 429s entirely. Every Cloudflare API call goes at 4 per second (the API allows 1200 per 5 minutes) and is aborted after 30 s. A 429 is waited out for its `Retry-After` (10 s without one, as Mux documents `x-ratelimit-*` but not `Retry-After`), at most 5 times. `renditions-ledger.json` sits beside the map and `we-moved-ledger.json` in the plan folder (both gitignored by `*-ledger.json`); each is rewritten whole after each change (write, then rename). `requested` assets and queued users are skipped on a re-run; `failed` and `legacyMp4Conflict` assets are looked at again. Dry runs write no ledger.
- **2026-10-04 · `we-moved` (ruling 15).** It refuses any `--env` but `production` before reading anything, then a plan folder whose `manifest.json` is missing or not for `production`, then a missing `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` / `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` (named, never printed). It reads production's `SITE_URL` and the `EMAIL_QUEUE` producer's queue (`smog-production-email`) from `apps/site/wrangler.jsonc` (`Bun.JSONC`), so the email follows whichever origin is committed (path W or D, ruling 2). The queue id comes from `GET /accounts/:id/queues`, every page. Each message is parsed by `emailMessageSchema` (`id` a new UUID, `idempotencyKey: we_moved:<userId>`, the user's locale through `emailLocale` (NULL → `nl`), `props: { url: SITE_URL }`); a row whose address the schema refuses is skipped and reported by user id. Batches hold at most 100 messages and 256 000 bytes of request body (256 KB read as the stricter 1000-byte KB), `content_type: "json"`. The dry run also reads the recipients and the queue id (so a wrong token shows before the send), and prints the counts, the batches, whether the "new address" sentence shows, the nl subject and one sample message with the address masked (`b***@e***.test`). A failed batch stops the run after saying how many were queued; the ledger holds them.
- **2026-10-04 · The `we_moved` copy.** `transactional/we-moved` takes `{ providers, url }` (D1 gives id, email and locale, no name), greets without a name, says the account moved with its favorite gestures and lists, shows "SMOG & Co heeft een nieuw adres: <host>" only when `SITE_URL`'s origin is not `https://app.smog.vlaanderen` (`WE_MOVED_OLD_ORIGIN`), lists the ways in without the old password (an email code, a magic link, "Wachtwoord vergeten", and Google and/or Apple only as configured: see the next entry), asks to update the app, and links to `<origin>/sign-in`. nl subject "SMOG is verhuisd", en "SMOG has moved", fr "SMOG a déménagé". The owner reviews the copy (with the legal sign-off round). It is in the admin email previews ("We zijn verhuisd").
- **2026-10-04 · `we-moved` after the review (findings 2 to 5).**
  - **Placeholder recipients refuse the run.** One address on a reserved or fixture domain (`.invalid`, `.test`, `.example`, `.localhost`, `example.com/org/net`, subdomains included) means production's D1 holds a staging or fixture import, so the whole run is refused before the queue is even looked up; only the count is printed. The tests therefore use an invented, unreserved domain (`smog-fixture-mail.be`) for the recipients, not `@example.test`.
  - **The provider line follows production's configuration.** Ruling 1 leaves Google and Apple optional, so `we-moved` reads production's secret names (`wrangler secret list --format json`, names only, the importer's wrangler runner gains `secretNames()`) and enables a provider as `@smog/auth` does: both its client id and secret. The template names "Google of Apple", "Google" or "Apple", or no provider line at all. A half-configured pair is a warning and is not named. The dry run prints the providers for the owner to confirm. Chosen over a `--providers` flag: the secrets are what the Worker actually enables, so the email cannot promise a route the site does not offer.
  - **Banned users are not recipients:** `… AND (banned IS NULL OR banned = 0)`.
  - **Each Cloudflare request has a 30 s timeout.** A timed-out batch may still have been accepted; it is not in the ledger, so a re-run sends it again, and the consumer's 7-day `email:sent:<key>` marker drops the repeats. Running it once, from the cutover `out/` and the deployed `master` commit, is a runbook rule (task 11).
- **2026-10-04 · The Bun programme of `@smog/migrate-convex` now reaches `@smog/email` and `@smog/video`.** `tsconfig.bun.json` gains `jsx: react-jsx` and the DOM lib (as `@smog/video`'s own config), and `test/email-ambient.d.ts` names the three Workers types `@smog/email`'s sender signatures use (`SendEmail`, `EmailAddress`, `KVNamespace`), like `d1-ambient.d.ts` does for D1, without the Workers runtime types that clash with Bun's.

## Apply, preflight, reset, and the integration suite (phase 8 task 10)

- **2026-10-04 · `plan` runs the five transforms and writes `preflight.json`.** `TRANSFORMS` is users, catalogue, learning, account, sponsorships (every child finds its user by `legacy_id`; favorites and lists need users and gestures; sponsorships need gestures and the reviewer). Each adds what `apply`'s preflight checks: the claimed addresses (users), the emitted slugs (catalogue), the share tokens (learning) and the Mollie ids (sponsorships). `plan` writes them, sorted, to `preflight.json`, and the manifest hashes it (`manifest.preflight`) like every SQL file. It is personal data, like the SQL (`out/`).
- **2026-10-04 · `apply` (ruling 14).** In order, writing nothing before the last check: the flags (production needs `--yes` unless `--dry-run` and refuses `--reset`; `--native-catalog` needs `--reset`); the manifest (it parses, has no blockers, its target fits the env: staging only staging, production only production, dev either, B2) and every listed file's size and SHA-256; then the read-only preflight. With `--dry-run` it prints every wrangler command and stops. Then `reset-imported-*.sql` (with `--reset`), `reset-native-catalog.sql` (with `--native-catalog`), the files in manifest order, a new `catalog:version`, and the verification. `apply-report.json` records the result (`refused`, `dry-run`, `failed`, `applied`, `verification-failed`), including the claimed addresses; the output names counts, ids and files, never an address. A failing file names itself and the earlier files stay applied (each file is idempotent; run apply again).
- **2026-10-04 · The preflight (`src/core/preflight.ts`) is core code over a read-only query.** The CLI runs it through `wrangler d1 execute --command`; the integration suite runs the same function on a D1 with every migration. It refuses: migrations below 0012 (`d1_migrations`; a missing table is "none", any other read error fails apply); maintenance not `enabled` on staging or production; an emitted slug held by a native row (`legacy_id` NULL, unless `--native-catalog`) or by another legacy id; an emitted share token or Mollie id on a row with another id; an emitted address whose account another legacy id claimed (task 7 review M-2); an import that leaves no admin (D1's admins with no ban in force, plus admins the plan inserts; a claimed account keeps its own role); and, on staging, any address that is not `@staging.invalid` (B2 again, even when a manifest was edited to say staging). It lists the accounts it claims (`claim`) or that already hold their legacy id (`present`), and reports a claimed role that differs from Convex's without changing it. Lists go into `json_each` in chunks of 300 values.
- **2026-10-04 · The verification compares the report's counts with D1's imported rows.** Imported rows are found by `legacy_id` (users, categories, gestures, sponsorships) or through them (keywords, links, FTS rows, favorites, lists, items, shares, payments, items, sponsors, invoice requests, events), plus `consent_event.source = 'import'` and `audit_log.action = 'legacy'`. Equal is `ok`; more is `more` (a note: rows the site added since, or a claimed account's own lists); fewer is `missing` and exits 1. `sponsorship_token` is not verified: a staging plan drops tokens and keeps its counts equal to production's, so no report count says how many were written.
- **2026-10-04 · `--native-catalog` (dev and staging).** The native gestures and categories whose slugs the plan uses (found by the preflight) are deleted by id, and the `gesture_fts` rows of those gestures and of native gestures in a deleted category are rebuilt. A native gesture with a sponsorship fails the file on its RESTRICT key, loudly; favorites and list items of a deleted native gesture go with it (cascade), which is acceptable on staging only. It runs only with `--reset`, and dev's seeded catalogue (`mama`, `papa`, `eten`, `familie`, `dieren`) needs it for the fixture.
- **2026-10-04 · The reset keeps every imported admin when no other admin exists.** The task 5 statement deletes an imported admin only while an admin outside the deleted set remains, so with only imported admins it keeps all of them (with `legacy_id` NULL), not just one; a re-apply claims them again by address and gives the same counts (tested).
- **2026-10-04 · The fixture's sponsorship gestures and admin log are part of the export.** `sponsorship-gestures.jsonl` (11 gestures) and `sponsorship-admin-logs.jsonl` (sp15's `mark_paid_manually`) are merged into `export/gestures` and `export/adminLogs`; the catalogue transform tests pin their rules on task 7's four gestures, and the account test counts the ninth audit row.
- **2026-10-04 · `@orpc/server` is a dev dependency of `@smog/migrate-convex` (deviation: task 1 declared the package's dependencies).** The integration suite calls the lists, favorites and sponsorships routers through oRPC's `call`, as each feature's own tests do; the package's runtime is unchanged. `bun.lock` gains that one line. `tsconfig.test.json` gains `jsx: react-jsx` (the suite reaches `@smog/email`'s templates). knip no longer ignores the workspace dependencies the suite now uses.
- **2026-10-04 · The integration suite (`test/integration/{harness,apply,catalogue,sponsorship-services}.test.ts`).** The fixture is planned in process with every input and applied as `apply --env dev` does (preflight, files, `bumpCatalogVersion`). The pre-existing admin is made with `admin:grant --create` for a fixture address whose Convex role is `user` (the stand-in for the dev seed's admin: the seed's own address is not in the fixture). The Mollie webhook is driven at the service level (`settleFromMollie` + `handlePaymentSettled`, what `handleMollieWebhook` calls), since the site's handler is not importable from a package. The re-edit link's `new Date()` is pinned to `--now` with Vitest's fake `Date`, because the fixture's token expires on 2026-10-08.
- **2026-10-04 · Old `/lists/<convexListId>` links, the default list's shares and edit links with editing off answer not found** (task 7, restated): the suite proves the view and edit tokens of shared lists keep working.
- **2026-10-04 · Task 10 review fixes.**
  - **I-1:** the preflight reads the maintenance key with the site's own parser (`parseMaintenanceSetting(raw)?.enabled === true`, `@smog/config/maintenance`), so a value the site treats as off (for example `{"enabled":true}` with no `bypassVersion`) is refused as `maintenanceOff`.
  - **I-2:** `preflight.json` (hashed in the manifest) now carries `plan: { target, blockers, reportSha256 }`. `apply` refuses, before any D1 or KV write: a manifest whose target or blocker count disagrees with it, a missing `report.json`, and a `report.json` whose hash, target or blocker count differs. The target guard and the blocker refusal then read the hashed facts; the verification uses the checked report. The staging address check stays as a second guard.
  - **M-2's sibling:** an emitted address on an unclaimed account whose legacy id is already on another row is refused up front (`legacyIdOnOtherAddress`), instead of failing on `UNIQUE(legacy_id)` mid-file.
  - **M-4:** on production, a table holding more rows than the report prints a `WARNING` line (still exit 0) and is recorded as `more`.
  - **M-5:** `apply-report.json` lists each file as it completes (`filesApplied`) and is `files_applied` before the catalogue bump and the verification.
  - **M-3:** USAGE says a dry run writes nothing to D1 or KV, only its local reports in `--out`.
  - **M-6:** the preflight chunk stays at 300 values: D1 refuses statements over 100 KB, and 1000 addresses of up to 254 characters could exceed it.
  - **M-1:** the suite resubmits sp07, the migrated `changes_requested` row, with its old raw token (`Date` pinned to `--now`): `rendering`, one render job, the token used, and the trail `legacy`, `resubmitted`, `render_started`.

## Hardening, the runbooks and the owner sequence (phase 8 task 11)

- **2026-10-19 · The runbooks and where they live (carries 5, 7, 9, 10, 13, 14, 18, 21, 23, 27 and the task 1 to 10 review carries).** `docs/deployment.md` (first-time setup per env) and `docs/cutover-runbook.md` (the domain decision, the timeline, the importer's commands, the rollback) are new; `docs/PROGRESS.md` holds the 12 owner steps in order and closes the 27 carries point by point; AGENTS.md gains `migrate:convex`, `check-deploy-config`, `SMOG_REQUIRE_SECRETS`, the four-label placeholder rule and the CSP endpoint; API.md and DATA_MODEL.md got the endpoint, the passthrough format, `admin:grant --create` and the migrated-data rules; spec §14 and §15 carry marked amendments (rulings 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 11, 14, 15, 18) and the importer's path. The reference branch's `docs/RELEASE.md` was not available to the session, so the style is this repository's own. Every command and flag was read from the code, run with `--help` or `--dry-run`, or run locally against the dev D1 and the Mux fake; the remaining names that only a dashboard can confirm are marked "(dashboard)" in `docs/deployment.md`.
- **2026-10-19 · The Mux production webhook secret exists before T-0, as a second endpoint.** `MUX_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is a required production secret, so the first production deploy (T-1) fails the check without it, but the production Mux environment's only webhook points at the old system until T-0 step 10. The runbook adds a **second** webhook endpoint for `<production SITE_URL>/api/webhooks/mux` at T-1 and removes the old one at step 10. It ignores the old app's events (their passthroughs are neither `gesture-upload:` nor `render-job:<env>:`). If the dashboard allows one endpoint per environment, the fallback is a random placeholder secret at T-1 (every delivery then answers 400) and the real one at step 10. Alternative rejected: making the secret optional in production (the check exists to stop that).
- **2026-10-19 · No early import; the reviewer gets demo gestures; the importer has no delta.** The tool is insert-only (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) and production refuses `--reset`, so an early import followed by a final one would leave every row changed in between stale, and the old system would have to stay frozen until cutover. The runbook therefore forbids it, and gives the app reviewer content another way: a few demo gestures created in the admin with names that do not exist in Convex, deleted before `apply` (the preflight refuses a native slug on production, and `--native-catalog` is dev and staging only), and a member account (not the `--create` admin, which shows the Admin menu). Path W's time-boxed review window is maintenance off for the days of the review. Alternative rejected: a catalogue-only import mode (a new feature, outside this task). The consequence for the owner is stated in the runbook: the app review sees demo content, not the migrated catalogue.
- **2026-10-19 · The maintenance bypass cookie is fetched from the browser console.** During a window every page but `/sign-in` answers 503, so a signed-in admin cannot reach the admin settings page's bypass card until they hold a cookie, and there is no button on the 503 page. The runbook gives the console call (`fetch("/api/maintenance/bypass", { method: "POST" })`, same-origin, admin session, 12 h) and notes that the endpoint also works while maintenance is off, so a cookie can be fetched just before a window. A button on the sign-in or 503 page would be a site change (and a CSP, i18n and test surface), so it is left as a possible later change; the console call is what the e2e does too.
- **2026-10-19 · `apply` has no reset-only mode, and `--reset` does not relax the preflight.** *[Amended (phase 9 task 11): phase 9 task 2 added `apply --reset-only` and a preflight that leaves out the plan's own rows under `--reset` (see "Tooling carries: importer reset-only, `we-moved --resend`, the turbo deploy path (phase 9 task 2)"); another export's rows still block.]* `--reset` deletes what this plan imports (`reset-imported-*.sql`) and then applies it again, and the preflight runs **first**: against an earlier import of another export (or of the same export for another target) it refuses with `slugTakenByOtherImport` or `legacyIdOnOtherAddress`, even with `--reset`. Run locally: a production-target plan onto a dev D1 holding a staging-target import was refused, and running the staging plan's own `reset-imported-001.sql` with `wrangler d1 execute --local --file` first made the production plan apply (and claim the `admin:grant --create` account). The runbook documents that manual route (task 10's carry "reset with the OLD plan folder first, then apply the new plan" means the old plan's reset *file*, not `apply --reset`, which would re-apply the old plan). A reset-only flag, or a preflight that accounts for `--reset`, is a phase 9 candidate (PROGRESS, Known gaps); it was not changed here because the guard's semantics are task 10's reviewed ones.
- **2026-10-19 · Path W forwards the Mollie webhook by proxy, not by redirect.** Whether Mollie follows a redirect on a webhook call is not verified, so the runbook proxies `/webhooks/mollie` on the old host (a Caddy `reverse_proxy` with `header_up Host {upstream_hostport}`) and redirects everything else (QR codes, share links and re-edit links need the query string kept). The safety net if a webhook is lost anyway: the hourly stale sweep asks Mollie about every open payment, and `paymentStatus` re-fetches an `open` payment when its return page opens.
- **2026-10-19 · The local end-to-end rehearsal (carry from the brief; the proof for P-40 and E-13).** From a clean dev D1 (`rm -rf apps/site/.wrangler/state/v3/d1`, `migrate:dev`, no seed): `admin:grant --env dev --create ada.fixture@example.test`; `plan --target staging` (0 blockers, 13 warnings, with the WorkOS, Mux map and overrides fixtures); `apply --env dev` (every one of the 18 verified counts `ok`, `catalog:version` bumped); `apply --env dev --reset` (the same 18 counts `ok`); `mux scan` against a Mux fake seeded with every fixture playback id (35 of 35 found, 13 old preview assets counted, 15 gesture assets with no rendition), `mux renditions --apply` (15 requested, a ledger written); `we-moved --env dev` refused (exit 2, "production only"). Two observations. (1) A **staging** plan pseudonymises addresses, so it cannot claim the `admin:grant --create` account (the preflight said "0 existing accounts will be claimed"); the claim was exercised by applying a **production-target** plan to the dev D1 after wiping the staging import with its reset file ("1 existing account will be claimed", every count `ok`). (2) `seed:dev` first would have collided with the native slugs (`mama`, `papa`, `eten`, `familie`, `dieren`), which is why the rehearsal skips the seed and the runbook's staging rehearsal documents `--native-catalog`. The full site e2e then ran on a fresh D1 (`rm -rf … d1`, `migrate:dev`, `seed:dev`), because the imported D1 collides with the seed's slugs; the result is in the task report.
- **2026-10-19 · knip: the importer's `src/cli/main.ts` entry is gone.** knip finds it from the package's `cli` script, and printed "Remove redundant entry pattern" for the explicit one; removing it leaves knip clean. `@smog/email`, `@smog/jobs`, `@smog/video`, `@smog/admin`, `@smog/favorites`, `@smog/lists` and the task 7 entries were already dropped from `ignoreDependencies` by task 10; only `cloudflare` (the generated types package every Worker-pool package ignores) remains for the importer. `@smog/account` was removed from the importer's dependencies in the interrupted first commit of this task (nothing imported it), so no ignore is needed for it.
- **2026-10-19 · Personal data: proposed deletion dates.** The export ZIP and the WorkOS file go at **T+7 days** (after the verification and the rollback window), `out/` after `we-moved` finished and its 7-day KV marker window passed and at the latest at the alias removal (**T+30 days**), since `we-moved` must run from the cutover `out/` (its ledger lives there). These are proposals for the owner to confirm in the runbook; the old Convex and WorkOS data are deleted by closing those services once the owner is satisfied.
- **2026-10-19 · Open questions for the owner that the runbook states rather than assumes.** (a) Mollie may not accept a workers.dev website for the profile, which would force path D; (b) Google's consent screen wants authorised domains on a domain the owner holds (`workers.dev` is a public suffix); (c) Apple's console may ask for a domain verification file (it can be added under `apps/site/public/.well-known/`); (d) the Email Service onboarding may need the `smog.vlaanderen` zone on Cloudflare DNS (which path D needs anyway); (e) the token names for Containers and Workflows stay unconfirmed until the first flip. None of these could be settled without credentials.
- **2026-10-19 · No check or test assumes either origin path (ruling 2's constraint, proved).** Three script tests hard-coded production's workers.dev host while reading the real `wrangler.jsonc` and `eas.json` (`release-config-check.test.ts` for the EAS profile and the launch origin, `eas-update.test.ts` for the production profile's env, `ensure-cloudflare-resources.test.ts` for the bucket's CORS origin), so the prepared domain commit would have failed 13 tests. They now derive the origin from the files (`PRODUCTION_LAUNCH_ORIGIN`, the parsed `SITE_URL`, the profile's own keys), and the tests that need a different origin use `https://elsewhere.zias.workers.dev`. Proof: with the domain diff applied (`SITE_URL`, `eas.json`'s production URL and host, `PRODUCTION_LAUNCH_ORIGIN`, a `custom_domain` route and `workers_dev: true`) `bun test scripts`, the mobile `app-config` and `api` tests and the importer's `we-moved` test pass, `release-config-check` is `ok` with no warning, and `CLOUDFLARE_ENV=production SMOG_RENDER_PIPELINE=1 bun run deploy --dry-run` in `apps/site` accepts the route (the built `wrangler.json` has `routes` and `workers_dev`). The diff was reverted; the runbook documents it. Alternative: leave the tests as they were and list them in the domain commit (rejected: a path-specific test is the kind of hidden assumption ruling 2 forbids).
- **2026-10-19 · Staging's deployed config is unchanged across the whole of phase 8.** `CLOUDFLARE_ENV=staging bun -F @smog/site deploy:dry` builds `dist/server/wrangler.json`; with `configPath` and `userConfigPath` dropped and the keys sorted, the file from `7c5dedf` (installed and built from a `git archive` in a scratch folder, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`) and the one from this task's tree have the same sha256, `25dcbfed502d449a23c7dbf326f4c9700a2e893d9004ea65dee031abfdd51e50` (the same figure tasks 7 and 10 recorded). Unstripped, this tree's file is `dde9080e…14398`, the same as the build at `984e836` (the first commit of this task, itself `68ccc9a` plus tests and a lockfile line) before any change of this task. This task changes no file under `apps/` or `packages/` (it adds docs, three script tests and a root `knip.json` entry change).
- **2026-10-19 · `bun audit` found four advisories published after the last task's run (a dependency change in a docs task, by necessity).** `SMOG_OFFLINE=1 bun run release:check` failed at `audit` with: GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w (sharp < 0.35.5, high, librsvg), GHSA-68fv-2mgg-jv7q (source-map-js 1.0.0 to 1.2.1, high, event-loop DoS through source-map section offsets), GHSA-rj75-hqrm-r3gf (postcss-selector-parser < 7.1.6, moderate, quadratic parsing) and GHSA-hp3w-g68c-fv3c (sprintf-js <= 1.1.3, moderate, DoS through unbounded precision). Handled like the undici advisory: **root `overrides` where a fixed patch release exists** (`sharp` 0.35.5, which `@smog/brand` already uses and which miniflare pinned at 0.35.4, and `source-map-js` 1.2.2: patch bumps of build tooling; the lockfile loses the nested `miniflare/sharp` copy and gains the two pins, nothing else re-resolved, `--frozen-lockfile` is clean); and **`bun run audit` ignores the other two**: postcss-selector-parser 7.1.6 is a major version and the old copy (6.1.4) is what Tailwind 3 under NativeWind and `@remotion/bundler` use, so forcing 7 could change how the mobile styles compile (the CSS it parses is ours); sprintf-js 1.1.3 is the newest release (no fixed version exists) and reaches the lockfile only through Expo, Jest and TanStack Start tooling that formats our own strings. Revisit both ignores when Tailwind or Remotion move to selector-parser 7, or sprintf-js publishes a fix. The rule "only task 1 changes `bun.lock`" was written before these advisories existed; the gate must be green, so this task changes it.
- **2026-10-19 · Fix round: the R2 jurisdiction option is withdrawn, the Mux webhook endpoint moves to step 4, the new overrides get a removal note.** (1) An R2 bucket with `--jurisdiction eu` cannot work today: `packages/features/sponsorships/src/server/logo.ts` hard-codes `<account>.r2.cloudflarestorage.com` for the presigned PUT (the CSP `connect-src` follows it) and `ensure-cloudflare-resources.ts` lists buckets without a jurisdiction, so it would create a second default bucket. `docs/deployment.md` keeps only `--location` hints for the bucket and `--jurisdiction eu` for D1 (no code depends on it); the endpoint-from-config and `-J` change is a phase 9 candidate. (2) The production Mux webhook endpoint (URL = the production `SITE_URL`, known before any deploy) and its signing secret are created in PROGRESS step 4, before the secrets of step 5; "T-1" wording is gone (the day-before step is "production back under maintenance"). (3) The `sharp` and `source-map-js` root `overrides` are removed when the dependents that pin the old versions (miniflare for sharp; postcss, Tailwind and react-email tooling for source-map-js) resolve the fixed releases on their own (check with `bun why <name>` after a `bun update`), like the undici override. (4) The retention dry run's rejected-video count is an upper bound: the purge only unlinks an asset that a gesture or a running sponsorship still uses; the label now says so.
- **2026-10-19 · Mobile builds share one identity (phase 8 review I-3).** *[Amended (phase 9 task 11): superseded by `APP_VARIANT` builds in phase 9 task 3: the `development` and `staging` profiles now have their own bundle ids and schemes; production is unchanged (see "Mobile: app variants, the accessibility pass and the sign-out confirmation (phase 9 task 3)").]* The `development`, `staging` and `production` EAS profiles all build `be.zias.smog` (iOS bundle identifier, Android package), the `smog` scheme and the name "SMOG & Co"; only the API origin and host differ (`apps/mobile/app.config.ts`, `eas.json`). This is **deliberate for now**, not an oversight. Why it is acceptable: (1) the only builds that reach a device are internal-distribution tests by the owner and a few testers, and the production app is not in the stores yet at the time of writing; (2) one identifier keeps one Apple Services/App ID registration, so staging's `APPLE_APP_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER` and the universal-link association work without a second App ID, a second AASA entry or a second provisioning profile; (3) a variant costs more than its lines of code: a second bundle id needs its own Apple App ID with Sign in with Apple, Associated Domains and a push/provisioning setup, its own Play listing or package for the Digital Asset Links of the staging host, and the store review forms stay on the real id only. The cost: a staging or development build installed on a device **replaces the store app** there and keeps its keychain and storage, so a signed-in production session can start talking to staging (and `smog://` links open whichever is installed). Rules until then: test builds go on devices without the store app (or accept the reinstall and sign out first), and `docs/deployment.md` §15 says so. The `development` profile points at `http://localhost:5173`, so it works in a simulator or emulator only; on a physical device run the app locally with `expo run` and an `apps/mobile/.env` with the LAN address (an EAS profile's `env` beats a local `.env`). **The alternative, an `APP_VARIANT` env set per `eas.json` profile that suffixes `ios.bundleIdentifier`, `android.package`, `scheme` and `name` for `development` and `staging` (and `associatedDomains`/intent filters follow `host`),** is a phase 9 carry, to be done before testers or the owner regularly run staging builds next to the store app, or as soon as the store app exists on a tester's phone. It needs the matching Apple App ID, the staging `APPLE_APP_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER` secret, the Associated Domains entry and a `release-config-check` rule, which is why it is not squeezed into the fix wave.
- **2026-10-19 · A missing Cloudflare token fails the production deploy (phase 8 review I-4).** `deploy.yml`'s "Check Cloudflare secrets" step still skips staging with a warning when `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` or `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` is empty (the staging environment may legitimately lack them), but exits 1 for `CLOUDFLARE_ENV=production`: a green Deploy job that deployed nothing is how the owner would later run `admin:grant` against a Worker that does not exist. `scripts/release-config-check.ts` (`checkSecretsStep`) holds the workflow to it, with a test that the real file passes and a copy without the block fails.
- **2026-10-19 · The cutover's data rollback is D1 Time Travel (phase 8 review I-1), and `we-moved` has no replay tool yet (I-2).** *[Amended (phase 9 task 11): `we-moved --resend` (tool-side failures) and the admin dead-letter replay (consumer-side failures) now exist, so "no replay tool yet" no longer holds (see the phase 9 task 2 and task 4 sections).]* Production refuses `apply --reset` and the hand-run `reset-imported-*.sql` can fail part-way, so the runbook (§6 step 6, §10) records a Time Travel bookmark (`wrangler d1 time-travel info`) before `apply` and names `wrangler d1 time-travel restore --bookmark` as the rollback, with what it does not undo (KV, R2, Mux, queued and sent emails). `wrangler d1 export` cannot export a database with a virtual table (`gesture_fts`), so the export is an optional `--table` copy, not the anchor. `we-moved`'s ledger records *queued*, not *delivered* (the consumer retries 5 times, then the DLQ, which has no consumer), so the runbook adds a hard gate before the store release (the email queue backlog and DLQ at 0, by dashboard: `wrangler queues info` prints no backlog) and a manual replay route; a `--resend` command is a phase 9 carry, because the replay's input is the DLQ (reachable only through an HTTP pull consumer on the Queues API, which could not be exercised without credentials) and a tool that clears ledger entries it cannot check against delivery is not "small and safe". The consumer's `email:sent:<key>` markers (written on success only, 7 days) are what make a hand replay safe. **Amended (phase 9 task 4):** the statement that the DLQ is "reachable only through an HTTP pull consumer" no longer holds and is withdrawn. A DLQ is an ordinary queue and takes one **push** consumer like any other: phase 9 attaches the site Worker as the consumer of `smog-<env>-email-dlq` and `smog-<env>-sponsorship-events-dlq` (`wrangler.jsonc`), which stores each message in D1 `dead_letter`, so the DLQ is no longer a place nobody looks and the admin screen can replay or discard. The earlier worry was a *pull* consumer on the Queues HTTP API (an API token as a Worker secret, a poll, and nothing that could be exercised without credentials); a push consumer needs none of that and is exercised by the Workers-pool tests with `createMessageBatch`. It is also preferred over polling the API from a cron for three reasons: no token and no new secret on the Worker, delivery is event-driven and retried by the platform (`max_retries` 20, `retry_delay` 300 s) instead of on the next poll, and the same `queue()` handler, dispatch and tests as the other two consumers apply. The `we-moved` replay route is now the admin replay screen (`we_moved` carries no token or code, and has an idempotency key, so it is replayable); the importer's own ledger side stays with Task 2's `we-moved --resend`. The gate "the DLQ at 0, by dashboard" becomes "no open dead letters" on the dashboard card.
- **2026-10-19 · The importer scrubs wrangler's failure text (phase 8 review M-5).** `failureReason()` masks quoted fragments (`"…"`, `'…'`, backticks) and address-like words in the line it forwards, because a D1 syntax or constraint error can quote part of the SQL, which may hold an address, and runbook §9 promises the importer never logs one.

## Playwright e2e as a CI job (phase 9 task 1)

- **2026-10-19 · E2E is an always-on CI job, five independent shards (ruling 1).** `ci.yml` job `e2e` has no `if:` and no `needs:`, so it runs beside the four `release-check` lanes and Deploy's reusable call of `ci.yml` waits for it unchanged. Matrix `shard: [public, a11y, admin, admin-a11y, serial]`, each a Playwright project with no `dependencies`, each on its own runner with its own dev server and D1 (`bun run test:e2e -- --project <shard>`). `public` is the old `chromium` (every spec not matched below: later `perf`/`routes-parity` specs land here), `a11y` is `a11y-*.spec.ts` (created empty so Task 5 never edits the config; the runner passes `--pass-with-no-tests` for it only), `admin` is `admin-*.spec.ts` minus the next two, `admin-a11y` is `admin-*a11y*.spec.ts`, `serial` is `admin-catalog` then `admin-settings` (alphabetical on one worker: the 100-gesture bulk smoke, then the maintenance window; the old `catalog` and `maintenance` projects). The `E2E_ENABLED` placeholder is gone. **Deviation from the plan text: a fifth shard, `admin-a11y`.** The planned `admin` shard took 18 minutes on a loaded machine, 13 of them in the two axe specs (4 + 4 runs of every admin screen, light and dark, 390 and 1280 px); the rule was "over 15 minutes locally is split", so they became their own project. `bun run test:e2e` (`scripts/e2e.ts`) runs the projects one after another, each as its own `bun -F @smog/site test:e2e` command (which carries its own `test-deadline.ts` wrapper, so the 25 minutes are per project and a full local run does not trip it); `-- --project <name>` runs one; `--keep-going` continues past a failing project. A test keeps the config's project names and the runner's list equal.
- **2026-10-19 · The Chromium comes from Playwright, not the sandbox.** CI runs `bunx playwright install --with-deps chromium` (`PLAYWRIGHT_SKIP_BROWSER_DOWNLOAD` is `"0"` for that step; the workflow-level `1` stays for the release lanes) with `~/.cache/ms-playwright` cached by `playwright --version`. `playwright.config.ts` still launches `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium` by path only when it exists (the sandbox's Chromium 141 is older than the one this Playwright expects). `reuseExistingServer` stays `!CI`: a port in use fails loudly in CI. The Mux and Mollie fakes and the always-pass Turnstile keys are unchanged; `RENDER_MODE` is `fake`; no secret is needed.
- **2026-10-19 · Flake policy (ruling 2).** `retries: 1` in CI with `trace: "on-first-retry"`, `0` locally. The CI reporter writes `playwright-report/results.json`; `scripts/e2e-flaky-report.ts` (an `always()` step) lists every test that passed only on its retry as a `::warning::` annotation and a job-summary table, and does not fail the job. A flaky pass is a bug to fix. `test-results/` and the report are uploaded on failure (7 days). The shards rely on the deadline wrapper and the 30-minute job timeout; no Vitest stall reporter is involved (see "CI test hang").
- **2026-10-19 · No sleeps.** The six `waitForTimeout(` calls are gone, each replaced by the condition it stood for: the screenshot settles (`settleForShot`: fonts ready, every image decoded or failed, two animation frames), the analytics "withdrawn in another tab" wait (`expectStorageEvent`: a `storage` event listener registered before the other tab writes, run after the app's own, so the "nothing is sent" assertion starts from a settled state) and the account switch's wait (`networkidle` after the click). `scripts/e2e.test.ts` fails when `waitForTimeout(` appears anywhere under `apps/site/e2e`. Two more flake fixes found by the repeated runs: `blockingViolations` waits (bounded, finite animations only) for transitions to end before an axe scan (a button fading in read as `color-contrast` once), and the sponsor keyboard test registers its `filechooser` listener before the Tab walk (it lost the event once under load; it passed on retry in one full run and not in isolation, so this is a hardening rather than a proven root cause: if it shows up in the flaky report, look there first).
- **2026-10-19 · [dep] Playwright through the catalog: `@playwright/test` 1.64.1 (newest stable, up from 1.63.0), `@axe-core/playwright` stays at 4.13.0.** Both are `"catalog:"` in `apps/site`. `@axe-core/playwright` 4.14.0 is the newest, but its updated axe-core reports `label-content-name-mismatch` (serious) on a Radix trigger in the admin shell on every admin screen, which is a finding for Task 5's accessibility pass, not a regression to wave over here. The catalog keeps 4.13.0 until Task 5 fixes the control and bumps it.
- **Skips (6, all deliberate, none hides a failure).** `ADMIN_SHOTS_DIR` unset: `admin-screenshots` ("every admin screen"), `admin-sponsorships-a11y` ("review screenshots"), `admin-settings` (the maintenance screenshots); `SPONSOR_SHOTS_DIR` unset: `sponsor.spec` ("review screenshots"); `csp.spec` ×2: no `TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` in `.dev.vars` (the widget test) and no `SCALAR_STANDALONE_JS` (the real-bundle test). Counts: 130 passed, 6 skipped.
- **Measured here (`CI=1`, `E2E_WORKERS=1`, fresh D1, three consecutive full passes, no flaky test and no failure in any of them, the last on the final code):** public 10.7 to 11.6 min, a11y 0 (empty), admin 4.2 to 4.4 min, admin-a11y 14.2 to 14.6 min, serial 3.7 to 3.8 min; a full local run about 34 to 35 minutes. Expected on CI (x1.5): public about 17, admin about 7, admin-a11y about 22, serial about 6 minutes: inside the 30-minute job timeout, but `admin-a11y` is above the 15-minute line on CI; Task 5 re-measures after wave B and splits it by theme if it stays above (the four admin axe runs are one test per theme and width, so a split is a `grep` away).
