The slowest tests: four `admin-a11y.spec.ts` axe runs at 2.2 min each, `sponsor.spec.ts` axe states 1.4 min, `admin-catalog` create/rename flow 1.1 min, four `admin-sponsorships-a11y` axe runs 0.9 to 1.0 min. The machine was shared with other sessions at load 3 to 18; the first full runs under load 15 failed on 60 s timeouts alone, which is why the number to trust is the unloaded one above.
- **Workers: 1 in CI.** Measured on `admin-a11y`: 9.6 min with `E2E_WORKERS=2` against 14.4 with one, but one test passed only on its retry (`admin-a11y.spec.ts` "the row menu opens the QR dialog by keyboard; Escape returns focus to the menu button", a focus assertion), which one worker never showed in three full passes. So the default stays 1 (the dev server compiles lazily and the specs share one D1); two workers need that test fixed and a clean CI run first (`E2E_WORKERS` overrides).
- **2026-10-19 · Review fixes (task 1).** (I-1) The e2e job's `timeout-minutes` is 45 and each shard's command has an explicit `SMOG_TEST_DEADLINE_MINUTES: "35"`, so a hung test is killed by the deadline wrapper (process tree report) and the artifact step still runs; the upload is `if: always()`, which also keeps the traces of a pass-on-retry (ruling 2). `admin-a11y` was not split by theme: 14.5 minutes here is under the 15-minute line and the 45/35 limits leave room for the x1.5 CI estimate (22 min) plus 3 to 6 minutes of setup; Task 5 re-measures. `checkE2eJob` enforces the 45 cap, a deadline at least 5 minutes below it, the flaky-report step, an `always()` upload, the browser-download override and the wrapper on `apps/site`'s `test:e2e`. (I-2) `settleForShot` skips offscreen lazy images and is bounded at 5 s; `e2e/helpers.spec.ts` (not skipped, `public`) exercises it. (I-3) The runner emits `--project=<name>` after the user's arguments, so `-- admin-settings` works, and passes `--pass-with-no-tests` whenever the user gave arguments. Local runs use `trace: retain-on-failure`. (turbo) `turbo.json` has no `test:e2e` task: the root script is a plain Bun script that needs the dev server and a D1 in the environment, which a cached turbo task would misrepresent, and `cache: false` would buy nothing over a script. (axe pin) The `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 pin is lifted by Task 5, once the Radix trigger `label-content-name-mismatch` is fixed.

## Tooling carries: importer reset-only, `we-moved --resend`, the turbo deploy path (phase 9 task 2)

- **2026-10-19 · `apply --reset-only` (ruling 10).** `apply --env <dev|staging> --out <plan> --reset-only [--dry-run]` checks the plan folder and the target guard exactly as `apply` does, then runs the plan's own `reset-imported-*.sql` and nothing else. Its preflight (`runResetOnlyPreflight`) is the connectivity of the migrations query, migrations at 0012 or later, and, on staging, maintenance on (kept from `apply`'s preflight: it is a destructive run on a live site, and the runbook's rehearsal already has maintenance on); none of the foreign-row checks, which would fail on the very rows being removed. Production refuses it with the message `--reset` has; `--reset` with `--reset-only` and `--native-catalog` with `--reset-only` are refused as contradictions. It prints, per table, the imported rows before and after (the same counts `verify` uses, so they match the plan's report) and the total removed; a claimed account keeps its row and loses its legacy id, and the last admin stays, both as in the reset file. A second run removes 0. **It also bumps `catalog:version`** (a deliberate step beyond "nothing else"): the reset deletes gestures and categories, and `apply --reset` bumps after its re-apply for the same reason, so a staging site must not keep serving the deleted catalogue from its caches. The dry run prints the bump with the reset files. The record (`apply-report.json`) gets `resetOnly` and a status `reset_done`.
- **2026-10-19 · `--reset` makes the preflight skip the plan's own rows (ruling 10).** `ownRowsOf` reads the plan's `reset-imported-*.sql` back (the files are hash-checked against the manifest before it runs; it parses only the `json_each('<json>')` lists of the tables the checks read, and fails loudly on a statement it cannot read) and the preflight then leaves out, with `--reset` only: a gesture or category holding a planned slug by an id the reset deletes; a share token or Mollie id held by a `list_share` or `payment` id the reset deletes; and the legacy ids the reset sets free, so an account that holds one of the plan's own legacy ids under another address is neither "claimed by another legacy id" nor a blocker for the plan's claim of its address. A row of any other source, **another export's import included, still blocks**, with or without `--reset`: the reset deletes by this plan's keys, so it could not remove it, and wiping another export needs that export's own plan folder (`--reset-only` with it). Tested in the Workers-pool suite and in the Bun CLI suite with a second fixture (the same export with every Convex id and Mollie id changed, `test/other-export.ts`): A's rows are ignored for A's `--reset` and block B, B's `--reset-only` removes none of A, and after A's own `--reset-only` B applies. Alternative rejected: adding the reset keys to `preflight.json` (it would change every plan's bytes and hashes for something the hash-checked reset files already say). Note: a plan of another export that shares keys with what is applied (the same Mollie payments) deletes those shared rows by its reset, and the delete fails on a foreign key when something of the other plan still references them: that is the existing loud, part-way failure of the reset (runbook M6), not a new one.
- **2026-10-19 · `we-moved --resend` (ruling 11).** `we-moved --env production --out <dir> --resend <ids-file>` or `--resend --failed`, dry without `--apply`. The ids file holds one ledger key (a user id) per line (`#` comments and blank lines allowed; a line that is not a plain id, an address included, is a usage error that names the line number, never the line). `--failed` selects the keys in the new `we-moved-failed.json`: a run whose Queues API call fails records the failed batch (`failed`) and every later batch (`not-sent`), and a key leaves the file once it is queued, so a successful plain re-run clears it too. A selected key is skipped when the consumer's marker `email:sent:we_moved:<id>` exists in production's KV (delivered; the output names the skipped ids), reported when it is not a current recipient, and otherwise sent through the same batches, rate and message as the first run (same idempotency key, a new message id). **The old ledger entry is replaced when the new batch is accepted, not cleared before**: a failure mid-way never loses the old entry, and a dry run changes no file. Ids only in every output, no address. Delivery failures that exhaust the consumer's retries and reach the email DLQ are not handled here: the admin dead-letter replay of Task 4 does that (the command's help says so). Cost: the marker check is one `wrangler kv key get` per selected key, so a large id list is slow; the list is expected to be a handful. The runbook is Task 11's.
- **2026-10-19 · The turbo bypass (ruling 12).** `bun -F @smog/site deploy` is now `turbo run build --filter=@smog/site^... && vite build && bun scripts/deploy-guard.ts && wrangler deploy --env $CLOUDFLARE_ENV`, and `deploy:dry:render` (the gate-on dry run, which has its own chain) starts the same way; `deploy:dry` calls `deploy`, so `release:check:core` runs turbo from `apps/site`. With turbo 2.11.7 the step finds no `build` task in any dependency (no workspace package has a build script), prints "No tasks were executed" and exits 0, and keeps working the day one gets a build. Turbo's strict environment mode would hide `CLOUDFLARE_ENV` from such a build task; the deploy's own steps read the shell. The staging `deploy:dry` hash is unchanged (`25dcbfed…`). `apps/site/scripts/deploy-script.test.ts` proves the order of the steps in the three scripts and that the turbo step exits 0.

## Mobile: app variants, the accessibility pass and the sign-out confirmation (phase 9 task 3)

- **2026-10-19 · `APP_VARIANT` builds (phase 9 ruling 9; supersedes "Mobile builds share one identity").** The `development` and `staging` EAS profiles set `APP_VARIANT`; `apps/mobile/app.config.ts` takes the name, `ios.bundleIdentifier`, `android.package` and `scheme` from `variantIdentity()` (in the same file: Expo's config loader resolves no relative imports of local TypeScript, which the first version of this change, a `src/app-variant.ts`, hit in `expo export`) (`SMOG Dev` / `be.zias.smog.dev` / `smog-dev`, `SMOG Staging` / `be.zias.smog.staging` / `smog-staging`). An unset or empty value, and `production`, is the store identity **byte for byte**: `src/app-config-production.test.ts` pins the whole config with a snapshot written from the config *before* the change, and the same test checks that `APP_VARIANT=production` and `""` equal the unset one. An unknown value (`stagin`) **throws** rather than shipping under the store identity. A variant claims **no universal links**: no `ios.associatedDomains` and no `autoVerify` https intent filter (the AASA and `assetlinks.json` name the store identifiers only, and a second claimant would not verify); its custom-scheme intent filter uses the variant's scheme. `+native-intent` accepts the build's *own* scheme (`appScheme()` reads the resolved config's `scheme`, `src/lib/app-scheme.ts`) and no other app's, so a staging build does not take a `smog://` link meant for the store app; `https` site links and `exp://` map as before. The Better Auth client gets the same scheme (`createExpoAuthClient`'s `scheme` option widened from the literal `"smog"` to `string`; the only `@smog/auth` change besides `server.ts`).
- **Trusted origins.** `packages/auth/src/server.ts`: staging adds `smog-staging://`, dev adds `smog-dev://` (with `exp://`), production adds neither; `smog://` stays everywhere, so the store app can still be pointed at staging. One test per env (`auth.test.ts`). Runtime code only: the staging `wrangler.json` hash is unchanged.
- **Where the cross-checks live (deviation from the DECISIONS promise of a `release-config-check` rule).** The variant rules are in `scripts/mobile-release-check.ts` (`variantProblems`, run before the exports: each profile sets the variant that names it, production none; the three resolved identities differ in name, bundle id, package and scheme; only the store app claims links; the store identity is `be.zias.smog`/`smog`) and in `apps/mobile/src/app-config.test.ts`, because `scripts/release-config-check.ts` is Task 1's file in wave A. It can move there later. Two small edits outside the brief's file list were unavoidable and are minimal: `release-config-check.ts`'s `EAS_ENV_KEYS` allow-list gains `APP_VARIANT` (without it `release:check:core` refuses any key outside the three origin keys), and `scripts/eas-update.ts` copies `APP_VARIANT` from the profile into the update's env (empty for production, so a stale shell value cannot turn a production update into a variant's): the variant changes the native identity and so the `fingerprint` runtime version, and an OTA published without it would never match a variant's build.
- **"Open in the app" on the site stays on `smog://` and the store package on purpose** (`apps/site/src/lib/open-in-app.ts`, unchanged). It is a web page opening a *store* app: a staging site linking to `smog-staging://` would only help a tester who has the variant, and every other visitor would get a dead link; the variants are for the owner and testers, who open the page's universal link or paste the path. `APP_SCHEME`/`ANDROID_PACKAGE` in `@smog/config/constants` stay the store's.
- **The mobile accessibility pass (phase 9 ruling 3, P-30 mobile, P-25).** What a machine can hold is held by tests: `packages/ui-native/src/a11y.test.tsx` renders **every kit component** (`src/test/kit-cases.tsx`, shared with the contract test, whose "covers every exported component" check therefore covers it) and audits it with `src/test/a11y-audit.ts` (exported as `@smog/ui-native/a11y-audit`): every interactive host element (focusable, or a `TextInput`) that a screen reader can reach has an `accessibilityRole` (a text field needs none), an accessible name (label or text, or `accessibilityLabelledBy`) and a hit area of 44 pt (its own height plus `hitSlop`; the width when the style sets one, and an icon-only control must set one: Jest has no layout, so an auto width is the content's). The audit proves it can fail (a self-test with a bare `Pressable`, a 20 pt one and an unnamed one). The same file asserts state (`disabled`/`busy`, `checked` for checkbox, switch, radio, segmented, chip, favorite, `selected` for tabs, `expanded` for a select), `accessibilityViewIsModal` on dialogs, alert dialogs, sheets, selects and menus, live regions and announcements (toasts, the offline banner, an error state, a field error, search), reduced motion both ways, and large text. `apps/mobile/src/a11y.test.tsx` runs the same role-and-name audit over **every screen of `screens.test.tsx`** plus the account (guest and signed in) and sign-in screens. The route tests have no compiled Tailwind (a `className` is no style there), so hit areas in the app are held by `apps/mobile/src/a11y-source.test.ts`: no touchable but `Pressable`, the one raw `Pressable` (the list row's drag handle) has a role, a label and `min-h-touch`, no fixed heights except `Skeleton` and the Turnstile WebView, no font scaling switched off, no literal `numberOfLines`. `packages/ui-native/src/a11y-source.test.ts` does the same for the kit (fixed heights allowed only in files that hold no text, each with its reason; every file that animates asks `useReducedMotion`). Colour pairs stay `@smog/styles`' (contrast tests there).
- **Gaps the tests found, fixed.** (1) `ModalCard` (Dialog, AlertDialog) had no `accessibilityViewIsModal` ("the native Modal is its own window"); it is now on the modal's root view, which also holds the backdrop and the toast viewport, so VoiceOver stays inside the dialog and its toasts stay reachable (a flag on the card would hide the sibling toasts). (2) Search announced only the count, and only on iOS; "Searching…" and "No gestures found" were silent on Android and the empty state on both. `SearchResults` now announces all three on iOS and the first two on Android (the count there stays its polite live region, so it is not read twice). (3) `VideoPlayer` autoplayed (muted, looping) whatever the system said; with Reduce Motion on it no longer starts by itself (WCAG 2.2.2, inventory P-25) and is not muted, so whoever presses play hears it. (4) Text cut off with `numberOfLines={1}` (list rows, gesture cards and rows, a select's value, the sign-in email echo) lost its end at large text sizes; `useLineLimit` (`src/lib/line-limit.ts`, exported) drops the limit from a font scale of 1.3 so it wraps. The accessible label of those rows already carried the full text.
- **Reduce motion has one source: Reanimated's `useReducedMotion`.** It is the OS setting that `AccessibilityInfo.isReduceMotionEnabled` reports, but synchronous at first render (no async read, so a video cannot start before the answer arrives). The kit's Jest setup already mocks it, so tests switch it both ways (the brief names `AccessibilityInfo.isReduceMotionEnabled`; mocking that would not reach Reanimated's hook). The kit's only other motion, the system `ActivityIndicator`, is the platform's.
- **U-11: sign-out asks first.** `settings/account`'s "Sign out" opens a native `Alert` (title, message, Cancel, destructive Sign out; nl/en/fr in the new top-level `signOutConfirm` block, cancel is `kit.cancel`); Cancel does nothing, Sign out signs out, toasts and goes to the welcome screen. The new app has no `/welcome` route (the old app's `replace("/welcome")` became the home screen), so it is `router.replace("/")`; the analytics identity clears as before, when the session turns signed out (`AnalyticsBridge`). A failed sign-out toasts the generic error and stays. Four tests (the offered buttons, Cancel, Sign out, failure).
- **Not done here (owner actions).** A VoiceOver/TalkBack pass, dynamic type and high contrast on a device (Task 11 lists them). What the variants need from the owner: an Apple App ID per variant (`be.zias.smog.staging`, `be.zias.smog.dev`) with **Sign in with Apple** (and no Associated Domains: the variants claim no links), the staging site's **`APPLE_APP_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER` secret set to the audience of the builds that sign in against it** (a single value in Better Auth, `appBundleIdentifier`: `be.zias.smog.staging` while testers use the staging variant, which means the store build can no longer use Apple sign-in against staging; the development variant uses a local dev site and no secret; `check-deploy-config` checks the Apple trio is present, not its value), a Google OAuth client per Android package (SHA-1 of each variant's signing key) if Google sign-in is used on a variant, and EAS credentials (iOS provisioning profile and distribution certificate, Android keystore) per profile.
- **2026-10-19 · Variant caveats (phase 9 task 3 review).** (1) **Magic-link sign-in reaches only the store app on variants:** the emailed link is an https universal link (`/magic-link/app`) that the variants deliberately do not claim, and the web page's "Open in the app" uses `smog://`, so on a device with a variant the link opens the store app (or the browser). Testers of a variant sign in with a code, a password or a provider. (2) **A local `expo run:ios`/`run:android` without `APP_VARIANT` installs under the store identity** (`be.zias.smog`, `smog`, "SMOG & Co") and replaces the store app on that device; set `APP_VARIANT=development` for a dev build next to it. (3) The kit reads Reanimated's `useReducedMotion` once per render of each component; a setting changed while a screen is open applies from the next render or mount (the video's autoplay is decided when its player is created). Accepted.

## Dead letters, replay and alerting (phase 9 task 4)

- **2026-10-19 · Both DLQs get a push consumer on the site Worker (ruling 5, ruling 14).** `smog-<env>-email-dlq` and `smog-<env>-sponsorship-events-dlq` are consumed by `worker/dlq-queue.ts`, which stores each message in D1 `dead_letter` (id = the queue message id, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) and acks it. `queueKind()` returns `dlq` for the two names and the dispatch takes that branch before the unknown-queue default (the old "a DLQ has no consumer" comment and its test are updated). Why a push consumer rather than polling the Queues API from a cron: no API token as a Worker secret, platform-driven retries, the same dispatch and tests as the other consumers (see the amended phase 8 entry above). **Built-config change (the one this phase allows):** staging's `wrangler.json` gains exactly two `queues.consumers` entries (`max_batch_size` 10, `max_retries` 20, `retry_delay` 300, no `dead_letter_queue`); the first staging deploy attaches them, so a message already in a staging DLQ is consumed (stored in D1) at once. `ensure-cloudflare-resources` needed no change (it already de-duplicates consumer and DLQ names).
- **2026-10-19 · No silent loss while D1 is down (ruling 5b).** A failed store calls `message.retry({ delaySeconds: 300 })`; with `max_retries` 20 a D1 outage of about 100 minutes loses nothing. Only when the last delivery (`attempts > 20`) fails too: `[dlq] lost {"id","queue","type"}` (never a body or an address) and the KV counter `dlq-lost:<yyyy-mm-dd>` (TTL 3 days; read then write, so two simultaneous losses may count once: the log line is the exact record). The daily digest reports and clears the counters. `release-config-check` (`CONSUMER_SETTINGS`, `checkConsumer`) now requires the two DLQ consumers with those settings and no DLQ of their own, and still refuses any other consumer.
- **2026-10-19 · Migration 0013 rebuilds `audit_log` (ruling 5a).** SQLite cannot alter a CHECK; `dead_letter.replay`, `dead_letter.discard` and the `dead_letter` target type must be writable (the admin guard counts the audit inserts), so the table is created anew, copied column for column, dropped, renamed and its five indexes recreated. Checked: no trigger exists on `audit_log` (0007's `user_keep_one_admin` is on `user`) and nothing references it, so the generated `PRAGMA foreign_keys` toggles (no-ops inside D1's migration transaction) were dropped by hand. The Workers-pool test seeds 30,000 rows (`legacy` rows with `data` and NULL `actor_id` included), applies 0013 and compares every row, the five index definitions, the triggers, the CHECKs and `ON DELETE SET NULL`: the rebuild took about 0.4 to 0.5 s in Miniflare. Risk: the deploy job runs it on staging D1 before the Worker deploys; the real table holds at most three years of admin actions (expected well under 10,000 rows), so the copy is a second or two; D1 Time Travel is the rollback.
- **2026-10-19 · Stored bodies, replay and erasure (ruling 5c).** `@smog/email/body-policy` classifies every template (`secret`, `link`, `none`; a mapped type, so a new template does not compile unclassified, and a test holds each class to the template's sample props: a `url`, `token` or `code` prop cannot be `none`, a `link` cannot carry a secret in its URL). Secret: the four `auth/*` emails and `renewal-reminder` (the only template with a raw sponsorship token today; there is no changes-requested email). A secret email, a key-less email and an invalid message are stored with `body` NULL and `replayable` 0 (reason codes `secret_template`, `no_idempotency_key`, `invalid_message` in `error`); nothing of them, the address included, is stored. **Replay refuses** a message without an `idempotencyKey` (whatever `replayable` says) and one already handled; an email goes back with its original key, so the consumer's `email:sent:<key>` skips one that was in fact sent. The admin list and detail never return a body (the list selects no `body` column; a test fails if one appears); audit data holds queue and type only. **Erasure:** `account.delete` removes the rows whose body's `to` equals the deleted address (case-insensitive, after Better Auth deleted the user; a failure is logged, not thrown, since the account is gone and retention removes the row within 14 days); the DSAR export does not include them; the daily retention deletes rows older than 14 days (`RetentionTable` `dead_letter`, `scripts/retention.ts` dry run). `docs/LEGAL-SIGNOFF.md` item 8 asks the owner to confirm the 14 days and the privacy text.
- **2026-10-19 · Replay is enqueue first, claim second.** The queue is written first (a failed enqueue changes nothing and writes no entry), then one D1 batch sets `replayed_at` only while the row is open, guards on `changes() = 0` and inserts the audit entry. Two admins replaying at once may both enqueue; only one claims the row and writes an entry (the loser gets `INVALID_STATE alreadyHandled`), and the second message is a no-op through the idempotency key (the test runs both through the real consumer: one email). Claim-first was rejected because a failed enqueue would leave an audit entry for a replay that never happened.
- **2026-10-19 · The daily digest.** The retention cron runs the purge, then `runDeadLetterDigest` (`@smog/admin/server`; each part runs even if the other fails, the first failure is rethrown). It sends the `admin_dead_letter` email, **one per admin** (`adminRecipients` is injected from `@smog/sponsorships/server`; keys `admin_dead_letter:<date>:<adminId>`), at most once a day, only when open dead letters exist, `dlq-lost:*` counters are non-zero or a sponsorship has been `render_failed` for more than 24 h (`updated_at`). Counts and types only. The KV marker `dlq-digest:<date>` (TTL 3 days) is written after every enqueue succeeded, so a failed send does not skip the day; with no admin to send to the marker is not written. The dashboard card shows the open count per queue, the oldest date (not an age: the page has no clock to keep fresh) and the existing `sponsorships.renderFailed` count rather than a second query.
- **2026-10-19 · Smaller choices.** `origin_queue` is `email` or `events` (a CHECK); `queue` keeps the DLQ's own name. The e2e seed builds the stored body from a `kind` (`email`, `events`, `secret`), never from the request, and deletes only `e2e-` rows. After a replay or discard whose row leaves the open list, the focus goes to the list region instead of falling to the page (the kit's dialog returns it to the opener only while the opener exists).
- **2026-10-19 · Review fixes (phase 9 task 4).** (1) `admin-new-sponsorship` is `secret`: its props hold a sponsor's contact, invoice address and VAT number, a person without an account whom erasure by recipient cannot reach, so it is stored without a body and discard-only (the admin sees the sponsorship in its own screen). `admin-render-failed` stays `link`: its `error` is cut to 300 characters by the producer and holds a renderer message, no personal data. (2) The consumer resolves `siteEnv()` and the database inside the same try as the store, so an environment that fails to validate on every delivery is retried (300 s) and then logged `[dlq] lost` like a D1 outage (the KV counter is skipped, and says so, when the environment itself is unavailable). (3) Stored `attempts` is the delivery count on the DLQ (normally 1), not the original queue's retries, and `received_at` is when the consumer stored it. (4) A replay of an email older than 7 days can double-send an email that was in fact delivered (the `email:sent:<key>` marker lives 7 days, retention 14): the confirm dialog says so for such rows. (5) The digest repeats every UTC day while an open row exists, up to the 14-day retention: deliberate, so an unhandled dead letter keeps being seen. (6) **Deploy note for the owner/runbook (Task 11):** take a D1 Time Travel bookmark (`wrangler d1 time-travel info`) before the first staging deploy of 0013; the migration is written for D1's transactional runner (a failure before DROP leaves the old table) and Time Travel is the rollback.

## Re-edit and renewal tokens in the URL fragment (phase 9 task 7)

- **2026-10-20 · The token moves from `?token=` to `#token=` (ruling 7; carries I-8, R-15, R-25).** `/sponsor/edit#token=<raw>` and `/sponsor/renew#token=<raw>` are what every new link looks like (the renewal reminder, the admin's "copy link" dialog, the request-changes and regenerate results). One builder, `sponsorshipLinkUrl` (`@smog/sponsorships/schema`), replaces the two copies in `lifecycle.ts` and `sweeps.ts`. A fragment never leaves the browser, so no CDN, Workers Log or `Referer` holds it. SSR renders nothing that depends on the token (the pages already read it on the client and sent `{ token }` in the body of the procedures and of `/api/sponsor/reedit-logo`), so the change is the reading side: `validateTokenSearch` is gone, `readLinkToken` (`lib/sponsor-search.ts`, pure) and `useLinkToken` (`components/sponsor/use-link-token.ts`) read `location.hash` after hydration. Until then the token is `undefined` and the views show the neutral "Checking your link…" state (no "Invalid link" flash); a missing or malformed token is `null` and shows the existing `LinkState` copy. The router's `search` is not used (it JSON-parses values).
- **Old `?token=` links keep working.** The hook reads the query when the fragment has none (the fragment wins when both are present), then `history.replaceState`s the same page with the token in the fragment and the other query parameters kept, so a reload, the history and a screenshot hold no query token (the router's history state is kept; no entry is pushed). A malformed query token is stripped from the address too. The legacy redirect `/sponsors/re-edit?token=` (and `/sponsors/renew`) has a `token: "fragment"` mode (since the phase 9 review fix wave also on the current `/sponsor/edit?token=` and `/sponsor/renew?token=`, the paths the reminders sent before phase 9 point at, so the first request is already redirected and the token never reaches the app, a CDN cache or a `Referer`; the client-side move below stays as the fallback): `Location: /sponsor/edit#token=<same>` (other parameters dropped; a fragment in `Location` is not logged) and **status 302** (a 301 for a URL with a token is cached indefinitely); without a token it stays the plain 301. Every other legacy entry is unchanged.
- **The accepted first-request log.** The request of an old `?token=` link (an email already sent, a migrated re-edit token, a copy an admin made) reaches the Worker with the token in its query, so Workers Logs and any proxy in front can hold it once. Accepted: the token is single-purpose, expires (7 days re-edit, `ends_at` renewal) and is revoked on use, reject, expire and cancel, and new links never produce that request. `/api/csp-report` keeps reducing every URL to origin plus path (its test stays).
- **Why not a POST-redirect-GET exchange cookie.** Trading the query token for a cookie on the first hit would still log that first request, would add a state (a cookie, its expiry, its scope next to the session) and a second route to secure, and the logo and submit calls already carry the token in the body. More state for no gain over the fragment.
- **`Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` plus `<meta name="referrer">` stay (DECISIONS 858), now pinned** by `lib/token-page.test.ts` and `test/security-headers.test.ts` (both the fragment and the old query URL).
- **The sign-in link did leak it (found by the e2e).** The header's "Sign in" link builds `?redirect=<current URL>`; with the token in the fragment the router's `hash` put it into that query (and made the server and client hrefs differ). `signInReturnPath` (`lib/redirect.ts`) now drops a fragment that holds a `token` (other fragments stay). Tested in `test/redirect.test.ts` and in the e2e.
- **No token in `console`, `sessionStorage`, analytics or error messages:** the hook only returns it; `use-link-token.test.ts` captures the console while it reads an old link; the e2e records every request URL and console line of both pages and asserts none contains the token.
- **The guard.** `apps/site/scripts/token-links.test.ts` (a bun test, part of `bun -F @smog/site test`) scans the non-test sources of `packages/**`, `apps/site/src` and `apps/mobile/{app,src}` for `?token=`/`&token=` outside comments and fails on a new one; the allow-list is the three Better Auth links in `packages/email/src/samples.ts` (`/api/auth/magic-link/verify`, `/reset-password`, `/api/auth/verify-email`) and the mobile `/magic-link(/app)?` link; a second test fails when an allow-list entry goes stale.
- **No mobile impact.** AASA and assetlinks claim only `/gestures/*`, `/lists/*` and `/magic-link/app`, and `native-intent` has no sponsor path, so no app opens a re-edit or renewal link. **Owner list (Task 11):** send one real re-edit link to a real mailbox behind a link scanner (Outlook Safe Links, Mimecast) and check that the opened URL keeps `#token=` (a scanner that rewrites the URL and drops the fragment would break new links; the old `?token=` form is the fallback to restore).
- **Task 7 is the new text of R-15's note:** the legacy re-edit redirect now carries the token in the fragment (302), no longer `?token=` (301).
- **2026-10-20 · Fix round (phase 9 task 7 review).** (1) The guard (`apps/site/scripts/token-links.test.ts`) is a tripwire, not a proof: besides `?token=` it fails on any non-fragment `token=` (concatenated or split templates), `searchParams.set/append("token"`, `URLSearchParams({ token`, and the sponsor page paths in non-test code outside `tokens.ts`, the legacy table, the routes, the generated route tree and the email sample; each bypass pattern has a self-test. (2) `useLinkToken` moves an old `?token=` through `router.history.replace` (the router's location and the address agree; `replaceState` only without a router, in tests). (3) The token-carrying 302 sends `Cache-Control: no-store`. (4) **By design the fragment stays in the address bar**, so browser history, history sync across devices and a screenshot hold `#token=`; the token is single-purpose, expires and is revoked on use. Owner note for Task 11: tell admins that a pasted "copy link" in a chat tool holds the token, and that the dev mail page shows it.

## Performance: budgets, lazy splits, cache policy and query plans (phase 9 task 6)

- **2026-10-20 · Measured first: the client's initial JavaScript is about 461 kB gzip, not the 200/300 kB of ruling 4.** `dist/client/.vite/manifest.json`, gzip of each file: the client entry plus its static-import closure was **54 chunks, 460.9 kB** before this task (entry chunk 131 kB; `@smog/ui-web` 104 kB; the three i18n catalogues 70 kB; `schemas` 26 kB; `legal-page` 21 kB; the QR dialog 7 kB), the plan's 456 kB re-measured. The Worker script is 1745 KiB gzip (`wrangler deploy --dry-run`: 7301 KiB raw, 182 modules).
- **2026-10-20 · Two named lazy splits, 27 kB gzip off every page.** (1) `legal-page` was in the entry because `privacy.tsx` and `terms.tsx` import `legalHead` and `validateLegalSearch` from it, and a route's `head` and `validateSearch` stay in the entry-reachable chunk while only the component is split. Those two now live in `components/legal-head.ts` (no legal text), and the texts reach the head through the route's `loader` (`await import("@/components/legal-page")`, `legalMeta(kind)`: title and first paragraph in the three languages, a few lines serialised into the page). The legal chunk loads on `/privacy` and `/terms` only. (2) `GestureQrDialog` (the QR encoder, 7 kB) loads when a QR code is first opened (`lazy-gesture-qr-dialog.tsx`, used by the gesture page and both admin screens). Result: **434.1 kB** for the closure alone (home route 438.2 kB), 27 kB less. **The 200 kB (home) and 300 kB (any route) targets are not reached, and cannot be by the files this task owns:** the shared closure is the React and TanStack entry (132 kB), `@smog/ui-web` imported through its barrel (104 kB), all three i18n catalogues in one chunk (70 kB, `packages/i18n`) and the zod schemas (26 kB). The next splits, for a later pass, are a per-locale catalogue (about 45 kB), `ui-web` imports by component and the `GuestImportSheet` mounted in the root (it needs its hook running on sign-in, so it cannot simply be lazy). Each budget below is recorded with the note of what blocks its target. Not done, on purpose: `GuestImportSheet` and the consent banner (root route, not this task's files).
- **2026-10-20 · Budgets in the deploy guard (`apps/site/perf-budget.json`, `scripts/perf-budget.ts`).** One budget per entry, in gzip bytes of initial JavaScript: the client entry, its static-import closure and the route's own split chunks (a visit may list several route modules, the entry is the largest): `home` 504, `gestures` 505, `gesture` 503, `lists` (with `/favorites`) 523, `sponsor` 517, `sponsor-pages` 508, `account` 506, `auth` 506, `legal` 525, `admin` 524 kB, each the **measurement after the splits + 15 %**, rounded up to the kB; the Worker's cap is the hard 5 MiB gzip of ruling 4 (measured 1.74 MiB, summed over `dist/server/**/*.js`). `deploy-guard` runs it in `--bundle` (after every `vite build`, so `bun run build` fails over a budget) and in the deploy path. **Ratchet:** a measurement over its budget fails with a "split or raise with a DECISIONS line" message; a budget more than 10 % above what `measured + 15 %` would be now prints `lower the budget to X` (a warning, not a failure: measured + 15 % is 13 % above the measurement, so the hint's 10 % is taken over that headroom). Every landed split lowers the number in the same commit. A route listed in a budget that has no chunk in the manifest is an error (a renamed route cannot go unmeasured). `checkClientRenderIsLazy` and `CLIENT_LAZY_MARKERS` are unchanged. **No source map ships:** `build.sourcemap: false` is explicit in `vite.config.ts` (a test pins the line) and the guard fails on any `.map` file or `sourceMappingURL` comment in `dist/`.
- **2026-10-20 · Cache policy by path class (P-18): `src/worker/cache.ts` and `public/_headers`.** Hashed `/assets/<name>-<hash>.{js,css,woff2}`: `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`. `_headers` (Workers assets, served before the Worker) has no pattern language beyond a splat, so the immutable rule is **by extension** (`/assets/*.js`, `*.css`, `*.woff2`, every hashed file Vite emits today), never a blanket `/assets/*`: the unhashed `/assets/logo.svg` of R-18 (Task 9) is an svg and gets the one-day rule, as do `/brand/*`, the icons and `og.png`. `/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml` and the manifest (Task 9): an hour (`robots.txt` was a day: its `Cache-Control` line in the route now says an hour). HTML: `private, no-cache` (it carries its CSP nonce). `/api/*`, `/webhooks/*`, `/dev/*` and every 5xx (the maintenance 503 included): `no-store`. `cache.ts` is the one table for the Worker's responses (`headers.ts` calls `cacheHeaders` through `setMissing`, so a route's own `Cache-Control` still wins; the former HTML-only line in `headers.ts` is replaced by it); `test/cache-policy.test.ts` checks it path by path and holds `_headers` to the same values. A new hashed extension (a font or image imported by the app) needs one more `_headers` line: the test lists `HASHED_EXTENSIONS`.
- **2026-10-20 · Query plans: a recording D1 and 5 plan tests, one index (migration 0014).** `@smog/db/testing` gained `recordingD1` and `scanReport` (`src/testing/plan.ts`): the real service code runs against a D1 that records every statement and its bound values, and each is explained with `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`; a bare `SCAN <table>` or a covering-index scan fails, an ordered `SCAN … USING INDEX` (a keyset first page, it stops at the LIMIT), a virtual table (FTS5, `json_each`) and a co-routine do not. Tests: `gesture-plan` (search and its ranking join, browse, the category keyset page, by slug and by legacy id, by ids, related, categories, sitemap), `favorites-plan`, `lists-plan` (overview, detail, containing, the writes, the share-token lookup, the shared view), `admin-plan` (users with each filter and the cursor, the dead-letter list, the sponsorship list with each filter) and `sponsorships-plan` (the expiry, reminder, stale and retention sweeps, the Mollie webhook's lookup by `mollie_id` and by metadata); the sponsor availability, audit keyset, render watchdog, retention selects and users' sponsor lookup already had theirs. **Accepted scans:** the category list reads every category (a dozen rows), and the used-token retention purge (`used_at` has no index, DECISIONS 2026-10-02; unchanged), and the users search (`instr`) walks `user_created_at_idx` in order to the page size. **One missing index, forced by its plan: `sponsorship_logo_key_idx` on `sponsorship(logo_key)` (0014, CREATE INDEX only).** The orphan-logo sweep's `NOT EXISTS (SELECT … FROM sponsorship AS other WHERE other.logo_key = ?)` scanned the whole table for every candidate key; it now seeks the index.
- **2026-10-20 · Worker limits and runtime perf, measured, not asserted.** `test/worker-limits.test.ts` times the first request, a server-rendered `/gestures` and a repeat (about 15, 230 and 55 ms in the workerd pool on a local D1) with a 2 s ceiling, the one assertion (ruling 2); the Worker's size is the deploy guard's. D1 timings noted, local D1 only: catalogue page about 5 ms, search about 20 ms, the retention purge about 18 ms. `e2e/perf.spec.ts` (project `public`, `vite dev`) checks CLS <= 0.1 on `/`, `/gestures`, a gesture page and `/sponsor` (after the data arrived, then a scroll to the end), a width and height or an aspect box on every image and `loading="lazy"` below the fold, the gesture video's poster and no autoplay with sound (`autoplay` absent or `muted`, `preload` never `auto`), and the real `Cache-Control` on `/`, a gesture page, `/api/health`, `/api/auth/get-session`, `/robots.txt` and `/sitemap.xml`. No request count or size is asserted against `vite dev`. `vite dev` serves `public/` itself, without `_headers`, so the static files' headers are held by the unit test, not the e2e. The spec found a real shift: the sponsor wizard's loading skeleton had no height (`Skeleton` is `h-4` and the grid's `aspect-3/4` lost), so the results pushed the page by 0.48; `h-auto` on the skeleton fixes it (`step-select.tsx`, one class). **Not done:** `preload="none"` on the Mux player (`@smog/ui-web`'s `VideoPlayer`, another task's file: the spec asserts it is not `auto`); Lighthouse (heavy, network-bound, as ruled).
- **2026-10-20 · Review fixes (phase 9 task 6).** (1) `perf-budget.json` is validated before use (`parsePerfBudget`: finite positive `gzip`, `target` and `workerGzip`, non-empty `routes`); a missing or misspelled number now fails the build instead of passing for ever. (2) A `src/routes/*?tsr-split=component` chunk that no entry lists **fails** the guard (a hint would be ignored in CI logs), so a new route or admin screen forces a budget decision; `dev/*` pages (compiled out of production) are exempt. (3) `/_serverFn/*` (TanStack server functions, session-dependent) is `no-store`. (4) An ordered `SCAN … USING INDEX` in a plan counts as bounded only when the statement has a `LIMIT` (`fullScans` takes `limited`; `scanReport` reads it from the SQL and passes it to `allowed(detail, sql)`). That exposed three by-design whole-index walks, now named in their tests: the published-gesture `count(*)` and the typo tier's catalogue projection (a few hundred rows, cached in KV), and the admin status-tab counts over `sponsorship`. (5) The perf spec waits for the loading skeletons to be gone instead of `networkidle`. (6) `_headers` marks immutable **by extension** while `cache.ts` requires a `-<8 character hash>` suffix: they differ only if an unhashed `.js`, `.css` or `.woff2` is ever placed under `/assets/` (it would be immutable at the asset layer, a day in the Worker policy); a new hashed extension (`.woff`, `.wasm`, an imported `.png`) needs a line in both. (7) The next free migration number is **0015**.
- **2026-10-20 · Follow-up (phase 9, not done here): the splits that lower the 200 kB and 300 kB targets.** Each lands with its budget lowered in the same commit. (a) **i18n per locale** (`packages/i18n`): the three catalogues are one 70 kB gzip chunk; lazy non-English locales saves about 23 kB for every visitor (`en` stays, it is the fallback), about 47 kB only if `en` is lazy too. `createI18n` is synchronous (`initAsync: false`, resources imported statically), created per request in SSR and shared with mobile, so a split needs an async load of the active locale before hydration (the client entry awaits it before `hydrateRoot`, or the loader dehydrates it; otherwise a hydration mismatch or a flash of keys), on-demand loading for the language switch, and every test that calls `createI18n` synchronously adjusted. Lowers every entry by about 23 kB. (b) **`@smog/ui-web` by-component imports** instead of the barrel (104 kB gzip chunk): lowers every entry, the largest single win. (c) **`GuestImportSheet` out of `__root`** (needs its sign-in hook kept eager; the sheet body lazy): lowers every entry by the sheet's size. (d) **zod schemas** (26 kB `schemas` chunk): split per feature so a page loads only its own. Without (a) and (b) the home budget cannot reach 200 kB and the others cannot reach 300 kB; the targets are therefore recorded as open, not met, and the budgets ratchet down with each of these.

## Web accessibility: axe on every public state, keyboard, reflow and motion runs (phase 9 task 5)

- **2026-10-20 · What the specs hold (ruling 3).** `e2e/a11y-public.spec.ts` runs axe over every public route and state at 390 and 1280 px, light and dark: the guest routes (home, `/gestures` empty, with a query, without results and in a category, a gesture, favorites, lists, account, sign-in, sign-up, forgot-password, reset-password with a token and with a bad link, verify-email both ways, the magic-link failure, privacy, terms, `/sponsor`, the 404), the header menus and sheet, the gesture menu, the save-to-list prompt and the QR dialog, a toast, the consent banner, the app banner and the open-in-app banner (phone user agent), the guest-import sheet, the error boundary, the sponsor wizard steps 1 to 3 (with the empty-submit errors), the signed-in empty states, the delete-account dialog (typed and untyped), favorites and lists with items, the list's menus, rename and delete dialogs and share sheet, the picker and its toast, a shared list as viewer, as editor (with its add sheet) and signed out, an unknown token, and sampled hover, focus and disabled states. `a11y-keyboard.spec.ts` (Tab, Shift+Tab, Enter, Space, Escape, arrows only), `a11y-reflow.spec.ts` (320 x 256, text spacing, reduced motion, landmarks, titles, `lang` in nl, en and fr, live regions, `aria-describedby` on a form error) and `screenshots.spec.ts` (the visual review, only with `PUBLIC_SHOTS_DIR`, never asserted) are the others. Findings are compared per rule id and target (`byRule`), never by count. **One page load per state, two themes:** each state is scanned in the light theme and then again after flipping the `dark` class on `<html>` (what the server sets from the cookie, which `shell.spec.ts` proves); the helper asserts the body colour changed, so a dark scan cannot be vacuous. That halves the page loads of a matrix that otherwise would not fit the shard. The shard was not re-measured on CI (the local machine was shared by several tasks; the three a11y specs took 11 to 20 minutes here on two workers): the controller re-measures it, and the guest-route and signed-in tests are the ones to split by width if it is over 15 minutes.
- **2026-10-20 · `@axe-core/playwright` 4.14.0 (the carry from task 1).** The pin at 4.13.0 is lifted: the catalog says 4.14.0 (axe-core 4.14.0, newest stable). Its new `label-content-name-mismatch` finding on the header's account menu button was real (WCAG 2.5.3): axe takes the avatar's visible initials as the button's label, and "Accountmenu" does not contain them. The kit's `Avatar` now draws the initials as generated content (`before:content-[attr(data-initials)]`, the `data-initials` attribute carries them; the image role and its name were already what assistive technology read), so a named control around an avatar has no visible text to disagree with its name. The same rule found the sponsor wizard's gesture cards (the card's name is the gesture, its visible text also held the categories): the cards are now `li`s whose button holds the still and the name only, with the categories and the badge beside it (the button's pseudo-element stretches over the card, the badge stays its `aria-describedby`, `has-focus-visible` draws the ring on the card). The unit test reads the badge through `aria-describedby`.
- **2026-10-20 · Findings fixed (what the runs found).** (1) The phone menu sheet stayed open after a section link was followed (the page changed under it): the header owns its state and `MainNavList` takes `onNavigate`. (2) At 320 px the signed-out header was 55 px too wide: below 360 px the sign-in link is its icon (the text stays as screen-reader text). (3) With the WCAG text spacing a display heading's long word scrolled the page at 320 px: `Heading` is `wrap-anywhere`. (4) A dialog opened by a menu item returned the focus to `<body>` (the menu item is gone): `useReturnFocus` returns it to the menu's button (Radix labels the menu by its trigger), for every menu in the site and the admin; kit test added. (5) A search with no results was never announced (the polite status was empty in that state, and the desktop rows had none): both say "No gestures found" (and "Searching…" while loading) in the status. (6) A failed sign-in, sign-up or code submit left the focus where it was: the form now moves it to the first invalid field, whose message is its `aria-describedby` (and an alert). (7) Sponsor cards' category badges wrap instead of being cut off under the text spacing at 320 px (and, now outside the disabled button, are not dimmed: axe checks their contrast). (8) Admin, one line: the table editor's categories and keywords buttons are named "<summary>: <edit title>", so the visible summary is in the name (axe 4.14 `label-content-name-mismatch`, found by the existing admin spec after the bump).
- **2026-10-20 · False positive: a modal menu hides the page from axe's point of view.** While a Radix menu is open it puts `aria-hidden` on the rest of the page and traps the focus in the menu; axe accepts that only for a dialog (`isModalOpen`), so `aria-hidden-focus` fires on every page with a menu open, and `page-has-heading-one` with it (the heading is inside the hidden part). The earlier guest pages passed only because the stubbed Mux player's own error dialog counted as an open modal. A scan taken with a menu open (`blockingViolations(page, { menuOpen: true })`) does not run those two rules, nothing else; the menu itself, its colours and its names are still checked. Alternative: `modal={false}` on the kit's `Menu` (the focus would leave the menu on Tab, and the admin screens would change with it); not taken for a rule that only looks at the hidden page. axe's own run moves the focus and dismisses the menu, so the dark scan opens it again.
- **2026-10-20 · No new `AXE_EXCLUDED` entry.** The list is still `mux-player` only. The Turnstile iframe does not exist in dev (no site key), and the Mux player's own dialogs are outside the scans (`closeWithEscape` and the dialog locators in the specs skip its `media-*` elements). Console noise from the player's stubbed, segment-less stream is filtered by name in the specs that watch errors, as `gesture.spec.ts` does.
- **2026-10-20 · What the brief asks for that the product does not have, and what stands in.** (1) The skip link's target is `main#main` (`href="#main"`, `tabIndex -1`), not `main-content`; the spec asserts the focus lands on it and that the next Tab is inside it. (2) The web site has no offline banner (mobile only, spec §10): the kit's `OfflineBanner` is scanned in both themes at both widths by `dev-ui.spec.ts`. (3) There is no export dialog: the export is a download button (`account.spec.ts` covers its file and toast); the delete dialog is scanned. (4) Dialog return focus: the Radix toast is the topmost layer, so while a toast shows the first Escape dismisses it and the second closes the dialog; that stays (a toast also has its close button and a timeout), the specs press Escape twice there.
- **2026-10-20 · Reduced motion.** `motion-reduce:animate-none` loses to `data-[state=open]:animate-*` on specificity, so the animations are declared; what makes them still is `styles.css` setting every `--duration-*` token to 0 under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The spec therefore asserts the longest computed animation or transition duration on the favorite button, the sheet, the dialog, the menu, a hover transition and the toast viewport is 0 s, and, as a control, that the same sheet and toast are longer than 0 s without the preference.
- **2026-10-20 · Focus ring.** The kit's ring is a 2 px `box-shadow` (`ring-2` with a 2 px offset), not an `outline`: the spec reads the computed `box-shadow` for the `--color-focus-ring` colour and the 4 px outer edge (or a 2 px solid outline), on the header link, the search button, the sign-in field and button, and the sponsor wizard's sticky bar button, in both themes.
- **2026-10-20 · Owner actions this creates (Task 11).** A screen-reader pass (VoiceOver, TalkBack, NVDA) over the sponsor wizard and sign-in; high-contrast mode and 200 % text on a real device. Nothing a machine can say about speech output is claimed here.
- **2026-10-20 · Review fixes (phase 9 task 5).** (1) Sponsor cards: the corner tick and the blocked badge are `pointer-events-none`, so a click on them reaches the button's stretched overlay; the keyboard spec toggles a card with Space and Enter and clicks its tick. (2) `useFocusInvalid` counts submits, so a second identical failure refocuses the field (spec case added). (3) The phone sheet closes only on a plain left click (not ctrl, cmd, shift, alt or middle). (4) Visual changes to note: the signed-out header shows the sign-in icon instead of the word only below 360 px, and avatar initials are generated content, which axe's `color-contrast` no longer sees (the token pair is the same `primary-strong` on `primary-subtle` that `@smog/styles` proves). (5) The axe helper has a self-test (an unnamed button is reported as `button-name`).

## a11y shard on CI (phase 9 task 5 follow-up)

The `a11y` shard failed on the first CI run (develop 12cb9c6) and passed locally: three tests hit their 5-minute timeout on both attempts (20 minutes burned, then the 35-minute deadline killed the run), one failed on both attempts at about 20 s, one was flaky.

- **2026-10-20 · Cause of the hangs: the browser.** CI runs Playwright's own Chromium headless shell (Chromium 156); this sandbox's `playwright.config.ts` launches the full Chrome 141 by path. Reproduced here by pointing `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_PATH` at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium_headless_shell-1194/chrome-linux/headless_shell` (do this before trusting a local green a11y run). In the shell, axe's run (`@axe-core/playwright` opens and closes a blank page in the context) does **not** dismiss an open Radix menu. `scanThemes` re-opens the menu for its dark scan by clicking the trigger, which the open menu's modal layer covers, so Playwright waited for the click until the test's own 300 s timeout (the trace's last call was `click Taal wijzigen`, never finished). Every hung test was a test that scans a menu (header, gesture actions, user menu). `scanThemes` now closes the overlay with Escape (and waits for it to be gone) before it opens the menu again, in either browser.
- **2026-10-20 · Cause of the `search, open a result, favorite it and save it to a list` failure.** The test pressed Escape twice in a row to dismiss the toast and then the dialog; in the headless shell the second key arrived while the toast was still leaving and went to the toast, so the dialog stayed open (`toBeHidden` timed out at 15 s). The test now waits for the toast to be hidden between the two presses (the public spec already did). The flaky `language and theme menus` test (16.6 s, passed on the retry) was the first menu use of a cold dev server and is not reproduced here (3 to 5 s in every run); its opening step is now retry-safe (focus and Enter again until the menu is open, never pressing on an open one, within 30 s), the one thing a cold server can change there.
- **2026-10-20 · A hang fails in seconds, on its line.** The contexts of the a11y specs set a 30 s default timeout (`STEP_MS` in `a11y-support.ts`; no global `actionTimeout`, which `request` calls and `waitForFunction` would inherit, phase 6 review M-8): a click on a covered button now fails after 30 s with Playwright's call log instead of waiting out the test. No test has a 300 s timeout any more: a test that scans a few states has 90 s (`STATES_MS`, measured at 10 to 20 s here, a CI runner is faster than this sandbox with the headless shell: the keyboard tests take 2 s there, 4 to 5 s here), the rest keep the 60 s default.
- **2026-10-20 · One test per state, not one loop.** The 300 s tests looped over 21 routes, a dozen overlays or three pages and scanned each in two themes inside one test, so the total scaled with the runner and a failure did not name a state. They are now one test per route (42), per overlay or banner state and per signed-in state, each loading its page once and scanning both themes. The guest-route, text-spacing, reflow, reduced-motion and landmark tests share one page per `describe` (`useSharedGuestPage`, `useSharedPage`: a context per test costs a cold load of the app's scripts, two thirds of a route test; each test opens its own path and starts with no console errors). The title-uniqueness check of the landmark spec is its own test per language. What the tests assert is unchanged; two waits were added (Escape then `toBeHidden`, on the `div[role="dialog"]`, because the Mux player's own `media-error-dialog` is also a `dialog`).
- **2026-10-20 · Three a11y projects (seven shards).** Splitting the tests adds some: `playwright test --list` was 42 `a11y` tests (214 in all) and is 185 (357 in all): `a11y` 63 (`a11y-public.spec.ts`: guest routes, overlays, banners, error boundary, sponsor steps, hover and focus; also takes any new `a11y-*` spec), `a11y-signed-in` 27 (`a11y-signed-in.spec.ts`, the signed-in axe states moved out of the public spec, and `a11y-keyboard.spec.ts`), `a11y-reflow` 95 (`a11y-reflow.spec.ts` and `a11y-landmarks.spec.ts`, the landmark, title and language checks moved out of the reflow spec). The other four projects are unchanged (public 118, admin 29, admin-a11y 15, serial 10); the seven add up to 357 with no overlap (`playwright test --list` per project). `scripts/e2e.ts` lists the seven in the config's order, `ci.yml`'s `e2e` matrix has the seven shards (`checkE2eJob` compares both with the config), and no project is allowed to be empty any more (`--pass-with-no-tests` only with a file filter).
- **2026-10-20 · Times.** Measured here with the headless shell, one worker, no retries: `a11y` 8.1 min, `a11y-signed-in` 5.9 min, `a11y-reflow` 12.1 min when pinned to two cores with `taskset -c 0,1` (the heaviest shard; not measured unpinned). This sandbox is slower than the CI runner with this browser (the CI log's `skip link` test took 2.0 s, 4.6 s here pinned to two cores), so a shard is expected to take about 3 to 5 minutes of test time on CI, far from the 35-minute deadline and 45-minute job timeout.

## Security review (phase 9 task 8)

Rulings 6, 14 and 16. Most of the header, authorization and webhook work already existed; the review pinned what was missing and fixed the two real gaps (P-10 and the static-asset headers). Nothing here changes the built `wrangler.json` (staging hash unchanged, see the task report).

- **2026-10-20 · Authorization is one explicit table, exhaustive over the router.** `packages/api/test/authz.test.ts` lists every leaf of `appContract` (the admin router included: 47 admin procedures) with its level (`public`, `session`, `token`, `admin`; `verified` and `webhook` are in the type and asserted unused) and runs an anonymous, a user and an admin call per row, and a banned one through the real `loadSession` interceptor (an `auth` whose session read answers null as Better Auth does for a banned user, started from a context that still carried a valid-looking session; the ban itself lives only in that shared read, proven in `@smog/auth`'s `revocation.test.ts`, and by a real ban through the Worker for seven procedures in `security-authz.test.ts`). A procedure missing from the table, or a stale row, fails; a stub contract with an extra procedure proves it. `verified` is unused on purpose: email sign-up needs the verification link before a session exists, so a session is a verified user. Webhooks are plain routes and are in the route table. The admin rows also carry their audit kind (`read`, `audit`, `exempt`), compared with `ADMIN_PROCEDURE_KINDS`, whose guard (`adminGuard`, phase 5) already forces the audit row into the write's batch at run time; the one `exempt` write is `mux.createUpload`. `apps/site/test/security-authz.test.ts` adds the full-stack cases with real sessions: a user cannot read or change another user's lists (every procedure answers `NOT_FOUND`), favorites or account export; an admin's `users.ban` revokes the target's session at once, so every signed-in procedure and `whoami` treat them as a guest. The `view` vs `edit` share-token rules stay in `packages/features/lists/test/sharing.test.ts`.
- **2026-10-20 · P-10 fixed: the RPC and OpenAPI transports refuse a body over 5 MiB (413).** `capRequestBody` in `apps/site/src/server/rpc.ts`, one named constant (`RPC_MAX_BODY_BYTES`), runs before the origin check, the rate limit and the session read. A declared `content-length` over the cap is refused at once (a malformed one is 400); a declared body within it passes untouched. A body with **no declared length is not buffered**: it flows through a counting `TransformStream` that errors past the cap, so nothing is held or copied before the `RL_API` limit and the session read, and the handler buffers only what a procedure that got that far reads (`serve` answers 413 when the stream failed the read). Why 5 MiB and not 1 MiB: the guest import's contract allows 100 lists of 500 gesture ids and 1000 favorites (about 3.5 MB at the worst), so 1 MiB could refuse a contract-valid input; everything else (admin batches of 50 to 100, forms) is far below 1 MiB, logos are presigned R2 PUTs and the logo upload route keeps its 2 MB limit. Accepted risk (memory): an unauthenticated client can still make a procedure that reads its input buffer up to 5 MiB, but only after the origin check, the per-IP limit and the session read have passed, so the limit bounds how many such requests one address can make; the first review version buffered before all three (fixed in the fix round).
- **2026-10-20 · The CSP report log is sampled per isolate (ruling 6).** At most 20 `[csp] violation` lines per minute, then one `[csp] suppressed {count}` line when the window closes (the next report after it: a Worker has no timer to rely on). The `RL_ANALYTICS` limit stays, the answer is 204 either way, and no `ratelimits` binding was added. Accepted: the suppressed line is emitted by the next report after the window, never by a timer, so a flood that stops dead leaves its last window's count unlogged (a Worker has no timer to rely on; the dropped lines were violations of the same CSP already logged 20 times a minute).
- **2026-10-20 · Static assets get the Worker's set where it applies.** `public/_headers` lacked `Permissions-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`; both are added with `Strict-Transport-Security` (also missing: static files never pass through the Worker; browsers ignore HSTS over plain http, so dev is unaffected). No CSP or `X-Frame-Options` there: the static files are images and JSON, never documents. `security-headers.test.ts` keeps `_headers` equal to the Worker's constants and pins the whole list per environment: nosniff, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy` with camera, microphone and geolocation off (the sponsor logo is a file input and the QR a link, so nothing needs them), COOP `same-origin` (Turnstile is an iframe and Mollie's checkout a navigation, neither a popup: the real Mollie and Turnstile are only proven on staging by the owner), `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, HSTS one year without `preload` (the preload decision is the owner's), and `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'self'`, `form-action 'self'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'` in the CSP, enforced in dev and production and `Report-Only` in staging. `form-action` has no Mollie host: the checkout is a redirect, no form posts there. Not added: `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` on pages (they would block Mux posters and Google profile pictures for no gain).
- **2026-10-20 · Webhooks.** Mux (HMAC, 5-minute tolerance) was covered; added the retry case: a signature replayed after 6 minutes is `expired`, the same body signed afresh (what a Mux retry is) is accepted, and the handler is idempotent per event id. Mollie has no signature: the handler re-fetches the payment by id and ignores the body, now pinned with a forged `status: "paid"` JSON body (an unknown id is `200 UNKNOWN` after one `GET /v2/payments/<id>`, a malformed id is `400` and no Mollie call). `/api/webhooks/mux`, `/api/webhooks/mollie` and the legacy `/webhooks/mollie` are listed in a test as exempt from maintenance and the origin check: a cross-site POST in a window gets the handler's own JSON, not the 503 page or the 403. Carry 21 (a real Mux retry on staging) stays an owner action.
- **2026-10-20 · Rate limits: the table is code.** `scripts/security-guard.test.ts` lists every route file of `apps/site/src/routes` with its limiter (and the source file that must name it) or a "none, because"; a new route without either, a stale row or a limiter its source does not use fails. `docs/API.md` prints the same table. Keys come from `cf-connecting-ip` only (`clientIp`); a test changes `X-Forwarded-For` per request and still hits the `RL_AUTH` 429. Accepted: a request without `cf-connecting-ip` shares one `unknown` bucket (dev and tests only; Cloudflare always sets it in front of staging and production). Accepted: `GET /api/logos/<key>` has no limiter (an admin session is required and a guest has no cookie, so Better Auth answers without a D1 read), `/api/health`, `/robots.txt`, `/turnstile-bridge` and `/dev/*` (dev only) have none by design, `/sitemap.xml` is cached an hour.
- **2026-10-20 · Secrets and personal data in logs: a scan, not a convention.** `scripts/security-guard.test.ts` reads `apps/site/src`, `packages/**` and `scripts` and fails for a `console.*` call whose arguments can print an identifier named like `token`, `secret`, `password`, `code`, `authorization`, `cookie`, `apiKey` or `email` (camelCase and snake_case words; a string passed to `get(...)` or an index counts too), unless it goes through `redactMessage`, `errorSummary` or `redactReportUrl`, or has an allow-list entry with a reason. The scan found three, all non-secret result codes (the Mux webhook outcome, a render-lane error code, a process exit code) and they are allow-listed. The scanner proves itself on its own cases and fails if it finds fewer than 150 calls. Tests also show that the Mollie and Mux clients' thrown errors and logs (a 500, a non-JSON 200, an unexpected body, a 429) carry neither the key or token, the `Authorization` value nor the response body, that the email sender's own error names the environment only, and that the email consumer logs a send failure's name and code, never its message (which may name the recipient). Known and accepted: `DevEmailSender` prints the message (recipient and text) to the console in dev, because the dev mailbox is its purpose; it is never constructed outside dev. Better Auth's debug logging is off (no `advanced.debug`/`logger.level` is set).
- **2026-10-20 · Observability (ruling 14): one top-level error line, no external reporter.** `fetch` already had `respondSecurely`; `queue` and `scheduled` now have the same backstop (`worker/top-level.ts`, wired in `worker.ts`): an escaping error is logged once as `[error] <entry> {"id","name","message"}` (`logUnhandledError` in `@smog/utils`: the name and a message with addresses, URL queries, credentials and long token-like runs masked and cut to 300 characters, never the stack or the error's properties; the id is the `cf-ray` for `fetch`, generated for the other two) and **rethrown**, so a queue batch retries and dead-letters and a cron run shows as failed. The consumers keep their own `[service] Failed to ...` lines. `respondSecurely`'s old line, which passed the error object, is replaced by this one. No Sentry-style reporter (spec principle 6, X-18; the privacy text names no such processor). The alert path is the dead-letter digest and card (task 4), `admin_render_failed`, and the Cloudflare notifications the owner enables (Workers error rate, queue backlog, cron failures: task 11 lists them). `observability` in `wrangler.jsonc` is unchanged. Limits, accepted: `redactMessage` strips the query of absolute URLs only; a relative path with a short token in it (`/lists/<short>`) survives unless the token is 32 or more characters (minted tokens are 43) or written as `token=`. `rpc.ts` no longer logs the escaping error itself (the top level does, once). The project's `[service] Failed to ...:`, error` convention (AGENTS.md) passes the error object to the console in many places; flagging them all in the log scan would contradict it, so the scan stays on interpolated identifiers, and the redacted top-level line is the one that must never carry an error's properties.
- **2026-10-20 · Audit re-evaluation (online, `bun audit --production`, 2026-10-20).** The ignore for GHSA-3wwx-pv8p-q78v (undici 7.29.0) is **dropped**: the root `overrides` pin to 7.29.1 (2026-09-29) already fixes it and the audit passes without it. The other seven stay, each re-read on 2026-10-20: GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq (uuid 7.0.3, only through `xcode` in `@expo/config-plugins`, a build-time tool; only when a caller passes `buf`; the fix is major 11+ and `xcode` asks `^7`), GHSA-vcc3-ghjq-m6fr (decode-uri-component 0.2.2 via `query-string` 7 in `expo-router`; 0.5.0 exists but is ESM-only and the consumer is CJS), GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99 (esbuild 0.18.20 via `drizzle-kit` 0.31.11 → `@esbuild-kit`, still the newest drizzle-kit; the advisory is about `esbuild serve`, never started; dev dependency of `@smog/db`), GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv (node-forge 1.4.0, still the newest release, no fix; Expo CLI and update signing at build time), GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm (braces 3.0.3, still the newest, no fix; glob expansion of our own patterns in build and test tooling), GHSA-rj75-hqrm-r3gf (postcss-selector-parser 6.1.4 under Tailwind 3 and NativeWind; 7.1.6 is a major the mobile styles could feel) and GHSA-hp3w-g68c-fv3c (sprintf-js 1.0.3 and 1.1.3, newest, no fix; Jest, Expo and TanStack Start tooling). None is reachable from the Worker or the exported app bundles (build-time and test-time tooling; the Worker build was checked for the packages in the task report). Revisit when Expo, drizzle-kit, Tailwind or the libraries above ship fixes; the next review is Task 10's final audit.
- **2026-10-20 · `bun outdated`.** Not newest stable: `@biomejs/biome` 2.5.14 (2.5.15), `knip` 6.39.0 (6.42.1), `turbo` 2.11.7 (2.11.9), `ultracite` 7.12.2 (8.0.1, a major) and `@types/bun` 1.4.2 (1.4.3). Held on purpose: the toolchain (Bun 1.3.11, Biome 2.5.14) stays until Task 10 decides, and a lint-preset major (ultracite 8) changes the rules every task is written against.
- **Deviations from the brief's file list:** `apps/site/src/worker.ts` (three lines: the request id and the two guards) and the new `apps/site/src/worker/top-level.ts`, because queue and scheduled need a wrapper in a file nobody was assigned; `packages/utils/src/{log,log.test,index}.ts` (the helper the brief allows if the review finds the need); `packages/api/test/authz.test.ts` and four new `apps/site/test/security-*.test.ts` files. No migration, no binding, no var.


## Parity audit (phase 9 task 9)

The audit walked every open row of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-feature-inventory.md` (51 rows, section 1.3, section 8) and gave each one a disposition with evidence. All 51 rows are now `[x]`; none is deferred. Ticks are in the inventory, evidence in the tables below. What the audit changed in the product is the first list; the rest is bookkeeping.

- **2026-10-20 · [parity] `/manifest.webmanifest` is implemented** (P-14, R-18). `apps/site/src/lib/manifest.ts` builds it with the old values (name and short name `SMOG&Co`, Dutch description, `lang` nl, `display` standalone, `orientation` any, `start_url` and `scope` `/`, background `#ebf4eb`, theme `#00805f` taken from the brand token, categories education and reference); `apps/site/src/routes/manifest[.]webmanifest.ts` serves it as `application/manifest+json` (an hour of cache, the Worker's table); `__root` links it once. Dutch only: a manifest is fetched without cookies, so it has no locale to follow. `apps/site/test/manifest.test.ts` pins the values, that every icon it lists exists in `public/`, and the single `rel="manifest"` link: that is the old release check's assertion, kept in a site test instead of `scripts/release-config-check.ts` (Task 1's file).
- **2026-10-20 · [rule change] The icon set is the generated one, not nine `android-chrome-*` sizes** (P-14). The manifest lists `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png`, `icon-maskable-512.png` and `icon.svg`, all produced by `@smog/brand`. Installable-app checks need 192 and 512 plus a maskable icon; the other seven sizes were never needed by a browser that scales.
- **2026-10-20 · [dropped] `browserconfig.xml` and `yandex-browser-manifest.json`** (P-14, R-18). They served legacy IE/Edge tiles and the Yandex browser; their `mstile-*` PNGs and the iOS startup images were never generated here, and no current browser reads them. `/browserconfig.xml` answers 404 (`apps/site/e2e/routes-parity.spec.ts`). The inventory's P-12 `msapplication` tile colour goes with them.
- **2026-10-20 · [parity] `/assets/logo.svg` exists** (R-18). `@smog/brand` generates it (the green logo, `compose.ts`, covered by `outputs.test.ts` and `drift.test.ts`) into `apps/site/public/assets/`, merged with Vite's hashed output. Unhashed, so it keeps the one-day cache of `public/_headers` and the Worker table (Task 6), not the year-long immutable rule, which is by extension for hashed `js`, `css` and `woff2` only (`apps/site/test/cache-policy.test.ts`).
- **2026-10-20 · [test] `apps/site/e2e/routes-parity.spec.ts`** walks the inventory's URL table over HTTP only (the `request` fixture, redirects not followed, about 30 requests, a few seconds): the legacy redirects with their status and `Location` (the re-edit token in the fragment, 302), the canonical pages, `/api/health`, the old `/webhooks/mollie` alias, the OpenAPI spec on dev, and the static and generated files. It stays in the `public` project (no browser, so it adds seconds to that shard, not minutes).
- **2026-10-20 · [test] `apps/site/test/parity-openapi-production.test.ts`** pins P-34 under a production env: every method on `/api/openapi/*` is a bare 404 before the OpenAPI handler is imported (the existing `apiDocsEnabled` test only checked the predicate).
- **2026-10-20 · [changed] P-34: the API reference moved to `/api/openapi`, dev and staging only.** The old `/api-reference` was public; spec §7 keeps the reference off production, where the typed client is the only consumer. The inventory said "no DECISIONS entry"; this is it.
- **2026-10-20 · [changed] X-02: Convex is replaced by D1, R2, KV, Queues and Cron Triggers, with no realtime.** The rewrite's premise (spec §5); live queries become TanStack Query invalidation and `staleTime` (so L-19's `lastUpdated` endpoint is gone). The inventory said "no DECISIONS entry for the whole switch"; this is it.
- **2026-10-20 · [dropped] P-38 and X-15: Docker Compose, Redis, Caddy and GHCR are replaced by Cloudflare** (spec §1 non-goals): a Worker with D1, R2, KV, Queues, a Workflow and a Container, deployed by `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`.
- **2026-10-20 · [changed] X-17: the contact address is `info@smog.vlaanderen`** (`CONTACT_EMAIL`). The inventory listed `hello@smog.vlaanderen` as well; no screen, email or document of the rewrite uses it.
- **2026-10-20 · [process] Section 1.3 has no open item.** All twelve "no DECISIONS entry" rules are recorded under "Rule changes recorded from the feature inventory (2026-09-29)" (guests, category slugs, auth provider, CSV columns, rate limits, Mux webhooks, typo tolerance, route names, web guests, the Favorites tab, legal pages, Turnstile); the audit confirmed each against the code. Item 7's "no explicit successor" is stale: the entry names the Damerau-Levenshtein tier (`typo-gate.test.ts`).
- **2026-10-20 · [process] R-15's note is updated** to Task 7's fragment redirect: 302 `/sponsor/edit#token=<same>`, 301 only without a token.
- **2026-10-20 · [changed] P-23: the developer tools ship as the 5-tap unlock plus the component gallery, on dev and staging builds only. The old Logs section is dropped for now.** The old screen exported, saved and cleared logs from an in-app logger (console patch, a 5000-entry ring, files of 1 MB, ten at most) and showed a viewer. None of that is ported: `apps/mobile/app/settings/developer-tools/index.tsx` shows the gallery entry only. Why: it is a dev and staging tool; the server side has Workers Logs and the structured `[error]`, `[csp]` and `[dlq]` lines, and the app's console is reachable through the Expo dev tools; and a native log ring that persists to the device adds on-device personal-data risk (a log line can carry an email or a token by accident) that the Worker-side log redaction of phase 9 task 8 does not cover. The unused `devTools.exportLogs` i18n keys remain for a later screen. **Carry (phase 10 or after launch, owner: the app team, only if the owners want it):** a redacted in-memory ring with an export action and no file persistence. The inventory row says `changed`, not `done`.
- **2026-10-20 · [changed] P-28: `GET /api/health` replaces `GET /` and `GET /health`** (the Docker and Caddy healthchecks are gone, P-38); it answers `{ ok, environment }` from the vars only and is exempt from maintenance mode (`apps/site/test/health.test.ts`, `apps/site/test/maintenance.test.ts`).
- **2026-10-20 · [not done] Three things the audit records and does not do**, each with an owner and a date, copied into Task 11's owner list (`docs/PROGRESS.md`, "Owner actions in order"; the cutover date is the owner's, so the dates are relative to it, with the step in `docs/cutover-runbook.md` that comes first): (1) **load and abuse testing of the rate limits** (the table is pinned by `scripts/security-guard.test.ts`, the numbers by DECISIONS "Rate limits: the binding only has 10 s and 60 s periods"; a real load needs the staging deploy and the owner's Cloudflare account; owner, before the production cutover); (2) **a cost review** (Workers Paid, Containers, Queues, Mux and Mollie fees against real traffic: needs a month of staging and production usage; owner, one month after the cutover); (3) **a backup-restore drill** (the runbook has D1 Time Travel and the R2 notes; the drill needs a real database; owner, before the cutover rehearsal in `docs/cutover-runbook.md`).
