A column of real links from `md` and a scrolling row of pills below it (`aria-current="page"`, 44 px, the `accent` tint for the open one), so keyboard, back button and deep links work and nothing is a tab widget. A guest sees `preferences`, `language` and `privacy` (a section a guest cannot open falls back to `preferences`); the sign-in prompt stays above the layout. Preferences and Language are the former combined card split in two (theme, language), and need no account read, so they render while the account loads. The provider-link callbacks return to `?section=sign-in`. Alternative: all sections stacked with an anchor nav; rejected because the brief and spec ask for a layout instead of one long page. The e2e specs that used the delete dialog, the consent switch or the password and passkey rows now open the section first.
- **2026-10-22 · The email code is `input-otp`.** `CodeStep` uses the registry `InputOTP` (six slots over one real input named `code`, so paste, autofill with `one-time-code`, the label and `FormData` keep working; spaces in a pasted code are stripped). `InputOTP` now takes the Field's id, description and invalid state through `useFieldControl`, like `Input` (no change to the export or its props). The other auth pages already sat on `AuthCard` with the side panel (Task 3); Turnstile is unchanged.
- **2026-10-22 · Sponsor wizard: logo choice as radios, the review summary as a table.** The logo add-on is a `RadioGroup` (without a logo, or with one at the price), not a lone checkbox, since the choice changes the price and the form below it; the invoice request stays a checkbox (it only reveals fields). The review's summary is a `Table` (row headers for the facts, the total in the footer; stacked rows below `sm`). The stepper, the empty and error states and the price summary were already on kit parts. Alternative: also `react-hook-form` for the wizard; not done, its state machine (`useWizard`) validates on submit and focuses the first error already, and a second form state would duplicate it.
- **2026-10-22 · Legal pages: the contents sit beside the text from `lg`.** A sticky contents list (anchors, numbered) in the left column and the text in a reading-width column; below `lg` the contents come first. Text, anchors, the translation notice and the consent control are unchanged.
- **2026-10-22 · Every route has an error page.** The router's `defaultErrorComponent` is `RouteError` (the kit `ErrorState` with the brand hand, retry), so the routes that set no `errorComponent` (account, auth, legal, sponsor sub-pages) no longer fall back to the framework's bare one. The 404 stays the root `notFoundComponent` (`EmptyState`, level 1, hand).
## Admin on the sidebar and the data-table pattern (phase 10a task 6)

- **2026-10-22 · The admin layout is the registry sidebar, sticky under the site header, with collapsible groups.** `routes/admin.tsx` wraps the screens in `SidebarProvider`; `AdminRail` renders the dashboard link and three collapsible groups (Content: gestures, categories; Community: users, sponsorships; Operations: dead letters, audit, emails, settings; `RAIL_GROUPS` is the one list, the groups of spec 2 and 3 are added to it later, shown by role). From `md` it is a `Sidebar collapsible="none"` that is `sticky top-16` (the registry's fixed full-height panel would sit over the site header and the footer, which the admin keeps), below `md` a `Sheet` driven by the provider's `openMobile`. The toolbar above every screen holds the `SidebarTrigger` (opens the sheet on a phone, hides and shows the panel from `md`, also `Ctrl/Cmd + B`) and the breadcrumbs (`Admin / Section / New | Details`, from the path). The nav keeps its name (`admin.rail.label`, "Beheer") and the active link its `aria-current="page"`, so the roles the specs use did not change. `SidebarInset` is not used: the root layout already owns the page's `<main>`.
- **2026-10-22 · The data-table pattern is built on the kit's `DataTable`, not on TanStack Table.** The kit table already sorts (`aria-sort`), takes row clicks and keeps the sticky header; the five admin tables filter in the URL and page by keyset cursor (`firstPage` / `nextPage`), which is what the API offers (no offset, no total), so the registry's page-number pagination and client-side filtering would be wrong for them. What the pattern still lacked is added in `components/admin/data-table-parts.tsx`: `useColumnVisibility` (per table, remembered in `localStorage` after hydration, so the server render and the first client render show every column and storage that throws changes nothing) with `ColumnsMenu` (a menu of `menuitemcheckbox` rows that stays open, "Show all columns", the name and the actions column pinned), and `RowActions` (a "..." menu with Open for the tables whose row click opens a sheet or page: users, sponsorships, audit; it stops the click and key events at its wrapper, because React bubbles them through the portal to the row's own handler). Gestures keep their own row menu (edit, QR, publish) and dead letters their inline replay and discard, each behind the existing `AlertDialog`; both gain the Columns menu. No `@tanstack/react-table` dependency was added (no new bundle weight in the admin entry).
- **2026-10-22 · The dashboard's chart is plain markup, loaded lazily and only in the browser.** The shadcn `chart` block wraps recharts; a charting library would add about 100 kB gzip to a chunk for six numbers, and the Worker bundle (capped) would carry it as well. The chart is horizontal bars with their values as text (colour never carries a number), the `chart-*` tokens as fills, one group for the sponsorship pipeline and one for the catalogue. It is `lazy(() => import(...))` behind a mount gate (the server render shows the skeleton), so it is its own chunk and not in the admin entry. If the chart ever needs time series, recharts goes in the same lazy boundary.
- **2026-10-22 · The gesture editor stays a page, not a sheet.** The brief asks for `sheet` for editors. The users panel and the audit entry were already side sheets; the gesture editor is a route (`/admin/gestures/$id`, `/new`) with the Mux upload and picker, the optimistic-concurrency conflict screen, a leave guard and its own back navigation, and the e2e and the API docs link to it by URL. Turning it into a sheet would change the route and the behaviours the brief says stay unchanged. Destructive actions keep the `AlertDialog` they already used.

## Mobile screens and tabs (phase 10a task 8)

- **2026-10-22 · The stack headers are the deeper green; the header's own buttons sit in `HeaderActions`.** `useStackHeaderOptions` paints `headerStyle` with `sidebar` and the title, large title and back button with `sidebarForeground` (the pair `contrast.ts` holds for the web header), `statusBarStyle: "light"`; the page below keeps `background`. A screen's `headerRight` (favorite, save to list, share, filter, menu, new list, add) is wrapped in `HeaderActions` (`Surface tone="header"`, transparent), which is what Task 7 left for this task: `IconButton`, `Button` and `Text` read white on the green, and a `source` guard (`a11y-source.test.ts`) fails a `headerRight` that is not in `HeaderActions` or a `Surface`. The auth modal's header is the `primary` green, the colour of the band under it.
- **2026-10-22 · Two small kit changes were needed (`packages/ui-native`, outside the brief's file list).** (1) `FavoriteButton` read `foreground`/`primary` directly, so on the green header the heart was dark on green: it now reads the surface (white, and the orange highlight when set, since green on green would vanish; the same rule as the kit's primary `Button` on a surface). (2) Overlays (`ModalCard`, `PopoverContent`, `SheetPanel`) keep React context from their trigger, so a menu or the list picker opened from a green header would have drawn white text on its white panel: each now wraps its content in `SurfaceReset` (the default tone). Both have tests in `primitives.test.tsx`. Alternative rejected: wrapping only the trigger in a Surface in every screen (it moves the trigger's layout and every future header would need the same care).
- **2026-10-22 · Home keeps its parts and takes the web's order: green hero, categories, "continue where you left off", featured.** The hero is a `Surface tone="primary"` with rounded bottom corners (the reversed logo in `primaryForeground`, the settings button, the headline `home.title`, the tagline and the search field with `home.searchPlaceholder`); the home screen draws its own top and switches the status bar to light while focused. "Continue where you left off" is `useRecentGestures` (the same hook as the site; nothing while empty or when the read fails) and the gesture screen calls `useRememberGesture`. The categories stay `CategoryChips`: native has no illustrated chip yet, and adding one is a kit change for a screen detail. The brand hand is not drawn in the hero (`@smog/brand` is not a dependency of the app; the empty states already carry it).
- **2026-10-22 · "Command-style" search on mobile is the palette's category match, not a dialog.** The web palette is a keyboard overlay; on a phone the search tab already is the full-screen search. It gains what the palette shows besides gestures: while words are typed, the categories whose name contains them (accent-insensitive, as the palette) appear as toggle chips above the results and set the filter. Recent searches stay the command-style rows (icon, text) of the empty field. Results stay the video-first `GestureCard` grid (the kit's `SearchResults`).
- **2026-10-22 · Auth screens are a green band over a page-coloured form.** The sign-in, sign-up, forgot-password and magic-link screens put the logo, the title and the step's hint on a `primary` surface and the form on the page below: inputs, errors (`destructive` text) and the secondary buttons keep their contrast, which a fully green screen would have put at risk for every status tone. The web does the same (the panel is brand, the form is the card).
- **2026-10-22 · The alias names are gone from `apps/mobile`.** `bg-surface` is `bg-card`, `useColor("foregroundMuted")` is `mutedForeground`; `surface-sunken` and `border-subtle` are roles of their own and stay. `a11y-source.test.ts` fails on the alias classes and roles, so Task 9 can remove `ALIASES` without a straggler.

## Phase 10a close (phase 10a task 9)

- **2026-10-22 · Index of the phase 10a sections.** Task 1 "Brand and design system foundations" (colour system, Figtree, brand outputs, the pin of the shadcn source), task 7 "Mobile foundations and primitives", task 2 "Web primitives swap", task 3 "Domain components on the registry parts", task 4 "Web shell, home, gestures and My space", task 5 "Web screens: My space, account, auth, sponsor, legal, errors", task 6 "Admin on the sidebar and the data-table pattern", task 8 "Mobile screens and tabs", and this one. Deviations from the plan, all argued in those sections: the shadcn CLI could not be used (hand-ported from the pin), no TanStack Table, no recharts, the gesture editor stays a page, no illustrated category chips and no hand in the mobile hero, `ui-native` is not copies of Reusables.
- **2026-10-22 · The colour aliases are removed.** `ALIASES` and the five role names it mapped (`surface`, `surfaceRaised`, `danger`, `foregroundMuted`, `focusRing`) are gone from `tokens.ts`, the generated theme and preset, `contrast.ts` and every screen and kit file (`bg-card`, `bg-popover`, `bg-destructive`, `text-muted-foreground`, `ring-ring`; `surface-sunken`, `danger-strong`, `danger-subtle`, `primary-strong` and `border-subtle` have no shadcn equivalent and stay). The alias value-equality test went with them; the contrast pairs stay, with `destructive` in `danger`'s pairs and an explicit `background`, `card`, `popover` list for the status icons (they used `slice(0, 3)` of a list whose head changed). Both `classes.test.ts` now assert that the retired classes build nothing, and `apps/site/scripts/retired-tokens.test.ts` scans every package and app source for the classes, the CSS variables and the role keys (mobile has its own guard too). A retired class would otherwise fail silently: Tailwind builds no CSS for an unknown colour.
- **2026-10-22 · Reverses the task 5 pill row: the account sections wrap.** The row of section pills scrolled sideways at 390 px and the open pill was not scrolled into view. It is a wrapping row now (`flex-wrap`, still the column from `md`): all five sections are visible at once, nothing scrolls sideways at 320 px, and no scroll position has to be managed. The cost is up to three short rows on a narrow phone.
- **2026-10-22 · The gestures search has its own row below `sm`.** The "Filters" button took a third of the 390 px row and truncated "Look up a gesture, for example dog". The field is `min-w-full` (the button wraps under it) below `sm`; from `sm` they share a row again.
- **2026-10-22 · Admin settings no longer overflow at 320 px.** A `grid` with only responsive column rules has one implicit `auto` column, which grows to its widest child (the long bypass button, the date field) and takes the card past the viewport. The settings, maintenance and bypass grids say `grid-cols-1`, and the bypass button wraps its label. (`/admin/emails` at 390 and 320 still scrolls sideways on purpose when the "Mobiel (390 px)" preview width is chosen: the preview is a fixed-width frame.)
- **2026-10-22 · Screenshots: no baselines are committed, and none were invented.** The repository has never held visual baselines (the screenshot specs write PNGs only when `PUBLIC_SHOTS_DIR`, `ADMIN_SHOTS_DIR` or `SPONSOR_SHOTS_DIR` is set, and assert nothing). Task 9 regenerated and looked at the public, account, My space, auth, sponsor and admin screens at 320 and 390 px; the loading, error and offline states of the new screens were exercised by failing every RPC and going offline on `/gestures` and `/my` (the lists came from the query cache, so the stale data stayed visible, as designed; a cold error was not forced). Nothing was looked at on a device.
- **2026-10-22 · Bundle, final (gzip, initial JavaScript per entry, `bun scripts/deploy-guard.ts --bundle --perf-report`).** home 475,552 (budget 504,000; phase 9 left it at 441,721, +33.8 kB, +7.7 %), gestures 476,474 (505,000), gesture 473,398 (503,000), my 492,538 (523,000), sponsor 488,321 (517,000), sponsor-pages 477,229 (508,000), account 475,757 (506,000), auth 480,717 (506,000), legal 491,476 (525,000), admin 495,561 (524,000); Worker 2,019,031 B (cap 5 MiB). No budget was loosened or lowered in the phase. The 200/300 kB targets stay unmet (the shared closure; Post-launch carries).
- **2026-10-22 · Staging's built config is unchanged by the whole phase.** `wrangler.json` hash `5c706b1c16adc89e253bdeaa5d73c1c36b1bbf1438aece8f0ba9799e66fc30bb`, equal to develop before phase 10a.

## Phase 10a review fix wave

- **2026-10-22 · The search palette survives a failed chunk.** `search-palette-provider.tsx` loads the lazy chunk through `loadPaletteChunk` (one retry of the dynamic import) inside an error boundary. When the import still fails (offline after the first load, a stale chunk after a deploy) the boundary closes the palette, shows a danger toast (`searchPalette.loadFailed`) and builds a fresh `lazy` component, because `React.lazy` caches a rejection and only a reload would recover otherwise. Before, the throw reached the root error boundary and replaced the whole page.
- **2026-10-22 · iOS large titles: `headerLargeStyle` is green too.** The stack options painted the large title white but only the collapsed bar green; the expanded large-title area kept the page background, so on the light theme the title was white on near-white. `useStackHeaderOptions` now sets `headerLargeStyle.backgroundColor` to the same `sidebar` green (the test pins it). Not verified on a simulator or device (none here): it needs the device pass in both themes (`docs/PROGRESS.md`).
- **2026-10-22 · The admin gate flake: the cause was an aborted request, the fixes are narrow.** `getAdminGate` (the `/admin` `beforeLoad`) runs on the client for every navigation and every `defaultPreload: "intent"` hover; a `page.goto` that starts while one is in flight aborts it, and the dying document logs `TypeError: Failed to fetch`, which `watchErrors` counted. Fixes: the admin rail links and the breadcrumb link set `preload={false}` (a hover preload ran the gate and the loader for a screen that is usually not opened); `auditScreens` moves the pointer to the corner and waits for network idle before each `openAdmin`; `watchErrors` drops a `TypeError: Failed to fetch` only while the main frame is navigating (from the next document's request to its `DOMContentLoaded`), so the same error at rest still fails a test; `RouteError` logs in an effect instead of the render body (one failure was logged four times). The Vite-reload theory was not supported. The mechanism (a hover preload started by the last pointer position) stays unproven, since the flake did not reproduce locally.
- **2026-10-22 · Toasts raised from a modal are announced; no change was needed, a test pins it.** The review feared that Radix marking the page `aria-hidden` while a dialog is open silences sonner's live region. It does not: Radix's `hideOthers` (the `aria-hidden` package) keeps every `[aria-live]` element and its ancestors, and sonner's `<section>` is `aria-live="polite"`; only the sibling controls are hidden. `toast.test.tsx` raises a danger toast inside a `Dialog` and asserts the region has no hidden ancestor and holds the toast. A second live region inside each modal was built and removed: it would announce every toast twice. If a Radix or sonner upgrade breaks the test, the fix is a visually hidden live region inside the modal content, fed by the toast store.
- **2026-10-22 · The palette says what it is doing.** With words typed, "Show all results" is always listed, so cmdk's empty state never rendered and the search gave no feedback. A `role="status"` line above the list now says "Searching…", the result count, "No gestures found" or (`searchPalette.error`) that the search failed.
- **2026-10-22 · Ctrl/Cmd+B leaves text fields alone.** The sidebar's shortcut listens on `window`; in an input, textarea, select, contenteditable or ARIA text widget it neither toggles nor calls `preventDefault`, so bold and the browser's own shortcut keep working. `isEditingTarget` moved to `@smog/ui-web` and the search palette's `/` uses the same function.
- **2026-10-22 · The admin rail is hidden below `md` in the server markup.** The server renders the non-mobile branch (it cannot tell a phone: `useIsMobile`'s server snapshot is `false`), which showed a 16rem rail beside the page until hydration, a layout shift and, without JavaScript, no reflow at 320 px. The panel carries `hidden md:flex`; the sheet below `md` is unchanged.
- **2026-10-22 · Known deviation: the bare `/` shortcut (WCAG 2.1.4, Character Key Shortcuts, Level A).** `/` opens the search palette from anywhere on the site; the criterion wants a way to turn a single-character shortcut off or remap it, or that it is active only while a component has focus. It has none of these yet. It is accepted for launch because it is not active in text fields, with a modifier, in another dialog or on repeat, every function it triggers is also a visible button (the header search). Fix when it is picked up (Post-launch): a "Keyboard shortcuts" switch in the preferences (stored with the other site preferences) that stops the listener, or limiting the shortcut to when the main landmark has focus. `Ctrl/Cmd+B` has a modifier and is not covered by the criterion.

## Data foundations for translate-everything (phase 10b task 1)

- **2026-10-22 · A rebuild of a referenced table must back up and restore its dependents.** D1 applies a migration inside a transaction where `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` is a no-op (the toggles drizzle-kit generates do nothing), so `DROP TABLE` runs the foreign key actions: a probe on the test D1 showed `DROP TABLE user` cascade-deleting every `session` row, and `sponsor` is the parent of a RESTRICT (`sponsorship`) that would make the drop fail on real data. Migration 0015 (the `user.locale` and `sponsor.locale` CHECK to foreign key rebuild) copies every dependent row (and the `audit_log.actor_id` and `sponsorship_event.actor_id` SET NULL columns) into `_bk_*` tables, deletes what would be restricted (`payment_item`, `sponsorship`), rebuilds, restores in foreign key order and drops the backups; it also creates `user_keep_one_admin` again, byte for byte as 0007. `packages/db/test/migrations-phase-10b.test.ts` seeds every dependent table (and 30 000 audit rows), runs the migrations and compares every table, and applies the last-admin scenarios; a plain generated rebuild fails it. Tables nothing references (`audit_log` in 0023, `gesture_keyword` in 0017) are rebuilt plainly. Run on the real `wrangler d1 migrations apply --local` path from a database at 0014 too. Owner action in the plan stays: record a D1 Time Travel bookmark before the first staging deploy of this migration.
- **2026-10-22 · Languages are data; the locale columns reference `language(code)`.** `language` is seeded with `nl` (default), `en`, `fr` and the Intl tags `nl-BE`, `en-BE`, `fr-BE` that `format.ts` uses today. `user.locale` and `sponsor.locale` (and every translation table) reference it with NO ACTION, so a language in use cannot be deleted; a trigger refuses to delete the default and a CHECK keeps it enabled. The TypeScript `Locale` type is unchanged in this task (the schema keeps `{ enum: LOCALES }` for the two old columns); task 2 widens it with the runtime.
- **2026-10-22 · `gesture_keyword.locale` defaults to `'nl'`.** SQLite cannot take a subquery as a column default, so the column default is the literal of the seeded default language; the FTS definition reads the default language from `language.is_default`. Changing the default language later means a data migration of the base columns anyway (they hold the default language's text).
- **2026-10-22 · `gesture_fts.locale` is the last column.** The bm25 weights (`0.0, 10.0, 5.0, 3.0, 1.0`) are positional; a column added after the five keeps them valid. The migration copies the old rows through a backup table (so the index is exactly what it was, as `nl`) and writes a row for any gesture that had none. `fts.ts` writes one row per (gesture, language): the default language from the base columns and one per `gesture_translation`; a language without a translation has no row, and the search falls back to the default-language row (spec 5.4). Until task 5 the search reads the default language's rows only (a one-line filter in `@smog/gestures`, so a translation written by task 4 cannot add rows the old query would match).
- **2026-10-22 · `user_role` is mirrored to `user.role` by triggers.** Ruling 5 keeps `user.role` as the legacy mirror. Code that still writes only `user.role` (the admin grant script, `admin.users.setRole`, Better Auth) would drift from `user_role` the moment this migration is deployed and before task 2 lands, so the migration also creates two-way mirror triggers (a user is `admin` in `user.role` exactly while they hold the admin row, whichever column is written; every trigger is conditional so they stop after one hop). Task 2 may keep them as the safety net or drop them in a later migration. `user_role_keep_one_admin_delete` and `_update` refuse to remove the last admin whose ban is not in force (`last_admin`, the rule of 0007); the delete trigger only fires while the user row exists, so deleting an account (a cascade) is not refused (`account.delete` guards that, as before). `user_keep_one_admin` on `user.role` stays.
- **2026-10-22 · `legal_text` seeds version 1 from the shipped texts.** The six rows (privacy and terms in nl, en, fr, published, effective `2026-09-30`) are generated from `packages/i18n/src/legal/*.ts` once and embedded in migration 0019 (about 60 KB of JSON; one statement per row, each well under D1's statement size limit). Migrations are append-only, so a later edit of the shipped TypeScript texts does not change the seed: the database is the source from then on and the TypeScript is the fallback. A test checks the shape (sections, anchors identical across languages, the consent control in the privacy notice), not equality with the TypeScript, for that reason. A draft carries the number it will publish as, and a CHECK ties `published_at` to the `published` status.
- **2026-10-22 · New audit kinds.** `AUDIT_ACTIONS` gained `user.role_grant`, `user.role_revoke`, `language.create`, `language.update`, `translation.string_save`, `translation.string_import`, `translation.gesture_save`, `translation.category_save`, `translation.audio_upload`, `translation.audio_delete`, `gesture.recut_done`, `legal.draft_save`, `legal.publish`, `legal.restore`, and `AUDIT_TARGET_TYPES` gained `language`, `ui_string`, `legal_text` (migration 0023, a plain rebuild). The admin audit screen has labels for all of them in nl, en and fr; their `data` schemas are added by the task that first writes each one.
- **2026-10-22 · The dev seed is frozen at 0014 for the older migration tests.** `gesture_keyword` now has `locale` in its primary key, so the regenerated `seed/dev.sql` no longer applies to a schema before 0017. The migration tests that stop before 0015 apply `packages/db/test/fixtures/seed-0014.sql` (the binding `LEGACY_SEED_SQL`); the 0013 test applies only through 0013 (later migrations add triggers and rebuild `audit_log` again).

## Roles end to end (phase 10b task 2)

- **2026-10-22 · The session carries `roles` through Better Auth's `customSession`.** `createAuth` adds the plugin: every session read (HTTP `/get-session` and `auth.api.getSession`) adds `user.roles` from `user_role`, one indexed read per session read on top of the user row, so nothing is cached and a grant or revocation applies on the next request (as a ban already does). The custom session's return type replaces Better Auth's inferred one, so our columns and the admin plugin's are named once (`SessionUserColumns`) to stay on `SessionUser`. The clients need no plugin: `toAuthState` reads `roles` from the session JSON (`AuthUser.roles`, web and Expo).
- **2026-10-22 · `admin` implies every role in one place.** `hasRole(roles, ...allowed)` (`@smog/auth/fields`, client-safe) is the only rule; `requireRoleUser` (session) and `requireRole(...roles)` (rpc middleware) use it; `requireAdminUser` is `requireRoleUser(session, "admin")`, so the admin gate now reads `user_role` (the mirror triggers keep `user.role` in step). `translatorProcedure` and `teacherProcedure` sit beside `adminProcedure` in `@smog/rpc`; the translation tasks build their procedures on them. A translator or teacher is not an admin: the admin gate, the maintenance bypass and the logo upload stay admin-only.
- **2026-10-22 · `users.setRole` is replaced by `users.grantRole` and `users.revokeRole`.** One row per role, audited as `user.role_grant` / `user.role_revoke` `{ role }` (the two kinds task 1 added; `user.role_change` stays in the enum for the history). The row and the audit entry go in one D1 batch, so a role never changes unaudited and a failed audit write changes nothing (the old Better Auth call changed first, then audited). The guards are the old ones on a set: `self` (own account, grant or revoke), `unchanged` (held or not), `lastAdmin` (the read, and the `user_role` trigger of 0020 for the race), `targetBanned` (only for the admin role). A concurrent revoke that finds the row gone still writes its entry (a D1 batch cannot make the audit insert conditional on the delete's row count); the pre-read makes it a narrow race and the entry is true to the admin's action.
- **2026-10-22 · The users list filters by role as a set.** `role` takes `user` (no row at all), `translator`, `teacher` or `admin` (an `EXISTS` on `user_role`'s primary key, no scan); rows carry `roles` from a correlated `group_concat`, one query with no bound ids (D1 caps variables at 100 per statement and a page can hold 100 accounts).
- **2026-10-22 · `admin:grant` mirrors explicitly.** The statements are the update (unchanged) plus `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO user_role ... 'admin'` with `granted_by` NULL. The 0020 triggers already copy a promotion; the explicit row fixes an account whose legacy value said `admin` without a row and keeps the script from depending on the triggers. It still only ever grants `admin`. The dev seed does the same for `admin@smog.test`.
- **2026-10-22 · Kept as is: the account export's `role`.** `account.export` still lists `user.role` (the legacy mirror) in the profile; the user's roles are not a separate export field yet (the export schema is a published contract; the legal review adds it if the DPO wants it). `account.me` and `system.whoami` gain `roles`.
## Runtime catalogue (phase 10b task 3)

- **2026-10-22 · `Locale` is a string; `ShippedLocale` is the union.** Ruling 2 made the default language data. `@smog/config/constants` now exports `type Locale = string` (a language code), `ShippedLocale` (`"nl" | "en" | "fr"`, the languages with a bundled catalogue, still `LOCALES`) and `LOCALE_CODE_PATTERN`; `@smog/config/locale` has `localeCodeSchema`, the shape check every input schema uses (account profile, sponsor wizard, the email message, the local-store preference). Whether a code names an enabled language is checked where the value is used: `isLocale(value, enabledCodes)`, `resolveLocale(sources, enabledCodes, fallback)`, the `setLocale` server function, the RPC context. Two places stay on `ShippedLocale` on purpose: the admin email preview (its sample subjects are built for the three shipped languages) and Better Auth's `user.locale` input validator (`packages/auth/src/fields.ts`, Task 2's file; the profile write path is `account.updateProfile`, which takes any code). The DB columns lost their `{ enum: LOCALES }` typing (the foreign key to `language` is the constraint). Payments (`mollieLocale`) and money (`formatMoney`) fall back for a language they have no table row for (`en_US`, the code itself).
- **2026-10-22 · The merged catalogue: shipped base, `ui_string` overlay on top, nl as the fallback.** `createI18n(locale, overlay?)` builds the i18next resources of the active and the default language, each the shipped JSON with the overlay's flat dotted keys written over it by copy-on-write along each path (`applyValues`: the shared JSON is never mutated, a blank value or a key that would replace a branch with a string is ignored). `returnEmptyString: false` makes an empty value count as missing, so a key resolves overlay of the language, shipped value of the language, overlay of the default language, shipped default value, and never to an empty string or the key name. A language that ships no catalogue (one an admin added) is its overlay over the default language. Plural forms are ordinary keys (`x_one`, `x_other`, `x_many`).
- **2026-10-22 · The overlay is read through `i18n:version` in KV, memoised for 30 s per isolate.** `@smog/i18n/server` (`loadCatalogue`, `loadLanguages`, `bumpI18nVersion`; pure, the D1 queries are injected, `@smog/db`'s `uiCatalogueSource` supplies them) keeps the languages and each language's rows in isolate memory under the version, like `catalog:version`. The version is read with KV's 30 s `cacheTtl` and an in-isolate memo of the same length, so a hot isolate asks KV at most every 30 s (the RPC context, the root loader and the email queue all read it; one KV read per request would have been the cost of the first version); the isolate that bumps sees its own write at once. An edit therefore reaches every other isolate within about a minute or two without a deploy; a D1 write without a bump stays invisible until the isolate is recycled (documented). A failing KV or D1 never fails a page: the shipped languages and no overlay are used and the failure is logged (`[i18n] Failed to load the catalogue`), which is also the offline behaviour of the apps.
- **2026-10-22 · What the browser gets: the overlay rows, not the base.** The root loader (`getShell`) returns `languages` and `overlay` (the active and the default language's rows) next to `locale`; the bundle still carries the shipped catalogue (about 70 kB gzip, unchanged: the perf budgets did not move). `createI18n(shell.locale, shell.overlay)` runs in the root component and in `head()`/`shellHead`, so titles and SSR text use the merged strings. A language change reloads the shell (`router.invalidate()`), which hot-swaps the instance. The shell is loaded once per document (`staleTime: Infinity`), so an edit shows on the next load, not in an open tab.
- **2026-10-22 · Only enabled languages are accepted at the edges.** `resolveLocale` and `isLocale` take the enabled codes (a disabled or unknown language in a cookie or an account falls through to the next source), `matchLocale` matches a whole tag (`pt-BR`) before its primary subtag, the `setLocale` cookie write refuses anything not enabled, and the RPC context (`createRpcContext(..., enabledLanguages)`) resolves the request's locale against the enabled languages, so a disabled language never reaches an email or a payment. The maintenance page keeps the shipped three (it runs without KV or D1 on purpose).
- **2026-10-22 · The language menu lists data.** `LanguageMenuItems` and `LanguageControl` list the shell's `languages` (the `language` rows in order) with each language's own `name`; the `language.nl|en|fr` catalogue keys stay for the label strings that still use them. `INTL_LOCALE` is gone: `formatDate`, `formatList` and `og:locale` read the `Intl` tag registered from the same rows (`registerLanguages`, called where a list is loaded; the shipped tags are the default).
- **2026-10-22 · Emails render through the overlay.** `renderEmail(..., { overlay })`, `deliverEmail({ overlay })` and `processEmailMessage({ loadOverlay })` take the recipient language's overlay; the site's email queue passes `emailOverlay(locale)` (a language that is no longer enabled reads the default language's rows). A loader that throws is logged and the shipped texts are used (`safeOverlay`): a catalogue problem never stops an email. The admin email preview still renders the shipped texts only (carry).
- **2026-10-22 · `system.i18n` is the mobile app's way to the catalogue.** A public oRPC procedure (`{ locale? }` to `{ languages, locale, overlay, version }`, a row in the authz table, behind `RL_API` like every procedure; `@smog/api` gained `@smog/db` and `@smog/i18n` in the boundaries for it) rather than a new route, so it needs no route guard row or perf budget. The app calls it at start through the persisted query cache (`system.i18n` joins the public procedures kept offline, a day, the existing buster), merges the overlay over the bundled catalogue, lists the enabled languages in the language picker and hot-swaps the i18next instance when the answer or the preference changes. Offline or failing, it runs from the bundle. This is the local cache the brief calls "the local store"; the guest-data store (`@smog/local-store`) holds user data and was not given a server-data field and a schema version for it.
- **2026-10-22 · One pure validator, shared with Task 4.** `validateTranslation(base, value, { maxLength })` (not blank, at most 4000 characters, the same `{{placeholders}}` as the source, `count` aside), `pluralProblems(locale, keys)` (every CLDR category of the language, none other, no plain key beside plural forms), `requiredPluralForms`, `splitPluralKey` and `placeholdersOf` live in `packages/i18n/src/validate.ts`; `catalog.test.ts` uses the same helpers, so the parity rules have one definition.
- **2026-10-22 · Writes that store a language check it against `language`.** `account.updateProfile` and `sponsorships.checkout` answer `VALIDATION` (`fieldErrors.locale`) for a well-formed code that is not an enabled language (`isEnabledLanguage` in `@smog/db`), instead of letting the foreign key fail with a 500 or accepting a disabled language; the input schemas only check the shape (`localeCodeSchema`). The email message schema checks the shape too (a malformed code is dropped as invalid; an unknown one renders in the default language).

## Translation admin (phase 10b task 4)

- **2026-10-22 · The audit guard and the entry builder moved to `@smog/rpc/audit`.** `adminGuard` (reads held read-only, a mutation must build and run exactly its mapped audit entry, `markUnchanged`) and `buildAuditStatement` / `trackAudits` / `AuditDataError` were private to `@smog/admin`, and a feature may not import another feature's `./server`. The translation procedures need the same rule, so the code moved as is to `packages/rpc/src/audit-guard.ts` (new dependency `@smog/utils` for `newId`; messages keep the `[admin]` prefix their tests read). `@smog/admin/server`'s `guard.ts` re-exports the names it had, `audit-writer.ts` keeps `auditStatement` / `writeAudit` with the admin schema map, and `AuditDataError` is re-exported from `@smog/rpc/audit`. Two tests import the builder from its new home. No behaviour changed (the guard and audit tests pass unmodified apart from the import path).
- **2026-10-22 · `@smog/translation` is a feature package (`./schema`, `./contract`, `./server`, `./client`).** It joins `FEATURE_PACKAGES`, mounts as `translation` in `appContract`, and imports `@smog/admin/schema` for the audit data schemas and `@smog/admin/contract` for the `AdminProcedures` kind type (both allowed: a feature may use another's `schema` and `contract`). The data schemas of its audit actions (`language.create`, `language.update`, `translation.string_save`, `translation.string_import`) live in `@smog/admin/schema`'s `AUDIT_DATA_SCHEMAS`, because the audit screen parses every stored row with that map. A test keeps the kind table (`TRANSLATION_PROCEDURE_KINDS`) equal to the contract (a `satisfies` plus the authz table row check). Entries carry the key and counts, never a text.
- **2026-10-22 · Who may call what: translator for the strings, admin for the languages.** `translatorProcedure`-style guards (`requireRole("translator")`, an admin passes) sit on the strings, the language list and the coverage; `requireAdmin` on `languages.create` and `languages.update`. The site gate (`getAdminGate`) now lets a translator through (`requireRoleUser(session, "translator")`) and the `/admin` layout narrows them: `/admin` redirects to `/admin/translation/strings`, only the strings screen (and its index) opens, everything else is the 404 page; the sidebar shows the translation group without the language list (`railFor`), the user menu offers "Vertalen" instead of "Beheer". These are conveniences: the procedures refuse again on every call (`apps/site/test/security-authz-translation.test.ts`, `admin-gate.test.ts`, the authz table).
- **2026-10-22 · The strings table is a model over the shipped catalogue plus the `ui_string` rows.** `catalogueKeys` lists every shipped key and, per plural group, the forms every enabled language needs; a form a language does not use is *not applicable* (French `_many` is a dash for Dutch). A cell is the override, else the shipped text, else missing. The default language is the source column and is editable (an edit of it makes the translations of that key stale, never deletes them). The model is pure (`packages/features/translation/src/server/model.ts`), so the table, the coverage, the export and the import share one definition of missing, stale and invalid.
- **2026-10-22 · Stale is `source_hash` of the stored source, not a timestamp.** A saved translation stores a hash of the default-language text it was translated from; it is stale when the source's current hash differs. The hash is two polynomial lanes (16 hex digits, no bitwise operators: Biome refuses them and cyrb53 needs them), only change detection. A translation that exists only in the shipped files has no hash and is never stale (the shipped en and fr texts are not flagged when an admin edits a Dutch text: nothing records what they were translated from). "Confirm" on a stale cell saves the same text again, which refreshes the hash.
- **2026-10-22 · Saving is optimistic-concurrency checked in the batch.** The editor sends `expectedUpdatedAt` (the cell's row timestamp, `null` for no row); the batch is a `failWhen` guard (the row is still the one the editor saw), the upsert or delete, and the audit entry, so a lost race changes and audits nothing and answers `CONFLICT { reason: "stale", current }` with the stored cell. The table shows a save at once (the text is held in the screen until the answer) and reopens the editor with the typed text on any failure; on a conflict it shows the other version with "use their version" and "save mine anyway" (which re-sends with the newer timestamp). Saving the text already stored writes nothing and audits nothing (`markUnchanged`, a `noop` kind); `value: null` reverts to the shipped text.
- **2026-10-22 · Import: a dry run is its own read procedure; apply skips bad rows.** `strings.preview` (a read: no audit, no write, so the guard proves it) and `strings.import` run the same `planImport`, so what the preview lists is what the apply writes. A row with an unknown key, an unknown or disabled language, a plural form the language does not use, wrong placeholders, a text over 4000 characters or a duplicate is listed and **skipped, not applied** (the alternative, refusing the whole file, would block a 2000-row file over one typo; the dry run shows exactly what is skipped); a blank cell is "no translation", never a revert. A file that cannot be read at all is `VALIDATION` with a code. The cap is 2,000,000 characters (the whole catalogue in every language is about 0.6 MB). The writes are `INSERT ... SELECT ... FROM json_each(?) ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, 400 rows per statement (D1 binds at most 100 parameters per statement), all in one D1 batch with the single audit entry (`applied`, `skipped`, `format`), then one `i18n:version` bump. An import overwrites without the single-save `CONFLICT` rule (it is a bulk replace; the audit entry records who and how many).
- **2026-10-22 · Export formats.** JSON is flat (`{ key: text }`, or `{ nl: {...}, fr: {...} }` for all languages); CSV is UTF-8 with a BOM and CRLF (Excel), with `key` and one column per language code, and a `source` column (the default language's text) in a single-language export for context, ignored on import. A cell a spreadsheet would run as a formula (`=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab, CR first) gets a leading `'` that the import takes off again (the same defence as the sponsorships CSV). Exports are reads: not audited (public interface copy, no personal data).
- **2026-10-22 · The table is paged on the server, 100 rows a page.** The brief allowed paging or virtualising; paging keeps the DOM small (a row has a button per language, not a textarea), makes the filters (namespace, text search ignoring case and accents, missing / stale / invalid, per language) one pure pass over about 1,800 keys, and fits the URL (`?q=&ns=&status=&lang=&page=`). `packages/features/translation/test/volume.test.ts` stores every key in every language (about 5,400 overrides) and asserts a page, a search, a stale filter, the coverage and a whole export each stay under 3 s (measured: the three list calls together 0.3 s, the coverage 0.15 s, the export 0.15 s in the Workers pool), plus a whole-catalogue import in one batch.
- **2026-10-22 · Languages are never deleted.** `languages.create` takes a canonical BCP-47 code (`pt-BR`, refused as `pt-br`: the code is the cookie value and the foreign key, so one spelling) and an `Intl`-valid tag; `languages.update` renames, reorders, retags and enables or disables. Disabling hides a language from the menu, the table and the coverage but keeps its translations; the default language stays enabled (`INVALID_STATE defaultLanguage`; the table CHECK says the same). A new language shows the default text until translated (the fallback chain of task 3) and its coverage starts at 0%.
- **2026-10-22 · Coverage counts shipped texts as translated.** `translation.coverage` is per enabled language: `total` (applicable keys), `translated` (an override or a shipped text), `missing`, `stale`, `invalid`. Dutch, English and French are 100% until a text is blanked; the card links each count to the table with the filter set. It is on the admin dashboard and above the strings table.
- **2026-10-22 · The admin email preview reads the runtime catalogue (a task 3 carry).** `admin.emails.list` returns one sample subject per **enabled language** (`Record<code, string>`, was the three shipped) and `admin.emails.preview` takes any well-formed code and renders with the language's overlay (`loadCatalogue`), so a translator sees an edit before the next email goes out; a well-formed code that is not enabled is `VALIDATION`. The screen lists the enabled languages by their own names. `@smog/admin` gains the `@smog/i18n` dependency.
- **2026-10-22 · The sign-up locale falls back to the default (a task 3 carry).** Better Auth's input validator for `user.locale` takes any well-formed code (`localeCodeSchema`); the `user.create.before` hook replaces a code that is not an enabled language (a disabled language, a stale app) with the default language's code (`defaultLanguageCode` in `@smog/db`), never a foreign-key 500. A malformed code is still `400`.
- **2026-10-22 · The cell button's name holds its visible text.** The first axe run flagged `label-content-name-mismatch` (WCAG 2.5.3): a button with `aria-label="Edit key in language"` and the cell text as content. The name is now a visually hidden prefix ("Edit key in language") followed by the visible text, so the accessible name contains the label. The import dialog asks for no dry run while a flat JSON file has no language chosen (the server would only answer `needsLanguage` as a 422, which the browser logs as a console error); it asks the person to choose the language instead.
- **2026-10-22 · E2E: the languages spec is in the `serial` project.** A language added in `admin-languages.spec.ts` shows in the language menu's data and the strings table for every visitor while it is enabled, like the catalogue spec's 100 gestures and the maintenance window, so it runs on the one-worker `serial` project (`SERIAL_SPECS` = `catalog`, `languages`, `settings`, alphabetical: the maintenance window stays last). It disables the language again in `finally` and `afterAll` (a language is never deleted, so a re-run only enables and disables it). The strings spec edits English texts of emails no other spec reads and reverts each override through the same procedure in `finally`. A new e2e seed operation `grantRole` (`translator` or `teacher`, never `admin`, one bound `INSERT OR IGNORE`) makes the translator for the sign-in spec. `ADMIN_PAGES` gained the two screens (axe, the CSP spec and the screenshot harness), and `admin-translation-a11y.spec.ts` covers the editor (also after a refused save), the import plan, the language dialog and the translator's view.
- **Deferred (not in this task):** per-language ordering of the table, an "approve" workflow for a translator's edits, a translators' note per string, deleting a language, and the gestures, categories, audio and legal editors (tasks 5 to 7). The `language.nl|en|fr` catalogue keys are still in the catalogues (mobile reads `language.device` and `language.label`; nothing reads the three name keys any more, a cleanup for the close task).
## Gestures and categories per language, search per locale (phase 10b task 5)

- **2026-10-22 · The catalogue reads take the request's language, not an input.** `gestures.*` use `context.locale` (the `locale` cookie, then `Accept-Language`, only an enabled language), so no procedure input changed and a client cannot ask for a disabled language. The web reaches it through the cookie it already sets; the mobile app sends `Accept-Language` from its stored choice (else the device's, `apps/mobile/src/lib/request-locale.ts`). The cost is that the TanStack Query keys carry no language: a language change resets the cached `gestures`, `favorites` and `lists` queries (`isLocalizedQuery` in `@smog/gestures/client`, called by the web's `setLocale` and the mobile `PreferencesRoot`) instead of threading a locale through every options factory and loader. A persisted mobile cache of the old language is reset the same way.
- **2026-10-22 · Translations are read with primary-key probes in the same statement, and the default language is data.** A summary's name, description, keywords and category names are correlated subqueries on `gesture_translation`, `gesture_keyword.locale` and `category_translation` (`packages/features/gestures/src/server/localized.ts`), `fallbackUsed` is `locale <> default AND no translation row`. The default is the `language.is_default` row (a scalar subquery on `language_default_uq`), not the literal `nl`, so the default language itself returns the base columns and `false` with no second code path. Query plans (nl and fr list, by-slug, search, browse) stay free of full scans (`test/localized.test.ts`, `gesture-plan.test.ts`).
- **2026-10-22 · Per-field fallback.** The name is the translated one or the default; the description falls back to the default when the translation's is blank (a detail page without text would lose its SEO description); keywords are the language's own when the gesture has a translation row (possibly none: nothing is invented), the default language's keywords when it has none. That is exactly what the full-text index holds (a row per language, the default row as the fallback row), so a result's matched field and the keywords shown agree.
- **2026-10-22 · Search (ruling 4).** `gesture_fts.locale = <language>` OR (`locale = <default>` AND no `gesture_translation` row for the language): the fallback is decided by the translation table's primary key (the virtual table's `gesture_id` is UNINDEXED), and a translation write rebuilds the rows in the same batch, so a translation row and its index row exist together. The typo tier reads the language's projection (names, keywords and category names in it, the default text for an untranslated gesture); the isolate keeps at most 8 projections (the enabled languages), all under the one `catalog:version`, least recently used first. `rankGestures`, `typoMatches` and `typoCandidates` take the language and sort ties with `Intl.Collator(locale)` (one collator per language; a tag `Intl` refuses sorts as `nl`). The browse order stays the default language's `sort_name` (the keyset index), as the brief says.
- **2026-10-22 · Translation writes are `catalogTranslation.*`, a translator-guarded contract of `@smog/admin`.** `admin.*` is `requireAdmin` as a whole router (oRPC applies a router's middleware to every procedure), so the writes a translator may make are a second contract in the same package, mounted as `catalogTranslation` in `appContract`: `requireRole("translator")` and the same audit guard (`adminGuard`) as the admin writes, with `translation.gesture_save` / `translation.category_save` (data schemas added; no translated text in an entry). A save is one batch: stale guard, upsert, this language's keywords replaced (an `INSERT … SELECT json_each` of one bound list, because four columns x 30 keywords would pass D1's 100 variables), the full-text rows (`rebuildGestureFtsSql` / `rebuildCategoryGesturesFtsSql`, every language), audit; then a new catalog version. The lock is the translation's own `updatedAt` (`null` when none): two translators of different languages never conflict, and neither conflicts with an admin editing the default text. A save that changes nothing writes nothing (`markUnchanged`, a `noop` kind).
- **2026-10-22 · The admin editor only touches the default language's keywords.** Task 1's carry: `admin.gestures.update` deleted every keyword of the gesture before inserting the new list, which would have erased the other languages'; it now deletes and writes the default language's rows only, the admin read returns those, and the keyword search of the admin list matches them. The importer's keyword keys stay as Task 1 left them (everything it writes is the default language).
- **2026-10-22 · One definition of "needs translation".** `gestureNeedsTranslationSql` / `translationReport` in `@smog/db` (`translation-report.ts`): no translation row, a blank translated description where the default has one, or no keyword where the default has some; categories: no translated name; the default language never needs one; every gesture counts, published or not (a translator should find the hidden ones too). The admin list's `needsTranslation` filter, the per-language list, the report of `catalogTranslation.reports` and the cards all use it, so they cannot drift apart. Task 4's coverage card should call `translationReport` (or `catalogTranslation.reports`) rather than count rows again.
- **2026-10-22 · Where the screens live.** The tabs per language are on the existing admin gesture editor (below the default-text form, each tab saving on its own: the default text beside the fields, a "needs translation" badge on the tab), the category names per language are a card on `/admin/categories`, and the report and the "needs translation in" filter are on `/admin/gestures`: components, no new route (so no perf-budget entry), reusable by Task 4's Translation group. The "needs translation" badge on a public gesture is for translators and admins only (`fallbackUsed` is in the public output, but the learner UI never shows it); the search results and cards do not show it.
- **Deferred or open.** A translation cannot be deleted (the table refuses a blank name), so reverting a gesture to the default text is a later decision. The per-language gesture list is a keyset page of 50 (up to 100); the per-language category list shows at most 200 categories (the admin category list is capped at 100 anyway). Mobile has no admin, so nothing there edits translations; the mobile app only reads the localized catalogue.

## Legal texts in D1 (phase 10b task 7)

- **2026-10-23 · The draft, publish and restore model.** One draft per (kind, language) is the editable row; publish turns it into the next published version and restore (admin only) copies an earlier published version into a new one, so history is append-only and nothing is deleted. There is no "discard draft" procedure: the schema has no audit action for it (migrations are closed at 0023 for this phase) and a draft is invisible to visitors; the editor discards its unsaved changes locally, and publishing or overwriting replaces a stored draft. Carry: add `legal.draft_discard` with the next migration if translators ask for it.
- **2026-10-23 · Versions are numbered at publish.** A draft is created with a provisional number after every existing row and renumbered after the highest *published* version when it is published (one batch, guarded by the draft's `updatedAt` and by that highest version). Reason: the public page takes the highest published version, and a restore made while a draft is open would otherwise take a number below the draft's, so publishing the draft afterwards would not show. A lost race is `CONFLICT` with the stored draft.
- **2026-10-23 · Where the validation lives.** `validateLegalDocument` (`@smog/i18n/legal/markup`, no zod, no shipped texts) is the one check, used by the save procedure, by the editor before the request and by the public loader (a published row that does not validate is logged and ignored, so a hand-edited D1 row can never break or inject into a page). New subpath `@smog/i18n/legal/markup`: everything about legal texts except the shipped texts, so the editor and the page never put the ~22 kB gzip of text in a client bundle. The rules beyond the shape: unique section anchors of the form the old `legal.test` kept (`rights`, `data-retention`), no HTML (`<tag`), links only `https:`/`mailto:`/site path (the `SAFE_HREF` the renderer already used, so a link the validator accepts is a link the page renders), and the privacy text has exactly one analytics switch (a text without it would drop the consent control from the page) and the terms none.
- **2026-10-23 · The public pages read the row through a server function, cached with the i18n version.** `getLegalTexts` (`apps/site/src/server/legal.functions.ts`) reads `loadLegalText` (`@smog/i18n/server`: a per-isolate map of the highest published row per (kind, language), dropped when `i18n:version` changes, which publish and restore bump and a draft save does not), then the shipped text, then the Dutch text for a language with neither. The route loader asks for the site language (from the root route's match) and, for `?lang=nl`, the Dutch text only, so a page carries one document. Because the texts are now read on the server, the client bundles no longer hold them: the `legal` perf budget drops from 525 to 500 kB; the other entries grew by about 1 to 3 kB (the new catalogue keys, the legal editor types in the translation schema) and their budgets rise by the same (home 505, gestures 506.5, sponsor 518, auth 510.5, admin 527), with the new admin route listed in the `admin` entry.
- **2026-10-23 · The consent version is not tied to a publish.** `CONSENT_POLICY_VERSION` stays the code constant it is today ("as today"): a published privacy edit never asks anybody for consent again, and one that does need fresh consent still needs a developer to bump the constant. Deriving it from the published version would re-ask every visitor for a typo fix, and a translator could trigger it (risk R1). Listed in `docs/LEGAL-SIGNOFF.md` (item 9).
- **2026-10-23 · Risk R1 is recorded where the owner signs.** `docs/LEGAL-SIGNOFF.md` item 9 carries the sentence the brief asks for ("the owner accepts that any translator can publish legal texts") with what the platform does about it and what it does not do (no second approval, no comparison with the Dutch text). The Legal screen says it too (an `Alert` above the editor).
- **2026-10-23 · The audit target id is readable.** `legal_text` entries use `<kind>:<locale>:<version>` as `target_id` (not the row's uuid): the audit screen can link it to the editor of that text and language, and the entry stays meaningful when a draft is renumbered. Data: `{ kind, locale, version }` plus `effectiveDate` (publish) or `restoredFrom` (restore); never the text.
- **2026-10-23 · e2e placement.** `admin-legal.spec.ts` (editing, publishing, restoring, the unsafe link, axe over the editor, preview, Dutch source, history and the version dialog, the translator's view) is in the `admin` project (`admin-*.spec.ts`), not in `ADMIN_PAGES`, so the already long `admin-a11y` shard does not grow. The publish test restores version 1 with the shipped date in `afterAll`, so the English terms every other spec reads read as before.


## Perf budgets raised after the phase 10b merges (tasks 4, 5 and 7)

The shared closure grew by about 5 kB gzip once the translation admin (task 4), the per-language gesture code (task 5) and the legal validator and catalogue keys (task 7) were merged together; each task had measured its own budget in isolation. The initial-JavaScript budgets of `home`, `gestures`, `gesture`, `my`, `sponsor`, `sponsor-pages`, `account`, `auth`, `legal` and `admin` in `apps/site/perf-budget.json` are raised to the measurement + 3 kB (rounded up to 0.5 kB; the amounts are in the diff). The cause is mostly the shipped catalogues, which passed 2,000 strings per language (the three catalogues are about 75 kB gzip of every entry). The per-locale split of the catalogues (only the active language in the entry, the others lazy), recorded earlier as a follow-up, is now worth doing and is scheduled in phase 10b task 10; the budgets come back down with it.
## Media: name overlay, needs-recut, audio (phase 10b task 6)

- **2026-10-23 · Audio procedures are flat on `translation.*` (`uploadAudio`, `finalizeAudio`, `deleteAudio`, `listAudio`), translator-guarded, an admin passes.** The brief named `translation.uploadAudio`; the finalize step it asks for, the delete and the per-gesture list sit beside it. `uploadAudio` and `listAudio` are reads for the audit guard (they write nothing); `finalizeAudio` is `translation.audio_upload` and `deleteAudio` `translation.audio_delete` (a clip that is not there is a `noop`). The audit entry names the gesture (`target_type` `gesture`, `name`, `slug`) and the language, never a key or a file name (`MEDIA_AUDIT_SCHEMAS` in `@smog/admin`'s audit schemas, next to the other blocks).
- **2026-10-23 · The upload URL is `sponsorships.uploadLogo`'s mechanism, written again for audio.** A feature never imports another feature's `./server`, so `audio-upload.ts` repeats the R2 presign (300 s, `Content-Type` and the declared `Content-Length` signed) and the signed same-origin fallback, with its own HKDF `info` (`smog-audio-upload`): a logo signature never opens an audio key and the reverse. No new secret, no new binding, no CSP source (the R2 origin is already in `connect-src` when `R2_ACCOUNT_ID` is set, the fallback is `'self'`). Staging's built `wrangler.json` is unchanged.
- **2026-10-23 · The server verifies the bytes, then stores a copy under a fresh key (ruling 6).** `inspectAudio` reads the whole object (at most 1 MiB): an MP3 is a run of MPEG Layer III frames after an optional ID3v2 tag (at least 90 % of the bytes must be frames, so a document with a stray frame header is refused), its duration the sum of the frame durations; an M4A is an ISO media file with `ftyp` first and a `moov` whose tracks are audio only (`hdlr` `soun`), its duration `mvhd`. The declared `Content-Type` must be the type the bytes show, else `format`. `finalizeAudio` then copies the checked bytes to a new `audio/<gestureId>/<locale>/<uuid>` and deletes the upload, the way the checkout claims a logo: a presigned PUT that is still valid cannot change what was checked. The key must name the gesture and language of the call and must not already be stored. A refused upload is deleted. The previous clip of the language is deleted after the batch that replaces its row.
- **2026-10-23 · `GET /api/audio/<gestureId>/<locale>/<uuid>` is public, immutable, ranged.** A splat route (`api/audio/$.ts`, and `api/audio/upload/$.ts` for the fallback PUT) instead of the brief's `audio.$key.ts`, because the key has three segments. A clip is as public as the gesture page that links it. `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (the key changes on every replace; a deleted clip's cached copy is harmless, the page no longer links it), `Accept-Ranges`, `206` and `Content-Range` (offset, length and suffix ranges), `416` for a malformed range, `nosniff`, the sandboxing CSP and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`. `RL_API` per IP (the guard table row). The cache table of `cache.ts` has the row (200 and 206 immutable, everything else under `/api/` stays `no-store`), and `cache-policy.test.ts` pins it.
- **2026-10-23 · The gesture detail carries `audioPath` and `needsRecut`.** `gestures.bySlug` answers `audioPath` (the clip of the viewer's language, a primary-key probe in the same statement, or `null`: never another language's) and `needsRecut`. Summaries do not (a card plays nothing). Both fields are required, so a fixture that builds a detail must say them.
- **2026-10-23 · The name overlay is decoration over a solid pill, never baked into the frame.** `VideoPlayer` takes `nameOverlay` (the localized name) and the caller passes it only while the video is textless (`!needsRecut`) and the viewer has not hidden it. The overlay is `aria-hidden` (native: `accessibilityElementsHidden`, `importantForAccessibility="no-hide-descendants"`), so the frame's own label, the gesture name, is the one accessible reading. The text sits on a solid `sidebar` pill (`sidebar-foreground` on `sidebar` is a pair in `contrast.ts`), so its contrast does not depend on the frame; a scrim gradient would. It fades in (`animate-fade-in`, native `FadeIn`) and the fade is gone under `prefers-reduced-motion`. Taps go through it to the player's controls.
- **2026-10-23 · The viewer's choice to hide it is `preferences.hideGestureName` in the device store.** Optional and additive (stored v2 data still parses, no version bump), set by a switch under the name on the gesture page (web and mobile), shown only on a textless video. Nothing renders from it before the store is read, so a viewer who hid the name never sees it flash; the default (shown) pops in with the fade.
- **2026-10-23 · The audio button never autoplays, loads nothing until pressed, plays one clip at a time.** Web: an `Audio` element made on the first press (a module-level "active" handle stops the previous one); mobile: `useAudioPlayer(null)` and `replace({ uri })` on the first press (`expo-audio` ~57.0.5, the Expo SDK 57 pin; `playsInSilentMode` defaults to true there, so the pronunciation is audible with the ringer off). The label is `media.audio.play` or `media.audio.stop`. A language without a clip shows no button at all (no fallback audio, ruling 6). The mobile URL is the API origin plus `audioPath` (`audioUrl`).
- **2026-10-23 · `expo-audio` is a native module, so the next EAS build is native.** It joins `apps/mobile` and `@smog/ui-native` (a peer dependency, mocked by `@smog/ui-native/jest-setup`: `mockAudioPlayers`). The next mobile build after phase 10b is a native build, not an OTA `eas update` (already true for Figtree, phase 10a). It needs no config plugin entry (playback only: no microphone, no background audio).
- **2026-10-23 · Needs re-cut: a flag, a list, one action.** `gesture.needs_recut` is 1 for every existing gesture (migration 0021) and for a gesture created without saying its video is textless. `admin.gestures.create` takes `textless` (default false; the editor's switch is on for a new gesture), so a new gesture with a textless video is never on the list. `admin.gestures.list` takes `needsRecut: true` (the partial index `gesture_needs_recut_idx` serves the filter and the order, a plan test pins it) and its `counts` gain `needsRecut`, which ignores that filter so the tab's number stays; the list shows a "name in video" badge. `admin.gestures.recutDone` clears the flag with one `gesture.recut_done` entry, keeps `updated_at` (it is no edit of the fields an editor holds, so an open editor does not get a stale conflict), and clearing a cleared flag writes nothing. Replacing a gesture's video does not touch the flag: the admin says so explicitly (the re-cut card).
- **2026-10-23 · Where the screens live.** The audio card (one row per enabled language, the default one included; play, replace, remove; the file input is a visually hidden control behind a visible button) and the re-cut card are on the existing admin gesture editor, no new route and no perf-budget entry. The audio card lists every enabled language, unlike the translation tabs, because the default language has a clip too.
- **2026-10-23 · E2E.** `audio-playback.spec.ts` (public: the button only in the language with a clip, nothing requested before the press, the response's headers, `Range`, the overlay and the viewer's switch, no overlay on a burned-in video), `admin-audio.spec.ts` (admin: upload, replace with an M4A, remove; wrong magic bytes, over 1 MiB and over 10 s refused, nothing stored), `admin-recut.spec.ts` (admin: filter, badge, action, the public page after) and `a11y-media.spec.ts` (a11y: axe at both widths and themes, the overlay as decoration, the fade and reduced motion, no CSP violation). Every gesture the specs create is deleted again.
- **Deferred or open.** An upload that is never finalized (a closed tab after the PUT) leaves an orphan object in `MEDIA`; the daily retention could sweep `audio/` objects that no `gesture_audio` row names and that are older than a day (the logos have such a sweep), not done here. The overlay's contrast was checked by axe on the pill and by the pair table, not over real video frames (the video is a cross-origin player). Audio plays through the platform element on web and `expo-audio` on mobile: no waveform, no seek bar, no download.

## Perf budgets raised again after the task 6 merge (phase 10b)

The media task (audio button, overlay, viewer toggle, new keys) added about 1 kB gzip to every entry on top of the previous raise; the six affected budgets in `apps/site/perf-budget.json` are set to the measurement + 3 kB. This is the last raise by hand: the shipped catalogues (about 75 kB gzip in every entry) are the cause of the creep, and the per-locale split (the active language in the entry, the others behind a lazy import) is scheduled as its own task before the close-out, which brings the budgets back below their phase 10a values.

## Per-locale catalogue split (phase 10b task 7b)

- **2026-10-23 · The browser entry carries no catalogue; each language is a chunk.** `@smog/i18n` holds the shipped catalogues in a registry (`store.ts`): `createI18n`, `buildResources`, `shippedMessages` and `formatList` read what is registered and import no JSON. Two entries: `index.ts` (the full one) imports `shipped.ts`, which registers `nl`, `en` and `fr` statically, and is what the Worker, the tests, the scripts and the mobile app resolve; `index.lazy.ts` is the same API without that import. The site's Vite config (`i18nLazyCatalogues`) resolves the exact specifier `@smog/i18n` to `index.lazy.ts` in the `client` environment only, so the SSR build keeps all three and renders any language synchronously, as before. The browser loads a language with `loadCatalogue` (one dynamic `import()` of its JSON, an `en`, `fr` or `nl` chunk of 27 to 30 kB gzip). `keys.ts` takes the `Messages` type from a type-only import of `nl.json`, so types still derive from nl and `catalog.test.ts` is unchanged. The `ui_string` overlay (task 3) is applied on top of whatever is registered, as before.
- **2026-10-23 · Where a catalogue is loaded.** (1) `src/client.tsx`, a custom browser entry (Start's default plus one step), awaits `ensureCatalogues(document.documentElement.lang)` before `hydrateRoot`: the server-rendered page is on screen in the right language meanwhile, so there is no flash and no hydration mismatch; interactivity waits for one extra request (the chunk is not preloaded: its URL is only known to the bundler, a documented cost of about one round trip). (2) The root route's `loader` awaits `ensureCatalogues(shell.locale)` after `getShell`, so a language change (`router.invalidate`) or a sign-in that brings the account's language loads the new chunk before the new shell swaps in: no reload, no render with an empty catalogue. It is a no-op on the server and for a loaded language. (3) `/dev/ui?lang=` loads its language in the gallery's lazy boundary and in `beforeLoad`. `ensureCatalogues` also loads `nl` for a language that ships no catalogue (an overlay-only one), whose missing keys fall back to it; a shipped language needs no fallback because en and fr match nl key for key (`catalog.test.ts`).
- **2026-10-23 · Mobile keeps all three.** `setup-native.ts` imports `shipped.ts`: the app starts offline in any language and has no web budget. The registry's dynamic `import()` of the JSON is statically resolved by Metro; the native bundles did not grow beyond that.
- **2026-10-23 · The budget now counts the active language's chunk.** `perf-budget.ts` adds the largest `locales/*.json` chunk of the manifest (fr, 30.3 kB gzip) to every visit it measures, because a page cannot render without one lazy catalogue; without it the split would have looked like a 75 kB saving where about 53 kB is real. `perf-budget.test.ts` pins it.
- **2026-10-23 · Numbers (gzip bytes of initial JavaScript, `bun scripts/deploy-guard.ts --bundle --perf-report`).** Before (three catalogues in the entry) and after (one counted):

  | entry | before | after | saved |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | home | 508,718 | 455,300 | 53,418 |
  | gestures | 509,645 | 456,213 | 53,432 |
  | gesture | 506,569 | 453,151 | 53,418 |
  | my | 525,714 | 472,301 | 53,413 |
  | sponsor | 521,501 | 468,065 | 53,436 |
  | sponsor-pages | 510,227 | 456,812 | 53,415 |
  | account | 508,946 | 455,552 | 53,394 |
  | auth | 513,864 | 460,430 | 53,434 |
  | legal | 503,262 | 449,857 | 53,405 |
  | admin | 529,941 | 476,516 | 53,425 |

  A visitor in Dutch or English loads 1.4 to 3.6 kB less than the counted worst case. The Worker is unchanged in kind (2,137,769 before, 2,140,562 after, the registry and the chunk indirection). Every budget in `perf-budget.json` is set to the measurement + 3 kB (rounded up to 0.5 kB), 59 kB under the values raised by hand after the task 6 merge. The 200/300 kB targets are still not met; the closure that blocks them (TanStack/React 132 kB, `@smog/ui-web` 104 kB, one catalogue 30 kB, the zod schemas 26 kB) is in each entry's `note`.
- **2026-10-23 · E2E.** `shell.spec.ts` gains "only the active language's catalogue loads; a language change fetches the next one without a reload" (a catalogue request log: `nl` alone on first load, `en` after the menu change in the same document, `en` alone after a reload). The layout-shift specs, the language-switch specs and `gesture-languages.spec.ts` pass unchanged. They run against the Vite dev server, which serves the JSON modules unbundled; the production chunking was checked in the build output (three chunks in the manifest, the entry reads `<html lang>`), not in a browser.
- **2026-10-23 · Pages that label text in another language load that catalogue.** The e2e run (`legal-links.spec.ts`) found the one place the site translates with `lng:` into a language other than the active one: the legal pages (the notice's Dutch link, "last updated" and "contents" in the text's language) and the admin legal preview. Before the split the default language was always in the instance; now it is not. `legalLoader` loads the catalogues of the site language, the text's language and `nl` (server and client navigation); `client.tsx` adds `nl` before hydrating on `/privacy` and `/terms` (a first load never runs the loader in the browser); `LegalWorkspace` loads the edited language and `nl` before it renders; the page attaches the loaded bundle to the instance (`useShippedBundle`, values already there, the overlay, win). A new `t(..., { lng })` into another language needs the same.

## Perf budgets re-measured after the catalogue split merge (phase 10b task 7b)

The final fix of the split (the legal pages and the legal editor load the catalogues they label themselves with) added about 6 kB gzip to every entry after the task had set its budgets. The budgets in `apps/site/perf-budget.json` are set again to the measurement + 3 kB; they stay about 53 kB below the hand-raised values of the earlier 10b merges.
